# Source and binary investigation record

## R580.196.07 class-list error path and R595.112.07 GV100 UVM closure — 2026-11-03

The R580.196.07 `nvGpuOpsGetGpuInfo`/`findDeviceClasses` path is now audited beyond selector field names. The v7 source parser checks `MC_GET_ARCH_INFO` status before assigning `gpuArch`, exactly two class-list queries, propagation of the sizing-query error, allocation failure before buffer use, propagation of the class-list read error before scanning, freeing on the shared error path, and early cleanup by the caller. The exact source archive SHA-256 is `21914a460f17190c43cffb75f0c35aef957e267bfefea267bc18638beae17084`; the pinned producer member SHA-256 is `98e4fcca21a17eb9b52793805f4d3a0f8ab9468a4f7154c0eddc2714b1443722`.

For the R595.112.07 candidate overlay, all six required GV100 table rows and selector fields are present, and its compiled UVM module resolves all **85** selected callbacks with no missing symbols, missing source definitions, or duplicate definitions. The candidate module hash is `dea278c8e2593ebf5054da6ff22622f6c2fd8e4a6c9186973a70222d481f99f5`; the path-free [receipt v3](../evidence/r595112-rm-gpuinfo-to-hal-module-closure-sanitized-v3-20261103.json) has SHA-256 `aa647f308097cfc2f87b95301cdc5f401e3f5cccc7cffafd84a2850216a3347f`. Focused parser tests pass **18/18** and receipt-sanitizer tests pass **6/6**. The full CPU/software suite passes **2,745/2,745** in **183.491 s**; see the path-free [suite receipt v6](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-driver-sanitized-v6-20261103.json), SHA-256 `d658477988f298dafae098f1d87e7e806982c654366f8266e43765317d5fcb48`. This remains `ANALYZED_STATICALLY` source and compile-time closure only: live RM values, UVM table initialization, module loading, GPU execution, CUDA, and hardware stability are unobserved.

## Closed-binary license boundary — 2026-11-03

The local R595.112.07 installer license clause 2.2 prohibits reverse engineering, decompiling, or disassembling its binary software. During this turn, a premature REA/Ghidra CLI attempt targeted its proprietary kernel object before the license was rechecked. The private ignored pseudocode and cross-reference outputs were removed; no binary-derived facts were retained in project evidence or used to support a design decision. No further closed NVIDIA binary analysis will be run without applicable written authorization. REA/Ghidra remains available for project-owned code and artifacts whose license permits analysis; NVIDIA work continues through public source, published interfaces, and authorized hardware observations.

## R595.112.07 modeset return-thunk differential — 2026-11-03

The exact Open GPU Kernel Modules commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2` has a conditional `-mindirect-branch=thunk-extern` flag in `src/nvidia-modeset/Makefile`, but no `-mfunction-return=thunk-extern`. The pinned source Makefile SHA-256 is `138c4f2777e03c68f5b2ffc1a7b31902af513315fda2b0522b93fb879db1f0a6`. Patch [`0002-open-modeset-rethunk-return-thunk.patch`](../patches/r595.112.07/0002-open-modeset-rethunk-return-thunk.patch) adds the same compiler-tested return-thunk flag to that x86 conditional. Its postimage SHA-256 is `c0a924e120846ea6e83c984a791f71afb0eda27f091bd25c7268cf31c28914e9`; the exact replay and drift-rejection tests pass **2/2**.

The controlled A/B rebuilt `nvidia` plus `nvidia-modeset` from separate copies of the same hybrid source with the same Linux 7.0.0-38 headers, configured GCC 15.2, environment, and `KBUILD_MODPOST_WARN=1`. The unpatched mode build produced 2,685 `naked return` RETHUNK warnings and 251 frame-pointer warnings. With the new return-thunk flag, the RETHUNK count fell to zero, while 1,715 frame-pointer call warnings and 236 “BP used as a scratch register” warnings remained. A separate probe adding `-fno-omit-frame-pointer` yielded zero objtool warnings. This shows the option's code-generation effect, but does not establish that the extra frame-pointer code is a performance-neutral production fix. The candidate module also retains a modpost warning for unresolved `nvidia_get_rm_ops` under this reduced link; the intended frontend bundle must resolve that dependency before any module-load test.

The [sanitized receipt](../evidence/r595112-modeset-rethunk-ab-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `6af94199de158c343557de3aef6dc5a5e799ce4039d409ba0b0e4e44903a793e`, binds the source, final patch, build-patch postimage, logs, and module hashes. Raw build logs are private and ignored. No module was installed or loaded; there was no GPU, CUDA, stability, or performance validation. These are compile/objtool results only.

Scope correction: the selected static inference bundle contains `nvidia`, `hybrid580_legacy`, `hybrid580_open`, and `nvidia_uvm`; it does not contain `nvidia-modeset`. The unresolved `nvidia_get_rm_ops` warning is specific to the reduced test link pairing R595 modeset with the renamed `hybrid580_open` object, not a failed import audit of the selected four-module bundle. Keep this patch as a separate source-build experiment unless a target stack actually selects the R595 modeset module.

After the final patch-context normalization and exact-patch hash assertion, the full CPU/software suite passed **2,735/2,735** in **182.986 s**. The runner verified **415** test sources and **164** implementation inputs were unchanged. Its sanitized [software-suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-driver-sanitized-v3-20261103.json), SHA-256 `a5bfdef18b6ded41eb3952d3f0522a6cb164f992ce3ed65f6f2f3c22dd040f76`, is qualified software-suite-only; it provides no additional driver-runtime or GPU evidence.

## Split-provider UVM owner-routed initialization handoff — 2026-11-03

The selected architecture runs an open RM provider for Blackwell and a separate R580 proprietary provider for Volta behind one UVM callback bridge. The bridge routes `nvUvmInterfaceGetGpuInfo` through `hybrid580_uvm_gpu_route_get_info` by UUID owner, creates the RM device through that owner's native session, then routes `nvUvmInterfaceGetFbInfo` by the opaque device handle recorded at creation. The alternative standalone R595 UVM candidate's `init_parent_gpu` class-table sequence applies only if R595 itself owns the GV100 RM calls; it must not be treated as the selected split-provider V100 runtime path.

`tests/fixtures/hybrid580_uvm_attach_handoff_test.c` composes the same production route helpers with two synthetic UUIDs and keeps both returned device handles live at once. Its legacy path carries the source-derived R580 GV100 selector tuple (`gpuArch=0x140`, implementation `0x1`, host `0xc36f`, CE `0xc3b5`). The open-provider tuple consists only of synthetic routing sentinels; it makes no claim about Blackwell RM values. The fixture checks registration, info retrieval, device creation, `GetFbInfo`, owner-specific destruction/unregister, and that an owner error does not fall back to the other RM. It passed with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` and UBSan; the focused route suites pass **4/4**.

The pinned full CPU/software suite passes **2,715/2,715** in **182.693 s**, with 410 test inputs and 160 implementation inputs unchanged. Its [sanitized receipt](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid580-dual-owner-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `1c3f8f625f22ba97be3b19623ec38763b855d7be736908cb8bd27eaa12ffe5a5`, is software-only. The fixture tests helper composition, not the full callback module, RM services, kernel UVM ABI at runtime, a loaded module, a physical GPU, CUDA, or concurrent GPU execution. The R580 tuple remains source-derived rather than captured from a running V100 RM.

## Split-provider four-module closure and warning inventory — 2026-11-03

The selected R595 frontend/UVM plus R595 open and R580 legacy provider set passes a static four-module dependency/CRC closure for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`: 311 frontend imports, 305 legacy imports, 461 open imports, and 331 UVM imports. The isolated depmod run resolves the two providers before the frontend and UVM. The path-free [build receipt](../evidence/hybrid-r580-r595-split-provider-build-sanitized-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `b1c8925aa5e467d75590f96ffcfebd8c0ba240b12aead3f0790f3a41bd85eaf0`, verifies the four artifact hashes, public R595 UVM ABI binding, cross-provider CRC closure, and available build-log hashes.

The receipt counts **57,906 build-warning lines** across the four modules. The R580 report partitions 46,746 lines into 44,978 naked-return objtool warnings, 1,756 frame-pointer warnings, and 12 aggregate warnings. The R595 open-provider log contains 11,157 lines, of which 11,156 remain unclassified in this receipt; UVM records two lines, one identified as a pahole-version mismatch and one unclassified; the frontend report records one pahole-version mismatch. The receipt preserves **11,169 unclassified lines** overall. The counts do not attribute opaque proprietary-core symbols or establish warning impact. No proprietary binary was reanalyzed for this inventory.

### Warning-message classification follow-up — 2026-11-03

The receipt's large “unclassified” count was a limitation of its first message classifier. A fresh pass with the v3 classifier binds the R595 open and R580 legacy warning classes to the same raw-log SHA-256 values. The R595 open-provider log's 11,157 lines classify as 11,141 `objtool` naked-return diagnostics, 8 `-Wmissing-prototypes`, 7 `-Wexpansion-to-defined`, and one pahole-version mismatch. The R580 log's 46,746 lines classify as 44,978 naked-return and 1,756 frame-pointer objtool diagnostics, 7 `-Wexpansion-to-defined`, 3 `-Wunused-variable`, one `-Wunused-result`, and one pahole-version mismatch. Both hash-verified logs now have zero unclassified warning lines. The [classification audit](../evidence/split-provider-warning-classification-audit-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `14a43430dc5fc1913b708afa9f73503813ccc86de8c4266bb32eb49fc511b4c7`, retains the old receipt hash and source-log hashes without local paths.

The prior receipt-bound UVM log (SHA-256 `d482cb7523d264d11ec75d1f1ded81655c6b2676049c36f17eeba56a52ddc36e`) was not recovered in a bounded search of preserved log candidates. A separate current UVM build log has a different hash and is not substituted. The one previously unclassified UVM line therefore remains unreconciled, so the old `11,169` figure is no longer an accurate description of the verified open/legacy message classes, but a complete four-module reclassification is still unavailable.

The current kernel configuration has `CONFIG_MITIGATION_RETHUNK=y` and `CONFIG_OBJTOOL_WERROR` disabled. NVIDIA's open-module maintainer describes this warning as a known RETHUNK interaction: it can remain non-fatal when objtool warnings are not errors, and NVIDIA identified a driver-side bug still requiring a proper fix. Linux's hardware-vulnerability documentation explains why safe return thunks matter for speculative-execution mitigations. Accordingly, these are now *classified but unresolved* warnings; this project neither suppresses them nor treats them as harmless. The module was not installed or loaded. See [NVIDIA issue #1021](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/issues/1021) and the [Linux RSB mitigation documentation](https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/hw-vuln/rsb.html).

The summarizer now emits warning categories by message type, checks the R580 aggregate counts against its raw log, and keeps unknown lines explicit. Its focused mutation tests pass **11/11**. This improves classification only; it does not change module bytes, build flags, runtime status, or the need to analyze the return paths before a canary.

### RETHUNK return-site correlation with REA/Ghidra — 2026-11-03

REA 6.8.0 with Ghidra 12.1.4 and the available JDK 21 analyzed the exact `nv-kernel.o_binary` whose SHA-256 is `c19870e43606b4358d9274c4d3dec2adb1822d65ce1203520a8184cf043cafe8`. The Ghidra-selected instance of `resIsPartialUnmapSupported_d69453` has a 7-byte body at `0xcde8b0`; its instructions are `ENDBR64` at `+0x0`, `XOR EAX,EAX` at `+0x4`, and `RET` at `+0x6`. The hash-verified R595.99.02 objtool log (SHA-256 `0c336ed6320f9c27f2a24f90a4dc671b5f9f3ea135f2a101693fc7d80036d9a6`) contains 94 warnings for this symbol at `+0x6` in linked `hybrid580_open.o`; `nm` finds 94 definitions of the symbol in the opaque input. A sanitized [correlation receipt](../evidence/r595099-open-rm-rethunk-ghidra-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `54adaf3703d79391fbf8567bf868035e0a21552dc176fe2c234ceb78648809f9`, records these facts and the input hashes without pseudocode or machine-local paths.

This is instruction-level static evidence that the opaque object contains a matching direct return, and a strong symbol/offset correlation to the linked-module warnings. It does not prove section-level linker provenance for every duplicate, that all 94 copies are byte-identical, exploitability, or kernel/module safety. The source-provenance audit remains name-based; no module was loaded. The new summarizer rejects mismatched artifacts, logs, provenance receipts, or return offsets; its focused tests pass **5/5**.

The preceding pinned CPU/software suite passed **2,726/2,726** in **179.332 s**, with 411 test inputs and 161 implementation inputs unchanged. Its path-free [suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid580-warning-receipt-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `0579b13bcd31e4862544ad3dbba696b1871e188b1bd779212a3b28549e4d56dd`, is qualified `software-suite-only`.

After adding the targeted RETHUNK correlation audit and tests, `python3 tools/run_software_test_suite.py` passed **2,733/2,733** tests in **183.153 s**; all 413 test inputs and 163 pinned implementation inputs remained unchanged. The [sanitized suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-driver-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `1cf4371485b02d061f9c9fc324c1ecabdcc011569c1f207bdf08a57784d767a8`, records the run as `software-suite-only`. Raw report and log remain private and are excluded from source control. This test result provides no module load/install, RM initialization, GPU access, CUDA kernel, UVM runtime, P2P, endurance, or performance evidence.

## R595.112 GV100 prefetch semantics and minimal callback reuse — 2026-11-03

A targeted review invalidated the first v11 overlay's claim that restoring UVM_ASSERT(parent_gpu->prefetch_fault_supported) restores a production capability guard. In the exact R580.196.07 and R595.112.07 sources, ordinary UVM_ASSERT is enabled only for debug/Coverity builds; the release branch expands to UVM_ASSERT_MSG_IGNORE. REA 6.8.0/Ghidra 12.1.4 and objdump of the project-built v11 wrappers corroborated the finding: the release wrappers call the Turing callbacks but contain no load of the capability field or conditional branch. The decompiler output is not treated as recovered source.

R580.196.07 initializes prefetch_fault_supported to true for GV100. R595.112.07 initializes its runtime fault_buffer.prefetch_faults_enabled state to true before replayable-fault setup. After removing the R580 debug assertion, the enable action, disable action, and shared register helper match the R595 Turing callbacks. The minimum overlay therefore leaves the R595 structure and Volta source untouched and maps the GV100 HAL directly to the existing callbacks; it adds no private capability field or wrapper.

The v12 builder pins the R580.196.07 archive SHA-256 21914a460f17190c43cffb75f0c35aef957e267bfefea267bc18638beae17084 and R595.112.07 commit d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2. It compiles and links the candidate for Linux 7.0.0-38-generic, with all six explicit parentless GV100 HAL rows and 85 callback references resolved. Candidate module SHA-256: ca7fcf8fc5cb1dfe0254273eff60f045a38e446db7f64b011730a8b0f18eaaf9; raw report SHA-256: af5fdfb8b938ed79fb909591a3df1b3658539f0fa0d26d0defaac7d0c6b396f7. The path-free [prefetch-semantics receipt](../evidence/r595112-gv100-prefetch-semantics-sanitized-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 20bdf5fc7f260161c21184e7bf8f6a45bf2a1cc8649bfd882b1db337d9c8c967, records source hashes from both release branches and leaves runtime/hardware validation false.

A second isolated build in a different scratch directory has the same 28 allocated ELF sections as the first, excluding the per-build ID note. Only .debug_line_str, .rela.debug_info, and .note.gnu.build-id differ; whole-file byte identity is false. The path-free [ELF comparison](../evidence/r595112-gv100-prefetch-semantics-elf-compare-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 a29a3cc3a1d8271b8d45658a78443d734c820ef3e641f174acbbea4c73eccfcf, qualifies the two builds as equal in loadable sections except build ID.

Focused tests pass **33/33** (27 HAL-builder tests and 6 receipt tests). The full pinned CPU/software suite passes **2,706/2,706** in **188.596 s**, with **407** test sources and **159** implementation inputs unchanged; its path-free [suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-r595-prefetch-semantics-sanitized-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 6261b4111e4a7c5a937160ac26881e67c6d1a33fae393fd0eee2e50496448ad5, is qualified software-suite-only.

This is source analysis, static ELF comparison, and compile/link evidence only. The candidate was not installed or loaded; no GPU, UVM runtime, CUDA kernel, stability run, or performance measurement was performed. Static callback/action equivalence does not establish that all R595 runtime dependencies required by GV100 have been restored.

## R580/R595 exact-source fault-buffer lifecycle host harness — 2026-11-03

The host harness compiled and executed the pinned fault-buffer source functions from R580.196.07 and R595.112.07 with synthetic RM, allocator, tracker, HAL, and ownership collaborators. It passed 420 assertions (210 per implementation) across 15 vectors per implementation under `-std=gnu11 -O2 -Wall -Wextra -Werror`. The [path-free receipt](../evidence/volta-fault-init-r580196-r595112-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `0916d2e929066abd6fe091e4b86fedcfc648cb2355e2b40e8748d44436720df1`, binds the raw private report and log by digest and pins both source profiles.

The comparison confirms the R580 GV100 prefetch capability is initialized true; R595 has removed that legacy field and initializes its runtime prefetch state separately. The disabled-prefetch R595 vector is explicitly a synthetic counterfactual. The test does not execute Linux UVM ABI, RM, a module, GPU, DMA, interrupt delivery, CUDA, or a live fault. It validates selected source-level lifecycle functions only.

The receipt generator validates exact release hashes, normalized lifecycle hashes, assertion set, counts, compiler flags, status, and a timezone-bearing timestamp. Mutation tests ensure source drift, changed counts, a runtime claim, or path-bearing timestamp cannot be promoted to a passing portable receipt. A separate source inspection records the R595 attach order: RM `DeviceCreate` and `GetFbInfo` occur before UVM HAL selection; after the six required CE/host/architecture/fault/access-counter/SEC2 rows are selected, it initializes HAL properties, MMU chunk sizes, optional HMM devmem, per-GPU state and ECC, then discovers peers. Missing required class rows fail closed with `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS`. The candidate's static rows do not validate those pre-HAL RM calls or actual GV100 class values.

The same source trace exposed an outdated limitation string in the v12 builder claiming the removed Volta prefetch member and wrapper were still present. `candidate_limitations()` now describes direct R595 Turing callback selection and preserves the runtime boundary; its regression test passes. The fresh complete software suite passes 2,714/2,714 with 408 test and 160 implementation inputs unchanged; [suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-v12-limitations-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `b055b30c1665903f6361a75b1719875c1866035871e736e5a3912a37c8a0cdb1`, is software-suite-only.

## R595.112.07 RM-to-UVM producer boundary — 2026-11-03

An exact-source trace of NVIDIA Open GPU Kernel Modules commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2` identifies the RM/UVM data boundary: `uvm_gpu.c` calls `nvUvmInterfaceGetGpuInfo`; `nv_uvm_interface.c` forwards through `rm_gpu_ops_get_gpu_info`; `nvGpuOpsGetGpuInfo` asks RM for architecture and implementation using `MC_GET_ARCH_INFO`, then resolves device classes including host and copy engine. The candidate `uvm_hal_init_gpu` uses the returned architecture and class selectors for exact table dispatch. This is established from public C source, not from a decompiled RM implementation or a live GPU.

The 11-field R580-to-R595 candidate-path receipt records the per-property source disposition. Its P2P scan is bounded to **625** candidate C/H files and reports no RM P2P producer definition there. It proves the open UVM consumer's direction index use only; producer semantics and values remain unknown. [Receipt](../evidence/r595112-volta-capability-paths-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `8bd3f32390483337b94a6b08fe4ca5cdad1f514df7dbb5204b911e0f4d4cbceb`.

REA 6.8.0 is available from the local pnpm cache; with Ghidra 12.1.4 and JDK 21.0.13, the CLI provider check reports Ghidra available. The REA MCP tools are not exposed in the current session, so analysis is using the supported CLI with explicit `GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR` and `JAVA_HOME`.

The local R595.112.07 installer includes an NVIDIA Driver License Agreement whose clause 2.2 restricts analysis of its binary software. The local copy is `build/NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-595.112.07-no-compat32/LICENSE` (ignored; SHA-256 `c692fb14ad4f499c6aee1b9e144e6905148c420e6225b17cb2937366c1e507ad`). An earlier private REA/Ghidra output and the later premature attempt are excluded from evidence and not used for conclusions; see the dated boundary entry above. Further closed-binary analysis is suspended absent applicable written authorization. Continue through public source, published interfaces, licensed open-source binaries, and authorized hardware observations.

REA/Ghidra 6.8.0/12.1.4 with JDK 21.0.13 completed a bounded analysis of `uvm_hal_init_gpu` in the exact project-built R595.112.07 `nvidia-uvm.ko`, SHA-256 `7284d09d2f3ddafc24805ce9702d3bc596a3f2919468f79c5d9a9ac586f3cd48` (`Dual MIT/GPL`, vermagic `7.0.0-38-generic SMP preempt mod_unload modversions`). The bounded pseudocode body refers to `gpuArch` (5 occurrences), `hostClass` (2), and `ceClass` (2). The path-free [receipt](../evidence/r595112-open-uvm-ghidra-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `972550cce64ab7047997ab4dda18326ddf370dd0af93efe0de88750ec75f0632`, retains artifact/tool hashes and aggregate field counts, never pseudocode or source paths. This does not show successful class lookup, callback dispatch, module initialization, UVM behavior, or hardware execution. The pinned source remains the authoritative explanation of the missing GV100 table rows.

## GV100 GET/OVERFLOW register contract across branches — 2026-11-03

The NVIDIA-published Volta `src/common/inc/swref/published/volta/gv100/dev_fb.h` was inspected in the R595.99.02, R595.112.07, and R615.78.08 source snapshots. All three files have SHA-256 `d2590fe14ddf7b2a7d388612c66319017b6bb3db797b7aaabb54b40eb4ee0900`; the R595.112.07 copy is pinned at commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2` ([official source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2/src/common/inc/swref/published/volta/gv100/dev_fb.h)), and the R615.78.08 copy at commit `1a82fbfda8f321b056eb3568850f9316c5e465c` ([official source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/1a82fbfda8f321b056eb3568850f9316c5e465c5/src/common/inc/swref/published/volta/gv100/dev_fb.h)). The map defines the GET register as `0x00100E2C + i*20`, two instances, PTR `19:0`, GETPTR_CORRUPTED `30:30`, and OVERFLOW `31:31`; both status CLEAR values are `1`.

The UVM `hwref/volta/gv100/dev_fb.h` used by the R580.178.04 baseline and R595.99.02 Volta compatibility overlay is separately pinned at SHA-256 `0175a270fa5af9d3a62d7fda2c9e536b58551db6bb1e9c123b84ffeec9a32584` and carries the same field values. The source-pinned v5 host harness records these fields, compiles the exact Volta GET/PUT callbacks and replayable-fault service bodies, and exercises synthetic register words. It passes 244 CPU checks and both modeled release-assert probes. Its path-free [receipt](../evidence/volta-gv100-get-register-cross-branch-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `44f73bdbab86076bad3330e69f414ff071a4208be502a496a531d9feca6dd235`, excludes paths and pseudocode.

This result supports reuse of the specific GV100 register layout; it does not establish native Volta dispatch in current R595, physical overflow behavior, MMIO ordering, or successful UVM/GPU execution. The current full software-suite result is [2,663/2,663](../evidence/software-verification-test-import-closure-sanitized-v2-20261103.json), SHA-256 `e5e7dcc94f3268638274e8c7b9984e248a6f5a888a98c06f5b10b7bcf3d7553b`, and remains software-only.

## REA/Ghidra setup and NVIDIA binary-license boundary — 2026-11-02

REA `rea-agents` 6.8.0 is present in the ignored project tool cache at `build/tools/rea-6.8.0` and is invoked from its local `.bin/rea`; Node 24.22.1 is available from the bundled runtime. The initial check found the Ghidra cache directory empty and no full JDK. Ghidra 12.1.4 was downloaded from the official GitHub release and its archive SHA-256 matched `ddac49f903da9d5bac833e5cc79395098b9c33cfd3279be5f31bd00387d2d4db`. Temurin JDK 21.0.13+9 was downloaded from Adoptium's official API and its archive SHA-256 matched `d7f5d5cf865a2902b70ff549b97586a7d3f234e016f041ab447ea740c7d66d1c`; both are isolated under `/home/pc1/.cache/codex-tools`, not installed system-wide.

With `GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR` and `JAVA_HOME` pointed to those verified directories, `rea doctor --provider ghidra` passes all eight Ghidra checks (`healthy=true`). Its broader `environment_healthy=false` result reflects absent optional IDA/Hopper/JADX/Kuna engines and missing or stale optional client registrations; these do not block Ghidra. The Codex registration reports stale, so this task used the REA CLI directly and did not edit Codex configuration. No commercial analysis software was installed. The optional Frida native build is not needed for this source-led audit.

The local R595.112.07 installer includes an NVIDIA Driver License Agreement whose clause 2.2 restricts reverse engineering, decompilation, and disassembly of binaries. The exact clause, local installer path, and disqualified analysis outputs are recorded above. Closed NVIDIA binaries are excluded from this project’s reverse-engineering evidence absent applicable written authorization.

Use REA/Ghidra only for project-owned binaries or targets whose applicable license expressly permits the analysis. For NVIDIA closed components, continue with published documentation, open-source kernel code, documented ABI contracts, source-level fixtures, and authorized hardware observations. Do not treat old pseudocode or conclusions derived from it as code or proof.

The [redacted boundary receipt](../evidence/nvidia-driver-re-license-boundary-v1-20261102.json) records tool setup and the restriction without storing binary-derived pseudocode. This changes the method for this project; it does not change the existing hardware proof boundary or make a source build equivalent to runtime validation.

### Hash-pinned GV100 fault-buffer worker — REA/Ghidra — 2026-11-03

REA 6.8.0 with Ghidra 12.1.4 and JDK 21.0.12.1 analyzed gv100_fault_buffer_process in the GPL Nouveau module for Linux 7.0.0-38-generic. The exact module SHA-256 is dee8078ff46dc1ad22900d7886bd21632327d4d84671632387144d19361cc0ea; the static result retains only the named call nvkm_fifo_fault, not pseudocode. The sanitized [receipt v9](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v9-20261103.json), SHA-256 ca0489f3afafc0eb10d47767ce385671332171722f3c1c0e5ac790d43fc4ed66, records ANALYZED_STATICALLY, with module_loaded=false and gpu_accessed=false. The reusable auditor bounds call matching to the target function body, masks comments and string literals, and binds the summary to the input digest; its regressions cover split Ghidra headers and false call matches.

For an independently pinned source reference, Linux tag v7.0 resolves to commit 028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8; drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/subdev/fault/gv100.c has SHA-256 10062d3bcb370a39dfa56f4fca675b125de7c03550316813654acdc794febd8d ([immutable upstream source](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/subdev/fault/gv100.c)). That source snapshots the fault-ring GET and PUT indices, decodes entries until GET reaches the captured PUT value, advances and publishes GET before dispatching each decoded fault to nvkm_fifo_fault, schedules the worker from the notification callback, and blocks notifications and flushes work during finalization.

The v3 source audit pins 12 files to that commit: the two Linux workqueue files, NVKM event/interrupt/subdevice dispatch, GP100/NV04 MC routing, the FIFO fault callback, and the Nouveau fault allocator/GP100/GV100 implementations. Its [sanitized receipt](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-fault-lifecycle-source-audit-v3-20261103.json), SHA-256 `8a16edfae34c20b3b44c4b93b480703e11f1e27ef93739e36672cbce8b0fcda2`, verifies 33 identity and order invariants. The GP100 MC table maps the fault subdevice to its interrupt bit; the core handler masks and dispatches under its interrupt lock, then rearms. The GV100 non-replayable register-fault branch calls FIFO recovery synchronously and acknowledges afterward. Replayable-buffer bits instead notify the event worker when their corresponding buffer exists. The FIFO layer delegates recovery through its configured MMU-fault callback. The GP100 MC constructor also returns `-ENODEV` when this Nouveau device is using GSP-RM mode, so this specific Nouveau MC path is not a generic GSP backend.

The v2 workqueue analysis remains applicable: Linux clears PENDING before invoking the callback, so an event during a running drain can queue a follow-up run; later events coalesce while it is pending. The teardown path removes the wait-enabled notifier under the same event rwlock held across callbacks, then flushes work before buffer release. This remains Linux/Nouveau source evidence only. It does not establish GPU-side PUT visibility/order, actual overflow recovery, hardware interrupt delivery, Ubuntu source-package equivalence, or NVIDIA RM/CUDA ABI behavior. No Nouveau code was copied and no proprietary NVIDIA binary was analyzed.

The project-local REA 6.8.0 CLI and the verified Ghidra/JDK cache installations are present. `rea doctor` reports the Ghidra provider healthy with explicit `GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR` and `JAVA_HOME`, while its broader environment is not fully healthy because optional engines and client registrations are absent or stale. REA/Ghidra analyzed a local copy of `/lib/modules/7.0.0-38-generic/kernel/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nouveau.ko.zst`; `modinfo` reports `GPL and additional rights`, `vermagic=7.0.0-38-generic SMP preempt mod_unload modversions`, and `srcversion=09A96B5334F6840A698DA43`. The uncompressed target SHA-256 is `dee8078ff46dc1ad22900d7886bd21632327d4d84671632387144d19361cc0ea`. The observed `gv100_devinit_new` pseudocode delegates to `nv50_devinit_new_` and passes `gv100_devinit`; this supports a Nouveau design observation about shared generic code and a GPU-specific function table. The expanded [v7 static receipt](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v7-20261102.json), SHA-256 `41c50fad05eb9d8056c9f1f2185775e28e15e7293e3302d798cd222cb51b40d2`, confirms the call chain `gv100_vmm_new → gp100_vmm_new_ → nvkm_vmm_new_` and `gv100_vmm_join → gp100_vmm_join` from the same GPL module hash. It also records Nouveau's existing ACR/GSP factory summaries and firmware declarations. This supports studying a generation-specific VMM descriptor over shared GP100/NVKM machinery; it does not make Nouveau's kernel APIs or memory manager reusable by NVIDIA RM. The exact matching upstream source revision for this Ubuntu module remains unknown, no Nouveau code was copied, and no CUDA/runtime behavior was inferred. The two new auditor regressions pass **15/15** with the existing targeted tests.

The companion [`r595112-hybrid-rethunk-provenance-v1-20261102.json`](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-rethunk-provenance-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `d9a8750309d53610246cd6b734ea46df2730724324bcfde7a2a56793c3367505`, classifies all **11,145** hybrid `MITIGATION_RETHUNK` warning rows by defined-symbol-name comparison: every row matched the opaque `nv-kernel.o_binary` symbol table, none matched any of **60** separately compiled C objects. The opaque object is SHA-256 `3fc6978a377b6303c8e0438897ba139729fea274345e8b528ecf289488ba1d1a`, equal to the package object in the standard R595.112.07 source tree. This is symbol provenance only; it is not disassembly, pseudocode, or machine-code proof.

The warning string is not unique to this prototype. NVIDIA Open GPU Kernel Modules issue [#838](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/issues/838) reports similar R575/Linux 6.12.22 warnings; the reporter said their official binary build produced similar warnings and later closed the issue after trying a local Linux 6.14.4 kernel. The report does not identify a root cause or establish that warnings are harmless; its separate BAR1/BAR2 initialization failure is not causally tied to objtool. The exact local Linux 7.0.0-38 `Makefile.lib` enables `--rethunk` under `CONFIG_MITIGATION_RETHUNK`. No objtool suppression was added.

A controlled comparison resolves the earlier mismatch between the stock and hybrid builds. The previous warning-free stock R595.112.07 build used a source-built core object SHA-256 `6d2bafae2e27162b05e4d9be4780eead71aa6939231d4d6c57e1ecb39d4889b5`; the hybrid package-core object was SHA-256 `3fc6978a377b6303c8e0438897ba139729fea274345e8b528ecf289488ba1d1a`. Rebuilding stock-named `nvidia.ko` with the exact latter object reproduced **11,145** RETHUNK rows / **10,952** unique names. The [A/B receipt](../evidence/r595112-stock-name-same-core-object-rethunk-ab-v2-20261102.json) and [symbol-name report](../evidence/r595112-stock-name-same-core-rethunk-provenance-v1-20261102.json) contain hashes and aggregate counts only. In the stock-named build, each warning symbol matched both the opaque core and at least one source object's symbol table, so these names cannot pinpoint the analyzed duplicate or prove control flow.

We then changed only the precompiled core input in an isolated copy of the hybrid tree to the source-built R595 object and rebuilt `hybrid580_open.ko`. Modpost completed and the build produced **zero** RETHUNK warnings, **zero** error lines, version 595.112.07, and the expected Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` vermagic. The [source-core build receipt](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-open-source-core-rethunk-build-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `7f75c6b123994423c580d540d97775cccedc0b427af2561a0bd066d9c6fe117e`, records module SHA-256 `7880311074045543b5f08d1f53ddb312e0e263b3497386054edda36c2500f6d0`. This makes the public source-built core the preferred next compile path. It does not establish equivalence with the package object, module initialization, GPU behavior, CUDA, UVM, or P2P. No closed binary was decompiled, and the candidate remains uninstalled and unloaded.

## R595.112.07 compiled UVM P2P consumer — 2026-11-02

REA 6.8.0/Ghidra 12.1.4 analyzed `get_parent_p2p_caps` from the project-built, open `nvidia-uvm.ko`, SHA-256 `b467e051c4af84011d512859751c20d0ef042e5c24d3281fbfb248173c18f4f2` (`Dual MIT/GPL`, vermagic `7.0.0-38-generic`). The hash-pinned [sanitized receipt](../evidence/r595112-uvm-p2p-consumer-static-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `77d678a0003224961e8af0e42263f322b2f5947fc19891d9faca433b268e7a29`, records REA Evidence ID `ev_2fbbd20784e91d7d5c63151b8c71d3e01c6ced032a1d9b7dc96d3410c4889e49` and the 5,929-byte private raw result hash. The MIT-notice-preserving source excerpt is pinned to the [official R595.112.07 source file](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_gpu.c), commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2`; its unmodified full-file hash is `66881c4ae46461314d41342b2a27df9119d68d68f90d181e3a32670dcc19f590`, and the tracked excerpt hash is `33913c7fffc5c5307969a1fd15ebdb735259705edc0e4923c3ae9897099a2af2`. The previously documented `b90bd772...` value was the hash of a patched provider-ID experiment file, not the upstream file; normalized extracted functions match the pinned source.

Source establishes ascending parent-GPU-ID ordering, a zeroed 72-byte output structure, the three-argument `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps` call under `uvm_rm_locked_call`, error-status return, and downstream rejection of `INVALID`/`C2C` links. Ghidra identifies the external call but omits the output-pointer argument in its pseudocode. Static disassembly of the exact object shows nine 64-bit stores and preserves the incoming `RDX` register through the call relocation at `0x116ed`, consistent with the source's third parameter. The new audit utility validates the target hash, REA report, source excerpt and disassembly, and writes only a compact receipt; tests reject target/provider drift and a changed pointer register. This is static analysis only: no module insertion, RM response, GPU operation, CUDA launch, or topology/P2P support was observed.

## R580/R595 P2P producer and ABI delta from licensed source — 2026-11-02

This differential uses the published MIT source, not closed-object pseudocode. NVIDIA's [R580.196.07 source archive](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source/) contains `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/nv_gpu_ops.c` (SHA-256 `98e4fcca21a17eb9b52793805f4d3a0f8ab9468a4f7154c0eddc2714b1443722`) and `src/nvidia/interface/nv_uvm_types.h` (SHA-256 `ee22643e79d6b7dae56ac52da517bae19b6594b1bf9564baeb68df10af6d5922`). Their source archive SHA-256 is `21914a460f17190c43cffb75f0c35aef957e267bfefea267bc18638beae17084`. The [R595.112.07 public tag](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/tree/595.112.07) resolves to commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2`; its `nv_gpu_ops.c` and interface header SHA-256 values are `e3cd52c0eb84554ad92e8ab6436e949492f45591307ff0b149a239367a837c8f` and `9bbe94713cfd6215ecb74e4626c19077e386009fe340fe156f94de9693ea635e`.

R580's output structure has seven fields and no PCIe-atomic bits. R595 preserves that prefix and appends two `NvBool` flags. Both pinned `nvtypes.h` files define `NvBool` as `NvU8`; compiling the exact struct excerpts for x86-64 gives sizes 64 and 72 bytes, respectively, and places the new R595 field at byte 64. This is a concrete source ABI difference, not a runtime failure report.

The producer behavior also differs: R580 supplies the two directional BAR1 DMA ranges and reports generic `UVM_LINK_TYPE_PCIE`; R595 reports the additional atomic capability values and classifies a BAR1 link as `UVM_LINK_TYPE_PCIE_BAR1`. The reusable [source auditor](../tools/audit_p2p_branch_delta.py), MIT-notice-preserving fixtures under `tests/fixtures/upstream/`, and [receipt](../evidence/r580-r595-p2p-abi-delta-v1-20261102.json) pin this comparison. The existing hybrid ABI adapter converts the old payload, derives BAR1 classification from its returned DMA ranges, and defaults the unreported R580 atomic flags to false. CPU tests cover the conversion and compiled layouts.

This does not establish that a specific BAR1 window is mapped, that PCIe atomics work, or that R580 and R595 provider-local RM sessions can share peer objects. Cross-provider UVM P2P remains fail-closed. The receipt is static/source evidence only; no GPU, module, CUDA transfer, or P2P runtime was tested.


## R580/R595 GV100 UVM prefetch-fault control — 2026-11-02

This is a source differential over the pinned R580.178.04 archive and experimental R595.99.02/GV100-HAL-v15 tree. In R580's `arch_table`, GV100 inherits GP100, whose enable callback is `uvm_hal_pascal_mmu_enable_prefetch_faults`; Volta initialization sets `prefetch_fault_supported=true`, and replayable-fault setup copies that value into `prefetch_faults_enabled`. R595/v15 selects Turing MMU enable/disable callbacks directly in the GV100 row and sets the runtime flag before dispatch. The callback wrapper loses R580's `UVM_ASSERT(parent_gpu->prefetch_fault_supported)` because that property no longer exists in candidate source.

The source bodies for `mmu_set_prefetch_faults` in `uvm_pascal_mmu.c` and `uvm_turing_mmu.c` normalize to the same SHA-256, `8265961e9733a05d6bdfbbb93b90ed16bb3d6c4f02837d1826ed290ddc4f8815`. Both call `nvUvmInterfaceTogglePrefetchFaults` when the RM does not expose `pPrefetchCtrl`; otherwise both read-modify-write `FAULT_CTRL.PRF_FILTER` to `SEND_ALL` or `SEND_NONE`. The candidate now passes exact architecture-row checks, so the callback is proven to belong to GV100's candidate row rather than merely occurring somewhere in `uvm_hal.c`.

The reproducible auditor is [`tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py`](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py); its pinned [v6 receipt](../evidence/volta-uvm-r580-r595-v15-capability-paths-v6-20261102.json) is SHA-256 `a984496aa99dae9253003830cf04c7176203c16a8019b11ecaeeeb42b123c61c`. Ten focused tests pass, including negative cases that move the candidate callback to TU100 or change `SEND_ALL`. This closes the static call-path question for GV100, not RM mapping safety, prefetch-fault delivery, replay, suspend/resume, or hardware behavior. A GV100 canary remains required.


## R580/R595 GV100 PCIe BAR1 `SYS_NON_COHERENT` path — 2026-11-02

The candidate adds a specific Volta peer path. `parent_gpu_peer_aperture()` chooses coherent `SYS` or `SYS_NON_COHERENT` for PCIe BAR1 according to `bar1_p2p_pcie_atomics_enabled[peer_index]`; NVLink and other peer links keep their peer aperture. `uvm_gpu_peer_phys_address()` adds the peer's BAR1 DMA base. The Volta CE HAL maps the new aperture to `NONCOHERENT_SYSMEM`, and the Volta `make_pte()` path uses the GV100 VER2 PTE aperture value and system-address field. R580's peer selector returns a peer-ID aperture for all links, and its Volta CE/PTE functions lack these branches.

The pinned R580 GV100 `dev_mmu.h` already defines the VER2 PTE aperture field at bits 2:1 and `SYSTEM_NON_COHERENT_MEMORY=3`; its `clc3b5.h` defines matching noncoherent source/destination CE modes. This is direct open-source hardware-interface evidence for the encodings, not a physical test. Candidate UVM comments describe the aperture as controlling PCIe atomics, and external-allocation PTE generation is requested from RM; the built-in `uvm_get_rm_ptes_test` compares RM PTEs against the Volta builder. Therefore the added builder path principally proves the local comparison/test path and the CE aperture path in source; it does not establish that production RM mappings and transfers work on GV100.

The [pinned audit v8](../evidence/volta-uvm-r580-r595-v15-capability-paths-v8-20261102.json), SHA-256 `5a9aeb8e5ec8d3f7965cdbb29b6aa7ca5e47378355581ad7df9e242108660e91`, captures the R580 and candidate function hashes and exact anchors. NVIDIA's R595 UVM comments define this aperture as a way to suppress PCIe atomics for BAR1 P2P and explain that external allocation PTEs are generated by RM ([official R595 source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/595.99.02/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_hal_types.h)). A separate official hardware reference shows the VER2 noncoherent PTE encoding as value 3 ([NVIDIA MMU header](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/main/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/hwref/turing/tu102/dev_mmu.h)); the pinned GV100 header is the architecture-specific evidence for this candidate.

Twelve focused auditor tests and the full **2,558/2,558** CPU/software suite pass. The suite receipt is [here](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261102T144451Z.json), SHA-256 `480d75895a48c38cf19708cc21a838e36566c0a81943a60649abd8c515149e68`. PCIe topology, RM capability values, BAR1 mapping, CE transfer, atomic suppression, concurrency, and recovery still require a physical GV100 canary.

## REA/Ghidra audit of the GPL Nouveau GV100 path — 2026-11-02

REA 6.8.0 with Ghidra 12.1.4 analyzed only the locally installed Linux Nouveau module, whose `modinfo` license metadata is `GPL and additional rights`. `dpkg-query` identifies the compressed source artifact as package `linux-modules-7.0.0-38-generic` version `7.0.0-38.38`; decompressing it reproduces the exact ELF64 x86-64 relocatable module hash `dee8078ff46dc1ad22900d7886bd21632327d4d84671632387144d19361cc0ea`. Its kernel release is `7.0.0-38-generic`, and `srcversion` is `09A96B5334F6840A698DA43`. The repeatable wrapper is [`nouveau_gv100_rea_audit.py`](../tools/nouveau_gv100_rea_audit.py), with focused tests in [`test_nouveau_gv100_rea_audit.py`](../tests/test_nouveau_gv100_rea_audit.py); its sanitized [static source receipt](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `cad92f02d77122c2eb534ff22eb15cbca3e316e403f88724947e58705d815b15`, pins the analyzed bytes and tool versions. The wrapper records dispatch summaries only and retains no pseudocode.

The wrapper's GPL-prefixed `modinfo` check is a fail-closed input filter, not an independent license classifier for arbitrary objects. This run was restricted to the distribution Nouveau kernel module identified above; it did not analyze or redistribute NVIDIA proprietary binaries.

The repeat run at `2026-11-02T04:59:13Z` is recorded in [receipt v2](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v2-20261102.json), SHA-256 `a319ee7b0947c1a326ab95a8d689b870dcef3a599461cc0ad948d6e0a549ce8d`. With `GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR` and `JAVA_HOME` pointed at the pinned local tools, `rea doctor --provider ghidra --json` reported `healthy=true`; all eight Ghidra checks passed with Ghidra 12.1.4 and JDK 21.0.12.1, and the Node 24.22.1 runtime check passed. A separate [sanitized doctor receipt](../evidence/rea-ghidra-doctor-v1-20261102.json), SHA-256 `85f9fbeb434b1ee17e94ffa8ad0612d0eaedb4eb8f4654aa81a2b363c9a3ea33`, preserves that result. Its overall `environment_healthy` is false because optional analysis engines, the REA skill package, and client registrations are unconfigured; the requested Ghidra CLI provider itself is available. No `rea setup` or client registration change was made. The receipt pins module SHA-256 `dee8078ff46dc1ad22900d7886bd21632327d4d84671632387144d19361cc0ea`, REA 6.8.0, and Ghidra 12.1.4. Focused auditor tests pass **7/7**. Receipt v2 repeats the same sanitized findings; it does not upgrade their evidence class.

A third current run with the same hash-pinned module and the project-local REA/Ghidra/JDK paths is recorded in [receipt v3](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v3-20261102.json), SHA-256 `d82cf4a671c40e8849ce247f246aef6652259caeb0238c981363807ae18f3f20`. REA 6.8.0 and Ghidra 12.1.4 reproduced the two factory dispatch summaries; the sanitized result retains no pseudocode and records `gpu_accessed=false`, `module_loaded=false`, and `hardware_execution_validated=false`. This confirms current tool operation on the GPL Nouveau module only.

Receipt v4 adds a hash-checked REA/Ghidra analysis of `gv100_gr_oneinit_sm_id` and retains only the related-call fact `gv100_gr_scg_estimate_perf`; the receipt SHA-256 is `2b5592792de77181c707af71ac99fdd9232ab2f0ce310da7940577cb470b9bfe`. Linux v7.0 source shows this routine constructs a topology-ordered SM-to-GPC/TPC mapping by repeatedly estimating the effect of removing candidate TPCs, then stores the selected GPC/TPC pair for each SM ([upstream source](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v7.0/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/engine/gr/gv100.c)). This is a Nouveau topology/SM-indexing reference for diagnostics and compatibility research; it does not establish a transferable NVIDIA RM mechanism or a performance gain, and no Nouveau code was copied. The Ubuntu module is pinned to kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`; exact source-package equivalence to the upstream `v7.0` tag was not established. As before, pseudocode is not retained, and the result is static only.

Receipt v5 extends the same GPL-only audit to `gv100_vmm_new`; REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 returns the hash-matched call summary `gp100_vmm_new_(&gv100_vmm)`. Its sanitized [receipt](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v5-20261102.json) is SHA-256 `f2ddaa6b64df9da15e4fc9b2571e0f7ff61aac8c95909a930fe25e3128606b7e`. The exact upstream Linux v7.0 [`vmmgv100.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v7.0/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/subdev/mmu/vmmgv100.c) source was separately fetched and hash-checked (`ddf0be77a946cf03c0cab7b9414aa65860f5aef4e6be36d65fac551651a3147d). It defines a GV100 VMM descriptor with its own `gv100_vmm_join`, while reusing GP100 VMM callbacks and page descriptors at shifts 47, 38, 29, 21, 16, and 12; the constructor passes that descriptor to `gp100_vmm_new_`. This is evidence of Nouveau's per-generation descriptor/shared-constructor design. It is a candidate architectural reference for reviewing memory-backend boundaries, not NVIDIA RM code, proof of source-package identity for Ubuntu's binary, proof of CUDA/UVM operation, or a performance result. No Nouveau code was copied.

Receipt v6 adds a hash-checked REA/Ghidra analysis of `gv100_vmm_join`, which calls `gp100_vmm_join` on the exact module hash ([sanitized receipt](../evidence/nouveau-gv100-static-source-audit-v6-20261102.json), SHA-256 `f0a0dba7c77447a37ef349d1a64e029ae14b0aaea14cf3c4f7774e1746102b28`). The independently fetched Linux v7.0 source file has SHA-256 `ddf0be77a946cf03c0cab7b9414aa65860f5aef4e6be36d65fac551651a3147d`: after the generic join succeeds, the GV100 routine maps the instance block, reads two words at offsets `0x200`/`0x204`, initializes 64 context entries, writes the bit-0 mask, and completes the mapped-memory operation. This is static evidence of a generation-specific memory-table initialization layered on a GP100 common join. It does not prove the writes succeed on hardware or that the same layout/API maps to NVIDIA RM. No pseudocode or Nouveau implementation code is retained.

Reproduce with the pinned tools and Node runtime:

```sh
python3 -B tools/nouveau_gv100_rea_audit.py \
  --module build/experiments/rea-nouveau-gpl-v1-20261102/nouveau.ko \
  --output build/experiments/rea-nouveau-gpl-v1-20261102/reproduced-audit.json \
  --rea-bin "$PWD/build/tools/rea-6.8.0/node_modules/.bin/rea" \
  --ghidra-dir "$PWD/build/tools/ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC" \
  --java-home "$PWD/build/tools/rea-jdk21-ubuntu26.04/usr/lib/jvm/java-21-openjdk-amd64" \
  --node-bin /home/pc1/.cache/codex-runtimes/codex-primary-runtime/dependencies/node/bin
```

The doctor check was run with `GHIDRA_INSTALL_DIR="$PWD/build/tools/ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC"` and `JAVA_HOME="$PWD/build/tools/rea-jdk21-ubuntu26.04/usr/lib/jvm/java-21-openjdk-amd64"`. The auditor refuses to overwrite receipts. Review the hash-pinned summary and its no-hardware status before treating a rerun as evidence.

`modinfo` declares four GV100 ACR firmware paths and no `nvidia/gv100/gsp/` path (519 firmware declarations overall, including GSP paths for other chipsets). ELF relocations inside the sized `nv140_chipset` table point to both `gv100_acr_new` and `gv100_gsp_new`; the latter constructor's decompiler summary calls `nvkm_gsp_new_(gv100_gsps, ...)`, while the ACR constructor calls `nvkm_acr_new_(gv100_acr_fwif, ...)`. The pinned [Linux source for the GV100 GSP factory](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/6628e7d4c6b25c8d18f1345e3a20d21829aae7e4/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/subdev/gsp/gv100.c) confirms the thin constructor and no-firmware predicate. The same pinned tree's [GV100 VMM source](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/6628e7d4c6b25c8d18f1345e3a20d21829aae7e4/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/subdev/mmu/vmmgv100.c) and [GR path](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/6628e7d4c6b25c8d18f1345e3a20d21829aae7e4/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/engine/gr/gv100.c) provide bounded MMU/ACR references. Recent [Nouveau/Linux discussion](https://lists.openwall.net/linux-kernel/2026/10/09/2557) separately documents that a GV100 GSP object may exist while its RM remains absent when GSP-RM is disabled or firmware is missing. Therefore a constructor reference is not proof that GV100 GSP-RM initializes or runs; the module's declared ACR path is a stronger static lead, not proof of a working CUDA backend.

This supports keeping Nouveau as a source of licensed Volta firmware/HAL and memory-management research, while the current CUDA experiment retains the R580 Volta provider. No Nouveau code was copied into the provider. REA's xref command did not resolve this relocatable object's data-table references, so the receipt uses `objdump -r` relocations and explicitly does not interpret empty normalized xrefs as absence of use. No module was loaded and no GPU was accessed; the evidence level is **ANALYZED_STATICALLY** only.

## R580 local RM control boundary and corrected R595 interpretation — 2026-11-01

The earlier static conclusion composed the debugger class’s null internal-object handle with the Resource Server’s object-map lookup and predicted `NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND`. A class-resolved stock RTX 5080/R595.99.02 trace now shows public `0x83DE0309` returning status 0 for a live `GT200_DEBUGGER`; the prediction is false for that observed public path. This does not validate the hybrid provider’s separate internal call.

For the historical R580.178.04 baseline, `KernelSMDebuggerSession` source maps public `0x83DE0309` to internal `0x83DE0405`, its internal-control forwarder delegates through `gpuresInternalControlForward_IMPL`, and its internal-object getter returns `NV01_NULL_OBJECT`. The physical RM wrapper directly dispatches only when the client and object are the GPU’s internal client/subdevice pair; other calls fall through to the internal RM API. The open `rmapi.c` assigns that API’s `Control` field to `rmapiControl`, but the function implementation is in `kernel-open/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary`.

GNU `nm`/`objdump` 2.46 found `rmapiControl`, `_rmapiRmControl`, and relocations from the latter to `serverControl` in the R580 binary. The inspected Resource Server source then resolves `RmCtrlParams.hObject` through `clientGetResourceRef`; a missing map entry returns `NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND`. This establishes a partial source-to-binary call graph. It does **not** establish that the R580 call carrying public `0x83DE0309` used this exact target class, nor that the internal RM call reached the resource-map lookup with a null object.

The PC3 V100/R580.178.04 historical capture records public command `0x83DE0309`, nonzero `hObject`, zero syscall and RM entry/exit status, and successful single-GPU SM70 kernels. Its v6 observer relates the client to a prior root allocation but cannot resolve the control target’s class. Therefore this result is a useful contradiction candidate, not proof of a Volta `GT200_DEBUGGER` class route. The original report is [here](../evidence/pc3-v100-rm-control-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `0bf93bda818387f0f6e5e68aa1fc3f7df32d95c036dac3e6d3faedb78bd11199`.

R595 source assigns `rpcRmApiControl_GSP` only when `IS_GSP_CLIENT(pGpu)` and carries client, object, command, parameters, and RM status over its RPC path. The stock RTX capture reports GSP firmware 595.99.02, so that route is plausible but not yet directly traced for this command. NVIDIA’s GSP guide says Turing and later GPUs can use GSP; that generation boundary excludes Volta/GV100. The historical R580 V100 result therefore cannot be explained by assuming the same GSP route.

The [redacted reconciliation record](../evidence/gt200-debugger-r580-r595-runtime-route-reconciliation-v2-20261101.json), SHA-256 `f6e65af5a3c009df9b083f406a2cf6e53fcd57136329343bd9fe215e19163c5f`, pins the source snapshots and binaries; its binutils log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r580-rmapiControl-binutils-v1-20261101.log`), SHA-256 `80a87de219d2cfa8f23806f3d96529312dcc8e5b70b98e4d9bebf0af60df33ce`, preserves the selected symbol and relocation lines. REA, Ghidra, Hopper, and IDA are not installed on this host; this pass used only available ELF symbol/relocation tools and did not reproduce binary pseudocode as source. The next discriminating experiment is a repeat V100 stock trace with v39 class/owner correlation; do not change the provider until it establishes which object received the call.

Official references: [CUDA architecture and last-driver matrix](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/drivers/latest/cuda-toolkit-driver-and-architecture-matrix.html), [R580.196.07 release notes](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/tesla-release-notes-580-196-07/index.html), [R595.112.07 release notes](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/tesla-release-notes-595-112-07/index.html), and [NVIDIA GSP firmware guide](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-aarch64/510.108.03/README/gsp.html).

## R595 debugger-control runtime result versus null-object source prediction — 2026-11-01

The hash-pinned R595.99.02 Open source snapshot at `build/experiments/r595-debugger-bridge-provider-build-v4-20261101/source` exposes this declared path:

- The generated `KernelSMDebuggerSession` method table maps public `0x83DE0309` to the fixed four-byte `NV83DE_CTRL_DEBUG_SET_EXCEPTION_MASK_PARAMS` method.
- The generated inline wrapper translates that request to internal control `0x83DE0405` and calls `ksmdbgssnInternalControlForward`.
- `ksmdbgssnInternalControlForward_IMPL` delegates to `gpuresInternalControlForward_IMPL`; the debugger's `ksmdbgssnGetInternalObjectHandle_IMPL` returns `NV01_NULL_OBJECT`.
- The generic GpuResource implementation calls `pRmApi->Control` using the resource client handle and the internal-object getter.

These source facts are hash-pinned in [the reconciliation evidence](../evidence/gt200-debugger-r595-runtime-route-reconciliation-v1-20261101.json), SHA-256 `e8a0936b716b5c4362ed3c6d3c5f8186e686f108a894cb7ff69dfe344ae93ddb`. They do not identify every runtime RM or GSP dispatch branch.

The earlier static composition predicted `NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND` because the inspected Resource Server lookup could not resolve the null internal object. That prediction is now contradicted for the observed stock RTX 5080/R595.99.02 path: the v39 PC3 trace resolves the public target to a previously allocated live `GT200_DEBUGGER`, and syscall plus RM status are zero. The exact mechanism that lets the declared source path succeed is still unknown; the capture does not prove the candidate hybrid provider works. Treat the old result as a path-specific static hypothesis, not a universal blocker, and do not invent a provider-local handle workaround from it.

The observer sampled only the documented four-byte user payload (`exceptionMask=0x3A`) at entry and return. Equal user-buffer bytes do not read back RM/GPU state or prove the mask's behavioral effect. The CUDA phase created and cleaned up one RTX context, but ran no kernel; there is no V100 or same-process hybrid result. The next discriminating work is to locate the runtime Resource Server/GSP path for this internal control and then validate the provider bridge without loading an experimental module on the active installation.

## Bounded exception-mask payload observation — R580/R595 ABI — 2026-11-01

The pinned R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 `ctrl83dedebug.h` headers are byte-identical (48,449 bytes; SHA-256 `716ea7c8ef9f92bad00b69ce87da79e8fc655b771a6a9922824562739ab16d19`). For `NV83DE_CTRL_CMD_DEBUG_SET_EXCEPTION_MASK` (`0x83de0309`), the documented payload is one `NvU32 exceptionMask`; the defined event bits are `FATAL=0x1`, `TRAP=0x2`, `SINGLE_STEP=0x4`, `INT=0x8`, `CILP=0x10`, and `PREEMPTION_STARTED=0x20`, with `NONE=0` and `ALL=0xffff`. The observer accepts subsets of the six event bits or the documented `ALL` sentinel and rejects other values.

Schema v39 samples only this exact four-byte buffer when the live target resolves to `GT200_DEBUGGER`, with null/size and stable-header-pointer checks on both sides of the ioctl. It emits the mask and a clearly named `exceptionMaskUserBufferEntryExitEqual` field. That field compares user memory before and after the call; it is not a driver-state query, and even syscall/RM success cannot show which event behavior was applied. The CPU harness exercises the actual helper. Hardware sampling and any readback/event-effect test remain pending while PC3 is off. This is observation tooling only; no frontend command forwarding or CUDA behavior was changed.

## RM root-handle lifecycle registry defect and corrected RTX capture — 2026-11-01

The v6/v7 GT200_DEBUGGER lifecycle observer produced a false negative: its control target appeared `not-observed-live-object`, and the historical allocation-equality flag was false. The observer's registry update used the `RM_ALLOC` entry packet. For an automatically allocated object, input `hObjectNew` may be zero while the assigned handle appears only in the successful exit packet. The root/client entry was therefore never registered, so mapping the later `hClient` back to a unique observed root failed before target-class lookup.

The pinned R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 SDK headers for `ctrl83dedebug.h` are byte-identical (48,449 bytes; SHA-256 `716ea7c8ef9f92bad00b69ce87da79e8fc655b771a6a9922824562739ab16d19`). They define `0x83de0309` as `NV83DE_CTRL_CMD_DEBUG_SET_EXCEPTION_MASK` with a fixed 32-bit `exceptionMask`; the documented purpose is filtering debugger notifications, including fatal, trap, single-step, interrupt, CILP and preemption events. They describe SM debug mode as useful for GPU features/workarounds and note that fatal-event subscriptions can defer recovery. The v8 live capture deliberately read only the fixed RM header, not the four-byte nested mask, and did not validate a readback or event effect. Thus `NV_OK` plus the live class/equality proves request routing and accepted status in this R595 run, not the configured mask or behavior.

The implementation now inserts successful allocations from the exit packet and still removes frees using the entry packet. A synthetic harness calls the actual registry update and lookup routines with a zero-requested root handle, returned `NV01_ROOT_CLIENT`, then a `GT200_DEBUGGER` child. It verifies the child class resolves and the prior debugger-allocation relation is true. The updated binary reproducibly builds (SHA-256 `53dc86709ea4cf5c517a9413bfc3c012379165d5a74a8259395ad32847eb3311`; [build receipt](../evidence/gt200-debugger-lifecycle-observer-v6-20261101.json)); focused harness and observer checks pass 1/1 and 80/80.

The v8 PC3 RTX 5080 capture now reports one debugger allocation, one resolved control and one free. For public control `0x83de0309`, both syscall and RM status are 0, the redacted `hObject` equality matches a prior successful `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation, and the live class resolves to `0x83de`. This identifies the target in this R595 runtime capture, but does not establish what the command changes. The isolated CUDA context/cleanup phase passed; numerical kernel execution was not part of this capture. [Capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-gt200-debugger-controls-v8-20261101.json), SHA-256 `ebaa90b2a8213c9917114558bf18e741cae0b9154297a1c090ea3a7918bc9e6d`.

An independent full acceptance run captured raw RM headers to a local mode-0600 evidence file and returned 77 for missing host SM70. Within that same trace, a redacted offline comparison found `0x83de0309`'s `hObject` equal to the immediately preceding successful debugger allocation in the same root; the RTX 5080's synchronous and asynchronous numeric kernel checks passed at five sizes. The full capture independently corroborates the corrected relation but does not validate the hybrid driver. The prior v6/v7 negative relation is an observer defect, superseded by this corrected capture. The local static finding below about a null internal object handle applies to a distinct forwarding path and remains conditional; do not conflate it with the live public control targeting the allocated debugger resource.

## Earlier R595 live control candidate versus static null-object path — 2026-11-01 (object correlation superseded by corrected observer)

The v6 stock-R595 PC3 observation found one `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation and free, plus one unresolved public control candidate `0x83de0309`. The command returned syscall status 0 and RM status 0. The observer reported `hObjectPresent=true` (nonzero), but did not correlate that numeric handle to the earlier debugger allocation and did not resolve a class in its selected-path live registry. Raw handles are not emitted. This is evidence of successful transport/RM status only; it does not show the debugger control targeted the observed `GT200_DEBUGGER` object or had a functional effect.

This narrows, but does not overturn, the static R580/R595/R615 Resource Server result below. That result concerns a call supplied with `NV01_NULL_OBJECT`; the live candidate's handle was nonzero. There is no basis to identify the observed command as that exact local dispatch path. The observer's registry is limited to the selected paths, so a non-match means “not observed in this registry,” not “the RM object does not exist.” Follow-up should locate the R595 ioctl/control route, determine whether an intermediate/internal object is created or selected, and expand capture only where the source path justifies it. Do not implement a forwarding route from the zero status alone.

The capture passed the corrected report contract after the v5 run exposed a stale payload-scope string in the local validator. Focused tests now cover the current scope string and zero/nonzero redacted `hObjectPresent` semantics. The [v6 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-gt200-debugger-controls-v6-20261101.json) is SHA-256 `cf347baa625fc6eda12551c9ef787284e3a50d0b6ac98682269b55a6ab7103d8`; the run log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/pc3-rtx5080-gt200-debugger-controls-v6-20261101.log`) is SHA-256 `fc04b94bab7bb537f2fcd56a613d8b683c5ffce5fc0efb2ff2d9fc3651489113`. PC3's RTX 5080 is on stock R595.99.02. The V100 remains vfio-bound to its active QEMU guest and was not accessed. Neither the hybrid module nor any candidate driver was loaded.

## R580/R595 SM debugger control dispatch and observer relation — 2026-11-01

In the pinned R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 source trees, `NV83DE_CTRL_CMD_DEBUG_SET_EXCEPTION_MASK` is the public control ID `0x83de0309`. The generated NVOC wrapper maps that request to internal command `0x83de0405` before the session's internal-control forward path. The public declaration and session implementation files are byte-identical in the two snapshots; generated wrappers differ in formatting but retain this mapping. The session returns `NV01_NULL_OBJECT` from its internal-object lookup. The generic `gpuresInternalControlForward_IMPL` then invokes Physical RM Control with the debugger resource's client handle and that lookup result as the object handle. This is the observed source call chain; the operational meaning of a null object handle on the target hardware is still unresolved. The [static source receipt](../evidence/gt200-debugger-r580-r595-source-dispatch-v2-20261101.json) records hashes for all five relevant files in each branch, and [`test_gt200_debugger_dispatch_source.py`](../tests/test_gt200_debugger_dispatch_source.py) protects the cross-version contract. This explains the two IDs; it does not establish frontend support, live Volta use, or that provider-local handles can safely cross the hybrid boundary.

The stock RTX observer run at 06:39Z captured one successful debugger allocation and one free, but no resolved control. It counted one unresolved control candidate; `debuggerTargetObjectClassWithinRoot` remained `not-observed-live-object`. Observer schema v38 emits the redacted field `object_handle_matches_prior_debugger_allocation`: equality between the numeric `hObject` and a successful debugger allocation handle seen earlier under that root. This relation is historical, can remain true after free, and does not establish object identity when handles are reused. The current class resolver independently consults the live registry. A C harness executes the observer's actual routines and verifies the same-root, cross-root, after-free, and failed-allocation cases. This instrumentation is not a control bridge. The current hybrid frontend still fails closed for debugger allocation/control routes until it has typed provider ownership, handle translation, object lifetime dependencies, and a command-specific safety contract. See the [v38 focused verification](../evidence/gt200-debugger-observer-v38-focused-tests-20261101.json) and [complete software-suite receipt](../evidence/software-verification-debugger-observer-v5-20261101.json).

## Null internal object handle versus Resource Server lookup — 2026-11-01

The static call chain now identifies a concrete obstacle in the pinned R580.178.04, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 branches. `ksmdbgssnGetInternalObjectHandle_IMPL` returns `NV01_NULL_OBJECT` (`0`); `gpuresInternalControlForward_IMPL` passes that value unchanged to `pRmApi->Control`. The ordinary local RM API binds `Control` to `rmapiControl`, which reaches `serverControl`. `serverControl` calls `clientGetResourceRef(pClient, pParams->hObject, ...)` before invoking `resControl`; `clientGetResourceRef_IMPL` uses `mapFind` and returns `NV_ERR_OBJECT_NOT_FOUND` on a missing key.

The inspected resource-map construction does not create a null-handle entry: root client resources use their allocated nonzero client handle (base `0xC1D00000`), and an allocation requesting handle zero is assigned a generated resource handle whose range start is asserted nonzero. A source regression test pins these facts for all three branches, including the normal `rmapiControl` binding and its route into `serverControl`. Therefore the **local Resource Server path** cannot dispatch this control with the null internal object handle as supplied. This is not yet a runtime failure capture: the exact `pRmApi->Control` backend selected in the target configuration and the resulting hardware status remain to be verified. The [169-test focused receipt](../evidence/gt200-debugger-null-object-static-focused-tests-v3-20261101.json) hashes the dispatch, Resource Server, client-map, and handle-allocation sources for all three branches.

The prior R595 UVM audit was also corrected: v2 now includes Map/Unmap sites in `uvm_rm_mem.c`. Its 26 checks show those inspected paging routes are SR-IOV Heavy proxy-gated in the pinned source snapshots. This is an applicability constraint to verify against the actual guest mode, not evidence that those routes ran on PC3.

## GT200_DEBUGGER lifecycle capture protocol — 2026-11-01

The next discriminating observation is the lifetime of the already-seen `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation and whether the stock Blackwell runtime sends class-targeted control calls. The v35 observer keeps successful RM object handles in a bounded, root-scoped in-memory registry, records only exact-class frees, and resolves control `hClient` to one observed root before resolving `hObject`. Ambiguous or absent identities fail closed. The emitted report contains relationship/class metadata rather than raw handles or addresses, and the observer never follows an RM_CONTROL nested parameter pointer.

This closes an instrumentation gap only. It does not establish that Volta creates the same class, that R580 and R595 object lifetimes align, or that provider-local handles may be forwarded between RM backends. Those require a successful lifecycle capture and a separate design for handle translation, parent lifetime, free ordering, and command-specific control semantics. No capture was run in this increment because PC3 was off; the reproducible binary and CPU contract tests are recorded in [`VALIDATION.md`](VALIDATION.md).

## GT200_DEBUGGER: integration boundary and safe-route requirements — 2026-11-01

The stock Blackwell capture establishes one successful `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation: outer parent `NV01_DEVICE_0`, `hDebuggerClient_Obsolete=0`, `hAppClient` equal to the RM root, and a nested `BLACKWELL_COMPUTE_B` object directly parented by `BLACKWELL_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A`. Static audit v7 finds the same resource ancestry in R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10; it does not show that Volta emits the analogous request. [Capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-gt200-debugger-parent-context-v1-20261101T0323Z.json), SHA-256 `0e09bb58d956d4686f18abcb5f67b38841a4f7de8118068e84a0c4721be900e9`; [static audit v7](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v7-20261101T032942Z.json), SHA-256 `633318e5fc679e66887e920c9f94c17b9d79759e9b4d09c15cd57225e0b65da3`.

Code inspection found four concrete gaps before a positive route is safe. [`hybrid_front_engine_alloc64()`](../src/hybrid_frontend.c#L5593) and its provider methods handle only four no-payload compute/DMA classes. The RM_FREE ledger allowlist has no `GT200_DEBUGGER` destructor route. The frontend does not retain the nested debuggee handle as a lifetime dependency. Finally, [`hybrid_front_rm_control_ioctl()`](../src/hybrid_frontend.c#L2337) exposes a fixed command allowlist and rejects `NV83DE_CTRL_CMD_SM_DEBUG_MODE_ENABLE`; it does not provide a provider-local RM control bridge. The allocation now fails explicitly as `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, without reading its user pointer or forwarding provider-local handles. The matching negative-path CPU harness passes **22/22** ([receipt](../evidence/gt200-debugger-frontend-negative-tests-v1-20261101T0333Z.json), SHA-256 `5cf9824e305f073ebee0ecd937d5ed58076b460c6dfb2c07bd858403e0a2e703`).

A candidate positive adapter must translate `hAppClient` and `hClass3dObject` using root-scoped live objects, prove that the debuggee's tracked channel and device belong to the same provider selected for the allocation, retain a debuggee lifetime reference until debugger teardown, and add typed allocation/free/control methods to both provider APIs. It must be tested against a V100 trace before assuming the `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A` ancestry. These are design requirements inferred from the current ownership model and observed Blackwell packet; no positive GT200 route is implemented or hardware-validated. PC3 is offline per the user's latest instruction, and no machine was contacted for this source review.

## `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation layout across R580 and R595 — 2026-11-01

[`tools/audit_gt200_debugger_alloc_static.py`](../tools/audit_gt200_debugger_alloc_static.py) pins the staged R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 `libcuda` binaries, the corresponding open RM headers/dispatch implementations in R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10, and the earlier PC3/RTX allocation-header summary. Fixed-window `objdump` checks show both user libraries constructing a 48-byte `NVOS64_PARAMETERS` for class `0x83de` using `NV_ESC_RM_ALLOC`: `hRoot` offset 0, parent offset 4, new handle offset 8, class offset 12, non-null allocation-parameter pointer offset 16, null rights pointer offset 24, `paramsSize` offset 32, flags offset 36, and status offset 40. The nested 12-byte `NV83DE_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` has `hDebuggerClient_Obsolete=0`, `hAppClient` from incoming ECX, and `hClass3dObject` from incoming R8D. The four open branches use the same class-header hash, and their second-client RM dispatch obtains `hAppClient`.

The live PC3 RTX 5080 context capture now resolves the key relationship without exposing raw handles: a successful R595.99.02 `GT200_DEBUGGER` allocation has outer parent `NV01_DEVICE_0` (`0x80`), the obsolete debugger-client field is zero, the effective application client matches the RM root, and `hClass3dObject` resolves to `0xCEC0`. The pinned R595.99.02 and R580.196.07 SDK headers are byte-identical for `clcec0.h` (SHA-256 `bbe3bcabc7f129972ed5ed635f58251b657d6e5616cac8cfc5bd817293da7d2e`) and name `0xCEC0` `BLACKWELL_COMPUTE_B`. Thus the observed Blackwell debuggee is a compute object even though the legacy payload field is named `hClass3dObject`; the source's `KernelGraphicsObject` acceptance is consistent with that observation. This confirms only the allocation contract on Blackwell: it does not establish Volta compatibility, actual handle values, provider ownership/translation, debugger functionality, or a safe frontend route. [Hardware capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-gt200-debugger-context-v3-20261101T0318Z.json), SHA-256 `77d149a5ec4ceb357049bd9eb6953af50ea9b5aa92973546223400bc2d16c485`; [static source audit](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json), SHA-256 `034709565f6f522c23523aa2b48ac18314fed2f6ae5f50b29490b5f42c6fe9be`.

The follow-up redacted live capture now resolves the `GT200_DEBUGGER` payload's `hClass3dObject` as `0xCEC0` and its direct parent as `0xC96F`; the outer allocation parent resolves to `0x80`. Pinned R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 headers/resource lists map these to `BLACKWELL_COMPUTE_B`, `BLACKWELL_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A`, and `NV01_DEVICE_0`. Both source trees list `BLACKWELL_COMPUTE_B` as `KernelGraphicsObject` with parent `KernelChannel`; `BLACKWELL_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A` is a required-parameter `KernelChannel`. They also define the Volta sibling `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A=0xC36F`. Static audit v6 validates this class ancestry across R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10, and records `RMCFG_CHIP_GV100=0` / `RMCFG_CLASS_VOLTA_COMPUTE_A=0` in all four OpenRM build configurations while Volta channel/user-mode and Blackwell compute remain enabled ([report](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v6-20261101T032643Z.json), SHA-256 `140be00bdb3351796335110b7205d5b9c4f7f82546b45a3c6576db223761832c`). This supports, but does not runtime-validate, the split-provider route: Blackwell stays on Open R595 and Volta requires its R580 provider. It does not establish that a V100 debugger request targets `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A` or that cross-provider handle translation is safe. [Capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-gt200-debugger-parent-context-v1-20261101T0323Z.json), SHA-256 `0e09bb58d956d4686f18abcb5f67b38841a4f7de8118068e84a0c4721be900e9`; observer binary SHA-256 `be74485bcbdf0386df0ae04754c2ef634c2cd40bb272b2ed1fa48b26bec562a7`.

## `NV50_MEMORY_VIRTUAL` evidence and remaining debugger-route gap — 2026-11-01

The stock V100 and RTX 5080 allocation summaries both record successful NVOS64 allocation of class `0x50a0` (`NV50_MEMORY_VIRTUAL`) beneath `NV01_DEVICE_0` (`0x80`). They record outer `paramsSize=0` with a non-null `pAllocParms`, but the allocation payload was not read. Therefore the trace establishes class and parent use only; it does not establish the exact payload or prove the class is required for CUDA initialization.

The R580 SDK identifies `NV50_MEMORY_VIRTUAL` as a typed memory object using `NV_MEMORY_ALLOCATION_PARAMS`; the historical RM path creates it with `NVOS32_ALLOC_FLAGS_VIRTUAL` and a VASpace. The hybrid frontend now dispatches that typed payload only to the backend owning the device and tracked VASpace, and frees it through the same provider. CPU tests exercise both providers, handle translation, missing-VASpace/flag rejection, and cleanup ordering. This is source and CPU-model evidence only; the trace payload and live allocation remain unvalidated.

The other observed class `0x83de` (`GT200_DEBUGGER`) still has no safe frontend route.
