Static field offsets and register sources are now decoded in the section above; capture payload values, runtime handle provenance, and frontend/backend ownership remain unknown. A class ID and static layout alone are insufficient for routing.

The updated private patch manifest is `3517b56ec561eef0a95b0ae11478b329c390a27c3ee9e67b20ce1708352a38ab`. The source-port run applies all **47** R580.178.04 patches to the pinned R580.196.07 tree, with zero fuzz but 23 recorded hunk offsets; the proprietary core hash remains unchanged ([receipt](../evidence/r58019607-memory-virtual-port-v1-20261101.json)). That is patch portability evidence, not compile or execution evidence.

## R595.99.02/R595.112.07 memory-source delta and P2P table fix (2026-10-31)

Hash-pinned source comparison: R595.99.02 archive `b8891cb617683e28ef1e4c3add8dbc30e648b8cd6429a3d521f20dde44633262`; R595.112.07 archive `567d4ba082a54ef733e7ce87c87401da488a3aabe29837c92feb19011c8250c4`. Across 2,172 C/H files in `kernel-open` and `src/nvidia`, 93 differ (17 and 76 respectively), with no files added or removed. The 213-file `kernel-open/nvidia-uvm` C/H manifest is byte-identical. The [reproducible audit](../tools/audit_r595_open_release_delta.py) emits [this report](../evidence/r595-open-kernel-source-delta-v1-20261031.json), SHA-256 `45aaa0ed93da7a84b7b58e96689e92fd562a67a557a2ed3f7cc8dd6680b5d255`.

The targeted change is in `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c`: `nvos_create_alloc()` allocated its `nvidia_pte_t` page table with `kvzalloc(pt_size, NV_GFP_KERNEL)` and `nvos_free_alloc()` released it with `kvfree()`. R595.112.07 states that `kvzalloc()` rejects sizes over `INT_MAX`; the newer code routes oversized page tables through zeroed `nv_vmalloc()` and the matching sized `nv_vfree()`. The functions are called for peer-I/O memory, user page registration, and system-page allocations, so the change is relevant to very large kernel mappings, including P2P-facing paths. It does not itself enable device-to-device DMA.

Backport [`0015-open-large-page-table-vmalloc.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0015-open-large-page-table-vmalloc.patch) reproduces the complete pinned R595.112.07 `nv.c` from the clean R595.99.02 source. In the adapted hybrid replay, the full file is pinned before and after the patch and both hunks must report the recorded 56-line offset with zero fuzz. [`r595_large_page_table_host_harness.py`](../tools/r595_large_page_table_host_harness.py) compiles the exact newer function bodies with host stubs and exercises the overflow and allocator/free selection. This is an exact-source CPU harness; it does not prove kernel allocation behavior, P2P mapping, use by the V100, or performance. The matching full candidate build still awaits PC3's exact Linux 6.8 build headers/toolchain and an available hardware window.

## Targeted necessity test for Volta third-party P2P RM requests (2026-10-30)

The stock V100 capture proves that a successful ordinary CUDA workload co-occurred with a BAR1 `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` allocation and controls `0x503c0102`, `0x503c0104`, and `0x503c0105`; it does not establish that any request is required for ordinary CUDA. To distinguish P2P-specific setup from a CUDA initialization dependency, the local experimental runner now supports one fresh-process fault at a time under the stock driver.

The `LD_PRELOAD` shim is built against six hash-pinned R580.196.07 SDK headers, with 21 non-system source/dependency hashes recorded before and after compilation. It intercepts only an fd whose character-device identity matches `/dev/nvidiactl`. Allocation mode denies every class `0x503c` request with a four-byte `flags=1` BAR1 payload in that probe process. Control modes require a previously successful tracked allocation and match the exact command and R580 payload size (16, 32, or 4 bytes); every matching invocation is denied and counted. Allocation/control/free transaction serialization protects the tracking table from handle-reuse races. If its 64 entries are exhausted, the shim emits an explicit overflow marker that the runner rejects. Other descriptors, classes, commands, handles, roots, sizes, and unreadable payloads continue to the real ioctl. Denials return `-1/EIO` and a mode-specific line on probe stderr. The interposed entry point is assembly so unrelated variadic ioctl arguments are passed unchanged; only the three fixed RM ioctl numbers enter C dispatch. CPU-only fake-ioctl tests validate a no-argument request, a scalar argument, matching and mismatching device identities, and overflow handling; no NVIDIA device is opened. See the current build and software-test receipts in [validation](VALIDATION.md).

The inference under test is that denying a P2P resource or registration may leave ordinary V100 CUDA usable if the mechanism is optional for single-device compute. A baseline and one A/B run per mode must test `cuInit`, context creation, device allocation, SM70 numerical kernel, synchronization, and cleanup. If the probe fails, the diagnostic only proves the intended request was denied; it does not identify whether that request was necessary unless the matching baseline passed and other variables remained fixed. A passing single-V100 test would still not demonstrate P2P DMA or hybrid V100/Blackwell behavior.

Status: analyzed statically; injector implemented and compiled; synthetic device-identity, overflow, and diagnostic tests passed. Hardware validation remains pending until PC3 is reachable and its V100 guest state is refreshed. No remote test has yet validated this shim on hardware.


## R580 `GF100_SUBDEVICE_MASTER` control and allocation route (2026-10-30)

**Direct observation:** the stock V100 capture in [`pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-control-header-comparison-v5-20261023.json`](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-control-header-comparison-v5-20261023.json), SHA-256 `1d9e193a1fc06c1cbb75194d6de854f48b17a7a99786beadc524e36872ba302b`, records one Volta-only command `0x90e60101`, declared `params_size=16`, one embedded status `0x00000000`, and no syscall failure. The capture records command headers only: it does not read or preserve nested payload bytes. It reports `hybrid_runtime_validated=false` and explicitly excludes cross-version payload safety.

**Pinned source finding:** R580.178.04's `NV_GPU_OPS` setup allocates `GF100_SUBDEVICE_MASTER` under `NV20_SUBDEVICE_0` with a four-byte `NvS32` scratch input; the path reuses one master object across ECC/NVLink setup and queries the fixed interrupt offset/mask control. R580.196.07 accepts the locally applied 47-patch port with zero fuzz and an unchanged proprietary core. This is source evidence, not reconstruction of proprietary binary pseudocode.

**Implementation:** the frontend now tracks the master as a typed legacy object, sends its allocation and `0x90e60101` only to the R580 provider, rejects malformed or cross-provider ancestry, and constrains its CPU mapping to the immediate owning subdevice. Added typed wrappers and declarations to the private provider, and handled compact/basic/extended kernel modversion records plus zstd-compressed UVM consumer modules in the frontend builder. The latter is required by the current kernel's `CONFIG_BASIC_MODVERSIONS=y`, `CONFIG_EXTENDED_MODVERSIONS=y`, and installed `nvidia-uvm.ko.zst` input.

The source-port, private-provider, and frontend reports are local under `../evidence/`. The frontend build validates 241 private symbol CRCs and 79/79 public UVM consumer CRCs. Those checks do not validate payload semantics or runtime behavior. No binary was loaded, no GPU was accessed, and same-process CUDA remains unproven. The next class investigation is `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` (`0x503c`); its observed allocation header alone still does not justify forwarding or defaulting its payload.

## Exact-target `UvmGpuInfo` layout and numeric selector domains (2026-10-29)

The exact R580.196.07 and R595.99.02 source trees compile the same `UvmGpuInfo` size and offsets for all 35 fields. The host probe finds `GV100=0x140` and `UVM_VIRT_MODE={NONE:0, LEGACY:1, SRIOV_HEAVY:2, SRIOV_STANDARD:3, COUNT:4}` in both branches. It finds the known numeric-domain exception in `sysmemLink`: the R595 `PCIE_BAR1` insertion shifts all later link values. See the hash-pinned [value-domain receipt](../evidence/r595-hybrid-gpu-info-value-domains-v2-20261029.json).

The callback adapter preserves `gpuArch`, `gpuImplementation`, `hostClass`, `ceClass`, `computeClass`, and `virtMode`; its compiled host test uses marker values to check that only `sysmemLink` is changed on success and that failures clear the whole output. In the exact UVM sources, `uvm_hal.c` looks up CE, host, and architecture operation rows from the corresponding numeric fields. R580.196.07 has Volta CE/host rows (`VOLTA_DMA_COPY_A`, `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A`); the R595 source does not provide a Volta UVM row, so the experimental candidate stages the matching legacy HAL and must keep provider class IDs paired with it. This is source/host-test evidence, not proof that the provider returns these values on hardware or that the HAL initializes successfully.

That follow-up confirms the R580 constants and table IDs align (`GV100=0x140`, Volta implementation `0`, host `0xC36F`, CE `0xC3B5`), and the already-built hybrid UVM contains the expected Volta objects. The source census finds no direct `computeClass` consumer in either UVM tree. The exact values, source hashes, row locations, and module-build reference are preserved in the [selector/dispatch receipt](../evidence/r595-hybrid-gv100-gpu-info-dispatch-v1-20261029.json). The [35-field consumer census](../evidence/r595-hybrid-uvm-gpu-info-consumer-census-v1-20261029.json) is explicitly regex-level and does not model aliases or runtime behavior. A direct source-body comparison follows.

## R580 access-counter workaround and remaining field comparison (2026-10-29)

R580.196.07 clamps `accessCntrBufferCount` to one immediately after `nvUvmInterfaceGetGpuInfo()` in `gpu_retain_by_uuid_locked()` (`uvm_gpu.c`, lines 2937–2938), explicitly citing Bug 5262806. The hybrid frontend calls the R580 provider directly, so the old R580 UVM consumer's workaround was not in the R595 path. R595 has no corresponding generic clamp in `uvm_gpu.c`; its `uvm_blackwell.c` applies the workaround in the Blackwell HAL after recording whether access counters are supported. The legacy-only callback now caps the value before returning `UvmGpuInfo` to R595 UVM. This preserves the old Volta-side bound without altering the Open/Blackwell provider path. The exact R580/R595 source hashes and callback build are in the artifact manifest (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r595-hybrid-gv100-access-counter-war-v1-20261029/ARTIFACT.json`).

The same source comparison finds identical calculations for `systemMemoryWindowStart/Size`, identical direct assignments for ATS and non-PASID ATS, and identical EGM and CDMM field assignments. The meaningful remaining difference is the conditional peer-window selection: R580 chooses the NVSwitch window before the direct-connect window; R595 chooses the direct-connect window first. If both `connectedToSwitch` and `nvlDirectConnect` are true, the selected window differs. No preserved PC3 V100 capture currently establishes whether that combination occurs, so the bridge leaves both source fields untouched and does not choose a synthetic rule.

## R580/R595 `UvmGpuInfo::sysmemLink` enum delta (2026-10-29)

The pinned R580.196.07 `UVM_LINK_TYPE` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r58019607-sync-boost-legacy-build-v2/source/common/inc/nv_uvm_types.h`) has `NONE=0`, `PCIE=1`, `NVLINK_1..5=2..6`, and `C2C=7`. The R595.99.02 `UVM_LINK_TYPE` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r595-syncboost-open-build-v2/source/kernel-open/common/inc/nv_uvm_types.h`) inserts `PCIE_BAR1=2`, so NVLink and C2C become `3..8`. The corresponding `UvmGpuInfo` field remains a 32-bit numeric value with equal structure size/offsets, meaning layout checks and module-version CRCs do not reveal this value-domain incompatibility.

R580 `build/experiments/r58019607-sync-boost-legacy-build-v2/source/nvidia-uvm/uvm_gpu.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) and R595's `uvm_gpu.c` consume `gpu_info->sysmemLink` in the GPU link classifier. The hybrid legacy info callback now translates all eight known values before handing the result to R595 UVM, zeroes the output when the backend fails, and returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` with a zeroed output for unknown values. It passes `has_bar1_dma=false`: `UvmGpuInfo` does not carry BAR1 DMA windows; the separate P2P-capability adapter handles the R595 BAR1 category from its own size/address fields.

The exact production callback is compiled into a CPU fixture with pinned R595 headers and UBSan. It checks all eight enum translations and a deliberately invalid value; the test cannot prove the proprietary callback's runtime values or hardware link classification. The [ABI test](../tests/test_hybrid580_uvm_abi_compat.py) and [host fixture](../tests/fixtures/hybrid580_uvm_legacy_gpu_info_compat_test.c) pass. Exact-target frontend build, patched UVM rebuild against its fresh `Module.symvers`, and four-module static closure are preserved in [`STATE.md`](STATE.md) and [`VALIDATION.md`](VALIDATION.md). No module was inserted and no GPU was queried.

## Differential `libcuda` UVM setup analysis: R580.178, R580.196, and R595.99 (2026-10-29)

REA `rea-agents@6.8.0` with Ghidra 12.1.4 performed targeted static analysis of three hash-pinned x86-64 `libcuda` binaries. Inputs: R580.178.04 `dcdf334aeb177c95b3385b797c9b871d03a287895713c6d107f79096b0985aa9`; R580.196.07 `a5c77bf2de83c0ec06ecd368d224c3e0d653d658d6f99319921e976f21867b14`; R595.99.02 `192d4eedb1b39f0568f2b5423d35227d13b32b9025599e1968a9081982da207d`. The targeted dossier contains 13 analyzed procedures for each R580 library and three for R595. Default Ghidra names such as `FUN_0056e830`, `FUN_0056e890`, and `FUN_00524600` identify analysis locations only; pseudocode is not source.

In the targeted setup paths, all three binaries show `UVM_INITIALIZE` (`0x30000001`) and `UVM_CREATE_RANGE_GROUP` (`0x17`) and a helper call carrying `UVM_MM_INITIALIZE` (`0x4b`). The exact R580/R595 headers compile to the same command values and selected payload layouts for those three requests. Do not confuse request `0x17` with event control: `0x17` is `UVM_CREATE_RANGE_GROUP`; `UVM_EVENT_CTRL` is `0x11`. Its Linux payload has a genuine branch delta: R580 includes `sessionIndex` (20-byte structure), R595 omits it (16-byte structure). The two captured stock R580 V100 context traces did not contain request `0x11`, and their payload bytes were not captured.

Evidence: [R580/R595 synthesis](../evidence/libcuda-uvm-crossbranch-static-analysis-v1-20261029.json), [compiled ABI report v4](../evidence/uvm-payload-abi-r580-r595-v100-sequence-v4-20261029.json), compressed R580.178 analysis (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r58017804-libcuda-uvm-targeted-analysis-v1-20261029.json.gz`), R580.196 analysis (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r58019607-libcuda-uvm-targeted-analysis-v1-20261029.json.gz`), and R595 analysis (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r595-libcuda-uvm-targeted-analysis-v1-20261029.json.gz`). The synthesis report records hashes for both compressed artifacts and their decompressed JSON. This is static code plus host-compiled UAPI layout evidence: it does not prove that R595 `libcuda` executes on a V100, that the decompiled caller reaches a live ioctl with a particular runtime payload, or that UVM registration, migration, P2P, or same-process hybrid CUDA works.

## Observed V100 UVM handler-body comparison (2026-10-29)

The [`compare_observed_uvm_handler_bodies.py`](../tools/compare_observed_uvm_handler_bodies.py) binds both repeated stock V100 request-metadata captures to the route receipt, verifies the pinned R580/R595 dispatch-source hashes, inventories each C translation unit, and compares uniquely selected `NV_STATUS` bodies. It finds **15/16 public handlers** token-identical, **4/5 direct helpers** identical, and **6/8 selected second-level helpers** identical; the two second-level differences are `uvm_map_external_allocation_on_gpu` and `uvm_user_channel_create`. Lexical token identity does not establish semantic or recursive call-graph equivalence. The [private comparison receipt v5](../evidence/uvm-observed-handler-body-compare-v5-20261029.json), SHA-256 `baadb7b05b644e0a3f91f452c582ce9a6363d56437bb221f4966f1e381d3b8c6`, records source hashes and line anchors. R580 `uvm_free` still validates a base/length range while R595 frees by base; captured ioctl payload bytes are absent. No proprietary pseudocode is treated as recovered source.

The `uvm_user_channel_create` delta stores `workSubmissionOffset` and `workSubmissionToken` into the R595 `uvm_user_channel_t` (source (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r595-gv100-hal-standard-nvidia-frontend-v15-20261028T004147Z/nvidia-uvm/uvm_user_channel.c`)); the searched UVM tree has one consumer of those stored members, `uvm_hal_ampere_host_clear_faulted_channel_register` (source (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r595-gv100-hal-standard-nvidia-frontend-v15-20261028T004147Z/nvidia-uvm/uvm_ampere_host.c`)). The combined candidate's staged `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A` host row instead resolves `write_gpu_put` to `uvm_hal_volta_host_write_gpu_put` and register-based fault clearing to the explicit unsupported callback ([build receipt](../evidence/r595-hybrid-gv100-linux68-build-v2-20261029.json)). That Volta writer operates through `channel->channel_info.workSubmissionOffset/Token` in the R580-derived source (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r580-nonreplayable-fault-drains-v3-20261028T0634Z/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_volta_host.c`). The source delta therefore appears Ampere-specific; it provides no evidence of a Volta feature or performance gain. The R595 UVM/Volta runtime route is still untested.

### Complete archive UVM ABI and Volta HAL comparison — 2026-11-02

[`audit_uvm_ioctl_archives.py`](../tools/audit_uvm_ioctl_archives.py) verifies full source-archive SHA-256 values before parsing and reads only six allowlisted UVM files (`uvm.c`, `uvm_hal.c`, `uvm_ioctl.h`, `uvm_linux_ioctl.h`, `uvm_api.h`, and `uvm_policy.c`) from R595.112.07 (commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2`, archive SHA-256 `567d4ba082a54ef733e7ce87c87401da488a3aabe29837c92feb19011c8250c4`) and R615.78.08 (commit `1a82fbfda8f321b056eb3568850f9316c5e465c5`, archive SHA-256 `590ee6a5a780f946322edd17f9d235c6664eb7e6839073ac0d68c0f263e7184f`). It reads the same allowlisted members from the pinned R580.178.04 archive (`060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`) and never extracts arbitrary tar entries.

The complete route inventories close: R595 has 39 stack-init routes, 43 total route macros, 68 definitions in `uvm_ioctl.h` plus two Linux-only direct ioctl definitions; R615 has 38, 42, and 67 plus two. R615 removes six range-group migration commands and adds five DMA-BUF/residency commands. For the six Volta UVM HAL tables, all GV100 targets are present in R580 and absent from the official R595.112.07 and R615.78.08 sources. Each branch's `uvm_hal_init_gpu` returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS` when the corresponding GV100 class lookup misses, before installing operations. The project’s hybrid UVM overlay is therefore still required on either Open RM base; the stock modern tables do not supply the Volta backend. This conclusion is conditional on RM supplying the documented GV100 class IDs and does not prove those values or callback semantics at runtime.

R580 defines and dispatches `UVM_ENABLE_SYSTEM_WIDE_ATOMICS` and `UVM_DISABLE_SYSTEM_WIDE_ATOMICS` to handlers in `uvm_policy.c`; `system_wide_atomics_set` rejects either operation when `scoped_atomics_supported` is true. Both ioctl definitions, dispatch routes, handler names, and the policy helper are absent from pinned R595.112.07 and R615.78.08 UVM sources. This resolves the old field as a guard for a retired UVM policy API; it does not establish CUDA C++ atomic-scope support or compatibility with older user-mode clients. The updated private [compatibility receipt v2](../evidence/uvm-archive-compatibility-audit-v2-20261102.json), SHA-256 `6a971cbc9236a6aa745f6b791f58b598f2ffd1dda7e0f8a72828ea0fc88e97c`, contains that closure alongside the prior route/HAL results. The route-only [receipt](../evidence/uvm-ioctl-route-archive-compare-v3-20261102.json) remains SHA-256 `7cc6ad537e69fbaed59481890c714497ba199549b107b57055f29cc3c97690f6`. Fourteen focused archive and candidate-path tests pass. These are static-source results: no module load, CUDA/UVM execution, GPU access, or performance measurement is implied.

### Corrected R580-to-R595 GV100 callback closure — 2026-11-02

The first v15 module report used the R580 callback list for module closure and falsely treated two intentional Pascal-to-Turing callback substitutions as unresolved. `audit_volta_uvm_hal_dispatch.py` now audits the callbacks actually resolved from each modern HAL row, checks their source definitions and linked-module symbols, and emits a per-operation delta. On the existing R595.99.02 GV100-HAL-v15 candidate, all six target rows resolve and all **82/82** selected callback symbols are defined in `nvidia-uvm.ko`; no callback definition is missing from the candidate source tree. Three differences from R580 are recorded: `enable_prefetch_faults` and `disable_prefetch_faults` select Turing callbacks, while the modern no-argument MMU-mode API selects a wrapper that calls the Volta mode with 64 KiB pages. The Turing bodies retain the same low-level prefetch-control path, but the R580 enable callback and caller assert `prefetch_fault_supported`; the R595 caller omits that guard. This is an unresolved semantic compatibility item, not proof of runtime safety. The corrected [candidate report](../evidence/r595-gv100-hal-v15-callback-closure-v3-20261102.json) is SHA-256 `1fefad587f85c64234c2197d59f9176f0951c2481f33e60f0ef2b5cf4b282204`; the earlier v1 diagnostic is retained but superseded. No proprietary NVIDIA binary was analyzed in this increment.

### R580-to-R595 Volta initializer property delta — 2026-11-02

[`audit_volta_uvm_property_delta.py`](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_property_delta.py) hash-checks the full R580.178.04 source archive, extracts only its UVM C sources into a temporary directory, parses `uvm_hal_volta_arch_init_properties`, and scans the candidate UVM C/H tree after excluding comments and string literals. The R580 initializer assigns 33 parent-GPU properties; the candidate R595 v15 initializer assigns 22. Eleven old properties have zero matching live-code tokens in the modern UVM tree: `can_map_sysmem_with_large_pages`, `fault_cancel_va_supported`, `gpfifo_in_vidmem_supported`, `non_replayable_faults_supported`, `plc_supported`, `prefetch_fault_supported`, `replayable_faults_supported`, `scoped_atomics_supported`, `sparse_mappings_supported`, `tlb_batch.va_invalidate_supported`, and `tlb_batch.va_range_invalidate_supported`. R595 v15 sets `tlb_batch.max_ranges = 8`, and its TLB batching structure stores range records, but this audit does not establish one-to-one semantic replacements for those eleven flags. It is a lead list for call-site tracing and tests, not a claim that eleven features are broken. The mode-0600 [receipt](../evidence/volta-uvm-property-delta-v2-20261102.json) is SHA-256 `ea550b37ede651f5396c441575cd79fe0e39c1b2d795b34ff9c56a18af418740`; focused parser tests cover comments, strings, duplicate assignments, and missing fields. No proprietary binary or REA output was used for this source-level comparison.

[`audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py`](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_candidate_paths.py) extends that lexical audit to exact consumers and candidate call paths. It pins the R580.178.04 archive and R595.99.02 GV100-HAL-v15 `uvm_hal.c` digest, rejects source symlinks, verifies all 11 R580 Volta assignments/consumer anchors, records candidate line anchors and a 242-file C/H manifest, and requires all 11 old field identifiers to remain absent from live candidate code. The updated [mode-0600 receipt v4](../evidence/volta-uvm-r580-r595-v15-capability-paths-v4-20261102.json), SHA-256 `44c093bfc2295e0bca74b8ad8beddc8f080d8835369c0dba4b700f0bc417cab3`, additionally pins the absence of both retired ioctl names, both handler declarations, both dispatcher references, and `system_wide_atomics_set` in the candidate.

The candidate source shows TLB invalidation dispatched through a selected Volta host callback, while range batching now counts records and falls back to invalidate-all above `max_ranges=8`; large-page selection is driven by the MMU page tree and a Volta 64 KiB MMU wrapper; replayable/non-replayable fault setup uses modern fault-buffer HAL rows; channel policy forwards GPFIFO location to RM; sparse PTE generation uses the MMU HAL with Pascal operations inherited by the Volta mode; and a GV100 `EnabledNoPlc` rejection runs before external overlap removal. These are static call-path findings only. `scoped_atomics_supported` is resolved as a guard for the retired system-wide-atomics UVM ioctls; user-mode ABI compatibility remains untested. The prefetch path selects Turing MMU control callbacks and has the previously identified removed-guard difference. Focused audit/property/capability tests pass **30/30**; every mapping still has `runtime_or_hardware_validated=false`.

### REA/Ghidra differential on the open UVM TLB callback — 2026-11-02

REA 6.8.0 with Ghidra 12.1.4 analyzed the redistributable, unstripped Dual MIT/GPL R580.196.07 and R595.99.02/v15 `nvidia-uvm.ko` artifacts. Their hashes are `5dcf1f18ad7c2a7f7337039657f2a519bd25bf7a9c9ffd0a3ccde482de32b5f0` and `21c6b16c4fc64d81b4326866ea708b8229735035724fcc8bfb95788b8c442b1b`. The complete `uvm_volta_host.c` files match byte-for-byte (SHA-256 `582c18a24986db6de49ce0c36fa98430062324b0bffd7e2390b2451e5b45f3fb`). Both ELF symbol tables report a 334-byte `uvm_hal_volta_host_tlb_invalidate_va` body.

Ghidra reconstructs the R595 body as 334 bytes (97 instructions, four basic blocks), with external/thunk and local-variable caveats retained in the REA dossier (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r595-v15-uvm-volta-tlb-ghidra-dossier-v1-20261102.json`), SHA-256 `7fb7d9414a5f1d2a331a51517c21c021a3303f725a647634dfa1320bc03f9121`. On R580, Ghidra assigns only the entry `CALL __fentry__` to the function and marks that thunk no-return, truncating its observed body to five bytes. A REA query by file offset maps to the same entry and preserves that limitation; the R580 dossier (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580196-v100-tlb-ghidra-dossier-v1-20261102.json`), SHA-256 `870737154c783b0453321345867041d97aad250dd2e8d85df040656ee18cb27c`, and file-offset record (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580196-v100-tlb-fileoffset-ghidra-v1-20261102.json`), SHA-256 `63618f32921fa85700c802dd049c0db274cfabda53195999f05857706fb13971`, capture this as a Ghidra flow-model limitation, not missing machine code. `objdump` decodes the full symbol in both artifacts.

ELF DWARF gives `uvm_parent_gpu_struct::host_hal` offsets 552 (`0x228`) in R580 and 1,592 (`0x638`) in R595. R595 adds `UvmGpuAccessBitsBufferAlloc vab_info` at offset 496 with size 1,040 bytes, which accounts exactly for the 1,040-byte shift. The matching source expression `parent->host_hal` therefore compiles to the corresponding offset in each branch; this evidence argues against copying a hard-coded machine-code load or changing the source callback merely to match R580. It says nothing about RM acceptance, TLB semantics, or runtime behavior. The Ghidra outputs include relocation/DWARF and external-flow warnings, so the conclusion is based on their bounded observations cross-checked against ELF symbols, DWARF, source hashes, and `objdump`, not on pseudocode alone. No proprietary NVIDIA binary was analyzed.

## R595 pageable-memory ioctl caller and kernel route (2026-10-29)

On the hash-pinned R595.99.02 `libcuda.so` (`192d4eed…2da207d`), the auto-named function at `0x4063d0` places request `0x27` in the ioctl argument register and calls the auto-named wrapper at `0x506890` (`0x4064c0`). The wrapper calls the imported ioctl thunk at `0x264140` and retries when errno is `EINTR` or `EAGAIN`. Ghidra returned 93 references to the imported thunk, 17 to the wrapper at `0x506890`, and four to the status wrapper at `0x5068f0`; these are static reference counts, not runtime call counts. Names and decompiled output are analysis artifacts, not original symbols/source. The runtime file descriptor and ioctl payload remain unknown.

R595.99.02 UVM source defines `UVM_PAGEABLE_MEM_ACCESS` as `UVM_IOCTL_BASE(39)` (`0x27`), routes it through `uvm_ioctl()` to `uvm_api_pageable_mem_access()`, and has the handler query `uvm_va_space_pageable_mem_access_supported()`. Source hashes, line anchors, compressed raw REA dossiers and limitations are recorded in the call-path receipt (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r595-libcuda-uvm-callpath-v1-20261029.json`). The raw caller dossier (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea/libcuda-r595-uvm-callers-v1-20261029.json.gz`) and targeted procedure analysis (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea/libcuda-r595-uvm-targeted-analysis-v1-20261029.json.gz`) are retained compressed with mode 0600. This static route does not establish that R595 `libcuda` runs on Volta or that a hybrid kernel accepts the call.

## R595.99 kmod ignores comma-separated SPDM soft prerequisites (2026-10-29)

Differential inspection found `kernel-open/nvidia/libspdm_ecc.c` in the R595.99.02 replay still contained `MODULE_SOFTDEP("pre: ecdh_generic,ecdsa_generic")`. On the host’s kmod 34.2, `depmod` preserved that text but `modprobe --show-depends` did not schedule either prerequisite. This reproduces the same kmod parsing defect previously fixed for R580; the R580 fix had not been replayed into the R595 source series.

The version-pinned patch [`0014`](../patches/r595.99.02/0014-open-kmod-softdep-space-separators.patch) changes the delimiter to spaces, rejects a source preimage mismatch, and asserts the exact postimage with zero fuzz. The replay builder applies this overlay before Kbuild and checks the resulting module metadata. Exact Linux 6.8 relink and kmod evidence are in the [build receipt](../evidence/r595-open-kmod-softdep-linux68-gcc13-v2-20261029.json), [four-module audit](../evidence/r595-hybrid-bundle-static-audit-linux68-v2-20261029.json), and [private depmod plan](../evidence/r595-hybrid-depmod-plan-linux68-v1-20261029.json). This is a loader-order correction only; it proves no firmware communication, module init, or GPU behavior. The reproducible exact-target staging path is [`verify_r595_linux68_hybrid_depmod.py`](../tools/verify_r595_linux68_hybrid_depmod.py); its actual kmod 34.2 plan is preserved in [report v3](../evidence/r595-hybrid-depmod-plan-linux68-v3-20261029.json), SHA-256 `c23c22a1c92612d160512d62438896c70507e245de357bc8323d80d49f0bda4d`.


## R595 public/private UVM CRC boundaries on Linux 6.8 (2026-10-29)

The 79-entry R595 frontend report originally labeled a mismatch when the CRC for public `nvUvmInterfaceX` differed from private provider exports named `hybrid580_open_nvUvmInterfaceX` and `hybrid580_legacy_nvUvmInterfaceX`. Those are different exported symbols and separate linkage edges. A tiny external-module probe against the exact Linux `6.8.0-147-generic` build tree exported two functions with the same C type under different names; their CRCs were `0x53fb455d` and `0x21f6ffa5`. The old equality requirement was therefore a checker defect, not evidence that all 79 payloads were ABI-incompatible.

The frontend builder now checks: (1) the public standard export exists, (2) each private import CRC equals the named Open or legacy provider export, and (3) an exact-target public consumer imports the same CRC as the frontend. The two R580 UVM event callbacks are mapped to their actual private legacy adapter names (`RegisterUvmCallbacks` and `DeRegisterUvmOps`). This keeps CRC checks strict at each real edge and leaves payload-layout/semantic audits separate.

For the R595.99.02 Linux 6.8 build, the public export/import consumer check matched **79/79**; private imports matched **239/239**; frontend imports totaled 300 with zero unresolved or duplicated owners. See the durable [receipt](../evidence/r595-hybrid-frontend-linux68-gcc13-v4-20261029T1237Z.json) and module (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r595-hybrid-frontend-linux68-gcc13-v4/nvidia.ko`). The reference UVM consumer is the clean R595 module, not the patched hybrid UVM candidate. This proves neither module insertion nor GPU/RM execution.

## Exercise both page-backed-PFN guard branches and bound the V100 kernel ABI (2026-10-29)

The exact-source host harness extracts the helper and `get_io_ptes()` logic from pinned R595.112.07 `os-mlock.c`, substitutes explicit stubs for the PFN predicates, and compiles/runs both configurations. It observes rejection before address publication for page-backed PFNs and acceptance of a contiguous unbacked I/O range. This is an isolated CPU harness, not a loaded kernel implementation or hardware result. [Harness](../tools/r595_pfn_backing_host_harness.py), SHA-256 `ef364742a151c772a340c5529f6bea7c12046365c6a87a9ba083b9e7e7f8c593`; [report](../evidence/r595-pfn-backed-io-host-harness-v1-20261029T1210Z.json), SHA-256 `1c03bbde0cba5db5d095f6f54139059b0965624511db8bf5c120a6e246140ec3`.

The candidate built for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` imports `mem_section` with CRC `0x211f9d4e`, while the hash-pinned V100 guest Linux `6.8.0-147-generic` `Module.symvers` exports it with CRC `0x3199fbeb`. This is a cross-kernel artifact mismatch, not a test of whether the source can compile for 6.8. A prior R580.178.04 provider build receipt confirms that the V100 guest once had the exact target kernel headers, but the current workspace and reachable PC6 do not contain that exact header tree, and PC3 is offline. The [comparison receipt](../evidence/r595-pfn-guard-cross-kernel-crc-v1-20261029T1215Z.json), SHA-256 `85ce2aace4b6a7ec14eddd7d4e48ee932e74c7756d3a77f7826054452c1f9b22`, preserves this boundary. No module was installed or loaded.

## R595.112 page-backed-PFN guard backported to R595.99 (2026-10-29)

An exact source diff between R595.99.02 and R595.112.07 `os-mlock.c` shows only the page-backed-PFN guard. The `nv_pfn_has_page_backing()` helper selects `pfn_is_map_memory()` under `NV_PFN_IS_MAP_MEMORY_PRESENT` and `pfn_valid()` otherwise; `get_io_ptes()` returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_ADDRESS` before placing the PFN-derived address into `pte_array`. On the inspected x86 Linux 7.0 build, the fallback contributes a `mem_section` import. The target kernel exports it in its `Module.symvers`; the full static bundle audit confirms the CRC closure.

[`0013-open-pfn-backed-io-guard.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0013-open-pfn-backed-io-guard.patch) applies to the pinned R595.99.02 source at zero fuzz, and the result matches the R595.112.07 source file SHA-256 `760a5ee564cd0f151aea337eca146578bed157c80c8152f9aa9b414db6576609`. Two pinned tests cover exact application and guard-before-publication source order. The build receipt shows the R595.99.02 Open module compiled for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`; the provider `Module.symvers` remained byte-identical to the shared-conftest control. The four-module static audit passes with zero issues.

This proves the overlay compiles and closes against the PC3 host kernel only. It does not show that the vulnerable mapping reaches this path on the V100, prove the guard's runtime behavior, or establish compatibility with the V100 guest kernel `6.8.0-147`, whose headers were unavailable here. No module was loaded and no GPU test or performance measurement occurred. See [build report](../evidence/r595-open-pfn-guard-r59599-build-v1-20261029.json), [audit v6](../evidence/r595-syncboost-nvidia-gv100-hal-bundle-audit-v6-pfn-guard-20261029.json), and the [full-suite report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-pfn-guard-overlay-v1-20261029T1204Z.json).

## R595.99 shared conftest control and R595.112 import delta (2026-10-29)

The R595.99 provider-only build selected a different generated feature map from the hybrid UVM candidate: its `conftest/types.h` omitted `NV_MEMORY_DEVICE_COHERENT_PRESENT`, while the UVM candidate defined it. UVM registers this probe in its Kbuild; therefore the provider build was not a valid control for comparing their conditional CDMM code. A manual isolated Kbuild with `NV_KERNEL_MODULES=nvidia nvidia-uvm` and the pinned frontend `Module.symvers` produced a provider header with the same normalized macro map as the UVM candidate. The provider `Module.symvers` hash remained identical, so this is not an added backend export or a new frontend ABI.

Compared with the mismatched provider-only module, the controlled R595.99 Open module adds the named kernel imports `get_dev_pagemap`, `__rcu_read_lock`, and `__rcu_read_unlock`, matching its coherent-memory probe branch. R595.112 adds only `mem_section` beyond that controlled module in the inspected import set. In R595.112 `kernel-open/nvidia/os-mlock.c`, `nv_pfn_has_page_backing()` and `get_io_ptes()` reject page-backed PFNs; on this x86 build the feature probe falls back to `pfn_valid()`, whose object relocation refers to `mem_section`. This is a safety behavior, not a performance optimization.

The complete static bundle auditor accepts the controlled R595.99 Open module with the pinned frontend, R580.196.07 Volta backend and isolated UVM module: **no CRC, export, identity or dependency issues**. The [control receipt](../evidence/r595-syncboost-open-shared-uvm-conftest-control-v1-20261029.json) (SHA-256 `3532476ca18854baf9bd2129227237080e0325190871f228046e62d15ddc26d7`), [symbol-count correction](../evidence/r595-open-shared-uvm-conftest-symbol-count-correction-v1-20261029.json), and [bundle audit v5](../evidence/r595-syncboost-nvidia-gv100-hal-bundle-audit-v5-shared-conftest-20261029.json) preserve the evidence. The original count receipt remains unchanged; its correction distinguishes total ELF `UND` table rows from named unique imports.

CDMM remains conditionally gated by kernel configuration, GPU coherent-memory capability and RM-provided `cdmmEnabled` state. No observation shows this route active on the V100, and no throughput gain is measured. Modules remain unloaded/uninstalled; CUDA, UVM migration and P2P on this candidate have not been exercised.

## R595.112.07 hybrid source port and compile boundary (2026-10-29)

The official R595.112.07 Open GPU Kernel Modules source matches the package OpenRM sources; its matching `kernel-open/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary` was extracted from NVIDIA's R595.112.07 installer and kept in the private vendor cache. The R580 private-Open integration chain required three line-anchor updates because upstream additions moved unchanged contexts: `nv-caps.c` 823→866, `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES` 5957→5969, and `SYSTEM_GET_FABRIC_STATUS` 6053→6065. The context was checked for uniqueness, and the resulting 43-patch replay reports no fuzz or offset. The 12 hybrid UVM patches also replay without fuzz/offset.

The replay is now reproducible from the canonical cached archive (SHA-256 `567d4ba082a54ef733e7ce87c87401da488a3aabe29837c92feb19011c8250c4`) and a retained R595.112 baseline symbol table. Its source tree manifest is SHA-256 `8e29bef1a16a00952024fbe0224d90ce115a72b393e89d5cff337fe5f908d2d1`; the source, symbol table and patch inputs stayed unchanged across all 43 applications. The implementation supports both pinned R595.99.02 and .112.07 profiles and has regression coverage for the three version-specific hunk anchors. A [correction receipt](../evidence/r595112-open-patch-port-v1-correction-20261029.json) explains that the initial report's human-readable `0006` line was 176 because the diagnostic regex selected the first hunk; the actual allocation hunk and tested logger target line 866. The full [replay report](../evidence/r595112-open-patch-port-v1-20261029.json) has SHA-256 `44fae48c7dbcbb9a26fedbe8beff5620436d56da82eee32d52bc82452d2dd9d9`.

The patched Open provider, `nvidia` frontend, and UVM consumer compile for `7.0.0-38-generic`. An initial module set without frontend `Module.symvers` failed `modpost` on the three hybrid UVM dispatch exports; passing the frontend symbol table together with the provider table closes those imports. A private `depmod -n` resolves `nvidia` to both private providers and UVM to the frontend and both providers. This is build and static dependency evidence, not a claim that the ABI works at runtime. The [hash-pinned report](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-stack-build-v1-20261029T1122Z.json) retains module hashes, exact source and vendor-core provenance, logs, overlay patches, and warnings.

The R595.112.07 track remains separate from the PC3-matched R595.99.02 candidate. Nothing was installed or loaded. GPU initialization, same-process V100+Blackwell CUDA, UVM/P2P behavior, prolonged stability, and performance remain untested.

## Driver branch base differential (2026-10-29)

The local PC3 candidate is pinned to the observed R595.99.02 host/user stack. I compared that exact OpenRM source against the official NVIDIA Open GPU Kernel Module tags for R595.112.07 and R615.71.10, then replayed the current 12-patch hybrid UVM series in disposable source copies. The tag objects/peeled commits, cache archive hashes, source-tree manifests, path counts, patch results, and qualification boundary are recorded in the reverified mode-0600 [branch differential](../evidence/driver-branch-source-diff-v2-20261029T1105Z.json), SHA-256 `8aacbe293d53f39da21ca6123c1f8923834e31a5d1f54d97583001a91897a2b3`.

| Branch | Exact observation | Integration consequence |
|---|---|---|
| R580.196.07 | NVIDIA's architecture matrix still identifies R580 as Volta's last driver family. The V100 proprietary backend is pinned to the R580.196.07 source/user package. | Keep this as the Volta legacy provider; do not expect a post-R580 standalone Volta stack. |
| R595.99.02 | This is PC3's observed Open driver branch. Its source list still includes `src/kernel/gpu/mem_mgr/arch/volta/mem_mgr_gv100.c`; its exact patched hybrid candidate remains uninstalled. | Keep as the only candidate matched to the present PC3 stack. Retained source is a porting seam, not proof of supported or working GV100 runtime. |
| R595.112.07 | NVIDIA published this production-branch update on 2026-10-22. Against .99.02, 105 files change and **none** are under `kernel-open/nvidia-uvm`; the same 12 hybrid UVM patches apply with no fuzz/offset, and `mem_mgr_gv100.c` remains listed. | Best next isolated modernization build. It is not the current PC3-matched candidate; static patches replaying does not qualify the new module or userspace. |
| R610.57.04 | NVIDIA's supported-driver matrix lists R610 as a New Feature Branch with EOL August 2026; the matrix page says it was last updated 2026-09-09, before R615.71.10's release. R610 source was not compared here. | Lower priority than the current R595 Production branch or the later R615 source experiment; verify release/source details before selecting it. |
| R615.71.10 | NVIDIA release notes date it 2026-10-19. Compared with R595.99.02, the OpenRM tree has 1,275 changed, 266 added, and 71 removed files. It removes GV100 `mem_mgr_gv100.c` and `nvidia-3d-volta.c`. Hybrid patch 0001 fails its first `uvm_va_space.c` hunk at line 42; two later hunks in that patch only apply at offset 133. | Do not use this as the current integration base. A deliberate UVM control-fd port and full module/import rebuild are prerequisites. |

This source-level result favors R595 for the present prototype: the same production line as PC3, a very small later update diff, unchanged UVM source across .99/.112, and a clean hybrid-patch replay. It does **not** show that the retained Volta-specific source is active, works on V100, or improves performance. No source branch in this comparison was built, loaded, or run on a GPU. R595.112.07 remains a separate next build target while the exact .99.02 candidate is preserved for any future PC3 canary.

Official references: [R580/R595/R610 driver and architecture matrix](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/drivers/cuda-toolkit-driver-and-architecture-matrix.html), [R595.112.07 release notes](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/tesla-release-notes-595-112-07/index.html), [R615.71.10 release notes](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/tesla-release-notes-615-71-10/index.html), and [NVIDIA Open GPU Kernel Module releases](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/releases).

## R595 GV100 UVM port bound to the standard-name frontend (2026-10-29)

The first complete bundle check caught a module identity mismatch rather than a symbol-body defect: a frontend compiled as `hybrid580_frontend` caused the R595 UVM consumer to record that as its module dependency. The standard module bundle requires `nvidia`; the failed audit is retained at [`r595-syncboost-gv100-hal-bundle-audit-v1.json`](../evidence/r595-syncboost-gv100-hal-bundle-audit-v1.json), SHA-256 `5e83ae4b88a8de2f19733c2561b38f28e0d2678a49bae3a3554db6f53131bbdf`.

The existing frontend builder supports `--module-name nvidia`. Rebuilding R595 Open plus R580.196.07 proprietary providers under that identity produced a standard-name `nvidia.ko`; the GV100 UVM HAL probe was then rerun against that build's exact `Module.symvers` SHA-256 `dbcf0cc653a970395534635d7ef533c3626112a41b12f8b5cbaf6504bb903e08`. The resulting UVM candidate compiles the six staged GV100 rows and all required objects, closes 79 standard R595 UVM imports and 145 private provider imports, and has no static CRC mismatch. The hash-linked bundle audit verifies 83/83 frontend, 146/146 Open and 146/146 legacy ELF exports against their symbol maps, 305 frontend imports, 329 UVM imports, identities, vermagic and dependencies. In a fresh private module tree, `depmod`/`modprobe --show-depends` resolves the expected order: `hybrid580_open`, `hybrid580_legacy`, `nvidia`, `nvidia_uvm`.

This closes compile-time module identity and symbol provenance for one R595 + R580.196.07 bundle only. It does not establish successful kernel insertion, device registration, GPU initialization, GV100 HAL correctness at runtime, CUDA/UVM/P2P operation or same-process V100+Blackwell execution. No module was installed or loaded; no GPU was accessed. See build, closure, bundle audit and isolated dependency-map hashes in [`VALIDATION.md`](VALIDATION.md).

## Root sync-boost query on stock V100 and hybrid dispatch gap (2026-10-29)

The corrected observer captured one successful `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_SYNC_GPU_BOOST_GROUP_INFO` (`0x0a04`) during the isolated PC3 V100 `enumerate` phase: ioctl return `0`, RM status `0`, flags `0`, fixed payload size `2244`, and `groupCount=0` only at exit. The output-only field is absent at entry. The capture preserves only the count; it does not reveal any group configuration. See the mode-0600 [v4 hardware report](../evidence/pc3-v100-sync-boost-groups-v4-20261029.json), SHA-256 `d6ad69f6559b49719cc291ac39e5339410ce0882adadaedabc53abd9b0bfbde4`.

The independent stock PC3 RTX 5080/R595.99.02 run now shows the same command during `cuInit`/enumeration: one direct `0xc020462a` request (32-byte wrapper), syscall `0`, RM status `0`, and exit-only `groupCount=0` from the same fixed-size payload. It used Driver API 13020 on Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`; only enumeration ran, not a numeric kernel. The [sanitized mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-sync-boost-groups-v2-20261029.json) has SHA-256 `afbdbac8838fa3c89765a63769e4e55205a0072006ddeeec652aa062bd3737d0`. These are matching stock observations on separate Linux kernels, not a hybrid-process result.

The pinned R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 SDK headers for this command are byte-identical (SHA-256 `173c0ccbc907c1fe8e3acf2f996c60bac873ece2e510587e7114613cb371102d`). They define `groupCount` at offset 0, up to 16 `NV0000_SYNC_GPU_BOOST_GROUP_CONFIG` entries, and commands `0xa02`/`0xa03` to create/destroy groups. In both inspected OpenRM source snapshots, `cliresCtrlCmdSyncGpuBoostGroupInfo_IMPL()` calls `gpuboostmgrQueryGroups()` through the system `OBJGPUBOOSTMGR`; that routine enumerates manager slots and returns each group's `gpuCount`, backend GPU IDs and `boostGroupId`. R580 `build/experiments/openrm580-baseline-v1/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/client_resource.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) and `build/experiments/openrm580-baseline-v1/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/src/nvidia/src/kernel/power/gpu_boost_mgr.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) hashes are `bea0ade8836094406ef9f73365c5884c2697878e54e9b7a8b057236847e25787` and `b50959a12d24f7e2f782f45c4f3470b490d36320c93fd10a6a818c30d74f6a79`; R595 `build/research/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/source/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/client_resource.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) and `build/research/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/source/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/src/nvidia/src/kernel/power/gpu_boost_mgr.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) hashes are `a682640a0adecbce80216b14a4f915441eebec45110a319806773046dcf0b935` and `fd08a2c0b030fcdbee3b203d7a0d9e4c5dfe01499033fc33700ed70542e1a918`.

At the time of the initial stock observations, `hybrid_front_rm_control_ioctl()` in [`hybrid_frontend.c`](../src/hybrid_frontend.c) had no `0xa04` route and returned `-ENOTTY`. A direct merge remains unsafe: each Resource Manager owns a separate system boost-group manager, and returned GPU/group IDs are provider-local with possible collisions. The first route therefore validates the mirrored root, queries both managers using typed kernel-owned packets, and publishes a fully zeroed fixed-size payload only if both queries succeed and both counts are zero. A nonzero count returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; provider/RM errors, missing output fields, invalid counts, split-root and copy faults fail closed. No IDs or group entries cross the frontend.

The frontend operation is now implemented and exercised by the host harness in [`test_hybrid_frontend.py`](../tests/test_hybrid_frontend.py). The harness covers direct `NV_ESC_RM_CONTROL` and wrapped `NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD`, the `0/0` success, a nonzero count from either provider, transport and embedded-RM errors, malformed provider output, invalid packet/command, split root, missing session, allocation/lock failure, and both control/payload copy faults. These tests execute the frontend route with stubbed provider calls; they do not execute a kernel module or Resource Manager.

The typed count-only provider is compiled for proprietary R580.196.07 and private Open R595.99.02. R595 required an exact source-specific include of `<ctrl/ctrl0000/ctrl0000syncgpuboost.h>` in the replayed control-session header; without it, its first isolated build failed at compile time because the packet type and command were undeclared. The corrected 43-patch replay applies without offset or fuzz ([report v3](../evidence/r595-syncboost-replay-v3.json), SHA-256 `5bdab15c82ec0e9865d372a0768900a676aa94199519ff7f4d137af5f0227c43`) and the R595 Open provider compiles with both root-control exports ([build report](../evidence/r595-syncboost-open-build-v2.json), SHA-256 `1b7ade33c57102877e6e889e2915b10b10561b7fc59a0dc22f25a0be22d5dc8f`). The frontend compiled against that Open provider and the R580.196.07 proprietary provider with 239/239 private import CRCs matching ([frontend report](../evidence/r595-syncboost-hybrid-frontend-v1.json), SHA-256 `e1908b0b1bf28dee60e8573c0dd41027a24389b4c735e69fce3c4ad914c5caf2`). This remains compile/static/software evidence only. No candidate module was loaded, and no hybrid RM or GPU executed; the stock zero-count observations do not establish what happens when the two hybrid providers are queried together.

The v1 synthetic test originally used `/dev/null`, whose ioctl returns `-ENOTTY`, but treated the seeded input count as returned output. The observer now captures `groupCount` only when both syscall return and RM status are successful. Corrected observer and V100 runner contracts passed **74/74** and **105/105**, and the previous pinned software suite passed **2,060/2,060** (software-only; no candidate module loaded). The new RTX runner mode additionally rejects stale output, redacts transient identifiers, and separates empty, failed, and successful query outcomes; focused suite passed **24/24**. The full suite for this latest runner change is recorded separately in [`VALIDATION.md`](VALIDATION.md). Observer v2 binary SHA-256 is `ece2d885cb95bef2afabd10ad06375e6c043e75685f99182408052327db85fbd`; build report [v2](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-sync-boost-groups-v2-20261029.json), SHA-256 `395e8d056146046d51df88cdedf4b7c125374306f0e29f40c792f70cbfcab8b3`.

## R595 PMM tracker-reserve OOM injection with hook ownership (2026-10-29)

The exact [patch 0012](../patches/r595.99.02/0012-test-only-tracker-reserve-oom-injection.patch) applies to pinned R595.99.02 source and modifies six UVM files only under UVM_TEST_TRACKER_OOM_INJECTION. It scopes reserve failures to one tracker and gives disarm the same tracker identity; a competing tracker-sanity or PMM test cannot clear another test's hook. uvm_pmm_gpu_free() checks successful hook acquisition, captures the count only for its matching root/source fallback, unlocks the root bitlock before waiting, and retains the normal path when the test macro is absent.

The opt-in PMM sanity test places pending GPU_INTERNAL and MEMOPS pushes behind one semaphore, rejects channel-pointer aliasing, seeds one pending root-tracker entry in the static slot, and calls the real PMM free path. The bounded worker releases the semaphore on timeout for cleanup and records that it observed the root bitlock clear after PMM signaled fallback entry. It stores the root index before the chunk is freed. [Python contracts](../tests/test_r595_tracker_reserve_failure_hook.py) verify exact patch application, ordering, ownership checks and mutations; **3/3 focused checks pass**. They do not execute the in-kernel test.

The forced R595.99.02 build for 7.0.0-38-generic passes with KCFLAGS=-DUVM_TEST_TRACKER_OOM_INJECTION; resulting `build/experiments/r595-tracker-reserve-oom-injection-v1-20261029/candidate/nvidia-uvm.ko` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) SHA-256 is a13a1a4e8a9ac6e4e5cf6a841e54f20c7bf7eb6682f28b3fb839e2f4040272cf. The full software suite passes **1,999/1,999** in **129.001 s**. Independent static review found no remaining concrete hook ownership or worker-lifetime issue in the reviewed scope. See mode-0600 [build report](../evidence/r595-tracker-reserve-oom-injection-v4-20261029.json), SHA-256 b7f493a6884d1b4cfed9511a1dd7e4a917470fcac88542cd9f4cbe2e425c8cdd, and mode-0600 [suite report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-tracker-reserve-oom-injection-v4-20261029.json), SHA-256 c6ac750a4ec59d05fca95df3f9532b61b8feee3eb752a5fcb396eced38db42a3.

**Evidence boundary:** this proves source-contract, compilation and software-suite behavior only. The UVM ioctl and GPU semaphore did not run; there is no runtime lockdep/KASAN/KCSAN evidence. The unsigned candidate was not installed or loaded. Hardware CUDA, UVM, P2P and V100+Blackwell same-process behavior remain unverified.

## R595 PMM OOM wait no longer holds the root-chunk bitlock (2026-10-29)

The baseline is the pinned R595.99.02 candidate `build/experiments/r595-hmm-migrate-aggregate-guard-v1-20261029/candidate/nvidia-uvm/`: `uvm_pmm_gpu.c` SHA-256 `753fe5ac7220b7035ae9e641fb6595769166bf556e1aadc3cdfa9ae5c6d5cb31`, `uvm_tracker.c` SHA-256 `1b7fd32a785b78af5492c30a297e148f421af3e04108a587a641932d4ca7499a`. In `uvm_pmm_gpu_free()` (baseline lines 694–725), PMM took the root-chunk bitlock and called `uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe()`. The safe helper waits on its source only after the tracker merge returns `NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY`; `uvm_tracker_add_tracker()` first reserves destination capacity, prunes completed entries and retries, then returns before its entry-copy loop if reservation still fails (`uvm_tracker.c:198–229, 262–269`). The root-chunk comment explicitly says synchronization can take a long time and should not block other PMM operations (`uvm_pmm_gpu.c:47–54`).

Patch [`0011`](../patches/r595.99.02/0011-pmm-wait-source-tracker-outside-root-bitlock.patch), SHA-256 `38d72f8c20c5b149c31f121a804cb24baa371005d7665f61beaaca65e9ef0e6d`, keeps the tracker merge serialized under the bitlock, releases that lock, and only then waits on the source tracker if allocation still failed. It preserves the existing rate-limited OOM diagnostic. Since the merge returns before copying any entries on OOM, waiting for the source completes the operations associated with the chunk; `free_chunk()` is still called afterward, so the allocated/temp-pinned chunk is not published for reuse during the wait. Other chunks may update the root tracker after unlock; this path does not access that tracker again. This is a source-level safety argument, not runtime concurrency proof.

The patch does not release the caller's VA-block or page-tree mutex. The non-null production callers inspected include VA-block cleanup (`uvm_va_block.c:2928, 4734, 8991`) and page-table deallocation under `tree->lock` (`uvm_mmu.c:220–225`). It also does not add provider filtering: successful merges can still contain a foreign-provider tracker, and this is distinct from the existing HMM producer guards. An independent read-only review found no concrete production lock cycle, but confirmed the conditional progress risk and recommends a two-failed-reservation test with a gated pending entry and a competing operation on the same root chunk.

The new [`test_r595_pmm_tracker_wait_outside_root_lock.py`](../tests/test_r595_pmm_tracker_wait_outside_root_lock.py), SHA-256 `f8b5d0bc33cdc5921b19083fa4ecf1834464da17da37cfff785a56ea72f34ad0`, pins both the post-patch order and the lower-level no-partial-copy-on-OOM premise; its mutation checks reject a lock-held or unconditional wait. Focused result: **3/3 passed**. The full pinned software suite passes **1,994/1,994** in **127.752 s**, exit 0; mode-0600 [report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-pmm-rootlock-wait-final-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `18f41960d2f144f42d0406c6fa800bf5cddf8505fc19675204f13cb44259cc59`, and log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r595-pmm-rootlock-wait-final-v1-20261029.log`), SHA-256 `7a49289075bbfe978c1c5c4681a7b93e890b851c225c0693238c28f05de4c706`, classify the outcome as `software-suite-only`. The final isolated candidate `build/experiments/r595-pmm-tracker-wait-outside-root-bitlock-final-v1-20261029/candidate` compiles `nvidia-uvm.ko` for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`, version `595.99.02`, module SHA-256 `c2d69cc6ddee4c629762342f38c8520a88930320eeecf7963861d55f3f47e251`; the static ABI closure reports 81 UVM imports, 79 frontend exports, 145 private imports, and zero issues. BTF generation was skipped because `vmlinux` is unavailable. The module was not installed or loaded. A kernel/UVM lockdep OOM injection, dual-provider tracker test, CUDA test, and hardware validation remain outstanding.

The bounded host harness in [`run_r595_pmm_tracker_wait_host_harness.py`](../tools/run_r595_pmm_tracker_wait_host_harness.py) compiles the exact hash-pinned bodies of `uvm_pmm_gpu_free()`, `reserve_for_entries_from_tracker()` and `uvm_tracker_add_tracker()` with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`. Under two failed reserve attempts and a gated source wait, the competing thread acquires the root bitlock, the chunk remains allocated until wait completion, and existing sibling entries remain unchanged. The lock-held-wait mutation fails as expected. The focused R595 PMM group passes **9/9** and the latest full suite passes **1,996/1,996**; mode-0600 [host-harness report](../evidence/r595-pmm-tracker-wait-host-harness-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `8ad51db8029cc0823c442ecff045b70f4d0e8834f20a3ab9e0751d4baf83445d`, and [suite report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-pmm-tracker-wait-host-harness-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `84ac9de7699fea8ebbc119a93b779f96bff965cc26c4cbf8ab5133ab4905f88e`, are software/isolated evidence. The harness stubs tracker completion and free-list publication; it does not execute kernel UVM, lockdep/KASAN, or hardware semaphores, so the root-lock claim remains unvalidated on a live module.

The v2 harness now also compiles the pinned `uvm_tracker_reserve()` implementation itself and injects allocation failure at the `uvm_kvmalloc` stub boundary. Both the initial reservation and post-pruning retry fail before tracker entry-copy; the gated wait and root-lock/chunk/sibling checks still pass, while the lock-held mutation is rejected. Focused checks pass **9/9**; the latest full suite passes **1,996/1,996** in **127.322 s**. Mode-0600 [v2 harness report](../evidence/r595-pmm-tracker-wait-host-harness-v2-20261029.json), SHA-256 `caa720acb077155687eb66a7127f132a2cae8076aefc10d3c45d9dfcf0d26308`, and [suite report](../evidence/software-verification-r595-pmm-tracker-wait-host-harness-v2-20261029.json), SHA-256 `2db29591ab0a3a8990d60cf00cdfca10a34305139ccf92125e3f57c099c11d14`, pin this isolated result. In-tree review found `UVM_TEST_KVMALLOC` tests allocation success/reallocation and existing PMA eviction injection does not target tracker growth. A kernel-side test still needs a purpose-built, test-only reserve-failure control; no live module or GPU was exercised here.

## R595 fault-service tracker contract on error paths (2026-10-29)

This source review uses the exact overlay at `build/experiments/r595-hmm-migrate-aggregate-guard-v1-20261029/candidate/nvidia-uvm/`. The pinned SHA-256 values are `83fd573b3f5187ded355dec0a835f293716b0d271204d2c617864346fac01888` for `uvm_gpu_non_replayable_faults.c`, `e2b98f550ad6ef3efb062d931834f09acd79ecd5fa140a2f54de8e77a5dfac2b` for `uvm_gpu_replayable_faults.c`, and `1b7fd32a785b78af5492c30a297e148f421af3e04108a587a641932d4ca7499a` for `uvm_tracker.c`.

Both `service_managed_fault_in_block()` and `service_fault_batch_block()` call `uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe()` after the locked block-service operation regardless of its status, before releasing `va_block->lock`. They then return the service error in preference to `tracker_status`. A nonzero service result therefore does not mean that the VA-block tracker is irrelevant. In the replayable parent service, the source explicitly notes that replays may already have been issued when an error is returned; the error path calls `cancel_fault_batch()`, which flushes through `fault_buffer_flush_locked()` with the same batch context. That flush pushes a replay using `batch_context->tracker`, and `push_replay_on_gpu()` passes that tracker to `uvm_push_begin_acquire()`. Removing the aggregate on error can therefore lose the ordering edge from partial block work to the error-path replay. This is a source-level ordering inference, not a runtime trace.

`uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe()` first attempts the merge. The lower-level merge reserves capacity, prunes completed entries and retries; only a remaining `NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY` causes the safe wrapper to wait on the source tracker. That successful wait is another way to complete the dependency, but it is conditional and can occur while the VA-block lock is held. The code review does not establish a deadlock or a reproducible memory-pressure trigger. The non-replayable wrapper likewise aggregates on error; its later clear-fault register path waits on the shared fault-service tracker before writing the channel register, while the current error path schedules channel termination and does not clear that tracker in `service_fault_once()`.

The six hash-pinned checks in [`test_r595_fault_service_tracker_error_paths.py`](../tests/test_r595_fault_service_tracker_error_paths.py) protect both unconditional aggregates, the replayable error-path tracker handoff, the non-replayable clear-fault wait, and the allocation-failure condition. Mutations that remove or status-gate an aggregate are rejected. They are static source contracts only. No generic “skip aggregation on error” patch is justified; the remaining concrete experiment is controlled tracker-capacity failure with a pending GPU entry under lockdep on an isolated candidate. The current PC3 module preflight is blocked, and no candidate was loaded.

## R595 HMM tracker and channel lifetime boundary (2026-10-29)

This audit is pinned to the latest compiled R595.99.02 UVM candidate preimage at `build/experiments/r595-provider-id-validation-v1-20261029/candidate/nvidia-uvm/`, incorporating tracker detach/prune patches `0006/0007` and provider-ID validation patch `0008`. `uvm_tracker_add_entry()` copies an entry by structure assignment (`uvm_tracker.c:137-154`); this path does not acquire a channel reference. `uvm_tracker_wait()` polls completion (`:331-357`), and its completion predicate calls `uvm_tracker_remove_completed()` (`:455-479`), which removes completed entries by replacing each slot with the last entry. Thus successful waits prune completed channel pointers. On a fatal-error result, `uvm_tracker_wait()` also clears the tracker (`:344-355`); the header explains this prevents stale entries surviving channel destruction. Lifetime safety is not supplied by the tracker entry itself.

The reviewed HMM paths instead rely on enclosing synchronization. The deferred eviction worker holds the VA-space read lock while it calls the HMM mapper (`uvm_va_block.c:12832-12845`); that mapper copies the block tracker under the VA-block mutex, unlocks the block, then waits for its local copy (`uvm_hmm.c:1415-1427`). MMU invalidation waits on the shared block tracker while holding the block mutex (`uvm_hmm.c:469-525`). GPU-VA-space removal detaches channels to a deferred-free list, removes HMM mappings while taking each block mutex, and drains deferred objects only after dropping VA-space and mmap locks (`uvm_va_space.c:1727-1772, 1818-1832`; channel enqueue at `uvm_user_channel.c:796-837`). Separately, `uvm_va_block_unregister_gpu_locked()` waits for the whole block tracker under the block mutex (`uvm_va_block.c:9282-9329`, SHA-256 `faf15872daf79ea83894ce24f7f8c4034809c03114a99da1fd0817288785c931`); final `deinit_gpu()` synchronizes PMM deferred frees before destroying the channel manager (`uvm_gpu.c:1825-1850`, SHA-256 `925ffe7bad8362431e71985c77b76c8a84f16ab3b08e7c32115c503ea81f760d`). The pinned regression also checks policy waits precede release of their VA-space and mmap locks.

**Finding (source inference, not runtime proof):** these inspected paths show no concrete tracker/channel use-after-free. The safety argument depends on those lock scopes, GPU unregister drains, and deferred release ordering because tracker copies do not retain channel objects. The six source-hash-pinned checks in [`test_r595_hmm_tracker_wait_lifetime.py`](../tests/test_r595_hmm_tracker_wait_lifetime.py) cover the named lifetime orderings and the isolation-guard bypass; they do not test a live race, lockdep, KASAN/KCSAN, dual-provider completion, or hardware behavior. A discriminating runtime test must hold pending entries from both providers, pause eviction after copying the tracker, race GPU-VA-space unregister, and verify unregister cannot release the channel manager before the local wait finishes; repeat with notifier invalidation and migration under lockdep and memory sanitizers.

**Separate progress/failure-path risk:** the source walk found 49 production `*_safe()` callsites across 15 files (31 `uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe`, 17 `uvm_tracker_add_push_safe`, one `uvm_tracker_overwrite_safe`; no production `uvm_tracker_add_entry_safe`). The fallback is conditional: tracker merges reserve, prune completed entries, and retry before `uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe()` waits (`uvm_tracker.c:190-229, 262-269`; API contract `uvm_tracker.h:126-152`). Representative lock-held contexts include `uvm_pmm_gpu_alloc()` and `uvm_pmm_gpu_free()` under the per-root bitlock (`uvm_pmm_gpu.c:50-54, 567-581, 711-721`) and the HMM eviction mapper under `va_block->lock` (`uvm_hmm.c:1415-1425`). PMM comments explicitly say tracker synchronization can take long and should not block other PMM operations. This identifies conditional lock-held latency/progress exposure; it does not prove a circular dependency or deadlock. A runtime falsifier needs reserve-failure injection with a pending source entry and lockdep/bounded completion.

The R595 push guard checks provider identity before channel acquisition (`uvm_push.c:214-229`, SHA-256 `ed8fecb3dd53f5a845f1eeaff411a699f46538edc03c5e8e75bfaa112f3c7b42`) but does not cover the OOM fallback in `uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe()`. Patch [`0009-r595-hmm-isolated-mode-cpu-fallback.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0009-r595-hmm-isolated-mode-cpu-fallback.patch) adds isolated-mode guards for HMM GPU-fault service and HMM GPU migration, after the CPU-fault and sysmem-fallback branches respectively, plus a deferred-worker provider check before mapping. The review then found that the inner migration refusal was insufficient: `uvm_va_block_migrate_locked()` still unconditionally called `uvm_tracker_add_tracker_safe(out_tracker, &va_block->tracker)` after `uvm_hmm_va_block_migrate_locked()` returned, while the VA-block mutex remained held.

Patch [`0010-r595-hmm-migration-aggregate-guard.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0010-r595-hmm-migration-aggregate-guard.patch) adds an early guard in that outer helper, before both the inner migration and tracker aggregation. It rejects only isolated-mode HMM GPU destinations for which `uvm_hmm_must_use_sysmem()` is false; CPU destinations and the existing must-use-sysmem path remain admitted. Its source contract pins the exact `uvm_migrate.c` preimage and verifies the guard precedes the inner call and `out_tracker` merge; two mutation tests remove the guard or broaden it over sysmem fallback. This closes the specifically identified “inner refusal then outer merge” path, not the generic safe-tracker OOM fallback.

The fault-service error-path contract is now covered by the preceding subsection: both aggregates remain unconditional because partial work and cancellation/replay ordering can survive a service error. The remaining unresolved risk is the conditional lock-held `NV_ERR_NO_MEMORY` wait, especially other HMM/PMM aggregation paths; no deadlock or runtime trigger is demonstrated.

Patch `0010` applies with zero offset/fuzz and its three source contracts pass. The derived R595.99.02 `nvidia-uvm.ko` builds for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` (SHA-256 `e0e6a992335c14252806ff036c4a439413909672ad90048527db9570b151fc57`). Static symbol closure passes with 81 UVM imports, 79 frontend exports, 145 private imports and zero issues; the exact four-module static bundle audit and isolated `depmod` also pass. The complete pinned suite passes 1,985/1,985 (software-only). See current build and run evidence in [`STATE.md`](STATE.md) and [`VALIDATION.md`](VALIDATION.md). The candidate remains unloaded/uninstalled; no UVM runtime, hardware, fault replay, lockdep, or sanitizer test has been performed.

This R595 containment adapts the R580 patch's CPU-first and sysmem-first guard placement, then adds a worker-time provider check because the R595 deferred eviction function is `void` and can queue remote mappings while holding the VA-block lock. Patch `0010` mirrors the inner migration refusal at the outer tracker-aggregation boundary. GPU-resident HMM faults/migrations remain unavailable in isolated mode; the sysmem-specific migration case and other fault aggregators still require source/runtime validation.

## UVM accessed-by command mapping and stock V100 observation (2026-10-29)

The command values in `uvm_ioctl.h` are decimal arguments to `UVM_IOCTL_BASE()`, while ioctl traces display the request number in hexadecimal. The pinned private R580.178.04 header defines `UVM_SET_ACCESSED_BY` as base command **46** (line 660; header SHA-256 `937a3fe1c0b2f4d8cfc18443d41ad10ee85c4445f954cc5c67b460ded2dd4916`) and `UVM_UNSET_ACCESSED_BY` as **47** (line 673). Thus traces display `0x2e` and `0x2f`. `UVM_PAGEABLE_MEM_ACCESS_ON_GPU` is command **70** (line 979), displayed as `0x46`; treating `0x46` as SET was an incorrect interpretation.

Pinned Open R595.99.02 defines the same commands at lines 562 and 575, and command 70 at line 859 (header SHA-256 `9cc909360f2d19135852390fb66a12fe4d22ca39c19e2609b2ff5e925df4bdf4`). Both R580 and R595 `uvm.c` route SET/UNSET to `uvm_api_set_accessed_by` and `uvm_api_unset_accessed_by`; R580 `uvm_policy.c` defines the handlers at lines 685/692 (SHA-256 `0aa0dfa810e67b1b78c3de96581089b37cc4421ed20f5adedecb4df638ac95db`), and R595 at lines 622/629 (SHA-256 `8843ff3468d51c9e93e1c08d04714244823638fcbc3c6e9fb021a11862cd2407`). R580 `uvm.c` SHA-256 is `6549ff9293e5a789d53f59c29cbc2af4d7d8aedd0bfe7519699c4239e7ad24b3`; R595 `uvm.c` SHA-256 is `5e0f8e0c531ee33f2add193ca7ca9c391e2428b194c6b8e3570ea0a99d7c0812`.

The guarded PC3 guest probe allocated a 4096-byte managed region on the stock Tesla V100-SXM2-16GB / R580.178.04 guest, issued SET then UNSET for that same device, and freed the allocation. In the 597-call trace, one probe PID issued UVM requests `0x2e` and `0x2f` in order; both syscalls returned 0. A later UNSET pair also appears during cleanup. The report says the CUDA phase and payload cleanup passed. This is direct evidence that the selected single-GPU R580 API path and UVM command transport accepted this bounded sequence. The trace metadata does not include enough payload to prove RM's embedded status, so no claim is made about the driver's internal semantic result. It does not exercise another GPU as the accessed-by processor, managed-memory migration/fault, or any hybrid path.

The probe and trace are [report v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-managed-accessed-by-v2-20261029T0318Z.json), SHA-256 `ff2ae68cd5ba1c990c5d12ee5be2fb72a1e23d249a97087e248edf0e301ef135`, and compressed strace (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-managed-accessed-by-v2-20261029T0318Z.json.strace.gz`), SHA-256 `a4d31dcf41fb9b350849f268b3a1b9417cf96892b734f986097f998eee2d39b9`. The earlier [v1 wrapper report](../evidence/pc3-v100-managed-accessed-by-v1-20261029T0316Z.json) is preserved as a diagnostic validation failure caused solely by the command-number expectation; do not interpret it as probe failure.

## CUDA node namespace and frontend coexistence boundary (2026-10-29)

**Observed in prior PC3 captures:** the stock RTX 5080 R595 reference resolves its control and GPU nodes to `/dev/nvidiactl` (195:255) and `/dev/nvidia0` (195:0), PCI `0000:02:00.0`. The stock V100 guest CUDA trace opens the same two node names and minors, plus `/dev/nvidia-uvm` (guest major 237:0). These are separate host/guest captures; they do not establish same-Linux hybrid execution.

**Established in project source:** the R580 Open provider has a private-device patch (`patches/r580.178.04/0004-private-open-chardev.patch`) that allocates its own dynamic major. The integrated frontend uses a different contract: `src/hybrid580-standard-node-devices.c` registers nodes at `NV_MAJOR_DEVICE_NUMBER`, while `src/hybrid_frontend.c` selects the CUDA control/GPU path by that major and its standard minors. On PC3, R595 currently owns major 195 and the stock CUDA libraries use its standard node names.

The exact R595 header defines major **195**, regular minors **0–247**, modeset minor **254**, and control minor **255**. The candidate frontend reserves `(195,0)` through `(195,247)` and `(195,255)` with `register_chrdev_region()`; its open router dispatches standard nodes only when `MAJOR(i_rdev)==195`. The fresh PC3 preflight v5 observes the active stock NVIDIA owner on major 195 and occupied nodes `195:0` and `195:255`. Thus the private dynamic major allocated to the Open backend does not solve frontend coexistence: an unmodified `libcuda` opens the same standard device names on both generations, while a second private frontend would need a separate, unproven user-space path/dispatch implementation.

**Architecture decision:** retain the single integrated frontend as the first hybrid prototype. It must own the CUDA-standard nodes so unmodified `libcuda` can route both architectures through one kernel/user ABI; it cannot coexist as a second standard-name frontend beside the active R595 module. A private-name frontend would require a separate, currently unproven CUDA userspace routing layer. The earlier R595/R580 static preflight is therefore a real activation gate, not a naming inconvenience: module signatures/trust and a controlled canary replacing the loaded provider are still prerequisites. No unload, signing, key enrollment, reboot, or driver replacement was performed.

**Inference and limits:** the node observations plus source establish the namespace collision and favor replacement-canary testing over two independently loaded standard frontends. They do not prove that the integrated frontend initializes either GPU, that one `libcuda` enumerates both, or that a future user-space dispatcher is impossible.

Evidence: [RTX 5080 stock R595 node/phase capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-r595-event-cleanup-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `893b6f7bcba305d29e596922cfa168322e02daf295b9412b1846a3c2ee77cd59`; [V100 context trace report](../evidence/pc3-v100-context-ioctl-sequence-v2-20261028.json), SHA-256 `b7857cabab5f2b121c79a7134a76f856462af7355f29a5b1ac60673a3694244a`; V100 strace sidecar (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-context-ioctl-sequence-v2-20261028.json.strace.gz`), SHA-256 `2e7f0f1227039793c7876b0eec4cb391a0d40f488b0a08479ba665287f61276f`; [PC3 v17 host snapshot](../evidence/pc3-canary-host-state-signing-preflight-v17-20261029T0155Z.json), SHA-256 `1e0dd74f5d57da6c7270ad048f9e3296f611765d82107a5fdac67754e7b905ab`; [blocked preflight v5](../evidence/pc3-canary-preflight-r595-r580-provider-identity-v5-20261029T0155Z.json), SHA-256 `f2d759be87fb8f7afe920f9e7129021db16b6fa1feb22f1d56eafea9ddbdd5ce`.

## PC3 Linux 6.8 R580 kernel dependency closure and kmod duplicate listing (2026-10-29)

The pinned PC3 guest capture supplied the exact `ecc`, `ecdh_generic`, and `ecdsa_generic` modules plus `modules.builtin`, `modules.builtin.modinfo`, `modules.dep`, `modules.order`, and `modules.softdep` for `6.8.0-147-generic`. The four candidate modules were staged with these inputs only in a private scratch tree. `depmod` 34.2 completed without warnings; its generated hard-dependency graph closes the Open provider's `ecc` import, the frontend's Open+legacy imports, and the UVM consumer's frontend import. `depmod` projected the Open module's soft dependencies as `ecdh_generic ecdsa_generic`.
