`modprobe --show-depends nvidia_uvm`, run against the scratch tree with `--set-version 6.8.0-147-generic`, emits the candidate paths in display order and prints `ecc` three times through shared hard-dependency paths. This command is a display-only query, not a module insertion or proof of the kernel's load result. The pinned [kmod v34.2 source](https://github.com/kmod-project/kmod/blob/v34.2/libkmod/libkmod-module.c#L3536-L3680) marks modules visited in recursive probe-list expansion, while `fill_softdep` appends modules from each dependency edge; that source detail does not establish runtime deduplication for this graph. The `modprobe` [show callback](https://github.com/kmod-project/kmod/blob/v34.2/tools/modprobe.c#L2980-L3035) prints the plan actions. Candidate insertion remains untested. PC1 already has `nvidia` and `nvidia_uvm` loaded, so the separate `--dry-run` that would be affected by these live names remains skipped.

Proof files: the mode-0600 [guest dependency capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-kernel-dependencies-20261029T011956Z.json), SHA-256 `a7438efbf1c2556b1979e3eea66df1f6de8baba5effa7b457256300bf1157850`; [isolated bundle/depmod report v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580-depmod-v2-20261029T0130Z.json), SHA-256 `0e7ba3238275479ba8467d5cb5b9a1ce1797e77c09017bf7315f17f75f4c3393`; [staging implementation](../tools/stage_pc3_v100_r580_depmod.py) and [CPU contracts](../tests/test_stage_pc3_v100_r580_depmod.py). No candidate was loaded, installed, or exercised on GPU hardware.

## R580 isolated UVM service-phase ownership (2026-10-28)

The pinned R580.178.04 path adds an isolated-mode refusal in the common push-acquire wrapper: `uvm_tracker_has_other_gpus()` detects outstanding work owned by a different GPU, and the wrapper returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` before reserving a channel. This protects the design from waiting for a foreign GPU while fault service holds the VA-block lock.

The relevant service chain is `uvm_va_block_make_resident_copy()` → two `uvm_va_block_unmap_mask()` batches → destination population/copy → `uvm_va_block_service_finish()` → revoke/map/SetAccessedBy mapping. Each unmap batch collects GPU pushes in a local tracker and merges them into the VA-block tracker at return. The later copy and mapping pushes acquire that block tracker. The source contains no tracker-wait/retry handoff between these transaction phases. A pending foreign-owner entry can therefore cause a conditional refusal even when no CPU-staged page is required. Timing is runtime-dependent; this static trace is not evidence that all direct migrations fail.

The reproducible [audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-isolated-service-phases-v2-20261028T1933Z.json), SHA-256 `67bfad69deb76681d4aed5ba51a344156276ef2aa08e3a1bb4ea5bf274bbdd85`, pins the three patched source files and checks nine call/ordering conditions. Two regression tests pass, including a mutation that removes the refusal and must invalidate source recognition. The full software suite passes **1,769/1,769** in **116.924 s**; its report hashes the auditor implementation. Both results are static/software qualification only. No candidate module was installed or loaded, and no UVM/CUDA runtime was exercised.

The safe next experiment is a restartable phase boundary for mapping invalidation: preserve the source residency, wait only after dropping the block lock, reacquire and recompute the invalidation set, then retry. It must prove that concurrent remap/unmap and error paths cannot expose stale mappings or duplicate tool events. Do not extend that retry to the two-copy CPU-staging case until staging has its own committed residency/lifetime state.

## R595 UVM device-P2P path preserves the owner control FD (2026-10-28)

The pinned R595.99.02 UVM ioctl structure `UVM_ALLOC_DEVICE_P2P_PARAMS` includes an input `rmCtrlFd`. In `uvm_va_range_device_p2p.c`, the coherent and zone-device branches both duplicated the allocation with `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemory()` and discarded that owner descriptor. The zone-device branch can then duplicate a second time in `alloc_device_p2p_mem()`, creating three production call sites in total. Since the hybrid bridge deliberately rejects the legacy no-FD duplicate API, these source paths would fail closed before provider routing.

Patch [`0004-uvm-device-p2p-control-fd.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0004-uvm-device-p2p-control-fd.patch), SHA-256 `f7e6b6ba565ddd0c4059a4ae100ae25e70b37261abd6651ada2983a0c4f68de8`, threads the ioctl descriptor into both branch helpers and the nested allocation helper. Each production call now uses `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemoryWithControlFd()`. Existing frontend ownership resolution selects the RM owning the control FD and rejects a source/target provider mismatch before RM duplication; the patch does not make cross-provider imports or peer access succeed.

The patch applies to the exact pinned source without fuzz or offset. An isolated full `nvidia-uvm.ko` build succeeds against kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`; the [build report](../evidence/r595-gv100-fd-aware-device-p2p-build-v1-20261028T1838Z.json), SHA-256 `994a5743ca6e7ac84dfa94beb25e3691993f6c74d3cec62500eb5de9ececc71d`, binds the source, patches, frontend export, candidate and log. The [static closure audit](../evidence/r595-uvm-device-p2p-static-closure-v2-20261028T1838Z.json), SHA-256 `9d8afefbfe98435b785e254af44ef7cf9389e4511b1bc787b99016c99be4411e`, finds all 78 native R595 imports and all three frontend hybrid extensions with matching CRCs; it reports zero issues. The exact-source P2P and external-map tests pass 4/4. The latest full [software suite](../evidence/software-verification-r595-dup-allocation-scope-v1-20261028T1840Z.json), SHA-256 `7bbb2dcbed9e3510b2d367acb4a11d171688878648a63c06f0bd15483b218a74`, passes 1,760/1,760.

Qualification stops at tested patch contract, compiled module, and static import closure. The module remains uninstalled and unloaded; no UVM ioctl, RM callback, P2P page mapping, DMA, GPU transfer, or kernel ran through it. The build therefore does not validate that `rmCtrlFd` is accepted by this experimental runtime, that a same-provider P2P map succeeds on hardware, or that UVM migration and cross-generation P2P work. The source-scope analysis below narrows generic `DupAllocation`; fault-service, pageable-memory, and managed-memory paths still need separate tracing.

## Scope of the R595 RM `DupAllocation` helper (2026-10-28)

A whole-file scan of the pinned R595.99.02 UVM C sources finds one production call to `nvUvmInterfaceDupAllocation()`: `uvm_rm_mem_map_gpu()` in `uvm_rm_mem.c`. The helper asserts the memory type is `UVM_RM_MEM_TYPE_SYS`; its header documents that mapping an allocation on a GPU other than its owner is supported only for system memory. The bridge resolves both registered RM address-space owners and refuses cross-provider pairs before calling either backend. This establishes a provider-affine internal system-memory mapping boundary, not general managed-memory migration behavior.

The production caller of `uvm_rm_mem_alloc_and_map_all()` is the system semaphore-page allocator. The provider-local semaphore patch replaces that operation with CPU mapping plus GPU mappings only when the frontend reports the same provider. The other occurrences are UVM in-module tests. The confidential-compute pushbuffer path instead allocates its unprotected system buffer using the channel manager's GPU and maps it to that same GPU; `uvm_rm_mem_alloc()` records that GPU as the allocation owner. The hash-pinned [scope report](../evidence/r595-uvm-dup-allocation-scope-v1-20261028T1837Z.json), SHA-256 `09aea3501aca5e2a1c4add47a20bd85911c7a125a8ae8015d3e4324cd1a038eb`, and three tests in [`test_r595_dup_allocation_scope.py`](../tests/test_r595_dup_allocation_scope.py) preserve these source boundaries.

This closes the specific concern that another generic production `DupAllocation` caller will map the first backend's internal system page onto the second backend during UVM GPU registration. It does not cover all fault-service, pageable-memory, or managed-memory migration code paths. Those must be traced separately before treating the UVM address space as cross-provider capable; cross-provider external allocations and explicit P2P remain rejected by policy.

## R595 cross-provider UVM semaphore registration barrier and prototype (2026-10-28)

The exact candidate source audit follows the startup ordering in `uvm_gpu.c`: `init_gpu()` creates/maps semaphore pools before calling `uvm_channel_manager_create()`. In the common non-confidential-computing path, the first GPU's CE pools are built eagerly. `channel_pool_add()` creates every channel, each `channel_create()` allocates a tracking semaphore, and an empty system-memory pool allocates a page. The page path uses `uvm_rm_mem_alloc_and_map_all()`, which maps through every currently registered GPU. When the next GPU registers, `init_semaphore_pools()` maps the existing GPU's pool to it before creating the new channel manager.

The owning and target address spaces then reach `uvm_rm_mem_map_gpu()` and `nvUvmInterfaceDupAllocation()`. The hybrid route resolves both opaque address-space owners and returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` if they belong to different Resource Managers, without forwarding either native handle. `init_gpu()` propagates this error. For a GPU with at least one usable CE and an existing semaphore page, the pinned source therefore predicts that the second provider's GPU registration will stop during semaphore-pool initialization. This is a source-level prediction, not an observed runtime failure. The hash-pinned chain and source locations are in the mode-0600 [audit report](../evidence/r595-cross-provider-semaphore-init-v1-20261028T181027Z.json), SHA-256 `b535796efa197fce6bc38fe3d5ea6b151c3ac44e0c51b9bff8481b8a04f8e898`.

The first prototype adds `nvUvmInterfaceAreGpuProvidersSame()`, which compares UUID ownership in the frontend registry while holding its callback mutex. R595 UVM now asks before mapping another GPU's semaphore page. Page allocation uses the owning GPU's RM, CPU mapping, and same-provider GPU mappings; cross-provider targets are omitted. Any provider lookup or same-provider mapping error takes the existing page cleanup path, which releases CPU and GPU mappings. The source patch is [`0002-uvm-provider-local-system-semaphore-pools.patch`](../patches/r595.99.02/0002-uvm-provider-local-system-semaphore-pools.patch). CPU fixtures exercise same-owner, cross-owner, unknown-owner and invalid-output cases; exact-source tests require the patch to apply without fuzz.

The nearby static-P2P path already has a separate policy. R595 UVM calls `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps()` while discovering parent links; the frontend detects cross-provider device pairs and returns `UVM_LINK_TYPE_NONE` with `NV_OK`, so discovery can continue without handing either RM the other's device handle. If explicit peer access is requested, the P2P-object route rejects the cross-provider pair with `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` before backend dispatch. This is a source-level contract only; it does not prove runtime peer-access behavior.

The R595 frontend compiled and exports the provider query only from the frontend; Open and legacy backends export no copy. The patched R595 UVM compiled against that exact `Module.symvers`, importing CRC `0x3b92280a` to match the frontend export. The mode-0600 [build report](../evidence/r595-gv100-provider-local-semaphore-build-v1-20261028T181602Z.json), SHA-256 `c8e6097b2730cb281f57ac39e0b5d1886f3352e56f46a338365b80f31cbb7e53`, records the candidate module SHA-256 `181ed866d383e37eb81a0fd6b49cd08a7eb87f7d88054711a13a8b60f010d86b`; the full build log is here (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-gv100-provider-local-semaphore-v1-20261028T181535Z-build.log`), SHA-256 `f5d826361ffdb7a226ea1ab83f4d209af6e4388c7f3f20b6830e63b7628db0bf`.

This patch covers UVM's internal semaphore pages only. Cross-provider RM allocation duplication is rejected by the frontend ownership boundary; application allocation imports and UVM migration remain unqualified. Automatic peer discovery declines cross-provider links, and explicit cross-provider P2P objects are rejected by source policy, so cross-provider peer access is unavailable in this design. The candidate has not been loaded: startup, teardown, lock ordering, mapping behavior, independent CUDA contexts, and simultaneous Volta/Blackwell kernels remain untested. This is a compile-tested experiment, not a compatibility claim.

## RTX 5080 VMM POSIX-FD stock reference (2026-10-28)

The guarded PC3 reference at [`pc3-rtx5080-vmm-posix-reference-v1-20261028T175700Z.json`](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-vmm-posix-reference-v1-20261028T175700Z.json), SHA-256 `233e372b060d248ba4b7405b0bf451d69e19c99ad3359523ecdeb32a9ffcecb7`, observed the stock RTX 5080/R595.99.02 stack at PCI `0000:02:00.0`, Driver API 13020. The device advertises POSIX-FD shareable VMM handles. The parent exported a 2 MiB allocation, and a separate child context imported it; the child verified the initial bytes and wrote back a second numeric pattern which the parent verified. Both sides released CUDA mappings/objects and closed their descriptors. The runner found no competing CUDA process, confirmed unchanged module/GPU/libcuda identity, and removed the temporary remote directory.

This supplies a current stock-Blackwell control for the VMM export/import and numerical-verification path. It demonstrates neither that the experimental frontend forwards these calls correctly nor that Volta can import the allocation. The V100 remains on a separate kernel/driver; cross-provider UVM initialization, system-memory/semaphore ownership, cross-generation VMM import and same-process dual-GPU execution remain open. In particular, `POSIX_FD` support on the RTX 5080 is not evidence that an R580 V100 backend accepts the same exported allocation.

## Mixed-branch bundle and fresh PC3 gate (2026-10-28)

The existing PC3 gate encoded a uniform R580 release and therefore could not truthfully assess the current R595 Open/UVM plus R580 proprietary backend. Extended the preflight to bind the exact four module paths, SHA-256s, names, dependencies, kernel vermagic, mixed versions and isolated kmod report to the bundle audit. Five new evidence-chain tests and a mixed-version gate contract pass; all existing R580-only checks remain intact.

The fresh PC3 snapshot sees RTX 5080 `0000:02:00.0` on stock R595 and V100 `0000:81:00.0` on `vfio-pci`, with Secure Boot/signature enforcement and integrity lockdown active. The [bundle preflight](../evidence/pc3-canary-preflight-hybrid-bundle-v1-20261028T175051Z.json), SHA-256 `36901840d4bebee7336c34ae15719f9ca94ca4176d109deccbec727a59dd8f92`, validates exact module evidence and is blocked by seven concrete host conflicts: active `nvidia`/`nvidia_uvm` names, unsigned modules and unverified trust, occupied major 195/device nodes, and the existing `nvidia-uvm` registration label. It is static-only and leaves `canary_ready=false`.

Candidate `nvidia_uvm.ko` is version `595.99.02`; `modinfo -F parm` lists its parameters and does not show the R580-only `uvm_hybrid_isolated_gpu_execution` parameter. This is a static module-metadata observation. It means the mixed candidate does not carry that explicit R580 isolation opt-in; it does not prove how R595 UVM behaves with both backends or establish cross-generation UVM safety. Keep UVM cross-GPU behavior as a runtime-critical unknown.

The full pinned suite passes **1,745/1,745** in **110.564 s**, but remains software-suite-only: [report](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-pc3-preflight-v1-20261028T175104Z.json), SHA-256 `6d4c74a68d75233a80bf11c0e6ab1e3de670c254513eed02dc9c88ae9cc988e8`. No candidate module was installed or loaded, and no same-process V100+Blackwell kernel was tested.

## Isolated kmod dependency map for the exact candidate bundle (2026-10-28)

Added [`verify_hybrid_bundle_depmod.py`](../tools/verify_hybrid_bundle_depmod.py), which accepts only a SHA-256-pinned passing bundle report, rechecks the four source module hashes and metadata, copies those exact bytes into a newly created private `lib/modules/7.0.0-38-generic` tree, runs `depmod -b`, and asks `modprobe -C /dev/null --show-depends` for the `nvidia` and `nvidia_uvm` plans. The three CPU contracts cover pin mismatch, module tampering, staged hashes, private report permissions, and the no-load command boundary.

### kmod 34.2 softdep output recheck (2026-11-03)

A fresh check against the exact R595.112.07/R580.196.07 four-module bundle initially failed because the verifier expected the loadable `ecdsa_generic` pre-softdep in the top-level `modprobe --show-depends nvidia` output. With installed kmod 34.2, that output contains only the hard provider graph; a separate `modprobe --show-depends ecdsa_generic` query resolves `ecdsa_generic.ko.zst`, while `ecdh_generic` is recorded as built in. The upstream [kmod `modprobe(8)` documentation](https://github.com/kmod-project/kmod/blob/master/man/modprobe.8.scd) describes `--show-depends` as the module dependencies plus the requested module but does not define softdep reporting there. The omission is therefore recorded as a direct observation for kmod 34.2, not a universal kmod guarantee.

`verify_hybrid_bundle_depmod.py` now accepts the top-level pre-softdep only when it is in the hash-verified staged closure, and still requires each softdep to resolve independently. `summarize_hybrid_split_provider_build.py` accepts either valid output form and normalizes the hard graph; `summarize_hybrid_bundle_depmod.py` emits a path-free receipt after validating commands, stage-contained module paths, hashes, `modules.dep`, `modules.softdep`, independent target results, and the no-load boundary. The new [depmod receipt v3](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-bundle-depmod-sanitized-v3-20261103.json), SHA-256 `361e95f8c4bfcf365ed8c40fce0bafe17b2cc0d1295818d3169002f78cf86e28`, binds all four module hashes plus the audit and tooling hashes. It remains isolated dependency-map evidence only; no module insertion or GPU test ran.

On the current host, kmod resolves Open and proprietary backends before the `nvidia` frontend, then the UVM module; querying `nvidia_uvm` returns all four modules in dependency order. The mode-0600 [report](../evidence/hybrid-module-bundle-depmod-static-v1-20261028T174217Z.json), SHA-256 `0c670dda21332a0d83de24a9b97befb4aa3265e13a634ea6d942c9f0c3cd74f7`, records the exact stage and outputs. The full suite passes **1,738/1,738**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-hybrid-bundle-depmod-v1-20261028T174217Z.json), SHA-256 `2a32e1be721a7f3908a2f48d31267f6a87b95cf20387eb7d9a823150e69e326c`.

This checks only `modules.dep` and kmod's printed resolution plan. It does not perform `insmod`, test signature trust, validate module insertion or kernel symbol acceptance, or exercise hardware. It is not a substitute for the preceding CRC audit, and neither check proves runtime compatibility.

## Hash-linked standard module bundle and symbol providers (2026-10-28)

The compiled four-module candidate now has an independent, reproducible static audit in [`audit_hybrid_candidate_bundle.py`](../tools/audit_hybrid_candidate_bundle.py). It verifies the module hashes against their build reports, module names and `vermagic`, dependency edges, ELF export tables against each module's own `Module.symvers`, and import CRCs against the candidate's kernel/backend/frontend export sets.

The set is internally coherent at the static ABI boundary on Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`: `nvidia` depends on both private backends; `nvidia-uvm` depends on `nvidia`; actual exports match 81/81 frontend, 145/145 Open, and 145/145 legacy `Module.symvers` rows. All 303 frontend imports and 329 UVM imports have one matching provider CRC. The stock UVM consumes 79 `nvUvmInterface` entries; the frontend exports two additional `WithControlFd` entrypoints that the frontend build report classifies as hybrid-owned and absent from both private providers. The v3 audit pins its own implementation plus four direct inspection dependencies. The [v3 report](../evidence/hybrid-module-bundle-static-audit-v3-20261028T173706Z.json), SHA-256 `083be8feb1cc4b2bee489d7318bb32c0505bdaf256838d08d21e6acf8b8780e6`, has no issues; eight adversarial CPU contracts pass.

The first audit iteration flagged the two declared extensions as unconsumed exports; it did not identify a CRC or provider mismatch. That [v1 report](../evidence/hybrid-module-bundle-static-audit-v1-20261028T173144Z.json) is preserved as a superseded audit-tool result. The revised checker separates stock UVM imports from explicitly reported frontend-only extension exports.

This evidence verifies module metadata and compile-time symbol closure only. It does not prove the kernel will accept the module set, Resource Manager initialization, runtime callback behavior, UVM fault/migration semantics, or CUDA execution. All modules remain unloaded and uninstalled; same-kernel, same-process V100+Blackwell kernels remain unvalidated.

## Complete GV100 UVM HAL overlay against the hybrid frontend ABI (2026-10-28)

The standard-name R595/R580 frontend now has a separately compiled R595 Open / R580 proprietary root at [`hybrid-r595-r580-client-share-policy-standard-name-v1.json`](../evidence/hybrid-r595-r580-client-share-policy-standard-name-v1.json). The UVM builder accepts this external symbol file only with a paired SHA-256 pin; its new CLI tests check the pair requirement, exact forwarding to Kbuild, and private report permissions.

Using the pinned R580.178.04 archive (`060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`) and the same Open R595.99.02 source base, [`probe_r595_gv100_complete_hal.py`](../tools/probe_r595_gv100_complete_hal.py) staged the explicit GV100 callback rows and compiled only `nvidia-uvm` with `KBUILD_EXTRA_SYMBOLS` set to that frontend file. This demonstrates a source/build ABI path between the compiled standard frontend exports and the R580-derived UVM source, plus compile/link closure for the selected HAL tables. Neither module was loaded or installed. See the mode-0600 [build report](../evidence/r595-gv100-hal-client-share-policy-frontend-v1.json), SHA-256 `53b78b781284427e2c8f18bd66eb1313f249c9c2d64a0e885db8bc09ca4f57fa`, and build log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r595-gv100-hal-client-share-policy-frontend-v1.log`), SHA-256 `c8fec0ad106d13f71e972a0c0bc1cd78a4f988f3555e2a5bea8345ad9b1f638b`.

The refreshed pinned software suite passes **1,726/1,726** in **112.537 s** and remains `software-suite-only`: [report](../evidence/software-verification-uvm-matched-frontend-v1-20261028T172357Z.json), SHA-256 `99594141e0e3da8c03a73dc615433abd26b641a817c9e911988785e175cd9c55`. The next useful static check is to verify the exact candidate module-version/import/dependency metadata as one hash-linked bundle; hardware activation remains blocked by the recorded PC3 identity, signature, node-ownership, and rollback gates.

## Root-client inherited share policy across R580/R595 (2026-10-28)

Both pinned NVIDIA SDK headers define `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_CLIENT_SET_INHERITED_SHARE_POLICY` as RM control `0x0d04`, with exactly one input `RS_SHARE_POLICY` member. The R580 header `build/experiments/r580-open-client-share-policy-matched-v1/private/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/src/common/sdk/nvidia/inc/ctrl/ctrl0000/ctrl0000client.h` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer) explicitly marks the command deprecated, states that it applies `CLIENT_SHARE_OBJECT` with `hObject=hClient`, and describes its effect as changing the client's inherited policy list. The equivalent R595 header is in the compiled replay tree at `build/experiments/r595-client-share-policy-native-build-v5/source/src/common/sdk/nvidia/inc/ctrl/ctrl0000/ctrl0000client.h` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer). The ABI size is fixed to12 bytes by the R580 Open, R580 proprietary, and R595 Open provider compile-time checks.

The stock PC3 observations resolve the caller shape without reading the policy payload: two calls on the R595 RTX5080 host and two on the R580 V100 guest each have `hClient` and `hObject` equal to the previously allocated root handle, flags0, parameter size12, syscall return0, and embedded status0. The exact report hashes and run limits are in the current [validation table](VALIDATION.md). These observations show that both installed stacks accept the request; they do not reveal the three policy words or prove that both proprietary and Open Resource Managers make identical internal state changes.

The experimental frontend now copies the fixed structure to kernel memory and routes it through each native RM using its mirrored root handle. It supports both direct `NV_ESC_RM_CONTROL` and the observed `NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD` wrapper, returns only the NVOS54 status, and never forwards a user pointer. Since the operation mutates RM state, malformed packets stop before dispatch and any backend transport/status ambiguity quarantines the frontend and root rather than permitting more operations on potentially divergent policy lists. The backend providers are [Open patch0047](../patches/r580.178.04/0047-private-open-client-share-policy.patch) and [proprietary patch0045](../patches/r580.178.04/proprietary/0045-private-legacy-client-share-policy.patch); R595 receives the provider through the exact-anchor replay recorded in the [R595 build report](../evidence/r595-client-share-policy-native-build-v5.json).

The direct/XFER forwarding, 12-byte layout, invalid-size rejection, and partial-failure quarantine are CPU-model tests. Open R580.178.04, proprietary R580.178.04, Open R580.196.07, and replayed Open R595.99.02 compile; paired frontends compile with all expected backend imports resolved. No candidate module was installed or loaded, and no experimental RM operation, same-process CUDA kernel, UVM migration, P2P transfer, or performance measurement has been run.

## R580 PMM tracker, owner-semaphore lifecycle and VA-block retry (2026-10-28 12:58 UTC)

The pinned R580.178.04 source gives a conditional path from multi-GPU mapping work into a PMM wait: migration selects policy/accessed-by processors; GPU mappings append pushes to a local tracker; the caller merges that tracker into the VA-block tracker; chunk cleanup can pass the block tracker to the PMM root tracker; and a later ordinary no-eviction allocation snapshots the root tracker before calling `uvm_tracker_wait_for_other_gpus`. Audit v10 adds the wait mechanism: its tracker polls the channel's GPU tracking semaphore through a CPU mapping and refreshes the cached value from the mapped payload. In sleepable context, `uvm_spin_loop` schedules after about 10 microseconds; the source comment identifies RM interrupts and UVM fault service as reasons scheduling is needed. The 30-second “stuck waiting” report only emits a diagnostic; it does not impose a wait deadline.

The owner/lifecycle checks follow the source chain: `channel_create()` gets the GPU from its channel manager and allocates `tracking_sem` from `gpu->semaphore_pool`; the semaphore page points back to that pool, whose `gpu` field identifies the owner. Tracker entries recover their GPU through `entry->channel` and `channel->pool->manager->gpu`. In isolated mode, the semaphore page is CPU-mapped in system aperture, peer-GPU pool mapping is skipped, and cross-GPU dependencies are rejected. This supports a source-level owner/visibility model; it does not prove DMA visibility, CPU cache coherence, or that a mixed-provider workload can create the required UVM state.

Teardown source order removes GPU references from other trackers and synchronizes PMM before channel-manager destruction, then destroys channel pools/channels and tracking semaphores before semaphore pools. Channel destruction force-updates queued progress when `queued_value > 0`, asks RM to destroy the channel, and then frees its tracking semaphore. These are source-order checks, not proof that every producer has stopped or that runtime races are impossible. The audit also verifies that the ordinary isolated no-eviction PMM allocation is performed outside the VA-block lock: patch [`0008-pmm-allocation-outside-va-block-lock.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0008-pmm-allocation-outside-va-block-lock.patch), SHA-256 `b532998cbca0a7ca566690a448f6984dff50f22219c60ccd9fa08578c7c40baa`, queues a successful chunk on the retry list, reacquires the lock, and returns `NV_ERR_MORE_PROCESSING_REQUIRED`. The pinned `UVM_VA_BLOCK_LOCK_RETRY` macro retries and the migration caller uses it; the stock/default path remains direct.

The [23-check audit v10](../evidence/r580-uvm-pmm-wait-lock-context-v10-20261028T1256Z.json), SHA-256 `b5aabd701f19f971191bb4e528d402ddc95cdf90f593b9ff28b05d71788b8477`, is `analyzed-statically` and `conditionally_mitigated`. Focused mutation tests pass **17/17**. The R580.178.04 pair and private UVM consumer compile for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`; the consumer matches 77/77 pinned frontend import CRCs. The full suite passes [1,610/1,610](../evidence/software-verification-r580-semaphore-lifecycle-v8-20261028T1256Z.json), SHA-256 `6ff4de029f9b785b27d819cba412875c3ea541ee902ebdabe84706b7ff46cc3e` (software-only).

The hybrid frontend routes GPU registration by UUID/provider, but its address-space and memory-duplication paths preserve provider ownership and reject cross-provider duplication. This means the earlier blanket statement that all second-provider UVM registration is blocked was too broad; device registration routing exists, while mixed-provider UVM VA-space and memory behavior is still unvalidated. No candidate module was installed or loaded, no GPU runtime exercised this patch, and same-Linux, same-process V100+Blackwell kernel execution remains unmet. Next, trace the exact VA-space duplication and memory-map call chain for two provider-owned GPU UUIDs and identify the first unavoidable cross-provider operation; turn that boundary into a minimal CPU route test before selecting any hardware canary.

## Eight R580 indirect-call slots and relocated code-address candidates (2026-10-28 12:06 UTC)

The mode-0600 [V45 capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v45-20261028T120352Z.json), SHA-256 `ed22454ec41c298ca1f825531951331ef2a33a96377d2f84e51167d699c6084f`, is pinned to PC3's stock V100 guest and `libcuda.so.580.178.04` hash `dcdf334aeb177c95b3385b797c9b871d03a287895713c6d107f79096b0985aa9`. The full `.text` pass found exactly one decoded indirect call at each of the following eight `.data` slots. It also captured a ninth `.bss` slot with no static relocation:

| Call RVA → return RVA | Operand slot VA | ELF section | Selected relocation |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `0x3279aa → 0x3279b0` | `0x5bcf9e8` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x331bc0` |
| `0x327a6a → 0x327a70` | `0x5bcf9f0` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x3302b0` |
| `0x327b2a → 0x327b30` | `0x5bcf9f8` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x331d80` |
| `0x327bea → 0x327bf0` | `0x5bcfa00` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x331fb0` |
| `0x327caa → 0x327cb0` | `0x5bcfa08` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x332190` |
| `0x32866a → 0x328670` | `0x5bcfa20` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x3346c0` |
| `0x32872a → 0x328730` | `0x5bcfa10` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x3349e0` |
| `0x37828a → 0x378290` | `0x5bcfa18` | `.data` | `R_X86_64_RELATIVE`, addend `0x392510` |
| `0xf57127 → 0xf5712d` | `0x5bd7a50` | `.bss` | None in the selected static relocation output |

The call opcode, six-byte boundary, RIP-relative operand, computed slot address, ELF section containment, and selected relocation records are checked independently by the host-side validator. The eight addends are code-address candidates from the dynamic relocation table. Only `0x332190` has an adjacent code context in this capture; the decoded instruction is `push rbp`, and its address is correlated to a separate observed stack return at `0x332226`. That correlation does not prove the relocated pointer was loaded or called on that execution. The other seven addends are not yet validated as instruction starts. [V44](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v44-20261028T115953Z.json), SHA-256 `380a566d7c446aff89e5b470981370da6bff37c0a33cc45d1cb795a73fc1abaf`, records an index increment, bound comparison, self-loop branch, and status branches/calls at `0x3eb469`; its purpose remains unknown.

This remains static stock-library evidence. The full `.text` scan reports 27 selected RIP-relative references, but `.init`, `.plt`, `.plt.got`, and `.fini` are outside the scan; computed writes, aliases, indirect reachability, live pointer values, and execution of any listed target are unresolved. No raw instruction bytes, symbol names, or decompiler pseudocode are retained. The focused suite is **24/24** and the full software suite is **1,587/1,587**; [full-suite report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261028T120500Z.json), SHA-256 `6bc1c308630994af101e85659df053ac5668fc5bc892dc442bc4eb0f1aa00b61` (software-only).

Next, extend the same-stream capture around each of the eight `.data` relocation addends and require the exact addresses to be decoded instruction starts. Use bounded context and inbound direct-reference checks to choose a runtime observation, while preserving the distinction between static candidates and executed callbacks.

## Complete eight-slot stock R580 dispatch cluster (2026-10-28 11:55 UTC)

The mode-0600 [V43 capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v43-20261028T115534Z.json), SHA-256 `d73bdb1c6a61f64cb38290a223f465fe6def44dbdf26061f4d5655df7d60d75a`, is pinned to the PC3 `pc3-v100` guest, Tesla V100-SXM2-16GB, R580.178.04, and `libcuda.so.580.178.04` SHA-256 `dcdf334aeb177c95b3385b797c9b871d03a287895713c6d107f79096b0985aa9`. Its complete linear `.text` scan feeds the bounded context collector and branch/slot scanners, so instruction addresses and operands are consistent with one decode stream. No raw bytes, symbol names, or pseudocode are retained.

| Global slot VA | Read RVA | Saved field | Replacement source | Store RVA | Following direct jump |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|
| `0x5bcfa20` | `0x3f1b01` | `[r12+0x40]` | `[rbx+0x40]` | `0x3f1b11` | `0x3f1b18 -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcfa18` | `0x3f1b1d` | `[r12+0x38]` | `[rbx+0x38]` | `0x3f1b2d` | `0x3f1b34 -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcfa10` | `0x3f1b39` | `[r12+0x30]` | `[rbx+0x30]` | `0x3f1b49` | `0x3f1b50 -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcfa08` | `0x3f1b55` | `[r12+0x28]` | `[rbx+0x28]` | `0x3f1b65` | `0x3f1b6c -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcfa00` | `0x3f1b71` | `[r12+0x20]` | `[rbx+0x20]` | `0x3f1b81` | `0x3f1b88 -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcf9f8` | `0x3f1b8d` | `[r12+0x18]` | `[rbx+0x18]` | `0x3f1b9d` | `0x3f1ba4 -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcf9f0` | `0x3f1ba9` | `[r12+0x10]` | `[rbx+0x10]` | `0x3f1bb9` | `0x3f1bc0 -> 0x3eb469` |
| `0x5bcf9e8` | `0x3f1bc5` | `[r12+0x8]` | `[rbx+0x8]` | `0x3f1bd5` | `0x3f1bdc -> 0x3eb469` |

For every row, the observed instruction metadata forms the same straight-line pattern: read the current slot into `rdx`, save `rdx` in the `r12`-based record, load a replacement value from the matching `rbx` offset, write that value into the same global slot, then unconditionally jump to the common sink. The source `.text` stream contains eight such sink jumps. The complete direct call/jump scan found no direct edge into any of the eight read-entry RVAs; it does not rule out indirect dispatch, an external entry, unreachable bytes, or another executable section. The register values, callback identities, semantic purpose, and runtime execution remain unknown.

The V39/V40 attempts that decoded this context by launching `objdump` at RVA `0x3f1b00` were rejected: the independently started window disagreed with the complete `.text` instruction boundaries at `0x3f1b01`. Those captures are not evidence. The collector was changed to retain only bounded, byte-free instruction metadata from the complete stream; V43 then passed its host-side context cross-checks. Focused tests pass **24/24**, and the full software suite passes **1,587/1,587** in **97.699 s**, with software-only qualification. The scanner and validator hashes are `4231acdbb24e65f8d43e3eafe42dfb37fa046a63baac6e4f616a78968de03e8f` and `0e1fbb47f9ad710365ae822217f4ae3ebda1117665421e42459c805886c2f936`.

Next, disassemble and validate the target window beginning at the decoded sink instruction `0x3eb469`, then inspect its indirect transfers and the data sources that select among these entries. This should reveal whether the cluster is a dispatch/context switch and provide a concrete target for a safe runtime observation; the static pattern alone does not establish that interpretation.

## R580 indirect-call slot references and local store context (2026-10-28 11:06 UTC)

The [v32 read-only PC3 capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v32-20261028T1106Z.json), SHA-256 `f9404028e8fafe2d04ccb5aa291ef55a3eee925a5e464c8d38249dd21f7f285e`, is pinned to the stock Tesla V100 guest, R580.178.04, and the exact `libcuda` SHA-256 already recorded below. Its in-guest `objdump -D -w -M intel -j .text` stream completed with exit status zero and contiguous address/byte coverage for `.text` VA `0x166f00–0x10e94e4`; 3,833,261 output lines / 225,118,939 bytes were scanned, while raw instruction bytes were discarded. The report identifies nine unsegmented RIP-relative references to three slots: `.data` slots `0x5bcf9e8` and `0x5bcfa08` (initial relocation addends `0x331bc0` and `0x332190`) and `.bss` slot `0x5bd7a50` (no relocation). Four other executable sections are explicitly unscanned.

The bounded window `0x3f1b40–0x3f1c00` shows two repeated patterns. For slot `0x5bcfa08`, `0x3f1b55` loads the slot into `rdx`, `0x3f1b5c` stores it to `[r12+0x28]`, `0x3f1b61` loads `rdx` from `[rbx+0x28]`, and `0x3f1b65` stores that register to the slot. For slot `0x5bcf9e8`, the analogous offsets are `+0x8` at `0x3f1bc5`, `0x3f1bcc`, `0x3f1bd1`, and `0x3f1bd5`. The code records these sequences as static context and does not claim the source `rbx` table, reaching definition, branch reachability, actual execution, or semantic role. The callsites `0x3279aa` and `0x327caa` statically consume the corresponding slots; the `.bss` slot is consumed by the indirect call at `0xf57127`, with no direct RIP-relative write to that slot found in this `.text` scan.

Focused tests pass 20/20 and the pinned software suite passes 1,583/1,583; both are software evidence. The first generated v31 attempt failed closed before producing a report because its guest script lacked the context-window constant; the source generator was fixed, its embedded code compile-tested, and the v32 capture then succeeded. No candidate module was installed or loaded, and no candidate CUDA context, kernel, UVM or P2P operation was exercised. The next static discriminator is the origin and call/control-flow path for `rbx` and `r12` around this repeated block; any later runtime probe must remain clearly separate from candidate-stack validation.

## R580/R595 GPU-name control ABI, callsites, and typed backend ownership (2026-10-28 10:37 UTC)

The exact command `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_GPU_GET_NAME_STRING` (`0x20800110`) appeared twice in each fresh stock RM_CONTROL header capture, but its payload is not branch-compatible. The compiled R580.178.04 SDK layout is 132 bytes with a 128-byte union covering ASCII/UTF-16; R595.99.02 is 68 bytes with a 64-byte ASCII field. The per-release capture sizes match the SDK probe. A same-size or generic forwarding assumption would be invalid. The [staged V100 trace v4](../evidence/pc3-v100-gpu-name-caller-stage-trace-v4-20261028T102004Z.json) was collected on the stock R580 stack through a test-only passive `LD_PRELOAD` observer. Its post-call stage snapshots place one matching 132-byte direct control in the interval ending after `cuInit` and one in the interval ending after explicit `cuDeviceGetName`; this attributes each event to an observation interval, not to the implementation of either API issuing the command. The trace retains no control payload or returned name contents.

Both direct controls return to RVA `0x48bc88`, matching the `ioctl` return in Ghidra's auto-labeled `FUN_0058bc40`; the next frame `0x495acc` is the return after the static call at `0x495ac7 -> 0x48bc40`. The nine-frame `cuInit` chain reaches `0x3279b0`, which Ghidra maps to the function named `cuInit`; the five-frame explicit-name chain ends at `0x327cb0`. The [bounded static follow-up v27](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v27-20261028T103730Z.json) covers all 11 unique return RVAs from trace v4. For every return address, an in-guest `.text` disassembly pass calculated the predecessor call's encoded length and verified that its end equals the observed return RVA; byte values were discarded before the small result was returned. It confirms five direct and six indirect calls. Ghidra correlation already contains the computed edge `0x3279aa -> 0x331bc0` and direct edge `0x331c2d -> 0x2d1e00`; five indirect destinations remain unresolved. The static call at `0x477204 -> 0x494f60` is present in v27, while that callsite is absent from Ghidra metadata; its callee entry is consistent with the Ghidra function range. The report is pinned to the exact [runtime trace](../evidence/pc3-v100-gpu-name-caller-stage-trace-v4-20261028T102004Z.json), SHA-256 `6cc2f9d20de78235af97fe3889f24e044e40d449cc9de1ac0d5d2aa3f0d6c774`. Function names remain analysis labels, not recovered source or semantic proof. The trace is redacted and contains no handles, payload bytes, or returned name contents.

The experimental frontend now marshals only the fixed R580 payload into a typed provider API. It resolves `NV20_SUBDEVICE_0` and its `NV01_DEVICE_0` parent through the active frontend root, requires the same backend owner and a live session, and selects only that backend's R580 provider. The provider takes a kernel-owned fixed packet through the native RM control path. The command is compiled only for `HYBRID580_UVM_ABI_R580`; R595 builds omit it and have zero imports of the new symbol. Unknown controls remain denied. The stock V100 trace establishes when R580 `libcuda` uses this control; it does not invoke the experimental frontend or prove its provider dispatch. Evidence: [paired stock capture comparison](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-control-header-comparison-v2-20261028T0955Z.json), [compiled SDK layouts](../evidence/rm-control-layout-r580-r595-v4-20261028T0945Z.json), [Open R580 provider](../evidence/private-open580-gpu-name-string-v2-20261028.json), [proprietary R580 provider](../evidence/private-proprietary-gpu-name-string-v3-20261028.json), [R580 frontend](../evidence/hybrid-frontend-r580-name-string-v1-20261028T1002Z.json) and [R595 guarded frontend](../evidence/hybrid-frontend-r595-name-string-guard-v1-20261028T1004Z.json).

The resolver's CPU/UBSan harness and the full frontend scenario test ownership and rejection conditions, direct RM_CONTROL and nested IOCTL_XFER marshalling, ASCII/UTF-16 return buffers, and copy-in/out fault behavior. Both frontends compile with zero unresolved imports. The stock-stack observer records the controls in the intervals ending after `cuInit` and `cuDeviceGetName`; it does not prove either API implementation issued them. The bounded instruction map verifies exact call/return byte boundaries but does not establish target semantics or experimental frontend behavior. Both candidate modules remain unloaded and uninstalled. Next, resolve the five open indirect targets and analyze their function boundaries; a real RM call through the candidate R580 frontend remains gated on canary signing, character-device identity, and rollback checks.

## Stock V100 numeric kernel reaches the R580 external-memory control-FD route (2026-10-28 09:10 UTC)

Observer v31 pins two exact R580 UVM payloads at compile time and reads only their 4-byte `rmCtrlFd` fields: `UVM_REGISTER_GPU_VASPACE` request 25 at offset 16, and `UVM_MAP_EXTERNAL_ALLOCATION` request 33 at offset 9,248. The latter payload is 9,264 bytes. Exact-header compilation reports identical request, size, and offsets across R580 proprietary, the R580 Open candidate, and R595 Open: [ABI report](../evidence/uvm-map-external-allocation-payload-abi-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `dc40cc00abae12cceb62cbe29b80d26d954ee4a07e4e7704fd16fd8b2b986d4a`. This is UAPI layout evidence only.

The stock V100 execution linked the packet to the source path. Pinned R580 `build/experiments/openrm580-baseline-v1/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_map_external.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer), SHA-256 `4115e9f2de1fc9ad460a61168e78e68ede19c57ac6216740dc57475b29d07511`, initializes `uvm_rm_user_object_t.rm_control_fd` from `params->rmCtrlFd` in `uvm_api_map_external_allocation()`, then calls the legacy `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemory()` in `uvm_map_external_gpu()`. Existing patch [`0003-pass-control-fd-to-dup-memory.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0003-pass-control-fd-to-dup-memory.patch) changes that call to `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemoryWithControlFd(user_rm_mem->rm_control_fd, ...)`. The bridge implementation first pins the memory owner using that control FD, derives the provider and backend handles from the pinned owner, and dispatches the duplicate route; see [`hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c`](../src/hybrid580_uvm_callback_bridge.c). The two hardware captures ran the unmodified stock R580 driver, so they confirm the actual request and source call site but do not execute the patched dispatcher.

The redacted observer sampled the field at both syscall stops and tracked standard open/dup/fcntl/close/close-range events across traced tasks. One context-only field capture recorded one request 25 and 26 request 33 calls. Two real numeric-kernel captures each recorded one request 25 and 28 request 33 calls; every call returned 0, the value stayed unchanged, the selected UVM descriptor matched `/dev/nvidia-uvm`, and the control descriptor matched `/dev/nvidiactl` at entry and return. Each kernel probe completed context creation, module load, allocation, kernel launch, synchronization and copy-back, yielding 700 as expected. Reports: [context](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-control-fd-context-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `8659e858223ed8e085d66399b5e4bb45123cbcace871a4c832e1bb26a1a8373d`; [kernel v1](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-control-fd-kernel-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `4ae7057ce893d68d387570508286b5373fdb8157fd4647a59c3b857c1d4da3af`; [kernel v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-control-fd-kernel-v2-20261028.json), SHA-256 `1fd7b50810ac51b8b52b731d76f8a2d417f6455f1cc0057e1c92b335442f79e0`. These mode-0600 reports retain inventory and runtime details; they intentionally emit no raw descriptor, TID, payload, transient file token, or ioctl argument pointer.

The current hash-pinned source audit [`r580-uvm-external-map-control-fd-v1-20261028.json`](../evidence/r580-uvm-external-map-control-fd-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `c33ed2d9a426f6f0dc3cb44e062214e48a70b091465fdfb610ba96d035508f1e`, identifies the most important limit of this route: it resolves the source owner from the control FD, pins the RM memory object, then permits duplication only when the destination device has the same provider. Cross-provider mismatch is rejected before RM duplication. The three remaining direct R580 P2P call sites still use the no-control-FD legacy ABI, which fails closed. The independent route model and source regressions cover provider mismatch and ownership collisions, but the audit itself is static; neither proves cross-provider memory import, P2P, DMA visibility, ordering, or coherence. Independent per-device CUDA contexts may still be viable without that facility, but same-process mixed-device execution remains untested.

A composed CPU harness now connects the production frontend owner-pin function to the production UVM duplicate-route helper. Its scenario assigns identical public root/device/memory handles to two frontend files while preserving distinct backend object values, then resolves each through a different control FD. The observed software behavior is that the pinned provider/root/memory reach the matching callback; a legacy-source to OPEN-target mismatch is refused before either backend callback; same-provider OPEN and legacy duplicates create and release typed child entries; and both source pins are cleared before teardown. The test compiles `src/hybrid_frontend.c` under UBSan and directly includes `src/hybrid580_uvm_dup_memory_route.h`; it does not call the native callback bridge or RM. The six focused checks and full 1,567-test software suite pass; [suite report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261028T091628Z.json), SHA-256 `04e68ce1b4f60e6f5356bbb948222f51ab32cbcfaa99a63faf745558e1a382a4`. The scenario's test-source hash is recorded in that report. This closes the owner-selection/route-model seam only, not runtime UVM interoperability.

The next wrapper boundary is also now executable: [`test_hybrid580_uvm_duplicate_memory_bridge_host.py`](../tests/test_hybrid580_uvm_duplicate_memory_bridge_host.py) extracts the exact `nvUvmInterfaceDupMemoryWithControlFd()` function body from the production bridge and compiles it with the production duplicate-memory route helper under UBSan. It verifies successful provider-affine duplication and memory-info copyout, cross-provider rejection before backend dispatch, fail-closed owner and allocation errors, and the quarantine path after a backend reports failure with a nonzero partial output handle. The owner-pin API and native RM callbacks are CPU doubles in this wrapper harness; the exact frontend owner-pin implementation is covered separately by the preceding harness. A mutation substituting the backend root for the backend memory handle fails as expected. The focused checks and full 1,568-test suite pass: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261028T092140Z.json), SHA-256 `2f633c70bddc2a142a6fafa62a8ee7647b059f2844a54aca70800bbdc539630f`. This joins the exact wrapper and route code in one executable host test, but still does not execute Linux UVM or native RM.

Observer source [`rm_ioctl_observer.c`](../src/rm_ioctl_observer.c) is SHA-256 `c5455de9e116d6738a7084c8e5296d7f426433b3013a3ff30199ef01566c06ee`; its reproducible build binary is SHA-256 `164ab413e80e1d5cce05afe71a090c5ca34269c2232e57c5af1cb70adedad97a`, with [build report](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-uvm-control-v31-build-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `ce046eaa62ce77359b4d3575b0541575bbba3e7fa53530e54749ad401440869d`. The full software suite after the context/kernel runner extension passes 1,566/1,566; it is separately qualified software-only. No experimental module was installed or loaded, and no same-kernel V100/Blackwell run occurred. The remaining race boundary includes untraced tasks, `io_uring` or externally injected FDs, and shared-field mutations that change and revert between samples; the lineage token is not a direct kernel `struct file` observation.

The next discriminating experiment is the user-space same-process route: map the stock R580/Blackwell `libcuda` node-open and ioctl requirements onto the standard-node frontend, then determine whether one Driver API instance can create independent contexts on both devices without cross-provider external-memory import. After source and build checks expose no missing endpoint/ABI blocker, exercise the exact patched R580 UVM consumer in an isolated loadable canary only after its current signature and V100 ownership gates are satisfied. Stock hardware evidence establishes the R580 call path; the CPU harnesses establish owner and bridge behavior only. Neither proves patched UVM execution or same-process cross-generation CUDA.

## Repeated stock V100 context trace maps the R580 UVM dispatch surface (2026-10-28 08:52 UTC)

Two complete `--phase context --ioctl-trace` runs on the same stock V100 guest and driver produced the same UVM ioctl counts and first-seen request order. Each has 675 selected-node trace events, including 136 UVM, 36 GPU-node, and 416 control-node ioctl calls; there are no UVM ioctl errors or unparsed trace lines. The trace includes successful context setup and cleanup, zero compute processes before/after, and no CUDA kernel. The report inputs are [v1](../evidence/pc3-v100-context-ioctl-sequence-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `8c7c9c2cb9369afcb7f0139ae03f82054103b35de485324022ddd566d1427588`, and [v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-context-ioctl-sequence-v2-20261028.json), SHA-256 `b7857cabab5f2b121c79a7134a76f856462af7355f29a5b1ac60673a3694244a`. Their syscall sidecars include raw syscall text and remain mode 0600; do not interpret them as payload captures.

The R580 Linux UVM dispatch in `build/experiments/private-open580-native-v4-softdep-v1-20261023/private/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer), SHA-256 `6549ff9293e5a789d53f59c29cbc2af4d7d8aedd0bfe7519699c4239e7ad24b3`, maps the observed request IDs to these handlers. Values are from the pinned Linux `build/experiments/private-open580-native-v4-softdep-v1-20261023/private/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_ioctl.h` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer), SHA-256 `937a3fe1c0b2f4d8cfc18443d41ad10ee85c4445f954cc5c67b460ded2dd4916`, plus `build/experiments/private-open580-native-v4-softdep-v1-20261023/private/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_linux_ioctl.h` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer), SHA-256 `0ffb5cf3679fb5e150745e54b72fab861e319823e419f36376468a60862c2748` for `UVM_INITIALIZE`.

| Observed request | Count per context | Pinned R580 dispatch handler |
|---|---:|---|
| `UVM_INITIALIZE` (`0x30000001`) | 1 | `uvm_api_initialize` |
| `UVM_MM_INITIALIZE` (`0x4b`) | 1 | `uvm_api_mm_initialize` |
