[`analyze_v100_uvm_context_sequence.py`](../tools/analyze_v100_uvm_context_sequence.py), source SHA-256 `b99bd929674510bc83b4d77eeeffb091ff0dee705aa770fa5f0b42bb0c921737`, resolves request names from those headers, checks that repeated captures use the same guest/driver and complete trace schema, compares their counts and first-seen order, and emits a normalized mode-0600 report. Its [aggregate](../evidence/pc3-v100-context-uvm-sequence-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `475fa6e4275328de55626161998e641dfe8de68ff246da6cf6e8ecc6dfc0c1bb`, confirms identical metadata in both runs and omits transient FD/TID/file identities, ioctl arguments, and payloads. The analyzer's seven focused regressions and the complete repository software suite pass **1,566/1,566**; the suite is software-only: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261028T085346Z.json), SHA-256 `68396c56995f296a741fda9042a82df8513d49d69eb6b4963aaada0f85bb7c99`.

The later [R595/R615 observed-route coverage receipt](../evidence/r595-r615-observed-v100-uvm-route-coverage-v3-20261029.json), SHA-256 `9397d2881927cde962b06697c82854a9da0e2dc14a41af1bdb4ff6ddde5be738`, validates request IDs against the pinned R580 headers and each reported count against the hash-pinned raw V100 trace, then checks both branch dispatch inventories. It confirms that the observed `UVM_CREATE_RANGE_GROUP` request `0x17` (8 per capture) has the R595 handler and no R615 definition or dispatch. Commands outside the compared stack-initialized dispatch set are marked outside scope; the report does not claim payload, R595-libcuda, or hybrid-runtime compatibility. Regressions reject forged counts and distinguish a missing dispatch from a same-name request-number mismatch.

The observation shows that context creation exercises more UVM registration and external-allocation routes than the isolated VA-space call alone. It narrows the frontend review to these exact dispatch handlers and their ownership/backend semantics, especially the repeated external range/map and channel register/unregister pairs. It does not show payload contents or prove the experimental frontend supports any route.

## Previous increment: concurrent-safe bounded observation of the V100 UVM control-FD route (observer v30; 2026-10-28 08:47 UTC)

The pinned R580 path in `uvm_api_register_gpu_va_space()` copies `params->rmCtrlFd` into `uvm_rm_user_object_t.rm_control_fd` and forwards it to VA-space registration. The exact `uvm_gpu.c` source is SHA-256 `b43bb94bbc465bbb4fb243e6f722cb450d6402f3071971843d61f1f7678bf700`.

Observer v30 adds a fail-closed guard around the field observation. It maintains transient file-identity lineage across selected-node open/dup/fcntl/close operations, checks for pending `close`, targeted `dup2`/`dup3`, and covering `close_range` operations from other traced tasks at ioctl entry and exit, and rereads the 4-byte field at return. An unobservable shared descriptor table (`unshare(CLONE_FILES)` or `CLOSE_RANGE_UNSHARE`) makes the trace incomplete. JSON output contains only booleans and syscall status, never raw FD/task/file tokens, pointers, or payload bytes. This is observed userspace FD-lifecycle lineage, not direct observation of the kernel `struct file` pointer. It does not cover `io_uring`, external FD injection, untraced tasks, or a shared-memory field change that occurs and reverts between the two sampling stops.

Three bounded context-only runs on the stock PC3 V100 guest (R580.178.04, Linux `6.8.0-147-generic`) each observed request `0x19` return 0, unchanged field value, matching tracked identity lineage at return, no in-flight tracked mutation at either stop, completed trace and cleanup, and zero compute processes before/after. Reports: [v7](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-rmctrlfd-context-observer-v7-20261028.json), SHA-256 `f13e1d59a78b68344a4ec61361ecda4949fe36091dc6a877061cfa6bc625ab0a`; [v8](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-rmctrlfd-context-observer-v8-20261028.json), SHA-256 `48d6355ac2f3da7f2c7672f5913e10d36b977a976aa0c56245b3ccff7a545361`; [v9](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-rmctrlfd-context-observer-v9-20261028.json), SHA-256 `d551848b16e22607580295069c1bc946f8517237980938a2a713f8f2680a4f5b`.

The pinned observer source `src/rm_ioctl_observer.c` (SHA-256 `fd93aba250b398134b9d3ba1aa97468ca292942683a7ee80cd54553fc3b70bc2`) builds reproducibly with GCC 15.2.0 to binary SHA-256 `a01689bd4298aa4860a436df1bf3d0cf31eda3f1265da14c558b17ecfad78e4b`; its [build report](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-uvm-v30-build-v1-20261028.json) is qualified build-only. The full pinned software suite passes **1,559/1,559**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261028T084618Z.json), SHA-256 `b21267d6de337d3fa3a995db001a47ff5e9a2fbb41d1366b9d2bb9930a840d38`.

These results constrain the stock R580 UVM registration path in these calls. They neither exercise the hybrid frontend nor validate a GPU kernel through UVM. Same-Linux/same-process V100+Blackwell CUDA remains open.

## Earlier direct observation of the V100 UVM VA-space control FD (observer v28; 2026-10-28 08:35 UTC)

The pinned R580 UVM route maps `UVM_REGISTER_GPU_VASPACE` (`0x19`) to `uvm_api_register_gpu_va_space()`. In `uvm_gpu.c`, that function copies `params->rmCtrlFd` into `uvm_rm_user_object_t.rm_control_fd` and forwards the object to `uvm_va_space_register_gpu_va_space()`. The source is `build/experiments/private-open580-native-v4-softdep-v1-20261023/private/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia-uvm/uvm_gpu.c` (local-only; excluded from GitLab transfer), SHA-256 `b43bb94bbc465bbb4fb243e6f722cb450d6402f3071971843d61f1f7678bf700`.

The new isolated observer pins the request number, payload size, and 4-byte field offset at compile time, then reads only the field at syscall entry. It compares `/proc/<tid>/fd/<rmCtrlFd>` with the chosen control node at entry and return and serializes only match booleans, a negative-value predicate, syscall result, and completion state. Raw FD, TID, ioctl argument, and payload bytes are omitted. Build artifact SHA-256 is `ec1ebd8aeff80d38c149903fcaa74abdf7f1fae29db0ed9f6587429ab8d5a4b3`; [build report](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-uvm-v28-build-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `377f1b45b61e7e6bd906dd821516ade006476cfdf66d3c6b12220672311948f0`. A CPU fixture passed against this binary.

Three context-only runs on the stock PC3 V100 guest, R580.178.04 / Linux `6.8.0-147-generic`, each captured exactly one successful request 25. The field was read at entry, nonnegative, resolved to `/dev/nvidiactl` at entry and return, and the request returned 0. Trace completion, guest cleanup, payload cleanup, and zero compute processes before/after passed on every run. Reports: [v1](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-rmctrlfd-context-observer-v1-20261028.json), SHA-256 `6beb57a5042104db275a86356e8003b23ed871167aed7991395ef9a0709bb189`; [v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-rmctrlfd-context-observer-v2-20261028.json), SHA-256 `f8c50390fcf0b4d6203aec7cb1fb6349d81068d7c79c71f0c2c292230e6f9561`; [v3](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-rmctrlfd-context-observer-v3-20261028.json), SHA-256 `0f8490d0b287a52bb110a18d851e4de2fce30eee77bc0f814f4e14acdba36bd3`.

This establishes that the observed field resolves to the control device at the two observation points in these three stock-driver executions. It does not prove the same `struct file` survived a concurrent close/reopen of that descriptor, exclude a thread changing the shared payload between the ptrace stop and kernel read, cover all libcuda paths, validate a patched kernel, or demonstrate a CUDA kernel in this context phase. The earlier strace below remains useful only for its historical descriptor lifecycle and its syscall result; its raw sidecar does not expose the field.

## V100 context reaches UVM VA-space registration (2026-10-28 08:15 UTC)

A bounded context-phase trace on the stock R580.178.04 V100 guest now captures UVM ioctl request `0x19`, which the pinned R580 UVM header defines as `UVM_REGISTER_GPU_VASPACE` (`UVM_IOCTL_BASE(25)`). The request returns 0. In the same TID, `/dev/nvidiactl` fd 8/file identity 2 was opened earlier and no close of that file identity appears before this ioctl or by trace end. The trace therefore observes the target registration call while the control-node descriptor remains open in that execution.

This improved on the October 23 open-lifecycle trace, which saw only `UVM_REGISTER_GPU` (`0x25`) and `UVM_CREATE_RANGE_GROUP` (`0x17`) and never reached VA-space registration. The [report](../evidence/pc3-v100-uvm-vaspace-context-trace-v3-20261028T0814Z.json) is SHA-256 `f9d2f2990809462ae6f28a967fe58a573c7059d8847f706df03304407d061950`; its compressed syscall trace is SHA-256 `f026c3942bf4c8b97704bfc6f751061540bfcdd760cee3e9292243a8d196e519` (raw trace SHA-256 `6110622d4434f5f04fa1cdd4dd3b9a6a8d6a90e4c42eb43f87227e6e7f2d6217`). The saved report/sidecar retain transient TID and descriptor IDs, and the syscall trace prints the ioctl argument pointer, but does not read the pointed-to payload. Its inability to correlate `rmCtrlFd` to fd 8 is historical and superseded by the payload-aware observer above. Concurrent close/reuse was not ruled out. This remains one stock-driver context run, not evidence for the hybrid frontend or cross-generation CUDA.

An exact-header host compile pins the target request's payload layout across R580 proprietary, R580 Open-candidate, and R595 Open trees. Each uses `UVM_REGISTER_GPU_VASPACE` value `0x19`; `UVM_REGISTER_GPU_VASPACE_PARAMS` is 32 bytes and `rmCtrlFd` is a 32-bit field at offset 16. The following fields are `hClient` at 20, `hVaSpace` at 24, and `rmStatus` at 28. The mode-0600 [ABI report](../evidence/uvm-register-vaspace-payload-abi-v1-20261028T0819Z.json), SHA-256 `06c42512f22d6a4c71f8b681203c8e74b04e2560607011f1f11e62e35b531a60`, records byte-identical layouts and zero command deltas. The source confirms how the kernel consumes the field; the later redacted runtime observation above confirms its selected-node mapping in three executions.

To make this observation reproducible, `tools/run_pc3_v100_guest_reference.py` now supports strict node-lifecycle tracing in `--phase context` as well as enumerate; `tests/run_hybrid_acceptance.py` exposes that mode, and regression tests syntax-check the embedded guest script and validate lifecycle aggregation. Focused tests pass 138/138; full pinned suite v88 passes 1,552/1,552. The suite report records software-only qualification, `runtime_or_hardware_validated=false`, and `loaded_or_installed=false`: [report](../evidence/software-verification-uvm-context-trace-runner-v88-20261028T0815Z.json), SHA-256 `1b13dade458c8bfe6a678df26ea93f1da66852e4bb3a623adad7908baf2a7475`.

## UVM pending-quarantine publication and control-FD observation (2026-10-28 08:06 UTC)

The frontend's UVM owner callbacks cannot sleep waiting for the frontend transaction mutex: they may be reached from an RM/UVM lock context whose global order is not proven. The memory and VASpace ambiguous-owner/unpin-underflow paths therefore attempt `mutex_trylock`; contention sets an atomic per-file pending poison. Subsequent RM ioctl and standard GPU-file ioctl routes check that poison and fail closed with `-EIO`, so routing around UVM through an already-open GPU fd cannot proceed. If a root-local quarantine is published with the mutex held, cleanup of a distinct healthy root remains possible. Exact source is [`src/hybrid_frontend.c`](../src/hybrid_frontend.c), SHA-256 `c35dded9ef2a11dcf7e6e7a1d46ac099f34f0c7dab652a29fc0e8e3d324121e2`.

Tests execute CPU models for memory/VASpace ambiguous owners, unpin underflow, lock contention, pending rejection through both ioctl routes, and cleanup of unrelated roots. The complete pinned suite v87 passes 1,550 tests; the frontend v8 and R580 UVM consumer v7 compile and the latter matches 77/77 standard imports. These remain software/build results, not a loaded-kernel test or hardware result.

The October 23 stock V100 `libcuda` open-lifecycle capture recorded only UVM request `0x25` (`UVM_REGISTER_GPU`) and `0x17` (`UVM_CREATE_RANGE_GROUP`); the later trace reached request `0x19` but did not inspect its payload. The payload-aware observer above now correlates the field to `/dev/nvidiactl` at syscall entry and return in three executions, but does not rule out concurrent descriptor close/reuse or shared-payload mutation between the syscall stop and kernel consumption. Patch [0006](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0006-pin-control-file-before-va-space-registration.patch) retains the `struct file` reference after `fget()` through registration dispatch; the unresolved interval is before that `fget()`. Earlier October 23 trace metadata: [report](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-open-lifecycle-v2-20261023.json), SHA-256 `67edd13a5c0616b340b549d32399d6b22594237ad22d981375affb08bdee6919`.

The exact PC3 candidate preflight remains blocked by six independent module, signing, device-number and character-device-label conditions. No candidate module has been loaded or installed. No same-kernel V100+Blackwell CUDA context or kernel validation follows from these artifacts.

## R580 UVM address-space duplication must retain the RM control-file identity (2026-10-28)

The pinned R580.178.04 UVM path creates a GPU VA space in `uvm_gpu_va_space_create()` by duplicating the user VASpace through `nvUvmInterfaceDupAddressSpace`. The call receives the public client and object handles, while the caller's `uvm_rm_user_object_t` also carries `rm_control_fd`. Those public handles are file/root scoped: the frontend model deliberately creates two frontend files whose public client, device, and VASpace values collide while their backend VASpace handles differ. A dispatcher that resolves by public handle alone can select the wrong RM namespace.

Patch [0005](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0005-pass-control-fd-to-dup-address-space.patch) routes this R580 call through the hybrid-only `nvUvmInterfaceDupAddressSpaceWithControlFd`. The frontend obtains a file reference with `fget`, accepts only its own control-file operations, resolves the exact client and `FERMI_VASPACE_A`, verifies the parent `NV01_DEVICE_0` and backend ancestry, and pins that VASpace across the provider's native duplicate call. It also verifies that UVM's registered device belongs to the provider selected by the VASpace. The stock ABI symbol remains exported for R580 ABI compatibility but fails closed because it cannot name the owning file. The custom symbol is owned only by `hybrid580_frontend`, CRC `0xa61570c4`; it is not a standard UVM API.

The exact R580 path calls `create_gpu_va_space()` before `uvm_va_space_register_gpu_va_space()` takes its explicit VA-space and mmap write locks. The full cross-driver lock order with frontend control transactions is still unaudited, so the frontend currently uses `mutex_trylock` rather than waiting until that order is proven safe. The caller checks the returned status and aborts GPU VA-space creation on `NV_ERR_NOT_READY`; this path has no automatic retry. Fail-fast preserves bounded ownership behavior but makes transient contention a functional risk. The FD-aware boundary is checked only at the time of `fget`; the bounded stock libcuda trace below records `UVM_REGISTER_GPU` and `UVM_CREATE_RANGE_GROUP` while the control node remains open, but does not capture the VA-space registration ioctl or payload and cannot exclude concurrent close/reuse by another thread.

The frontend model executes colliding-handle owner selection, invalid FD/client rejection, lock contention, and pin-protected/free-after-unpin lifetimes. A pinned-source test asserts that R580 duplicates the VASpace before explicit VA-space/mmap write locks and propagates errors before lock acquisition, with no in-function retry. The R580 patch applies without fuzz. Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` compile evidence shows the frontend module exports the new dispatcher with no unresolved imports; a clean R580 UVM-only build against its exact `Module.symvers` matches 77/77 standard UVM imports and includes the dispatcher CRC. See the [frontend build report](../evidence/uvm-address-space-control-fd-frontend-v5-20261028073344Z.json), [UVM consumer report](../evidence/uvm-address-space-control-fd-consumer-v3-20261028073344Z.json), and [suite v83](../evidence/software-verification-uvm-address-space-owner-v83-20261028073422Z.json). This remains source/model and compile evidence only: no module was installed or loaded, no native RM call ran, and no UVM migration, CUDA kernel, or GPU was exercised.

## R580/R595 non-replayable fault-buffer parent lifecycle (2026-10-28)

The host harness now compiles and calls the exact `uvm_parent_gpu_fault_buffer_init_non_replayable_faults` and `uvm_parent_gpu_fault_buffer_deinit_non_replayable_faults` bodies from pinned R580.178.04 and R595 v15 UVM sources, alongside exact replayable init/reinit/deinit and parent init/resume/deinit bodies. Fifteen vectors per source pass 210 checks each (420 total), with six expected source-assertion guards. The parent caller scenarios exercise RM-init failure and partial-handle clearing, replayable allocation unwind, failure at each non-replayable allocation point, and successful resume/teardown. The non-replayable helper call totals are checked: three init and five deinit invocations per source.

R580 source `uvm_gpu_non_replayable_faults.c` SHA-256 is `588e72e5e3163f2e9a3c1b0d78a5450c97a4393b5b0c8e024915c41460d0250b`; R595 is `83fd573b3f5187ded355dec0a835f293716b0d271204d2c617864346fac01888`. The init-body hashes are R580 `fb754753dcdcb2e85c33e0169d13aa267fb910964ffed87be0b49e65e0c25702` and R595 `9bfd56443847d2b34c7c9f268ef757fdf98138d8d0c16efb484920b6e69f4759`; deinit is identical at `46be8c999dca2c53f460f5ad03025243b48703bae62d75093dcc1aeb0b171946`. After removing the R580-only assertion on `non_replayable_faults_supported`, the normalized init bodies match. R580 declares the capability field, asserts it in the helper, conditionally initializes/deinitializes non-replayable state, and sets the flag true for GV100. R595 v15 removes the three legacy capability fields and unconditionally calls the helper; it omits the assertion.

The host model supplies an RM buffer size of 96 bytes and a HAL entry size of 32 bytes, so the exact source helper computes `max_faults=3`. The helper's `sizeof(*fault_cache)` expression is evaluated with a synthetic 16-byte cache-entry type, yielding a 48-byte allocation. That representation is not the kernel's `uvm_fault_buffer_entry_t`, so the allocation byte count is only a host-model result. RM interfaces, allocator, tracker, HAL, and all hardware-facing behavior are synthetic; no Linux UVM, RM-owned buffer, GPU fault, interrupt, DMA, module, or CUDA context is exercised.

The [v5 report](../evidence/volta-fault-buffer-parent-lifecycle-host-v5-20261028T062100Z.json), SHA-256 `c18bfa5568e04c22507c32d4eb95823a31ddea7e489364683ff00475f287de6f`, supersedes the [v4 parent-lifecycle report](../evidence/volta-fault-buffer-parent-lifecycle-host-v4-20261028T061343Z.json), which used synthetic non-replayable helper callbacks. The private log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/volta-fault-buffer-parent-lifecycle-host-v5-20261028T062100Z.log`) is SHA-256 `5277dd75fd36f3c1b7de314eeba1a9818bc7f33bcfcd0bbb126eb04192c3682f`. Focused test and full pinned suite v70 pass; the suite reports **1,530 tests** in **98.861 s**, software-only: [report](../evidence/software-verification-volta-fault-buffer-parent-v70-20261028T062100Z.json), SHA-256 `a20d2408fa02eb16d3c19d54e96453b840bf6ea1bf3457cabad0f05cf4ee852e`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-volta-fault-buffer-parent-v70-20261028T062100Z.log`), SHA-256 `45a7c28505adf5e6d2653a06e59c6440ba66f596a482aa85fc64828a136101e6`.

Next, inspect the non-replayable fault service and suspend/drain consumers, then establish the real kernel `uvm_fault_buffer_entry_t` layout and allocation size. The v5 helper run itself is not evidence that those service paths or the ABI layout are compatible.

## R595 deferred-channel fault drain backport to R580 (2026-10-28)

R580.178.04's deferred channel-free path flushes replayable faults, but has no corresponding RM-backed non-replayable shadow-buffer drain. R595 v15 adds a per-parent flush before destroying a detached channel to avoid reusing an instance pointer while an old queued notification can still be attributed to it. The pinned R595 `uvm_gpu_non_replayable_faults.c` source is SHA-256 `83fd573b3f5187ded355dec0a835f293716b0d271204d2c617864346fac01888`; the helper's normalized function hash is `d7f5c4b2fe33745ec7a6ce8c95612921b6480a32f5733158c30a646b1581a778`.

The new [R580 patch](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0002-flush-nonreplayable-on-deferred-free.patch) adds that helper, asserts the ISR spinlock contract in the pending-fault query, exposes the existing R580 scheduler to the helper, and calls the drain once per parent before `uvm_user_channel_destroy_detached`. It retains R580's `uvm_gpu_fault_buffer_flush(gpu)` name and flushes replayable/access-counter buffers only for GR channels; the new non-replayable flush covers every detached engine channel, as in R595. The per-parent masks avoid duplicate queue flushes when several channels from one physical parent are freed in one list. The patch test pins the R580 inputs, checks the scheduling/lock/queue order, verifies that the normalized helper body equals R595, and checks that each flush precedes channel destruction.

The helper takes `interrupts_lock` only while scheduling, releases it before waiting on `bottom_half_q`, and drains that queue even when the scheduler sees no pending entry. Its R595 header contract warns that faults added during the call are not guaranteed to be flushed; it also does not guarantee that the hardware channel-faulted state is cleared or that RM has serviced fatal faults. The deferred-free API is documented as potentially taking ISR and RM locks, and the R595 VA-space-mm caller explicitly invokes it after dropping the VA-space lock. Runtime lock ordering and the no-new-fault assumption after detach remain untested on the candidate.

A new host C harness compiles and executes the exact helper bodies from the R580 candidate source tree and R595 v15. Both normalize to SHA-256 `d7f5c4b2fe33745ec7a6ce8c95612921b6480a32f5733158c30a646b1581a778`. Four synthetic cases (each source with the scheduler returning 0 and 1) observe `lock → schedule → unlock → queue flush`; assertions verify the scheduler holds the interrupt lock and the queue wait does not. The [mode-0600 report](../evidence/volta-nonreplayable-flush-host-v1-20261028T0637Z.json), SHA-256 `69d7b2f977a2d5e17b7cf67496efab0b2efc9b46e5b7f1d9c2525d4d7b74d050`, and log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/volta-nonreplayable-flush-host-v1-20261028T0637Z.log`), SHA-256 `77e01bdd7f56f4c6f3e23b77894a4a6cabc2e44b410621dafff54a296f3ff39e`, pin the source inputs and tool output. The collaborators model API calls; this does not execute Linux spinlocks, workqueues, RM, interrupts, or GPU faults.

The backport and the separate existing R580 suspend-drain patch now compile together from a clean pinned R580 archive, after the three isolated-UVM/control-fd/shared-Kbuild overlays. `0001-suspend-drain-non-replayable-faults.patch` runs before `0002-flush-nonreplayable-on-deferred-free.patch`; both apply with zero fuzz to the package-root `kernel-open/` tree. The resulting `nvidia-uvm.ko` is 62,571,040 bytes (SHA-256 `8876d1424551d8f708b37c8a3d074721b135721641b8079cd572b7824b3d3132`); `modinfo` reports `nvidia_uvm`, Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` vermagic, and only `hybrid580_frontend` as dependency. All 77 UVM imports match the pinned frontend CRCs, and its symvers file was unchanged. The reproducible [combined build report](../evidence/r580-nonreplayable-fault-drains-build-v3-20261028T0634Z.json) is SHA-256 `c1a334275a6d9297fa572f66b262a3c28f8666733576d482bc6a3413570b6dd6`; its log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/r580-nonreplayable-fault-drains-build-v3-20261028T0634Z.log`) is SHA-256 `5ee0cd4d815b5aec99e808d9a1e248f502754fe2667e7cad48a4d9c1a3a1ddfc`. Build output has the same upstream DisplayPort overloaded-virtual and `pahole`-version warnings; there are no compile/link errors.

This is **compiled and frontend-bound** evidence only: the candidate module was not installed or loaded and no GPU fault was triggered. Full pinned software suite v73, including the exact-helper host-execution tests, passed **1,536 tests** in **97.971 s**, software-only: [report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-nonreplayable-flush-host-v73-20261028T0637Z.json), SHA-256 `a7fdef446f11ce6e07809daa23f3f8cb358f1501cd9c168e1a83fc04fbc4d54a`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-nonreplayable-flush-host-v73-20261028T0637Z.log`), SHA-256 `77b778f1b2f5a74e2c0e1f4f485482ba454a52dcd0dc62484ee3af3f634e7dbb`. The prior v72 combined-build suite remains recorded in [VALIDATION.md](VALIDATION.md).

### Exact deferred-free list-walker and channel execution

The exact `uvm_deferred_free_object_list` bodies from the pinned R580 candidate and R595 v15 are identical after normalization (`fe8bdb2d7e37de6133eed2013e9f0c2298ccbfb1c23d8c4050d32c1aad824fe0`). The runner compiles them together with the exact channel callback bodies and synthetic Linux-style list links, processor masks, object layouts, and RM/UVM callbacks. Both snapshots pass an empty-list case, a mixed list containing channel, GPU-VA-space, external-allocation, and device-P2P objects across two parents, and two sequential lists. Mixed dispatch produces `000lNDlVlRADlElPlNRAD`: the three masks are initialized; each object is unlinked before dispatch; per-parent fault queues are drained before channel destruction; and the other object callbacks retain list order. The second invocation repeats the non-replayable drain, showing per-call masks do not leak across lists.

The [mode-0600 report](../evidence/volta-deferred-free-list-host-v1-20261028064601UZ.json), SHA-256 `98267620759afd143c624c715ed8e01b9f7942d9e96d973ae9ee5d3f1701032f`, and log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/volta-deferred-free-list-host-v1-20261028064601UZ.log`), SHA-256 `df8ed8fde5a783ea83ae763886bcdfa98f452f8edbe2b6b695c80f78d8497350`, preserve six passing executions. The harness's list primitive, mask operations, and callbacks are synthetic; this does not validate Linux list internals, kernel lock ordering, RM ownership, GPU fault delivery, module loading, CUDA, or hardware. Static source inspection located **13 deferred-free callers in each** pinned tree. Their surrounding paths release the VA-space lock, and where held, the mm/range locks before draining; this source inspection does not prove runtime lock state under concurrency. The pinned full software suite v75 passed **1,541 tests** in **97.444 s**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-deferred-free-list-host-v75-20261028T0648Z.json), SHA-256 `8e99480fa6911ab16a99a22666b1bf94c490e3641bb7dbd408f8479e6eebf681`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-deferred-free-list-host-v75-20261028T0648Z.log`), SHA-256 `efb5f0eb11004e04e7531b6d320ef432434d13a4472a8e9481788a586d368ff9`.

The follow-up [caller audit](../tools/audit_deferred_free_callers.py) pins and scans the actual R580 v3 candidate caller files and R595 v15 counterparts. It finds 13 calls per tree and, for each, a recognized direct lock-release token before the call with no recognized direct reacquire token in the remaining window. The mode-0600 [audit report v2](../evidence/deferred-free-caller-audit-v2-20261028T0656Z.json), SHA-256 `19d5de2be7bf6a00e550b9a6c83a3cb2c726d525e1bd102de1be24f533bcc152`, has no scanner issues. Its 96-line lexical window is not path-sensitive and does not inspect locks inside helpers or establish runtime lock state or concurrency safety. Full suite v77 passed **1,543 tests** in **99.069 s**: [report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-deferred-free-caller-audit-v77-20261028T0657Z.json), SHA-256 `f4032a9e1220f7cf869be5ea79dae2ee5bf49c2e77f2fc5c3ec79aed0ad4e65b`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-deferred-free-caller-audit-v77-20261028T0657Z.log`), SHA-256 `31082136dd4fb11c33333e03509b7556356642ca7aab82fd374335a161808018`. The suite is software-only.

## R580/R595 GV100 replayable fault-buffer lifecycle (2026-10-28)

The exact `fault_buffer_init_replayable_faults`, `fault_buffer_reinit_replayable_faults`, and `fault_buffer_deinit_replayable_faults` bodies from pinned R580.178.04 and R595 v15 UVM trees compile and execute in a host C harness. Ten vectors per source exercise successful setup, failure at each of three CPU allocations, RM page-fault-ownership failure, two reinit states, four deinit/prefetch states, partial allocation release, tracker teardown, and pointer clearing. The harness passes **127 checks per source, 254 total**, plus six expected source-assertion rejections.

Function hashes: R580 init `9fab37556fa02c9b7efd4c7ad59a5d6d92a3b6a2963800b5102a7ad566f8b473`, reinit `6b4cb874e7637d6fe5ad6be1ffcb724d6be5ce090bd38b14c645b3dde14c2bf4`, deinit `8af117f2c38f592c1e1facf2c7c913d3d5f715233759a0f29b9d1d48c2cfe0a5`; R595 v15 init `675c94f2d66a0614991f4042bf75c0006517ffbe38b42e46fc69ba3920935f2f`, reinit (same hash), deinit `0dce92b4c39fb4db283cb1d60aef691f5856e2b8daaea2010f7b7cc90be11372`. The init bodies normalize identically after replacing only the assignment from the R580 `prefetch_fault_supported` field with R595's unconditional `true`. The deinit bodies normalize identically after replacing only the R580 capability conjunction with R595's unconditional disabled-state check. GV100 initializes the legacy R580 capability true; the R595 tree has removed that field. The harness's `prefetch_supported=false` on R595 is explicitly a compatibility-shim counterfactual, not a possible field value in R595.

The exact reinit body is now invoked: synthetic GET/PUT callback values change between calls and the test verifies cached values refresh, then confirms reinit enables or disables prefetch according to the recorded state. Deinit is invoked after partial allocation failures and verifies each non-null CPU allocation is released and each stored pointer is cleared. The earlier [v2 report](../evidence/volta-fault-init-deinit-host-v2-20261028T013455Z.json) compiled but did not invoke reinit; the [v3 report](../evidence/volta-fault-buffer-lifecycle-host-v3-20261028T013820Z.json), SHA-256 `14ba15ebca4d3ff2005d832cce86430fdff26a14aed210eac3f452566e14e155`, supersedes it. Its private log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/volta-fault-buffer-lifecycle-host-v3-20261028T013820Z.log`) is SHA-256 `197d4d0e092bbb3c2f5e309e42bfac7e2ae500d6b2e93769fd913f3d3ce6ccc1`; see the [runner](../tools/run_volta_fault_init_host_harness.py) and [test](../tests/test_volta_fault_init_host_harness.py).

This proves extracted C helper behavior only. CPU allocator, tracker, RM ownership API, GET/PUT register callbacks, and prefetch callbacks are synthetic; it does not prove Linux UVM locking, RM-owned buffers, GPU MMIO, DMA, page-fault interrupts, or candidate-module behavior. Full pinned suite v68 passes **1,530 tests** in **119.508 s**, with `runtime_or_hardware_validated=false` and `loaded_or_installed=false`; its [report](../evidence/software-verification-volta-fault-buffer-lifecycle-v68-20261028T013835Z.json) is SHA-256 `49dc9b8b7dd1d2908471dd31515c571d3de1742834f697e13f4375549c80dbbd`, and its log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-volta-fault-buffer-lifecycle-v68-20261028T013835Z.log`) is SHA-256 `1cef7a44bf5101f1eddb3edb235caf46a43009a404057db99760ce7a7c4c2ce0`.

The next vertical slice is the exact outer `uvm_parent_gpu_fault_buffer_init`, `uvm_parent_gpu_fault_buffer_resume`, and `uvm_parent_gpu_fault_buffer_deinit` sequence. Its R580/R595 source differences include the R580-only replayable-capability assertion and conditional non-replayable setup/teardown versus R595's unconditional calls. RM behavior and non-replayable helper bodies must remain identified as synthetic unless their exact source is also extracted.

## Full Volta replayable fault-buffer flush contract (2026-10-28)

The exact R580.178.04 and R595 v15 `fault_buffer_flush_locked` bodies compile and execute on the host with instrumented collaborators. Eight scenarios exercise the full control flow: cached ring drain/wrap/empty, error while waiting for an invalid slot through both global and channel status paths, `WAIT_UPDATE_PUT` with tracker wait and hardware PUT refresh, tracker failure, hardware-flush failure, and parent-vs-GPU replay dispatch. The test also executes the exact `read_get`, `read_put`, and `write_get` Volta callbacks, so the PUT-refresh branch uses the selected callback against generated GV100 field definitions.

Normalized function hashes are R580 `2bccc613de4d63a923f5fb07dbad7bda8c3bde0952ddb466b133c5eb2edab81a` and R595 v15 `84bd10cc2b37265fb93c1b8e289928a785f481736c575684eb49f9aae13c6260`. Removing only the R580 `UVM_ASSERT(parent_gpu->replayable_faults_supported)` makes the normalized bodies identical. R580 `uvm_gpu.h` includes the support flag and R580 `uvm_volta.c` sets it true for GV100; R595 v15's `uvm_gpu.h` has no such field. The candidate R595 Volta property adaptation also removes that initialization assignment. The test sets the synthetic R580 property true, matching the pinned GV100 initialization source; it does not prove candidate init or runtime capability safety. See the [host report v3](../evidence/volta-fault-ring-host-v3-20261028T012437Z.json), [runner](../tools/run_volta_fault_ring_host_harness.py), and [R595 build/adaptation report](../evidence/r595-gv100-hal-standard-nvidia-frontend-v15-20261028T004147Z.json).

These init, reinit, and deinit helper contracts are now host-executed in the lifecycle section above. The enclosing parent-GPU init/deinit call path and its RM-init failure behavior remain unexecuted and are the next source-driven validation target.

## Replayable fault-ring drain service in R580 and R595 (2026-10-28)

The R580.178.04 and R595 v15 `uvm_gpu_replayable_faults.c` snapshots have different whole-file hashes (`c7991f9a9e029ae1e9dc4aba579ecb37c64408d9600117abdeec82fbc1b245b3` and `e2b98f550ad6ef3efb062d931834f09acd79ecd5fa140a2f54de8e77a5dfac2b`). The normalized `write_get`, `fault_buffer_skip_replayable_entry`, and `while (get != put)` bodies are identical: `4689c6c01ba20d747b3449a9b401db8dea39bd830056b9698b57bfa2b11eeff90`, `72fcb5878b63f49fecab9ffd27da0008b89c663b881341a800342db645a24b11`, and `37038ea4a1bcef5be1231f47f5186e2326d7873fc61bfc6ed5511de1e955e829`, respectively. The loop waits for a valid slot, checks GPU or global error status while waiting, clears each replayable entry, wraps GET at `max_faults`, and finally commits GET. If status fails on an invalid slot, it commits the blocked slot and propagates the status before replay is issued.

The exact-source CPU harness executes three service vectors per release: a wrap across the end of an eight-entry ring, an empty ring, and a status error at the first invalid entry after one entry has been cleared. Both releases pass the same 69 assertions per source, with identical output. This proves the extracted C control flow against synthetic callbacks and CPU words only. The semaphore check, status source, entry validity, and MMIO are substitutes; the whole caller, interrupt scheduling, hardware ordering, and UVM runtime are not exercised. See the [v2 report](../evidence/volta-fault-ring-host-v2-20261028T011911Z.json) and [runner](../tools/run_volta_fault_ring_host_harness.py).

Static comparison of the enclosing `fault_buffer_flush_locked` function shows one notable source difference: R580 asserts `parent_gpu->replayable_faults_supported`; R595 v15 does not. The remainder normalizes identically (`2bccc613de4d63a923f5fb07dbad7bda8c3bde0952ddb466b133c5eb2edab81a` vs. `84bd10cc2b37265fb93c1b8e289928a785f481736c575684eb49f9aae13c6260`). The consequence has not been tested by executing the complete function. Next, test the complete flush body and its PUT-refresh/replay-dispatch branches; retain the assertion difference in the compatibility record rather than assuming it is behaviorally irrelevant.

## GV100 Volta fault-ring index and overflow callbacks (2026-10-28)

The effective GV100 HAL table in both R580.178.04 and the R595 v15 overlay selects `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_read_put`, `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_read_get`, and `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_write_get` directly from the GV100 row. These callbacks use the generated GV100 `_PFB_PRI_MMU` register definitions: PUT pointer bits 19:0 and `GETPTR_CORRUPTED` bit 30; GET pointer bits 19:0, `GETPTR_CORRUPTED` bit 30, and `OVERFLOW` bit 31. The source in both snapshots is byte-identical (`uvm_volta_fault_buffer.c` SHA-256 `d0b8cfcf715246b49741314e0009b78c798719b634269ee44a75ee12f9874e77`); `dev_fb.h` matches SHA-256 `0175a270fa5af9d3a62d7fda2c9e536b58551db6bb1e9c123b84ffeec9a32584`.

The host harness executes the exact three function bodies with instrumented volatile CPU storage. It confirms bounded index extraction, rejection of a corrupt PUT pointer and out-of-range GET/write indices, and the source's two-step overflow recovery command: write the new GET pointer first, then write `GETPTR_CORRUPTED_CLEAR | OVERFLOW_CLEAR`. When confidential-computing mode is on, the exact source returns after the first pointer write. Those observations verify source control flow and register-word composition only; the CPU shims do not model MMIO/W1C semantics, posted writes, GPU fault arrival, ordering barriers, or hardware interrupts. See the [host report](../evidence/volta-fault-ring-host-v1-20261028T0120Z.json) and [runner](../tools/run_volta_fault_ring_host_harness.py).

### Overflow status bit versus the Volta GET debug assertion — 2026-11-03

The NVIDIA-published, MIT-licensed [GV100 register reference](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-doc/blob/9fdf5c4062007929d9f4e6cbad9c9771fe61b880/manuals/volta/gv100/pri_mmu_hub.ref.txt#L186-L207) defines GET pointer bits 19:0, GET overflow at bit 31, and the corresponding PUT status fields. The [fault-buffer manual](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-doc/blob/9fdf5c4062007929d9f4e6cbad9c9771fe61b880/manuals/volta/gv100/dev_mmu_fault.ref.txt#L58-L68) describes overflow as a recoverable buffer condition for replayable faults. In both hash-pinned UVM snapshots, `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_read_get()` checks the entire raw GET register against `max_faults` before extracting its 20-bit PTR field. Therefore, if OVERFLOW is present in the readback, the pinned callback trips a debug assertion before returning the valid pointer. This is a static inference from the field map and exact callback source, not a hardware observation.

The exact `uvm_common.h` hashes in R580.178.04 and the R595 v15 candidate state that `UVM_ASSERT` is enabled only for non-release/Coverity builds and omitted in Release. The local v4 host report (SHA-256 `9e37ef239fdf7ab24b13cfeeb63e743c5aca69c92f804d7aa2e1342e3c8a4d15`) adds an expected debug-assert probe and a separate test-only Release-assert model; its path-bearing contents remain outside the Git snapshot. R580 and R595 both trigger the debug shim on GET PTR=3 plus OVERFLOW; both return PTR=3 when assertions are compiled away. The tracked focused test and current pinned software suite cover this harness, with the prior 244 callback/service checks unchanged and **2/2** Release-mode status probes passing. A separate inspection of the exact v15 R595.99.02 `uvm_volta_fault_buffer.o` Kbuild command found no `-DDEBUG`; rerunning its preprocessing flags yields `UVM_IS_DEBUG()=0`. The compiled object and containing module hashes are `1d72bf4103055fa784896f575e3882dac9561026de3203cf84bc05a16e94a181` and `21c6b16c4fc64d81b4326866ea708b8229735035724fcc8bfb95788b8c442b1b`; the module was not installed or loaded. This confirms the v4 Release-model path matches that compiled R595.99.02 overlay. The sanitized [build-mode receipt](../evidence/r595-volta-assert-build-mode-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `7b7d4632c60a6a1516b6be8c6b69695318e073b8cefce9cbb5ff9f08e603979f`, records the source, header, object, Kbuild command and module pins.

The newer R595.112.07 hybrid UVM module is a separate source tree. Its reproducible module hash is `b467e051c4af84011d512859751c20d0ef042e5c24d3281fbfb248173c18f4f2`; its pinned 446-file tree contains no `uvm_volta_fault_buffer.c`. Thus the Release-mode conclusion above applies to the older R595.99.02 Volta overlay, while the current R595.112.07 build still has no Volta fault-buffer dispatch. The source-tree identity and qualification are in the [R595.112 reproducibility receipt](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-uvm-reproducibility-sanitized-v2-20261102.json). Hardware register behavior remains untested. No production driver source was changed. The path-bearing mode-0600 raw log (SHA-256 `c70b45c9afdfb4e1dff33ca23b51f9472bb08c1c8470c3aaf1462f3e03d31764`) remains in local evidence storage.

## GV100 fault-buffer parser and Pascal VALID callback composition (2026-10-28)

The R580.178.04 `kernel-open` snapshot and the candidate R595 GV100 v15 overlay both pair the Volta C369 replayable/non-replayable parser with Pascal fault-entry validity callbacks. The dispatch differs structurally: R580's GV100 row inherits `entry_is_valid` and `entry_clear_valid` from its GP100 parent, while R595 v15 assigns the same Pascal functions directly in the GV100 row. The effective callback names were resolved through the `fault_buffer_table` parent chain, rather than inferred from declarations. Both tables select `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_parse_replayable_entry`, `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_parse_non_replayable_entry`, and `uvm_hal_volta_fault_buffer_get_fault_type` for GV100.

The Pascal implementation uses B069 field definitions for the common 32-byte buffer entry. In both pinned snapshots, B069 and Volta C369 report the same entry size (`32`) and VALID encoding (`MW((31+7*32):(7*32+31))`). The host harness adds compile-time assertions for the packet sizes and the VALID field's generated word, shift, and width, then compiles the exact selected Pascal `entry_is_valid`/`entry_clear_valid` bodies with the exact Volta parser bodies. The Pascal source hashes are R580 `a4cafceee01aac86cb2a4a0527729f28ec6cbc17ff34ec7150d5a5fa0dd7df52` and R595 v15 `ba3cd6e3bfb4972f6e970b307e7b6343e2b4f265842803a1947993331f306050`; the normalized bodies of both callbacks and the entry accessors are identical across these snapshots.

Host execution covers the normal packet VALID path during 146 synthetic parser vectors per source and two direct CC metadata callback cases. In the latter, `metadata.valid` is authoritative even when the packet's VALID bit disagrees, matching the selected Pascal source branch. The compiler and CPU do not establish GPU packet coherence, fault-buffer DMA ordering, RM interrupt delivery, confidential decryption, or end-to-end UVM behavior. See the [v6 host report](../evidence/volta-c369-fault-decode-host-v6-20261028T0110Z.json) and [runner](../tools/run_volta_fault_decode_host_harness.py).

## UVM character-device discovery and helper identity contract (2026-10-27)

The stock CUDA node-lifecycle comparison records `/dev/nvidia-uvm` opens on the R580 V100 guest and R595 RTX 5080 host, in separate kernels and driver stacks. Pinned R580.178.04 source registers the UVM cdev using `NVIDIA_UVM_DEVICE_NAME`; it does not itself create the userspace node. The earlier private-owner build kept that registration label standard while renaming module, procfs, and log identities. Its [build report](../evidence/r580-uvm-private-owner-stock-chardev-v2-20261027T214000Z.json) records the source hashes and 77 UVM imports matching the private frontend.

NVIDIA's `nvidia-modprobe` upstream head is still commit `4ffadd6b30c4c8ea7d17495c8b26c8ab2b4688b2` (verified with `git ls-remote` on 2026-10-27). The pinned helper checks `/sys/module/nvidia_uvm/initstate` before it calls `modprobe nvidia-uvm`, then checks the same standard-name path again after a successful spawn. `nvidia_get_chardev_major()` scans the character-device section and returns the first line whose registration text ends in `nvidia-uvm`; `nvidia_uvm_mknod()` uses that major for `/dev/nvidia-uvm` and `/dev/nvidia-uvm-tools`. See the [pinned source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/nvidia-modprobe/blob/4ffadd6b30c4c8ea7d17495c8b26c8ab2b4688b2/modprobe-utils/nvidia-modprobe-utils.c#L99-L115) and [lookup/node code](https://github.com/NVIDIA/nvidia-modprobe/blob/4ffadd6b30c4c8ea7d17495c8b26c8ab2b4688b2/modprobe-utils/nvidia-modprobe-utils.c#L657-L682).

The new CPU harness compiles that exact upstream helper fixture and redirects only `/proc/devices`, `/proc/sys/kernel/modprobe`, and `/sys/module` to temporary paths. A stubbed effective UID, PCI match, `posix_spawn`, and `waitpid` make root/non-root branches deterministic; the harness never launches modprobe or calls any node-creation function. Its five tests establish that the helper recognizes `nvidia_uvm`, misses `hybrid580_uvm`, and still fails its final check when the spawn stub returns success but does not create the standard sysfs identity. Duplicate `nvidia-uvm` registration lines resolve to the first major. This is direct execution of pinned helper source with synthetic inputs, not a run of the installed helper or a kernel load.

Consequently, the `alias nvidia-uvm hybrid580_uvm` experiment proves kmod resolution only; it is not enough to integrate the private module with NVIDIA's helper. A new compile-only candidate retains the standard `nvidia_uvm` module, device, and procfs identities while its 77 UVM imports bind solely to `hybrid580_frontend`. It built for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` as `nvidia-uvm.ko` (SHA-256 `fb38c0e48ccc105448c21c7b8fcf5d54c35b4bc02871546c54f70ed5ddc06c8e`), but cannot coexist with the currently loaded R595 UVM owner. Fresh PC3 [snapshot v29](../evidence/pc3-canary-host-state-v29-20261027T220500Z.json) and [preflight](../evidence/pc3-canary-preflight-standard-uvm-v1-20261027T220500Z.json) show six blockers for this candidate: loaded standard UVM, unsigned modules, unverified signing trust, active major-195 nodes, major-195 ownership, and the registered `nvidia-uvm` label at major 511. No module was signed, installed, or loaded; no GPU or VM state changed.

## Private R580 UVM identity and canary blockers (2026-10-27)

The isolated-execution builder was extended with the owner namespace hybrid580-uvm. It makes exact, prevalidated edits to the R580.178.04 UVM Kbuild target and its module, device, procfs, and log identities, then builds only nvidia-uvm against a pinned frontend Module.symvers. The resulting module reports the embedded name hybrid580_uvm, depends only on hybrid580_frontend, and imports 77 UVM interface symbols whose CRCs all match that frontend. The generated UVM consumer Module.symvers is empty (0 bytes); do not interpret the build as a provider-export inventory. The [build report](../evidence/r580-uvm-private-owner-v1-20261027T213009Z.json) pins the source archive, patches, input symvers and edited source hashes, module hash, and full build log.

This proves the private module identity and compile-time consumer ABI contract only. It does not show that two UVM sessions can register concurrently, that libcuda opens /dev/hybrid580-uvm, that callback routing reaches the intended RM backend, or that UVM works on a GPU. PC3's fresh [static preflight](../evidence/pc3-canary-preflight-v23-20261027T213154Z.json) confirms the UVM-name conflict is gone but still blocks the candidate for signature policy/trust and standard character-device ownership: major 195 and minors 0/255 are active under R595.99.02. The RTX 5080 remains host-bound and the V100 is vfio-pci-bound in this snapshot. No module or GPU was exercised by the build/preflight.

The next discriminating source/CPU prototype is the user-space-to-frontend namespace seam: prove how the matched R580 libcuda finds its control endpoint and UVM node while the stock major 195 remains allocated, then model exact open/ioctl routing and teardown. A renamed UVM node without a verified discovery path is not usable CUDA integration. Separately, a private major alone does not resolve libcuda's standard-node expectations or signature enforcement.

## Matched SM70/SM120 kernel phase and event-ioctl shape (2026-10-27)

To compare the stock R580 and R595 paths without conflating different probe artifacts, both runs used the same current probe (167,888 bytes; SHA-256 `c5bfd5740ff757d1d48d78634b85b7a0209eadc806220075c0397755dffad2d2`) and SM70+SM120 fatbin (19,016 bytes; SHA-256 `74cd97aabd47ab1abe720493bf54012a494472d9047b249d049300cda57e1741`). Both used ioctl observer v26 (SHA-256 `4f783c6480757442ba5cf246e176fc6f26a1b7fb62bde1f7128e27d6f39ffe2d`). The tested operation was a single-device, single-process phase: initialize Driver API, enumerate the selected GPU, create/set a context, load `hybrid_arch`, allocate, launch, synchronize, copy the result back, and clean up.

On guest `pc3-v100`, Tesla V100/SM70 at `0000:00:05.0`, stock R580.178.04 exposed Driver API 13000 and `libcuda.so.580.178.04`; the kernel returned **700**, matching its architecture-specific expected value, and cleanup passed. The current [mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-ioctl-kernel-phase-v1-20261027T042839Z.json) has SHA-256 `f03a98a5ee776834c562536d71129789ccf20b8e69c600729c776cf0b36dcbfd`. The selected-GPU compute-process query returned zero at the initial inventory, immediately before launch, after the probe, and at final inventory; memory returned to 0 MiB. The observer recorded 40 successful selected requests in a complete trace: 25 MAP_MEMORY (`0xc038464e`), one UPDATE_DEVICE_MAPPING_INFO (`0xc028465e`), seven ALLOC_OS_EVENT (`0xc01046ce`), and seven FREE_OS_EVENT (`0xc01046cf`). The event operations paired within seven transient task/descriptor groups; the report contains only aggregate counts, not those identifiers. An earlier successful capture preceded the new process guard and is superseded for the current reference.

On PC3 host, RTX 5080/SM120 at `0000:02:00.0`, stock R595.99.02 exposed Driver API 13020 and `libcuda.so.595.99.02`; the kernel returned **1200**, matching its expected value, and cleanup passed. The [mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-ioctl-kernel-phase-v2-20261027T0420Z.json) has SHA-256 `b04ec0f3fbf98c1e58c4faf4d0b5623f93b251e8a469fdb49b34ed2df85ee95d`. It recorded 43 successful, completely snapshotted selected requests: 26 MAP_MEMORY, two UPDATE_DEVICE_MAPPING_INFO, eight ALLOC_OS_EVENT, and seven FREE_OS_EVENT. Seven transient groups balanced; one observed event allocation had no matching free ioctl before the trace ended. A prior successful capture returned the same per-request counts. The repeated imbalance is a bounded observation, not proof of a leak. Static inspection of both public source snapshots found root-client-free and file-private-close cleanup routes, plus refcount-delayed final freeing, described below. The current trace did not observe those cleanup predicates, so it cannot explain the specific unmatched allocation.

### Static cleanup routes in R580 and R595 source

The R580.178.04 snapshot is under `build/experiments/private-open-binapi-object-v1-20261021/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/`; the R595.99.02 snapshot is under `build/research/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/source/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/`. The relevant paths are present in both versions:

| Mechanism | R580.178.04 source anchors | R595.99.02 source anchors |
|---|---|---|
| Event allocation records `hParent`, `nvfp`, and GPU-node `fd`; teardown marks the event inactive and may defer final free while its reference count is nonzero. | `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:372-403,506-528` | `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:464-495,598-636` |
| Successful `NV_ESC_RM_FREE` of the root invokes `rm_client_free_os_events(root)`, which removes events associated with that parent. | `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/escape.c:440-456`; `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:447-452` | `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/escape.c:509-527`; `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:539-544` |
| The device-file close callback calls `rm_cleanup_file_private`; that path disables clients and calls `free_os_events(nvfp, 0)` for remaining events associated with the file-private object. | `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c:2114-2138`; `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:2900-2956,490-491` | `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c:2161-2185`; `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:3018-3074,582-583` |

SHA-256 pins for the inspected files: R580 `osapi.c` `bbb08710a4674408530e40146913b2ea328e2ba921c61f2a05bc31f8593416aa`, `escape.c` `dad7e4a427d6f045d2e11a60837e2f73567321426e24e399cf5b45d0bcf724a7`, `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c` `2de02f2709e1009bb4c34aef33500871a6f762108fcb47c0d70d3da313de5548`; R595 `osapi.c` `e05c1d00b6bc3e8d08ff7b901050dd654051e1ca88ec065acb314c9f977e708a`, `escape.c` `39f28fe5883582f3447d367834c7272ae2a3374d2b97b1e0bc2b110efb7e5fc9`, `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c` `5d31dc828b62e74366e41884d27cdddc01fb44219ecdb3900cab7a3f1758757e`.

These code paths establish that a missing explicit FREE_OS_EVENT ioctl does not by itself prove a leaked event. They do not establish that the unmatched RTX allocation belonged to the root client or the descriptor later closed in the probe.

### v27 redacted last-file-close observation on the RTX 5080

The v27 observer now tracks open-file identities across `open`, `dup`, `dup2`/`dup3`, `fcntl` duplication, `close`, and `close_range`; it decrements an alias reference count and treats a close as final only after the last alias disappears. `close_range` rows retain fd/file-id pairs only in the temporary observation. The runner validates and reduces them in memory; saved evidence contains only aggregate counts. A successful `close()` resets both the identity and descriptor role, so a later range sweep does not mistake a previously closed fd for an untracked selected node. CPU tests exercise a control fd closed before a broad `close_range` from another thread plus two aliases of one GPU file.

For a discriminating PC3 run, the isolated SM120 probe used `--close-fds-on-exit`: after context/resource teardown and destruction of the dynamically loaded Driver API object, it called `close_range(3, UINT_MAX, 0)`. This closes descriptors only in that short-lived process and preserves stdin/stdout/stderr. The strict runner verified the RTX 5080 UUID/PCI, R595.99.02/libcuda identity, zero selected-GPU compute processes before and after, unchanged host snapshot, and remote workspace cleanup. The mode-0600 [report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-event-close-lineage-v3-20261027T2124Z.json), SHA-256 `c85a3f66ca7e121281dfcf3e9c87f102da540f6435b667c36bfe607219b5015c`, records a successful SM120 result of **1200**, 8 event allocations and 7 explicit frees, one successful root-free predicate before any event allocation, and one pending event allocation associated with the final selected GPU-file close. No selected GPU descriptor identity remained open at the end of the trace. The persisted report contains no records, TIDs, fds, or file-id tokens.

This is evidence that the unmatched event allocation remained associated with a GPU file until the last selected file description was closed. It makes the statically identified file-private cleanup route a runtime candidate, but does not observe `rm_cleanup_file_private`, `free_os_events`, event-reference drain, or object destruction inside the driver. `causal_attribution` therefore remains `not-proven`, and the event lifecycle remains partial. The earlier failed forced-close captures are preserved: [v1](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-event-close-v1-20261027T2120Z.json), SHA-256 `486f2234b2052bb3d0fe404967bfdbb73754441545dc81fd9ab20504994661b1`, and [v2](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-event-close-v2-20261027T2122Z.json), SHA-256 `ce03847ce3a233a977dbf4ce7cb77a783627242c4267ba9710a4731fba7ff6c9`. V2 rejected the trace because a successful close left a stale role in the observer's descriptor table; v3 clears that role and the regression confirms the later range sweep is complete.

Interpretation is deliberately narrow. The shared CUDA probe artifact executing the architecture-specific kernel on each stack validates the SM70 and SM120 kernel paths independently and confirms that the fatbin is usable by both stock stacks. The installed drivers, kernels, CUDA contexts, and processes were separate. These experiments do **not** establish a shared CUDA Driver API namespace across both GPUs, same-process cross-generation execution, merged Resource Managers, compatibility of cross-backend handles, UVM/P2P, or hybrid performance. Successful ioctl return and complete shape snapshots do not prove the semantics of the event objects or MAP/UPDATE payloads.

The V100 evidence path also exposed a packaging defect: the remote worker invoked a shape-selection validator that existed only in the local runner. The first [attempt](../evidence/pc3-v100-ioctl-kernel-phase-v1-20261027T042311Z.json), SHA-256 `7649a966853d8a8557be54886026d08639fabe33a7b7652791e7c0748283ad70`, failed during remote input validation before guest inventory or CUDA. The helper is now embedded in the worker, with a regression assertion that compiles and checks the embedded script. The same patch ensures raw TID/FD fields are validated in memory then removed before evidence is persisted, including invalid-observation handling. The V100 runner now also refuses a selected GPU with active compute PIDs and checks the count at four execution boundaries. Runner SHA-256 is `2ee6718e58b279cbb641cc6a317fa594e7b0c7a3c115e68e0db515a702846a47`; its embedded worker SHA-256 for the current run is recorded in the V100 report. Focused V100 runner tests: **98 passed**; full pinned CPU/software suite: **1,450 passed** in 64.726 s. [Suite report](../evidence/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261027T042859Z.json), SHA-256 `3de5c048aff4a9987c428ca88662efbe57caec7682aa584d4da48795408e6b5c`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-openrm-hal-matrix-v1-20261027T042859Z.log`), SHA-256 `c9501cb412e736e1ef9e935d49080a15490f3934f0e72ea3b9e28798abad4f8a`. Suite qualification is software-only (`runtime_or_hardware_validated=false`, `loaded_or_installed=false`).

## PC3 V100 ioctl shapes cross-checked against R580 and R595 source (2026-10-27)

The isolated [PC3 V100 capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-ioctl-shapes-v1-20261027T0351Z.json), SHA-256 `e005351a28cd00eedbd64d05ba1fa8f527f37b25050d2dd4be185a15ffdf5353`, completed on the stock R580.178.04 guest. Its schema-v26 observer reported a complete trace with no errors and 33 successful selected requests: 25 `0xc038464e` (56-byte control-node packets), one `0xc028465e` (40-byte control-node packet), and seven `0xc01046ce` (16-byte GPU-node packets). The CUDA/SM70 reference, observer, remote guest cleanup, and staged-payload cleanup all passed; credentials were neither logged nor persisted. The guest driver/module and PCI binding remained unchanged. This is one ordinary V100 run, not a hybrid-driver test.

A follow-up [event-lifecycle capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-ioctl-event-lifecycle-v1-20261027T0400Z.json), SHA-256 `9b22d55142c3a52242fa6a7e637c6ff6a6991d3a3c6abbd4c910578640ce73e2`, used observer SHA-256 `4f783c6480757442ba5cf246e176fc6f26a1b7fb62bde1f7128e27d6f39ffe2d`. It recorded 40 successful requests in a complete, error-free trace: 25 map-memory, one update-mapping, seven OS-event allocations, and seven OS-event frees. Every allocation and free was a 16-byte request on the GPU-role descriptor and returned success; the seven allocations and frees matched as a multiset by thread and descriptor. No event-packet byte offsets changed in the observer's entry/exit snapshots. It retained no payload bytes, handles, or pointer values, but its report included transient descriptor and task identifiers; the latest V100 runner now strips those before writing new evidence. Guest module hash was unchanged before/after (`fab88676e569054a612a7cf450f8d780cb657709164ab97056d9bc2495d7a305`); probe and observer hashes also matched before/after, staged-payload cleanup passed, and credentials were neither logged nor persisted. This validates one stock R580 V100 event-IOCTL lifecycle observation only; it does not validate the frontend, a second RM, or hybrid CUDA.

The new [ABI layout test](../tests/test_ioctl_shape_abi_layout.py) pins the public R580.178.04 source files to their known hashes and the public R595.99.02 source archive to SHA-256 `b8891cb617683e28ef1e4c3add8dbc30e648b8cd6429a3d521f20dde44633262`; selected R595 files must also match the archive bytes. Its C fixture compiles against each SDK and asserts the encoded ioctl numbers, sizes, and field offsets. Both trees produce these layouts:

| Observed request | Public source operation | Fixed 64-bit x86 layout | Dispatch behavior in both snapshots |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0xc038464e` | `NV_ESC_RM_MAP_MEMORY` | `NVOS33_PARAMETERS` is 48 bytes; the wrapper adds `fd` at offset 48 for a 56-byte packet. Fields: handles at 0/4/8, offset at 16, length at 24, returned address at 32, status at 40, flags at 44. | Control-node-only, exact-size check, `Nv04MapMemoryWithSecInfo`, then mmap-context creation on the supplied GPU fd; failed context creation triggers an RM unmap rollback. |
| `0xc028465e` | `NV_ESC_RM_UPDATE_DEVICE_MAPPING_INFO` | `NVOS56_PARAMETERS` is 40 bytes: handles at 0/4/8, old/new CPU addresses at 16/24, status at 32, then four bytes of tail padding. It has **no size field**. | Control-node-only, exact-size check, converts the two `NvP64` fields and calls `rm_update_device_mapping_info`. |
| `0xc01046ce` | `NV_ESC_ALLOC_OS_EVENT` (`NV_IOCTL_BASE + 6`, where the base is 200) | Four 32-bit fields at offsets 0/4/8/12 (`hClient`, `hDevice`, `fd`, `Status`), total 16 bytes. | OS-layer event-descriptor allocation using the selected open file and supplied fd; it is not a GPU-memory allocation. The R580 source comment explicitly allows this request on `/dev/nvidia0`. |
| `0xc01046cf` | `NV_ESC_FREE_OS_EVENT` (`NV_IOCTL_BASE + 7`) | Four 32-bit fields at offsets 0/4/8/12 (`hClient`, `hDevice`, `fd`, `Status`), total 16 bytes. | OS-layer event-descriptor release using the selected open file and supplied fd; it is not a GPU-memory free. |

The SDK compile assertions passed against exact R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 snapshots. The semantic regression test also pins and checks the source dispatch paths: map and update remain control-node operations, while event allocation and free are handled in the OS layer. R595 adds a central `RmValidateIoctl` size table for these operations; R580 validates size in the corresponding cases. The event helper changes its `active` assignment from a plain Boolean store in R580 to `portAtomicSetS32` in R595. These implementation differences do not change the packet layouts and do not establish cross-branch RM compatibility.

On this V100 sample the observer retained only nonzero/changed byte positions, never payload bytes, handles, addresses, or pointer values. The changed map-packet positions fall in the returned-address and flags fields; that is consistent with the public source flow, but it does not disclose the returned address or prove its meaning. The single update-mapping packet and all seven event packets had no changed positions; that observation does not mean their handlers are no-ops. The capture tracks syscall boundaries per task, so another thread could still mutate shared memory between stops.

The stock guest runs NVIDIA's proprietary R580 module, whereas the R580 source archive is the public Open module source. The source mapping therefore identifies the published ABI and matching operation definitions; it is not proof that every proprietary implementation detail is identical. Nor does equal R580/R595 public packet layout prove that a frontend can safely forward every request: object ownership, device-node registration, and branch-local RM state still matter. The existing [`hybrid_frontend` CPU model](../tests/test_hybrid_frontend.py) checks that both `NV_ESC_ALLOC_OS_EVENT` and `NV_ESC_FREE_OS_EVENT` on a registered standard GPU node go only to that GPU's selected backend and are rejected on the control node. That is model coverage, not execution through a loaded frontend or either RM provider.

## Stock V100 CUDA VMM shareable handles: POSIX FD succeeds, FABRIC blocked (2026-10-27)

The pinned [probe](../src/system_features_vmm_fabric_probe.py), SHA-256 `b7a903bf0dcad5f925ddc04fa1108b22b980a2a023c2998678ceb30e38be58a3`, creates a device VMM allocation with one requested shareable-handle type, exports it, passes the POSIX descriptor only to a fresh child (`pass_fds`), then checks a 16-word read/write exchange after import, address reservation, mapping, and access setup. It records API stages and numeric data, and closes/unmaps/releases resources on both sides. The V100 runner injects and validates the pinned guest identity; a separate strict SSH runner injects the exact RTX 5080 host identity and R595 library path.

For `CU_MEM_HANDLE_TYPE_POSIX_FILE_DESCRIPTOR` (handle type 1), the Driver API support attribute 103 was 1 and the two-process numeric exchange passed for all V100 process-local feature masks `0x9`, `0x8`, `0x1`, and `0x0`. The latest [mode-0600 V100 report v5](../evidence/pc3-v100-cuda-vmm-posix-sensitivity-v5-20261027T0328Z.json), SHA-256 `5421ee113d81b30867b5a3df68aefc37989a29cab346da66edbff84d089a2360`, records equal capability and transfer outcomes. This corroborates that this POSIX VMM path on stock R580 V100 does not depend on the tested feature-bit mask.

The same source passed independently on PC3's host RTX 5080 under R595.99.02 / Driver API 13020: the RTX exporter and a fresh importer process verified initial values, writeback, FD closure, and CUDA cleanup. The [mode-0600 host report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-vmm-posix-reference-v1-20261027T032639Z.json), SHA-256 `2a100a1ae39234ac95e17cb77abd4a3aac368c545caa4fad8edbeaf21b2736ce`, records the exact UUID/PCI/library/module identities and unchanged post-state; [`run_pc3_rtx5080_vmm_posix_reference.py`](../tools/run_pc3_rtx5080_vmm_posix_reference.py), SHA-256 `49fbedbc6375a155af575f2ed6d9117ff31d926231e4b1b3bbb6d7be9aef79d5`, uses strict host-key checking and a private ephemeral workspace. The successful runs are still separate: the RTX is in PC3's host OS and the V100 is in the guest OS. POSIX handles cannot cross that OS boundary, and these observations do not establish a cross-RM handle import or hybrid process.

For `CU_MEM_HANDLE_TYPE_FABRIC` (handle type 8), the capability result and actual creation result diverge: attribute 128 is set for masks `0x9`/`0x8`, yet `cuMemCreate` returns code 800 (`CUDA_ERROR_NOT_PERMITTED`); when attribute 128 is masked off (`0x1`/`0x0`), creation returns code 1 (`CUDA_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE`). The latest [mode-0600 report v5](../evidence/pc3-v100-cuda-vmm-fabric-sensitivity-v5-20261027T0328Z.json), SHA-256 `3d47ac2a9c4667d1b187f4904e3a9a52fccfebe6e521a92fe4a011976543b098`, shows a registered IMEX major but no channel directory/nodes. NVIDIA's [CUDA VMM guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/virtual-memory-management.html) requires administrator-enabled IMEX channels for FABRIC handles, and the [Driver API reference](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-driver-api/group__CUDA__VA.html) specifies matching IMEX channel access for import. Do not infer that enabling IMEX alone is sufficient for cross-VM sharing; it was not configured or tested.

The experiments distinguish the supported mechanisms: POSIX-FD validates within each OS, while FABRIC requires IMEX. PC3 runs the RTX 5080 on its host and the V100 in a separate guest OS, so neither POSIX test bridges the current boundary and the FABRIC attempt fails at allocation. No userland feature mask can replace the missing kernel/device/authorization prerequisites. The remaining blocker is same-Linux dual-backend access; solving it requires a common-OS topology or a supported, configured FABRIC path, neither of which was changed in this read-only increment.

The early V100 POSIX attempt at [`v2`](../evidence/pc3-v100-cuda-vmm-posix-sensitivity-v2-20261027T031407Z.json), SHA-256 `56bf9b9ac8eee0b381e14c8aa59b21dbf2a8d50ec7b15a22d4308aeadaa17ab7`, exposed an uninitialized `handle_data` path before importer launch; typed share-kind validation and regression tests fixed it. A later V100 rerun at [`v4`](../evidence/pc3-v100-cuda-vmm-posix-sensitivity-v4-20261027T0328Z.json), SHA-256 `36658739d7e555ec55247bb049d417e5c787bdc70c4665eec9c43db7e469eaa8`, stopped before any CUDA call because the guest template omitted the pinned expected-PCI value; the generator now injects it and its contract test asserts the generated value. A first RTX wrapper attempt retained a successful remote exchange but the local validator used the wrong phase spelling; the final report above is the validated rerun. All failed reports remain preserved and are excluded from the hardware conclusion.

## Stock V100 two-process CUDA memory IPC (2026-10-27)

The guarded [IPC probe](../src/system_features_ipc_memory_probe.py), SHA-256 `c843bd6b9118e1c3392ba14351b9844c18ad5d8633a13458795637c2d689ecbd`, uses a normal Driver API device allocation, exports its `CUipcMemHandle`, and sends the 64-byte handle through a pipe to a distinct child process. The child opens the handle in its own V100 context, checks 16 initial 32-bit values, writes a deterministic transformed pattern, synchronizes, and closes its mapping. The parent then synchronizes and checks the shared allocation's writeback before freeing it. This follows NVIDIA's documented legacy IPC model: process-local device pointers cannot be used directly in another process, so the handle must be transferred and opened there; this IPC path is restricted to devices with unified addressing and does not support managed allocations. See the [CUDA Driver API memory reference](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-driver-api/cuda_driver_api/group__CUDA__MEM.html) and [CUDA Programming Guide IPC overview](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/13.2.2/cuda-programming-guide/04-special-topics/inter-process-communication.html).

On the exact PC3 V100 guest with stock R580.178.04/libcuda (Driver API 13000), stock and preload-pass-through controls matched. Both importer readback and exporter writeback succeeded under process-local masks `0x9`, `0x8`, `0x1`, and `0x0`; each process trace retained the kernel answer `0x9` and the exact exposed mask. All pre/post process, module, GPU, and temporary-payload checks passed. See the [mode-0600 hardware report](../evidence/pc3-v100-cuda-ipc-sensitivity-v1-20261027T024658Z.json), SHA-256 `5ea694373dc4bc256163dceb34d6b2cf4579d504736cd06fdc0d6bc5182b0a49`.

This establishes only that this tested same-GPU legacy `cuIpcGetMemHandle`/`cuIpcOpenMemHandle` path works on stock Volta even when the process-local feature result has bit 3 cleared. It does not identify the meaning of the bit-3 readers: same-GPU legacy IPC is not a test of CUDA VMM POSIX/FABRIC handles, UUID-based cross-GPU sharing, IMEX authorization, peer access, UVM, or a mixed V100+Blackwell process. The four branch targets remain statically observed readers; their owning routines and runtime reachability during other APIs are unresolved. No cross-backend conclusion follows.

The initial host wrapper report at [`024616Z`](../evidence/pc3-v100-cuda-ipc-sensitivity-v1-20261027T024616Z.json) is retained as failed because validation succeeded remotely but final host-side report assembly referenced an absent compatibility key. The runner contract was fixed and the complete guarded experiment was repeated into the report linked above.

## Direct readers of the bit-3 output address (2026-10-27)

The R580.178.04 `libcuda` was inspected in place on the pinned PC3 V100 guest; the library was not copied. The read-only collector verified its 96,284,520-byte size and SHA-256 `dcdf334aeb177c95b3385b797c9b871d03a287895713c6d107f79096b0985aa9`, then streamed GNU `objdump` output through a bounded parser. [Mode-0600 report v3](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-rip-datarefs-v3-20261027T023805Z.json), SHA-256 `f70716ad63bb18bfb8ba2c15a1076af667c6b95db758a55216873878bd39562c`; [collector](../tools/pc3_v100_libcuda_rip_references.py), SHA-256 `2b69f6053923311c7c1767e3c4ba52672ee67af45309db257e9b2f89fd6a0a41`; focused CPU tests **11/11**.

Address mapping is explicit: ELF load base `0`, target ELF VMA/RVA `0x5bdc70c`, Ghidra analysis image base `0x100000`, corresponding Ghidra address `0x5cdc70c`. The complete linear sweep found four decoded RIP-relative operands targeting that VMA: three byte comparisons against zero at RVAs `0x26a342`, `0x26afc2`, and `0x26b01b`, plus the address-materializing `lea` at `0x49d4b5` already correlated with the `cuInit` path. The retained linear context shows the compares branch to `0x26b07b`, `0x26a770`, and `0x26a641`; in the latter two windows, the nonzero fallthrough reaches a call at `0x261520`. All three references share the nearest retained symbol-anchor address `0x263450`, but symbol names were intentionally discarded and that anchor is not a proven containing-function boundary.

Combined with the earlier bounded observation that the helper conditionally writes byte value `1` through the pointer passed at `0x49d4b5` when the successful `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES` result has bit 3 set, the new scan establishes several direct zero/nonzero reads of the same addressed byte. It does not prove runtime execution at those three compare sites, prove the helper clears the byte when bit 3 is absent, identify the callback target, or attach the byte to a source-level CUDA API. The sweep is not a CFG; it covers decoded direct RIP-relative operands and cannot rule out GOT-mediated, indirect, or dynamically formed references. The documented bit-3 description motivates an isolated CUDA IPC test, but does not itself prove that these readers implement IPC or that memory sharing works.

The scan checked the separate PC3 R595 RTX 5080 host and V100/R580 guest, ran no CUDA workload, and changed no driver, module, VM, PCI, firmware, or boot state. Full pinned suite: **1,387 passed**, zero failures/errors/skips in **61.017 s**; [report](../evidence/software-verification-rip-references-v1-20261027T023805Z.json), SHA-256 `3fda846b3a12b738de3de13feaa9bab20707d7fd28d46d2e2e64542004273a1d`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-rip-references-v1-20261027T023805Z.log`), SHA-256 `f43524fed6a1fd18842e11c4e386ccf49c8e4af3a40cfdf2fae13582b1b825ba`.

## Follow-up: feature payload into the RM_CONTROL frame (2026-10-27)

The read-only collector now combines the pinned caller scan with bounded blocks around the dispatch continuation and general command target. It retains only instruction addresses, mnemonics, and operands from the exact R580.178.04 `libcuda`; no library copy, raw instruction bytes, symbol annotations, or pseudocode are stored. [Mode-0600 report v23](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v23-20261027T022029Z.json), SHA-256 `c974f7401b569671eb0b0e81295354c6e51765c8fdd2bc70e70f55bc680ab0e5`; collector SHA-256 `a299206265f353336797f7e0ca067c0d84861023125fca2bb93bb3a2bdaeb259`; focused test SHA-256 `148d14c6a4af03f3c7787c2cca2b8df292909222be0d9571d73b9a3121f935e9` (**17/17**).

For the observed parent call, the static call-site values are command `0x1f0`, size `4`, and a zeroed four-byte local at `[rbp-0x14]`. The callee copies `rcx` to `r12` and `edx` to `ebx`. The command-specific compare/branch sequence leads through the target at `0x496861` to the frame path; the bounded collector also accounts for the alternate `r12` and `r10` destinations which those observed branches bypass. The frame stores command at base `+0x8`, the parent payload pointer at `+0x10`, size at `+0x18`, and sets the status pointer to `+0x1c`. The wrapper call setup forwards the frame base in `r8`, uses request `0xc020462a` with encoded size 32, and passes the status local in `r9`. This matches the pinned R580 `NVOS54_PARAMETERS` layout and the SDK's four-byte `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES.featuresMask` payload.

This is a pinned, path-specific static match with runtime return-address correlation for the ioctl wrapper call during the tested `cuInit` path. The command table contents and the value that selects the generic path remain unknown; the filtered instruction scan is not a complete CFG or formal machine-level reaching-definition proof, and runtime stack correlation does not reveal the arguments. It establishes this observed `libcuda` request path only. It does not establish that bit 3 works as a general memory-sharing capability, identify every consumer, or validate hybrid operation, UVM, P2P, or same-process multi-GPU CUDA.

The full pinned software suite passes **1,376 tests**, zero failures/errors/skips in **63.394 s**: [mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-feature-query-dataflow-v2-20261027T022040Z.json), SHA-256 `17ca8cb1baae937a5f803ec25ae37ea2f0558edb35028fb1ea6a32e62d6add73`; mode-0600 log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-feature-query-dataflow-v2-20261027T022040Z.log`), SHA-256 `9ab76fb432f75d26781fc9442779b453e04e5876aa649e9b2337a29765b3c391`. Software-suite-only qualification; no candidate module loaded or installed.

## Earlier R580 `libcuda` caller and bit-3 flow (v13 baseline, 2026-10-27)

The read-only collector verifies the exact guest `libcuda.so.580.178.04` (96,284,520 bytes, SHA-256 `dcdf334aeb177c95b3385b797c9b871d03a287895713c6d107f79096b0985aa9`), PC3 PCI ownership, the pinned V100 guest identity, and the ELF load base. The earlier v13 capture retained bounded instruction windows and a filtered caller-prefix scan as addresses, mnemonics, and operands only. No library copy, instruction bytes, symbol annotations, or pseudocode were retained. [Mode-0600 report v13](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-instruction-window-v13-20261027T020107Z.json), SHA-256 `5898325f5515c4c6b5fab052f429c1eb47eedc34b9917d78ac8edf3d74168200`.

At the runtime-correlated parent call site, the bounded function-entry prefix has no earlier branch or call before RVA `0x4600f7`. It sets `edx=0x1f0`, `r8d=4`, sets `rcx` to `[rbp-0x14]`, zeros that dword, and calls RVA `0x494f60`; the return is at `0x4600fc`. This is a branch-free call-site observation, not a recovered source-level prototype. The pinned R580 SDK header defines command `0x1f0` as `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES` with a four-byte `featuresMask` output. The called helper's result is tested at return: a nonzero result skips the feature-bit test; a zero result tests `BYTE PTR [rbp-0x14] & 0x8`; when set, the caller writes `1` to its output byte, and when clear it skips that write. This statically establishes that this caller consumes bit 3 of this successful feature-query result. It does not prove that the advertised capability works or that all consumers treat the bit the same way.

At the earlier v13 stage, lexical `r12`/`r10` references had not closed the payload route.
