The v23 follow-up above now matches the command-specific branch path, bypassed alternate register definitions, and `NVOS54.params` frame store, while retaining the explicit limitations on the sparse CFG, callback table, and runtime argument visibility. Ghidra `FUN_*` labels are generated labels, not NVIDIA source symbols.

## Runtime mask sensitivity through one SM70 context and kernel (2026-10-27)

The process-local `LD_PRELOAD` probe now has a separate `sm70-kernel` workload. Its checked-in Python Driver API probe is bound to SHA-256 `d90f6cce9f46031d4f1c42c23cb6e21c57689633b1a88bc63edfd220d066b596`; it embeds the cubin built from the current `kernels/hybrid_probe.cu` source, SHA-256 `8e175174646caaabd3590d13f6bc0d3269708a9c07246c409e62e5d7c967ad50`. It calls `cuInit`, enumerates and verifies the pinned PCI BDF, creates a context, loads `hybrid_arch`, allocates four output bytes, launches, synchronizes, copies the result back, and checks `700`; cleanup statuses are recorded separately. A CPU fake-libcuda fixture exercises successful results and kernel-failure cleanup without hardware.

On the pinned PC3 V100 guest (R580.178.04, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, API 13000), stock and preload-pass-through controls passed. Separate processes under exposed masks `0x9`, `0x8`, `0x1`, and `0x0` each passed the same single-context/single-kernel path and returned `700`. The successful root ioctl returned kernel mask `0x9` in every run, while the process observed its requested mask. The CUDA phase output and tested device attributes remained equal across these runs. Pre/post checks found no unrelated compute process, module/GPU identity change, reset, or temporary payload. [Mode-0600 hardware report](../evidence/pc3-v100-system-features-kernel-sensitivity-v1-20261027T012326Z.json), SHA-256 `747f771a93497421c8d8904e500de34b8a60f6e5cb48b85e032954bfdd6868bd`.

This narrows the mask's significance for this one V100 `cuInit`→context→small architecture-kernel path; it does not establish semantics for other `libcuda` entry points, managed memory, UVM, P2P, multiple contexts, or hybrid-driver operation. It does not infer that either advertised feature bit is globally optional.

The separately captured runtime call chain resolves to Ghidra-analyzed functions through `cuInit`. Five of seven adjacent static-call edges match; the remaining edges are explicitly unresolved, including one nearby computed reference whose target differs from the runtime predecessor. The reports retain only bounded addresses, function ranges, instruction counts and references, not pseudocode: multi-target Ghidra metadata (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-ghidra-call-chain-multi-v1-20261027T010757238216Z.json`), SHA-256 `39a70bd6526a2d9790c4db91a4bfc5249b63c6ab43dae301f2031c8d50f2ba37`; [runtime/static correlation](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-call-chain-correlation-v2-20261027T011514Z.json), SHA-256 `3e1d39b64cc64742ec3fcafac57fbf7fe7f6efa915d6f3a2d6ef2fb0ed4990c8`. These generated `FUN_*` labels are not recovered NVIDIA source symbols, and the stack does not prove mask dataflow.

## R580 `libcuda` SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES callsite and caller correlation (2026-10-27)

The process-local V100 trace resolves each successful root `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES` ioctl to `libcuda.so.580.178.04` return RVA `0x48bc88`; the retained disassembly places the five-byte `ioctl@plt` call at `0x48bc83`. `readelf -Ws` shows that the nearest exported symbol before the return address, `cuVDPAUCtxCreate` at `0x3a56e0`, is only 191 bytes long. Its displayed `+0xe6578` offset was an `objdump` nearest-symbol annotation, not a valid containing-function attribution; the library has no `.symtab`.

A temporary copy of the exact 96,284,520-byte guest library was SHA-256 verified as `dcdf334aeb177c95b3385b797c9b871d03a287895713c6d107f79096b0985aa9`, analyzed with Ghidra 12.1.4, then deleted with its temporary project. Ghidra identifies the callsite inside auto-named `FUN_0058bc40`, whose RVA body is `0x48bc40, 0x48bd43)` and contains 82 instructions; decompilation completed. The mode-0600 [metadata report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-ghidra-function-v1-20261027T004011711527Z.json`) has SHA-256 `16fda0d6366b36cea2e11231aba71940ddffad47924f3734bea63374862c40aa`. The analyzer deliberately discards decompiler output. `FUN_0058bc40` is a Ghidra-generated label, not a recovered NVIDIA source identifier.

The follow-up Ghidra metadata report adds call references for the same bounded function. Its incoming references are capped at 64 records and marked truncated. One record points from instruction RVA `0x495ac7` in auto-named `FUN_00594f60` (entry RVA `0x494f60`) into `FUN_0058bc40`; the call-reference list is a sample, not an exhaustive caller set. The v2 mode-0600 report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-ghidra-function-v2-20261027T005038430029Z.json`) has SHA-256 `1b450727a9ee288223fb8cdeab8e1f50c574bc2f0f8fd059ca97b4ef212186b9`.

An opt-in process-local trace then recorded the immediate caller return RVA `0x495acc` on all four mask runs. It matches the static source RVA plus five bytes, and the remaining same-image return offsets were identical across masks: `0x4600fc`, `0x49d4c2`, `0x2ba6e9`, `0x2d2340`, `0x331c32`, and `0x3279b0`. The independently validated [correlation report](../evidence/pc3-v100-libcuda-call-chain-correlation-v1-20261027T010255574183Z.json), SHA-256 `6cf0af46c76b8f0cddef6c1f5c8efc6ad4d4d42aae6857602fe08c36eca8dae3`, records this result without retaining code or pointers. `FUN_00594f60` is a Ghidra-generated label, not a recovered NVIDIA symbol.

This establishes a runtime/static caller-address correlation during the tested CUDA initialization/enumeration path. It does not establish the caller's semantic role, whether or where the returned mask is consumed, behavior in other Driver API paths, or hybrid-driver behavior. The separate single-context/single-kernel sensitivity result is recorded above and has the same narrow scope.

## Fabric Manager readiness control on the stock V100 path (2026-10-24)

The read-only v24 observer captured the stock R580 path on PC3's V100 guest (`Tesla V100-SXM2-16GB`, `0000:00:05.0`, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, driver `580.178.04`, CUDA Driver API `13000`). The isolated `enumerate` phase passed `cuInit` and device enumeration. It recorded exactly one root-control `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_SYSTEM_GET_FABRIC_STATUS` (`0x0136`) request: flags `0`, `paramsSize=4`, ioctl return `0`, RM status `0`, and `fabricStatus=1` at both entry and exit. The observer read the four-byte payload exactly twice and emitted no raw pointer or handle. The mode-0600 [hardware report](../evidence/pc3-v100-fabric-status-v24-20261024.json) has SHA-256 `d66c04bf125b2b2ad3a7b859af6228f66e8b9ec3ac45b7f0f6a37f6104434bf1`.

The pinned R580 SDK header (SHA-256 `a0026d3c25deaf9d3be726d1b3487a30c2db0e751da6962ef72c42b00ae49295`) defines `SKIP=1`, `UNINITIALIZED=2`, `IN_PROGRESS=3`, and `INITIALIZED=4`; its command comment says RM returns `SKIP` where Fabric Manager is not used. Thus the observed exit enum is `SKIP`. Entry and exit were both `1`, so this is an observed successful output value, not evidence that the field changed during the call. It does not report NVLink topology, P2P capability, or whether a different fabric path is available.

The capture proves only the stock V100 driver's root-global readiness query and successful CUDA initialization/enumeration. It does not exercise the experimental frontend/provider, execute a kernel, or validate a mixed V100/Blackwell process. Driver/module/PCI/VM state remained unchanged. The exact observer build is recorded in [v24 build report](../evidence/rm-ioctl-observer-build-v24-20261024.json), SHA-256 `e1f74dd2644be2f65c7c8c1869e8fd3c728c6782c814af1f4075dd94e34e73df`; repeated builds produced binary SHA-256 `3a3c492f74f6c554d18d28623f2ca11b78efeb873637a90046a630379dbff829`.

## Root-global SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES: source, ABI and stock-hardware evidence (2026-10-24)

The pinned R580 SDK defines `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES` as `0x01f0` with one 4-byte `NvU32 featuresMask`; R595 retains the same ABI. The header labels bit 0 as SLI support, bit 3 as UUID-based memory sharing, and bit 4 as RM test-only code. The public Open R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 implementations of `cliresCtrlCmdSystemGetFeatures_IMPL` have byte-identical 846-byte function bodies (SHA-256 `e4df47226cc7b8f7282227708ab0a9c9bbd8a8b907107a1cfe45626be0895155`). That implementation sets bit 3 only when IMEX is supported and a channel exists, sets bit 4 for the RM test-only property, and does not set bit 0. Its source files are pinned by SHA-256 `bea0ade8836094406ef9f73365c5884c2697878e54e9b7a8b057236847e25787` (R580) and `a682640a0adecbce80216b14a4f915441eebec45110a319806773046dcf0b935` (R595). A complete patched R595 Open module also builds with the private provider export: [R595 build report](../evidence/r595-full-patched-open-build-system-features-v1-20261024.json), SHA-256 `1e29380d141f6d2ff7f84269f20f8c08e5b938173e8087a494bf9578cb4e6839`.

The isolated observer then captured the exact fixed payload during successful CUDA enumeration on both stock references. V100-SXM2 in PC3's guest (Linux `6.8.0-147-generic`, R580.178.04, Driver API 13000) returned `0x9`; the PC3 host RTX 5080 (Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`, R595.99.02, Driver API 13020) returned `0x8`. Both requests had flags 0, `paramsSize=4`, zero entry mask, and successful transport/RM status. The mode-0600 reports are [V100](../evidence/pc3-v100-system-features-v26-20261024.json), SHA-256 `010e4b45f09e4410e5470f7f42b6aa176d2498a2d5b786b6b1031f0600c3287e`, and [RTX 5080](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-system-features-v27-20261024.json), SHA-256 `917e8cdf3f3715b31acb2f17cb8839a8a605ca81ab3aef70712e66cf4ff86608`. These are separate kernels, not a hybrid run. The V100's stock R580.178.04 `nvidia.ko` on-disk and loaded GNU build IDs match; three selected RM function hashes also match the local proprietary R580.178.04 baseline ([identity report](../evidence/pc3-v100-loaded-module-identity-v3-20261022.json)). That ties the observation to the expected installed module build, but does not establish every loaded byte or the independent `libcuda` binary's internal consumer path.

The extra V100 bit 0 is not explained by the public Open implementation, and the header description alone does not show how R580 `libcuda` consumes it. The experimental frontend currently routes this root-global query through one Open provider and deliberately does not merge provider masks. A single provider result has not been validated as semantically correct for both generations. Keep the backend output unchanged; do not OR, suppress, or synthesize bits until a bounded R580 userland consumer analysis and a falsifiable compatibility test establish the rule. This capture proves only the stock root query and CUDA initialization/enumeration on each separate stack; it does not exercise the hybrid frontend, execute kernels, or validate SLI, memory sharing, P2P, or a common CUDA process.

### V100 process-local feature-mask sensitivity

The new test shim changes only a successful root `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES` four-byte answer inside one process and records both the original kernel mask and the value exposed to that process. It was first tested against fake direct and XFER ioctls, including stock/pass-through behavior; the PC3 runner refuses to qualify results unless the stock and shim-pass-through CUDA observations match. Two earlier shim iterations failed that control and remain explicitly classified as incomplete evidence.

In the complete V100 guest run, four fresh Driver API processes received user-space masks `0x9`, `0x8`, `0x1`, and `0x0`; each corresponding kernel answer was `0x9`. The stock and pass-through controls matched exactly. All six processes returned `cuInit=0`, Driver API 13000, one V100 at `0000:00:05.0` / SM70, and the same queried memory attributes `(1,0,0)`. No other GPU compute process was present before or after; the loaded R580.178.04 module path, version, and file hash were unchanged; the temporary payload was removed. See [the mode-0600 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-system-features-sensitivity-v1-20261024T025601Z.json), SHA-256 `2fe44de2f5b408050b9853c745a6c633d36874d273aa66cc4a6086a6ce503e2f`.

This result narrows the observed behavior: these masks do not change the tested `cuInit`, enumeration, or three memory-attribute outputs on this R580 V100 stack. It does not establish that R580 `libcuda` never reads the bit, because this probe did not create contexts, allocations, peer mappings, or exercise other feature-specific API paths. It also supplies no evidence that the hybrid frontend can safely choose or combine backend masks. Keep the value unchanged and extend the consumer trace only where a concrete feature path can be tested.

## OpenRM HAL registration and lookup: seven-release CPU execution (2026-10-23)

[`test_openrm_legacy_hal_lookup.py`](../tests/test_openrm_legacy_hal_lookup.py) now compiles and runs the actual generated enum, `chipID` table, `REGISTER_ALL_HALS` dispatch family, and `halmgrGetHalForGpu_IMPL` from seven hash-pinned public source archives: current R580.196.07 (`21914a…be17084`), R580.178.04 (`060749…391d8`), R595.104.02 (`da4e45…33190`), R595.112.07 (`567d4b…250c4`), R610.57.04 (`619d7b…9be681`), R615.71.10 (`c01605…f40f6c`), and R615.78.08 (`590ee6…e7184f`). R580.196.07 is pinned to NVIDIA's official source archive and public GitHub release commit `02e64bf229e77873c9df22ebcfed6a3e0b813206`. The runner [`run_openrm_hal_lookup_matrix.py`](../tools/run_openrm_hal_lookup_matrix.py) records exact archive tags/commits, report hashes, and a private raw log.

Across all seven CPU harnesses, the real `REGISTER_ALL_HALS` body invokes every generated registration function. The harness replaces only the `registerHalModule_*` implementations with opaque non-NULL tokens, because the actual HAL objects and callbacks are outside this source contract. GV100's generated chip row remains zero and its HAL slot stays NULL; the real manager lookup returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` without changing the caller's output. TU102, GB202, and GB203 controls select their generated enum slots, while a NULL TU102 slot is rejected. Archive traversal and absolute selected-member names are rejected by separate tests.

This is executed-source evidence that the current R580 Open profile, as well as the other examined generated profiles, does not lead from the tested generated lookup to a GV100 HAL. It rules out an ID-table-only repair for these inputs. It does not show that every GV100 machine-code body is absent, establish a complete physical HAL, or prove that no custom restoration can work. R580 here is the public Open profile; the proprietary backend used by the V100 reference remains a different implementation. Qualification remains `executed-source-contract-on-cpu`, not RM/runtime/hardware validation. See the [v2 matrix report](../evidence/openrm-hal-lookup-matrix-v2-20261023T165236Z.json), SHA-256 `8e2fefba1cabc4d216fdeeab33f056a2ee0d711b366550664324b785319044ff`, and raw log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/openrm-hal-lookup-matrix-v2-20261023T165236Z.log`), SHA-256 `3a0f0bb9cd32b2d9373061b09178fb0af63cb9bfac9c5a78ea56952f2904a910`.

## CUDA Unified Memory capability capture and managed-allocation baseline (2026-10-23)
## CUDA Unified Memory capability capture and managed-allocation baseline (2026-10-23)

The CUDA probe now queries and reports all three Driver API attributes that distinguish the Unified Memory paradigms: `CU_DEVICE_ATTRIBUTE_CONCURRENT_MANAGED_ACCESS` (89), `CU_DEVICE_ATTRIBUTE_PAGEABLE_MEMORY_ACCESS` (88), and `CU_DEVICE_ATTRIBUTE_PAGEABLE_MEMORY_ACCESS_USES_HOST_PAGE_TABLES` (100). It rejects non-boolean results, includes them in the per-device report and phase output, and declares probe contract v4. The CPU fake-driver fixture models the observed Volta tuple `(1,0,0)` and RTX 5080 tuple `(1,1,0)`; it does not emulate hardware memory migration.

NVIDIA's [CUDA Programming Guide, Unified Memory paradigms](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-programming-guide/02-basics/understanding-memory.html#unified-memory-paradigms) defines `concurrentManagedAccess=1` as full managed-memory support. With `pageableMemoryAccess=0`, only explicit CUDA managed allocations receive that support. With `(concurrent,pageable,host-page-tables)=(1,1,0)`, all system allocations use software coherence; the third attribute distinguishes hardware coherence when it is 1. These API attributes classify support; they do not by themselves prove migration quality, oversubscription behavior, concurrent CPU/GPU access, or performance.

The same built probe and SM70+SM120 fatbin were run on the two separate PC3 Linux environments in managed-memory mode:

| Device and stock stack | Observed attributes `(concurrent,pageable,host page tables)` | Managed kernel and cleanup | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tesla V100-SXM2-16GB, R580.178.04, CUDA Driver API 13000, SM70 | `(true,false,false)` | Numerical kernels, synchronous and asynchronous paths, managed allocation, and cleanup passed; exit 0. | [V100 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-managed-memory-unified-attributes-v1-20261023.json), SHA-256 `78f46baabb512df14b78d1e7f3f71361efa871163b3a257fa1ebb2cd07a68211`. |
| GeForce RTX 5080, R595.99.02, CUDA Driver API 13020, SM120 | `(true,true,false)` | Numerical kernels, synchronous and asynchronous paths, managed allocation, and cleanup passed. The acceptance command exits 77 because SM70 is absent from that Linux instance. | [RTX 5080 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-managed-memory-unified-attributes-v1-20261023.json), SHA-256 `f29d20135cdc9eb47c72e8cbdd6280cb70f38b425e67f9e45e1a1d8d061ea60b`. |

Both reports declare contract v4 and probe SHA-256 `41ff0dadd20d5a0546e271bc1f9f6bc49306914ae10d3cab7fb35d01f2ab2aa6`; the two kernels therefore prove each architecture's separate baseline, not a hybrid process. The report JSON files are mode 0600. The RTX exit 77 is the deliberate incomplete-hardware result, not a kernel or managed-allocation failure. No ordinary `malloc`/`mmap` buffer was passed to a kernel in this experiment.

The clean complete software suite passed **1,081 tests**, zero skips, exit 0, with the v4 probe pinned in both `HYBRID_CUDA_PROBE` and `HYBRID_PROBE`. The [mode-0600 report](../evidence/full-suite-unified-memory-attributes-v2-20261023.json) has SHA-256 `84627d77b214b3228c36e294449a81b3cf9eb195e64726a318a21064391eb624`; its mode-0600 log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/full-suite-unified-memory-attributes-v2-20261023.log`) has SHA-256 `d469da6710d1f56d1cc7cbf0030c2b3f2ba5415f3eb182f07b650d189b9cfe61`.

Status: implemented, compiled, full software suite passed, and managed-memory baseline validated separately on both cards. Same-Linux/same-process use, UVM migration/oversubscription, P2P, and performance remain unvalidated. Ordinary system-pageable-memory access is now separately validated on the RTX 5080 and classified unsupported on the V100, as recorded below.

## Ordinary system-pageable memory: separate Volta and Blackwell tests (2026-10-23)

The isolated `system-pageable-memory` phase in [`hybrid_cuda_probe.cpp`](../src/hybrid_cuda_probe.cpp) initializes CUDA and checks one selected PCI device, then requires `PAGEABLE_MEMORY_ACCESS` and Unified Addressing. If either capability is false it emits `status=unsupported`, exit 77, null allocator results, and returns before constructing `ContextGuard`. The negative fake-driver test verifies no context or allocator API is called on that branch. When supported, the phase CPU-first-touches ordinary `malloc` and anonymous `mmap` buffers, passes their raw addresses directly to the kernel, synchronizes before checking the result at sizes 1, 31, 256, 4096, and 65537, and calls no CUDA allocation, registration, prefetch, or copy API.

The stock RTX 5080/R595.99.02 host passed both allocators on SM120: `PAGEABLE_MEMORY_ACCESS=true`, Unified Addressing true, exit 0. The mode-0600 [hardware report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-system-pageable-memory-v1-20261023.json), SHA-256 `9e7c201559a5f6b90b4c3718961e9a0ac207af459e86a85be6721a6b10e4404c`, records five successful sizes for each allocator and verified remote scratch cleanup.

The same probe and fatbin were then run in PC3's separate V100 VM using its stock R580.178.04 stack, Linux `6.8.0-147-generic`, CUDA Driver API 13000, SM70. The driver returned `PAGEABLE_MEMORY_ACCESS=false` and Unified Addressing true; the phase reported the matching reason, `status=unsupported`, exit 77, `allocators={malloc:null,mmap:null}`, and clean teardown. The mode-0600 [V100 report](../evidence/pc3-v100-system-pageable-memory-v1-20261023.json), SHA-256 `e53c34d836f2005c160c36726fd00b7991bac1d2d2a94af223eb9afe1a19c964`, verifies the guest GPU/PCI identity, `libcuda.so.580.178.04`, unchanged driver/module/VM/PCI scope, artifact hashes before and after, and guest cleanup. This is a capability-gated expected result, not a V100 failure.

An independent code audit found Python's `True == 1` behavior could let a malformed size vector pass validation. The local and selected-GPU validators now require exact integer size entries, the remote RTX wrapper checks the full report/device/allocator shape, and unsupported reports must name a capability that is actually false. New negative tests reproduce and reject the malformed boolean-size and inconsistent-unsupported cases. The fresh full suite passed **1,093 tests**, zero failures, exit 0. [Corrected mode-0600 report v2](../evidence/software-verification-v100-pageable-runner-v2-20261023T160829Z.json), SHA-256 `098b10f11afd7999682c19c348366f26c03fe8eb1c392eb395550f7b35e86153`, supersedes a collector report whose summary string was wrong; the underlying mode-0600 test log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-v100-pageable-runner-v1-20261023T160730Z.log`), SHA-256 `999636aa8acbe77b56c472a8d9e49c1eeacb0ba3cd2c27812a7dd20c73d01fa3`, ends with `Ran 1093 tests ... OK`.

These are two single-GPU tests on separate Linux kernels. They do not show concurrent CPU/GPU access, cold-page fault behavior, oversubscription, pageable-memory performance, same-Linux V100+Blackwell CUDA, P2P, or an experimental hybrid driver. The next architecture-critical investigation is R615's generated GPU catalog and GV100 registration/gating path; do not infer runtime support from retained GV100 symbols alone.

## R580/R615 Volta UVM capability map (2026-10-23)

The reproducible [capability-map audit](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_capability_map.py), its [17 regression tests](../tests/test_volta_uvm_capability_map.py), and [machine-readable report v3](../evidence/r580178-vs-r6157110-volta-uvm-capability-map-v3-20261023.json) compare all eleven R580.178.04 Volta capability assignments with representative R580 consumers and curated R615.71.10 evidence. The report is mode 0600, SHA-256 `1a4aae1908bf47ac13db4a57f26d54f6ca53c4179e5e2ce7283ce65950e6f5dd`. Inputs are pinned to R580 archive SHA-256 `060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8` and R615 package SHA-256 `41e16cfbb3ba475c55b6ce5b2595e1a87a76948873a1a83f1b261f6b7ba0bf5b`. The R615 scan covers 222 `.c`/`.h` files; its path/content manifest is `994da262850e82910b5e9a9fa980f96a03fd33142b1ed6ad0fc44109347a2fbf`.

The decisive prerequisite is now explicit: R580 contains the Volta target in each of six UVM HAL tables; R615 contains none. Its `uvm_hal_init_gpu` checks for a missing class and returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS` before installing the corresponding HAL pointer. Thus compiling legacy Volta objects does not make the modern UVM dispatch GV100. Four shared R580/R615 call-site anchors (TLB invalidation, non-replayable fault initialization, VA fault cancellation, and sparse-PTE generation) are identical, but this caller-level match does not prove that the selected HAL implementation or RM behavior is compatible.

| R580 Volta capability | R615 source observation | Static classification |
|---|---|---|
| Targeted and range TLB invalidation | Targeted invalidation and a `max_ranges` fallback remain, but the old capability split is gone. | Reworked HAL/policy; GV100 callback semantics unproven. |
| Large sysmem pages | Page size is selected from the active MMU page tree and chunk size. | Reworked; the page tree/PTE path must be implemented and checked for Volta. |
| Prefetch faults | Runtime state is enabled during replayable-fault setup; architecture HAL controls the mechanism. | Reworked; no GV100 dispatch is present in R615. |
| GPFIFO in video memory | Allocation policy selects VID/SYS and forwards the location to RM without the old feature gate. | No UVM capability gate; GV100 RM acceptance is unknown. |
| Replayable/non-replayable faults | Both initialization paths run without the old per-GPU booleans and depend on RM/HAL data. | Implicit contract; GV100 layouts and dispatch are unproven. |
| VA fault cancellation | Cancellation dispatches through `host_hal->cancel_faults_va`. | HAL operation exists; GV100 implementation is unproven. |
| Scoped atomics | No matching field or legacy system-wide-atomics policy was found in the scanned R615 UVM sources. | No equivalent found in open UVM; RM/user ABI remains outside this evidence. |
| Sparse mappings | Sparse PTE generation dispatches through the MMU HAL. | HAL operation exists; a Turing callback does not establish GV100 encoding. |
| PLC compression policy | UVM validates the enum and forwards the requested compression type to RM; no old per-GPU PLC gate remains. | RM rejection behavior is unknown; preserve the R580 Volta restriction until proved otherwise. |

The R615 scan finds **zero executable-code occurrences** of the eleven old identifiers. It finds one textual occurrence of `non_replayable_faults_supported`, inside a comment in `uvm_user_channel.h`. The scanner masks comments and literals, handles LF/CRLF and GCC horizontal-whitespace line splices, and maps results to physical LF-delimited lines; it does not preprocess macros or conditional compilation. Consumer evidence is explicitly representative, not exhaustive.

The PLC trace follows the R580 guard at `uvm_map_external.c:861`, before overlap removal at line 870. R615's `uvm_map_external_allocation_on_gpu` removes overlaps, then proceeds through UVM mapping to an RM PTE query carrying `compressionType`; an error path tears down the new map. If RM rejects a mode for GV100, the previous mapping may already have been removed. This is a **conditional source-level failure-atomicity risk**, not a proven RM rejection or observed hardware failure; RM behavior on GV100 is unknown. The tool requires exact assignments/anchors and the unchanged source manifest, and marks every row `runtime_or_hardware_validated=false`.

Capability-map v3 has 17 focused tests; the archive-provenance writer has eight tests covering exact regular-file paths/bytes, private output permissions, and symlink rejection. The fresh pinned full suite passed 1,012 tests in 47.177 seconds ([report](../evidence/software-verification-capability-map-v3-20261023T1234Z.json), SHA-256 `79c4691f73186e584e980c732f9cb68eef4134478929f271a9ef1ed84b6245d4`). These are static, CPU, and source/build checks only. This audit does **not** establish R615 UVM initialization of GV100 or proprietary RM support. The next source experiment must restore the missing six-row GV100 class/parent closure and adapt the MMU callback contract without weakening historical page-size behavior; any later load and hardware test still needs a recovered canary.

## R580/R615 UVM ancestry probe v10: compile boundary and callback changes (2026-10-23)

The new [R615.71.10 source-only probe](../tools/probe_volta_uvm_port.py) imports 17 selected UVM objects from the SHA-pinned R580.178.04 archive (archive SHA-256 `060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`) into the NVIDIA R615.71.10 package source (archive SHA-256 `41e16cfbb3ba475c55b6ce5b2595e1a87a76948873a1a83f1b261f6b7ba0bf5b`). The exact v10 overlay passes compilation, `modpost`, and a fresh module link for Linux `7.0.0-38-generic`. [Report](../evidence/r61571-r580178-full-uvm-ancestry-probe-v10-20261023.json), SHA-256 `87d2c291cc975c461714b9f36674f624fb3e4952ef3af02e064c3e0fe866b5ee`; raw build log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r61571-r580178-full-uvm-ancestry-probe-v10-20261023/uvm-port-build.log`), SHA-256 `ed215d7a6bea2e3ffdfee1a22377fd94d63fdd41f3477e16a454ffb44edb72f3`. The produced UVM module is an uninstalled, unloaded compile artifact, not a driver candidate.

The static sources establish three relevant interface breaks. First, R580 architecture initialization writes 11 capability fields that do not exist in the R615 UVM source. Second, R580's MMU lookup callback accepts a big-page-size argument; R615's callback type has no arguments. R580 Volta accepts 64 KiB and explicitly returns `NULL` for 128 KiB, so the compile adapter selects 64 KiB only. Third, the R615 `uvm_mmu_mode_hal_t` uses `unmapped_pte(void)` and `poisoned_pte(uvm_page_tree_t *)`, while R580 Maxwell/Pascal use different callback signatures.

For this compile-only experiment, Maxwell/Pascal architecture-property functions in the scratch are fail-fast stubs. This is bounded by the table implementation: `ops_init_from_table` copies an ancestor operation only when the child slot is null; the GV100 row explicitly supplies `uvm_hal_volta_arch_init_properties`; and `uvm_hal_init_properties` calls only the final selected `arch_hal->init_properties`. The Maxwell MMU workaround redirects only the four table slots (two per 64/128 KiB mode) to R615-signature wrappers that assert; the original R580 page-size-aware function bodies and their internal calls are retained. These facts make the scratch link possible; they do not qualify other GPU IDs, data layouts, page-table contents, or runtime callback order.

Build progression is preserved: v7 stopped at removed Maxwell capability fields; v8 compiled those property shims but found four MMU function-pointer type errors; v9 changed a legacy helper signature and exposed its internal sparse-PTE call; v10 corrected that by preserving the historical functions and changing only the unused-in-GV100 Maxwell table slots. The v10 build has 11,849 warnings (11,713 objtool `MITIGATION_RETHUNK` warnings, 131 missing prototypes, four format warnings, and one pahole mismatch). The warnings are not suppressed or treated as proof of successful runtime behavior.

The UVM dispatch source audit still finds six GV100 rows missing from modern R615; restoring those rows in a scratch does not add GV100 to the modern Resource Manager. No UVM or RM module was loaded, no GPU was operated by this experimental artifact, and CUDA, UVM migration, P2P, memory safety, and long-run stability remain unvalidated. The next useful analysis is a field-by-field mapping of the 11 R580 Volta capability assignments to R615 gates and callbacks, with a test for each mapping before replacing any shim.

## R615.71.10 Volta UVM overlay: compile and dispatch boundary (2026-10-23)

The exact NVIDIA Linux driver source archive `nvidia_driver-linux-x86_64-615.71.10-archive.tar.xz` is pinned by SHA-256 `41e16cfbb3ba475c55b6ce5b2595e1a87a76948873a1a83f1b261f6b7ba0bf5b` in the [provenance record](../evidence/r61571-r580178-volta-overlay-provenance-v1-20261023.json). The reference archive is R580.178.04, SHA-256 `060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`.

### GV100 dispatch rows restored in a pinned source-only probe

The new [`probe_r615_gv100_dispatch.py`](../tools/probe_r615_gv100_dispatch.py) validates the complete archived `kernel-open` subtree (457 regular files; manifest SHA-256 `cda8418daa9d2a512f27235c4f3800abdfb523cdbbc2ed9598e9183a72318a2d`) against the exact R615 archive before copying it into a fresh experiment directory. It restores the six R580 GV100-selected class rows and their callback ancestry in that copy, then runs the same dispatch parser and a Kbuild compile/link. The audit resolves 5–33 callbacks per table, finds all six selector contracts, and the build recompiles all 17 selected Maxwell/Pascal/Volta objects before `MODPOST` and a fresh R615 `nvidia-uvm.ko` link. Module SHA-256: `253678e1aeea65fb167f98adb2ef49566a728ccf4401e2f0e8379b37cf822fc1`; `modinfo` identifies version `615.71.10` and vermagic for `7.0.0-38-generic`.

This is the first compile/link artifact in this sequence whose UVM dispatch tables contain the six static GV100 rows. It does **not** establish that R615 RM returns those class IDs, that imported callback behavior is correct, or that the compile-only adapters are safe at runtime. The no-argument R615 MMU selector still needs a behavior-preserving page-size design and targeted mapping/migration validation. Build warnings remain: **11,849** (11,713 objtool warnings, 131 missing prototypes, four format warnings, one pahole-version warning). The mode-0600 [probe report](../evidence/r61571-r580178-gv100-dispatch-compile-probe-v2-20261023.json), SHA-256 `1484645721fa5a1fd1bb8b7d22890a1c4535bed62b922678650e3476cc67543f`, and raw build log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/r61571-r580178-gv100-dispatch-compile-probe-v2-20261023/uvm-port-build.log`), SHA-256 `8dcbfe713afff0d7090de5e3aa1a6833766c0bf1e6981ee6c030cc1a7b6c0349`, are source/build evidence only; the module was never installed or loaded.

The full-package, isolated R615 build compiled and linked `uvm_volta.o`, `uvm_volta_ce.o`, `uvm_volta_fault_buffer.o`, `uvm_volta_host.o`, `uvm_volta_mmu.o`, and the compile adapter `uvm_pascal_mmu.o`. The fresh module SHA-256 is `2ea1901d94818ae04d147c57356d0e5e096ef4ec37101b3d9526ba86b008c5df`; the build log is retained in `build/experiments/r61571-r580178-volta-overlay-probe-v2-20261023/`. This is compile/link evidence only. The build had 11,742 warning lines (11,713 objtool warnings in the proprietary RM object, 24 missing-prototype warnings in the imported Volta sources, four format warnings, one pahole-version warning) and zero compiler errors. The module was not installed or loaded.

The [new dispatch audit](../tools/audit_volta_uvm_hal_dispatch.py) reuses the source-table parser and selector checks, labels the modern source explicitly, verifies required-row inheritance, and records source hashes. Its [R580.178.04 versus R615.71.10 report](../evidence/r580178-vs-r6157110-uvm-dispatch-v2-20261023.json), SHA-256 `3158da834675820e192bfbf9177fa085ae8322e2794cf25d4505a5c683db29e5`, finds all six required rows in R580 and none in R615: `VOLTA_DMA_COPY_A`, `VOLTA_CHANNEL_GPFIFO_A`, and GV100 architecture rows for architecture, fault buffer, access-counter buffer, and SEC2. R615's `uvm_hal_init_gpu` selects these tables by exact RM class ID and returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS` for a missing row. The compile adapter therefore does not make Volta dispatchable.

There is also a callback ABI change in the pinned source. R580's `uvm_hal_lookup_mode_hal_t` takes `NvU64 big_page_size`, and `uvm_hal_mmu_mode_volta` consumes that size. R615 changes the callback to `uvm_mmu_mode_hal_t *(*)(void)` and calls it with no arguments from `uvm_mmu.c`; page-mode details are now supplied by the returned HAL. A mechanical signature adaptation cannot establish equivalent page-size selection, PTE layout, or migration behavior. A runtime-capable port must restore the class/parent closure and implement this new contract from the old Volta hardware behavior, then prove it with targeted map/unmap and managed-memory tests.

Status: static source audit and compile/link probe completed; no GV100 UVM runtime, GPU mapping, migration, or hybrid CUDA behavior validated.

### GV100 code retained in the R615 Resource Manager object (static ELF audit)

[`audit_r615_gv100_kernel_object.py`](../tools/audit_r615_gv100_kernel_object.py) hashes the exact R615 archive member before comparing its defined symbols, relocation names, ELF file markers, and printable product strings with a pinned local R580 Open RM build artifact. The mode-0600 [report v3](../evidence/r615-r580-gv100-kernel-object-symbol-audit-v3-20261023.json), SHA-256 `7f2839678752eb253805b1f4b02aea2490bf4f4262fb687d776bb865b3223829`, records the tool/test hashes and the GNU binutils 2.46 versions used. The R615 `nv-kernel.o_binary` is an ELF64 x86-64 relocatable object, 19,962,112 bytes, SHA-256 `7158f51f1f84622f3d04abb465b6db75e87e2e52f7232248c2250702238fd294`; its bytes match the corresponding member of the SHA-pinned R615.71.10 package exactly.

The R615 object defines **83** GV100-suffixed functions; all 83 names also occur in the local R580 Open object. Six additional names occur only in that R580 comparator: `kfifoConstructUsermodeMemdescs_GV100`, `kfifoRingChannelDoorBell_GV100`, `kgmmuServiceMmuFault_GV100` and its `.cold` clone, `memmgrGetMaxContextSize_GV100`, and `memmgrScrubMapDoorbellRegion_GV100`. R615 retains 11 GV100 ELF source-file markers, 157 relocation records naming GV100, generic RM class-database/architecture anchors, both Volta class parent arrays and their two rights-required arrays. The R580 object has 12 GV100 source-file markers and 241 such relocations; it also contains `rmcfg_IsVOLTA_CLASSIC_GPUSorBetter`, which is absent from R615. `isDeviceVoltaPlus` is present in both. Printable `Tesla V100`/`Quadro GV100` strings occur zero times in R615 and 12/1 times in the R580 object.

This evidence changes the working hypothesis: R615 has substantial GV100 RM machine code and Volta resource metadata, so the modern RM backend remains a credible first prototype; the omissions point to missing catalog/gating and selected code paths worth tracing. It does **not** show that R615 registers GV100 in its GPU catalog, calls any retained GV100 function, or initializes a V100. The R580 comparator is a pinned local Open build artifact; the pinned R580 source archive does not contain `nv-kernel.o_binary`, so the comparison is not a source-to-source vendor-binary equivalence claim. The report is static metadata only; no disassembly/decompilation, module load, or hardware test was performed.

## R595 UVM import closure and AccessBits API difference (2026-10-23)

The installed R595.99.02 `nvidia-uvm.ko.zst` reports 79 standard `nvUvmInterface*` imports under `modprobe --dump-modversions`. The pinned R595 header also declares 79 unique standard APIs. R580's header has 76; the additional APIs are `nvUvmInterfaceAccessBitsBufAlloc`, `nvUvmInterfaceAccessBitsBufFree`, and `nvUvmInterfaceAccessBitsDump`. Exact R595 prototypes use `UvmGpuAccessBitsBufferAlloc *` for alloc/free and the same pointer plus `UVM_ACCESS_BITS_DUMP_MODE` by value for dump. The enum values are AGGREGATE=0, DIFF=1, CURRENT=2; pinned R580 has no corresponding types, APIs, or RM ops.

R595 Open `nv_uvm_interface.c` is a trampoline to `rm_gpu_ops_access_bits_buffer_alloc/free/dump`; the underlying RM semantics are not exposed by this source inspection. Its namespaced exports carry CRCs `0x2af38616` for alloc/free and `0x2fb45dfc` for dump. Blackwell UVM sets `access_bits_supported=false` and gates those operations, so an exported shim is needed for module import closure but does not demonstrate a usable hardware feature. The frontend routes by the device's registered provider, preserves the R595 payload and enum for Open, and returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` for legacy R580.

The [static closure audit v2](../evidence/r595-uvm-symbol-closure-v2-20261023.json) matches all 79 stock imports to frontend export CRCs and all 145 private UVM imports to their Open/legacy provider CRCs. Its v1 precursor was invalid because kmod omitted long names held in extended ELF modversion sections; v2 parses those sections. No proprietary reverse engineering was used for these findings. No module was loaded and no GPU/RM behavior was observed.

Date: 2026-10-18. This file distinguishes primary-source facts, local static inspection, and inference. It does not treat pseudocode as original NVIDIA source.

## OpenRM PCI-to-HAL admission audit (2026-10-23)

The fail-closed [source auditor](../tools/audit_rm_gpu_probe_admission.py) traces the PCI table, generic device predicate, RM probe gates, `halmgrGetHalForGpu_IMPL`, and generated GV100 profile from exact archive snapshots. The [v4 report](../evidence/rm-pci-to-hal-admission-v4-20261023.json), SHA-256 `81ebd910c4e8cfdbf33fc96cea444fbe8ad1dcd09c0b28261b9eae5b3e920dc7`, is mode 0600 and covers R580.178.04, R595.112.07, R610.57.04, R615.71.10, and R615.78.08. The companion [generated-profile matrix v7](../evidence/openrm-gv100-generated-release-matrix-v7-20261023.json), SHA-256 `f8c591f63e31ea2ddcff239589c2529f2d697af4770e93dfc43a448323e373fb`, includes these releases plus R595.104.02. It records source archive digests and peeled repository commits. The R610 release tag resolves to `e4a5faa2567f28c8eabe0ebb6422b6d0abcf37eb`; its pinned archive hash is `619d7b5ce1f79c3211afdbf87d02b2174d268b10d005c5b8f994be22299be681`.

Across the five PCI-to-HAL versions, `nv-pci-table.c` is byte-identical (SHA-256 `bcb34b1ccec6310a428fc7019a842d03d4c8f19577f57a30535489f029a19cd3`) and matches NVIDIA VGA/3D devices with wildcard device IDs. The active probe order is `rm_is_supported_pci_device` → `pci_enable_device` → `rm_is_supported_device` → `rm_init_private_state`. The generic filter checks NVIDIA vendor `0x10DE`, display class `0x03`, VGA/3D subclasses `0x00`/`0x02`, device ID at least `0x20`, and the legacy-device predicate. `rm_is_legacy_arch` returns false in these sources; the later RM support check selects a HAL and propagates a no-match `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`. The firmware-capability branch does not turn that failed selection into success, and the HAL manager skips null entries.

All five generated profiles set `RMCFG_CHIP_GV100=0` and `RMCFG_CHIP_VOLTA_CLASSIC_GPUS=0`, have a zero GV100 chip row, and lack a GV100 chip/HAL label and legacy registration symbol. The supported conclusion is that **no GV100 admission path is established in the generated OpenRM profiles examined**; this is not a claim that every GV100 method or machine-code body is absent. The R580 archive is the public Open profile, not the proprietary R580 backend that operates the V100. This static result rules out an ID-table-only fix for Architecture A; it does not validate Architecture C, hybrid CUDA, or hardware behavior.

The auditor's regression suite includes hostile fixtures for calls hidden in comments, a contradictory `NV_TRUE` legacy-architecture path, an unused PCI classifier result, and class-test branches that do not return `NV_FALSE`. Each was observed failing against the earlier parser and passing with the code-only, branch-aware checks. No driver was installed or loaded by this audit.

NVIDIA’s [R570 Open module README](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/570.144/README/kernel_open.html) states that Open and proprietary module flavors are mutually exclusive, proprietary supports Volta and supported generations before Blackwell, Blackwell requires Open, and Open depends on GSP introduced in Turing. NVIDIA’s [current module guide](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/tesla/driver-installation-guide/kernel-modules.html) describes the published modules and branch installation model. The release date/current branch facts are volatile and should be refreshed before any base-source update.

## R580 descriptor lookup callsite: static and bounded runtime evidence (2026-10-22)

The pinned proprietary R580 object `nv-kernel.o_binary` has SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`. GNU objdump 2.46 and the new [hash-pinned callsite auditor](../tools/audit_r580_lookup_callsite.py) verify that `_nv031364rm+0x92` is a five-byte x86-64 `call` whose displacement relocation begins at the next byte and targets `_nv031399rm` using `R_X86_64_PC32` with addend `-4`. The structured report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-lookup-callsite-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `d63aa4bdb70286347af3a3f9710a22daf7f473a72351a14422678443454820bb`, and objdump log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-lookup-callsite-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `ffd8549dff4f99a1fd7aa8ff4752ddee35a3cd7bb9d594c8df3d76c84b4f4167`, are mode 0600. Five tests reject the wrong call offset, opcode, target, and absent relocation. This proves only the instruction and relocation in that exact object.

A separately reviewed, bounded PC3 V100/R580 observer captured one callsite sample paired with one `_nv031399rm` entry and no dropped samples. The value-bearing [canary report](../evidence/pc3-r580-callsite-active-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `431005c103640170fc48fb9c7ffc38a76377a881cf1f7ddc7b339ec45d847b36`, is mode 0600; raw register scalars are intentionally not reproduced here. The reviewer accepted the narrow callsite finding only. It did not establish which caller branch ran, the first argument's type or meaning, a selector's hardware meaning, cache hit/miss, CUDA behavior, or whether the loaded guest module exactly matches the audited object. No second live capture is needed to substantiate the static offset.

The [read-only loaded-module inspector](../tools/inspect_pc3_v100_loaded_module_identity.py) first compared `/sys/module/nvidia/notes/.note.gnu.build-id` with the GNU build-id from the installed `nvidia.ko.zst`. Its [mode-0600 report v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-loaded-module-identity-v2-20261022.json), SHA-256 `96d40d456fd62fe4f025b204c5c1521d85dc1a45e64066902758edf3cb262013`, confirms the same build-id on the PC3 V100 guest running R580.178.04, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`; the decompressed module SHA-256 matches the earlier read-only module inventory. The v2 report did not read back all loaded text/data bytes or establish identity of the separate `nv-kernel.o_binary` input.

Inspector v3 adds a bounds-checked ELF64 parser and compares the exact bytes for three named function symbols in the decompressed on-disk guest module against the pinned `nv-kernel.o_binary` (SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`). The [mode-0600 report v3](../evidence/pc3-v100-loaded-module-identity-v3-20261022.json), SHA-256 `ccd62d50d16c39a77c940abea8e7e64b1fad19789efa709b1b9c30b2f311ca24`, reports exact size and SHA-256 matches for `_nv031364rm` (3506 bytes), `_nv031399rm` (353 bytes), and `_nv049612rm` (120 bytes). This establishes byte identity for those three on-disk function bodies, not for the complete embedded object or the module's live in-memory text. The matching build-id ties the loaded module note to this on-disk module build; it is not a readback of those three loaded functions. The remote query remained read-only: no experimental module, driver, PCI binding, trace event, or VM state changed.

The exact byte match grounds the static lookup-callsite and helper-accessor offsets in the installed V100 module's **on-disk** code for these three functions. It does not retroactively strengthen the earlier canary, which still has independent entry/callsite counters and no stack correlation.

### Same-invocation return-site prototype

The independent source audit checked Linux v6.8 x86-64 Kprobe emulation: the five-byte `E8 rel32` call is emulated by placing its continuation on the task stack before transferring to the callee. The relevant [Kprobes core](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/kernel/kprobes/core.c) and [text-patching implementation](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.8/arch/x86/include/asm/text-patching.h) support that static model; Ubuntu's exact `6.8.0-147.147` source comparison found no change to the relevant call emulation. The residual question was whether a live PC3 guest entry probe could safely read and match that word.

The GPL observer now resolves the `_nv031364rm+0x92` target only while disabled, retains the expected five-byte continuation privately, and unregisters that resolver before arming the workload probes. At `_nv031399rm` entry it uses `copy_from_kernel_nofault` on the saved stack pointer and reports aggregate match/read-failure/unmatched counters only. It does not retain cross-CPU or global pending state and does not expose the address or stack word. The exact-kernel [build-only report](../evidence/pc3-r580-return-site-build-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `25cf107229e39639796b7dea9187b0177eb3061ba0dcb84efb16b8af5e167fbf`, confirms compilation and symbol resolution on the pinned guest kernel; it did not load the module.

The bounded [mode-0600 runtime report](../evidence/pc3-r580-return-site-active-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `284489662ce9dea436fb186574256c0bcb23c08fe340eadfcfc156959a023e49`, recorded one selector entry, one lookup entry, and one exact return-site match during the successful `nvidia-smi -L` window, with zero read failures and zero unmatched samples. The observer was removed immediately; no probe/module remained, scratch was removed, and GPU identity, R580, probe list, and kernel taint were unchanged. This is one same-callsite/callee-entry correlation on the installed guest, not proof that the trigger caused that route, not a decode of private fields, and not evidence of cache hit/miss, CUDA functionality, a hybrid driver, or performance. The 46 focused observer source/runner tests and the 850-test full pinned suite passed; details and hashes are in [validation](VALIDATION.md).

### Join-predecessor count mode

To classify which audited block reached the shared join during the same bounded workload window, the observer now has a separate opt-in mode that enables the five fixed predecessor counters with the return-site check. The [mode-0600 build report](../evidence/pc3-r580-join-return-route-build-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `ca98bf4d240d80435e0b9ff2f89d2861ce32b3a18f31967a852ec0fd2929b9b3`, confirms this GPL module builds and modposts against the exact guest kernel without loading it.

The [mode-0600 active report](../evidence/pc3-r580-join-return-route-active-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `bcfc69b4abcbf390f44e5a9602e40b35c5bb9928da670dfa44c38a6607bb53b8`, records one selector entry, one helper entry, one hidden return-site match, zero read failures/unmatched samples, and exactly one predecessor count at `_nv031364rm+0xaaa`; each other audited predecessor remained at zero. All signals were measured in the same `nvidia-smi -L` window. The separate counters do not carry a thread/stack key, so this establishes **co-window consistency** and does not prove that the `+0xaaa` count and return-site match came from the same invocation. It also does not prove command causality or a private-field/cache interpretation. The probe module and Kprobes were removed, scratch was removed, and stock driver, identity, probe list, and kernel taint passed final checks. The 49 focused tests and 853-test pinned software suite are indexed in [validation](VALIDATION.md).

## R580 lookup branch and callsite join canary (2026-10-22)

The hash-pinned lookup-branch audit v4 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-lookup-branch-canary-v4-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `097752de1bba0ebdd9ef30b8f119d27d46dba4eeb3b9fba9cf0fbe5cfdb8a2ae`, now records all five direct jumps into `_nv031364rm+0x8c` and their immediately preceding, count-only probe sites: `+0x31d`, `+0x9c1`, `+0xa94`, `+0xaaa`, and `+0xdaa`. The pinned disassembly log is mode 0600 and SHA-256 `ffd8549dff4f99a1fd7aa8ff4752ddee35a3cd7bb9d594c8df3d76c84b4f4167`. This corrected an earlier probe list that was displaced by `0x100`; one attempted offset landed inside a call. The candidate JBE path at `+0xa81` remains a static possibility, not an assumption about the observed runtime path.

The corrected GPL observer compiled and modposted for the PC3 guest's exact kernel `6.8.0-147-generic` in [build-only report v4](../evidence/pc3-r580-lookup-join-build-v4-20261022.json), SHA-256 `2778ff1951c90d739a9bb012bc0937f5a3d1aade745864067ad682226e0c1796`; it was not loaded in this step. The subsequent [mode-0600 active report v4](../evidence/pc3-r580-lookup-join-active-v4-20261022.json), SHA-256 `9f3e2b3accba81867a26697555d7ecc6b524cddaed4c8dc76e09f86b33ec843b`, records one selector-entry delta, one lookup-entry delta, and one callsite sample paired with a lookup. The JBE decision at `+0xa81` and its target at `+0xd7e` each had zero hits; the counter at `+0xaaa`, immediately before the `+0xaad` jump to the shared callsite, advanced once. Thus the single observed call window is consistent with that other predecessor route, not the candidate JBE path. The report keeps raw scalar arguments private and records GPU identity, stock driver, taint, probe-list and cleanup checks as unchanged/passed; the observer unloaded, no kprobe remained, and guest scratch was removed.

The active report's classification is bounded to that `nvidia-smi -L` window. It does not prove trigger causality, same-stack-frame pairing, descriptor-cache hit/miss, the meaning of the selector, CUDA execution, or exact byte identity of the loaded embedded RM object. Its parent CLI initially returned exit status 1 despite the report's passing route status because that new status was missing from the success set; the runner now recognizes it, with a regression test. The current [pinned software suite](../evidence/software-verification-r580-lookup-join-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `02d270217c766c56563ac803244e0e00fc7866a1de672e75e32036f53307feac`, passes 831 tests with zero skips. This remains diagnostic evidence against the stock V100 driver, not hybrid-driver or CUDA qualification.

The subsequent static-only join-inputs report v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-lookup-join-inputs-v2-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `de4a89f5e47fc9028ee8b96b6e30c18f6eb4b399ea734bcb060379b81bd92d50`, pins the exact R580 object and maps each of the five predecessor blocks' gates, argument sources, and stack-local arithmetic. The `+0x31f` edge requires `[rbx+0x60] != 0` and `[rbx+0xf78] != 0`, then sources ESI from `+0xf70`; `+0x9c3` requires `[rbx+0xf78] == 0`, then sources ESI from `+0x12c8`; both clear EDI and EDX. The `+0xa97`, `+0xaad`, and `+0xdad` paths pass the `_nv049612rm` return in EDI, zero ESI, and derive EDX from a prior input or saved value. Their additional gates test the same opaque byte and, for two edges, bits 24–29 of the helper result. These are register/dataflow facts only; the private fields and local stack word have no established semantic names. In particular, the active `+0xaaa` predecessor count does not establish same-stack pairing with the callsite sample.

The focused observer/auditor set passes 53 tests and the current pinned software suite passes 835 tests; both are source/CPU/build evidence. No additional hardware observation or CUDA qualification was performed for this mapping.

## PC3 V100 trace-event registration and notrace boundary (2026-10-22)

The direct [PC3 kprobe report v4](../evidence/pc3-v100-elf-kprobe-address-v4-20261022.json), SHA-256 `db7611cbe32731b269db5800ea7d3567e9c5d3f19c3c410c17c8eec9b3975b8f`, was corrected by the [lifecycle record](../evidence/pc3-v100-kprobe-semantics-correction-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `492e4c798f41e3ce4fda9fb1223805ddd120f6f8e0c1801496759c8cd8fe72bc`: the successful `do_sys_openat2` control events were registered while disabled and then removed. Their handlers were never enabled. Two NVIDIA target definitions failed with EINVAL before an event was created. Thus the older aggregate `kprobe_attached=false` was misleading for the successful control registrations, and that experiment transiently changed tracefs. It did not change the stock NVIDIA module, GPU, or PCI binding.

The newer [read-only module report v4](../evidence/pc3-v100-module-ftrace-v4-20261022.json), SHA-256 `3b053042bb5b07fadc022162f34bbca0a5bbf9475cd38a368eadcb810107f5e8`, identifies Ubuntu 24.04.5, `linux-image`/`linux-modules` `6.8.0-147.147`, and R580.178.04. The uncompressed stock module SHA-256 is `dc6abc8c7624c97446c04108b493378d2cabc820adda65d2ef09a8baeb1c4fcd`. The ELF has an allocated `__mcount_loc` section of 6,264 bytes and `.rela__mcount_loc` of 18,792 bytes. Complete objdump headers and instruction bodies were verified for `_nv000794rm`, `_nv000790rm`, `_nv031399rm`, `rm_init_private_state`, and `rm_init_adapter`; all five lack `__fentry__` and `mcount` references and all are absent from `available_filter_functions`. Guest configuration is `CONFIG_DYNAMIC_FTRACE=y`, `CONFIG_FUNCTION_TRACER=y`, `CONFIG_KPROBE_EVENTS_ON_NOTRACE=n`. The query made no tracefs writes, event definitions, module changes, or PCI changes.

The exact Ubuntu source package was compared with upstream. The guest's package index records `linux` version `6.8.0-147.147`; the official [`linux-headers-6.8.0-147` package page](https://packages.ubuntu.com/noble-updates/linux-headers-6.8.0-147) lists the matching source version and the diff MD5. The downloaded [`Ubuntu source diff`](https://archive.ubuntu.com/ubuntu/pool/main/l/linux/linux_6.8.0-147.147.diff.gz) has SHA-256 `370fa76631a470323e92a0b25dca6012287c4ada448c7f2e4954f45a628e936c` and MD5 `841fc1a5c106c6c97a71da01796c40da`; both match the descriptor and the published package page. The descriptor's PGP signature was **not verified** because its key was unavailable in the local keyring. The [patch audit v4](../evidence/ubuntu-6.8.0-147-kprobe-source-audit-v4-20261022.json), SHA-256 `abe07d1097d6f56d308ce7ccef37f7610336a25546862829264736198201f8dc`, parsed all 25 `trace_kprobe.c` hunks in the Ubuntu diff. None overlaps the upstream v6.8 [`notrace guard`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/kernel/trace/trace_kprobe.c) (source lines 433–475) or registration/disabled path (478–513). Ubuntu patches other trace-kprobe behavior, so the exact source comparison is necessary; the relevant guard and registration path are unchanged.

With dynamic ftrace enabled, `KPROBE_EVENTS_ON_NOTRACE` disabled, and no ftrace callsites observed in these module targets, upstream `within_notrace_func()` returning true and causing `-EINVAL` is now the strongest source-backed explanation. This remains an **inference**, not a proven runtime return site: the generic trace error log does not identify the branch taken, and this audit did not install a probe on an NVIDIA function. The reusable [module inspector](../tools/inspect_pc3_v100_module_ftrace.py) and [Ubuntu patch auditor](../tools/audit_ubuntu_kprobe_source_patch.py) are covered by nine focused tests; the full suite passes 753 tests. Do not retry trace-kprobe registration on these targets. The next runtime path, if needed, must separately review its attach/detach and failure-recovery risks before use.

## BPF attachment paths for the PC3 R580 functions (2026-10-22)

The read-only [PC3 capability report v3](../evidence/pc3-v100-bpf-attach-capabilities-v3-20261022.json), SHA-256 `f7dd99e2790d889068551d685d4409d72b8133ee236a1855d21a52c9aececa50`, confirms BPF syscall/events, BTF, dynamic ftrace and fprobe are enabled on Ubuntu 6.8.0-147. `bpftool` and `bpftrace` are installed; clang is absent. The guest has the relevant effective capabilities and readable bpffs/BTF/tracefs. No BPF program was loaded, and no runtime attachment was attempted.

The [hash-pinned Linux 6.8 path auditor](../tools/audit_linux68_bpf_kprobe_path.py), SHA-256 `9a5baf6c4d320a45e0702a50e3b6fc9b75e022777a4b6347ecedbd16f5f8edc1`, applies the selected Ubuntu source changes with zero fuzz to the upstream source snapshots and checks them against the exact guest/module reports. Its [report v3](../evidence/ubuntu-linux68-bpf-kprobe-path-v3-20261022.json), SHA-256 `a83225b0ecec86cfa2e88efa3470e3635953a5e068d049ecbac5aa35c53eeccb`, establishes these two route-specific results:

- The perf-event kprobe route reaches `create_local_trace_kprobe()` and the same `within_notrace_func()` guard used by trace-kprobe registration. It does not bypass the rejection.
- Ubuntu's `kprobe_multi` implementation resolves symbols to ftrace IPs and registers an fprobe through the ftrace filter path. The five examined NVIDIA targets (`_nv000790rm`, `_nv000794rm`, `_nv031399rm`, `rm_init_adapter`, `rm_init_private_state`) have no observed `__fentry__`/`mcount` references and are absent from `available_filter_functions`, so this route cannot attach to these targets on the inspected guest.

The exact source files are upstream [`trace_event_perf.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/kernel/trace/trace_event_perf.c), [`trace_kprobe.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/kernel/trace/trace_kprobe.c), [`bpf_trace.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/kernel/trace/bpf_trace.c), and [`fprobe.c`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/kernel/trace/fprobe.c), compared with the [Ubuntu 6.8.0-147 source diff](https://archive.ubuntu.com/ubuntu/pool/main/l/linux/linux_6.8.0-147.147.diff.gz). The source patch changes the trace-fprobe frontend, not core `fprobe.c`; the audit preserves that distinction. **6/6 auditor tests pass.**

This closes only the two BPF routes audited here. It does not prove the exact errno site from the earlier tracefs attempt or rule out a direct kernel module calling `register_kprobe()`. That route has not been reviewed for symbol resolution, module lifetime, preemption/recursion constraints, detach races, or recovery, and must not be tried on the live guest until those are assessed. The read-only capability and source audit made no module, driver, GPU, PCI, VM or boot change.

## Direct `register_kprobe` runtime canary on PC3 (2026-10-22)

The separately reviewed observer [`pc3_r580_kprobe_observer.c`](../probes/pc3_r580_kprobe_observer/pc3_r580_kprobe_observer.c), SHA-256 `1f76c6bcc73335d2cbe2c8f2ec81b97d1931d022d12f87391e978fb4c4851a4b`, uses direct Linux `register_kprobe` on the exported `rm_init_adapter` symbol. Its pre-handler only increments an atomic counter; it does not inspect registers, dereference a GPU/kernel pointer, allocate, sleep, lock, or log. The module is GPL-only and can be armed only by explicit opt-in.

The [PC3 canary report](../evidence/pc3-r580-kprobe-active-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `a4c5b04e7b623ede73c75240604a53e926b5e3716df04d21bde4f332c869d5da`, provides direct runtime evidence on Ubuntu `6.8.0-147-generic`, V100 guest, and R580.178.04: disabled symbol resolution succeeded first; active insertion succeeded; the counter moved from 0 to 1 during the bounded successful `nvidia-smi -L` trigger; and removal succeeded immediately afterwards. The report confirms no observer module or probe remained, temporary build data was removed, and GPU/driver/PCI identity was unchanged.

This establishes that a direct kernel-module kprobe can observe an entry into `rm_init_adapter` on this guest. It does **not** identify the exact caller, prove a CUDA/initialization outcome, reveal function arguments or state, validate a hybrid backend, or demonstrate a performance effect. The result is one count in a controlled trigger window; background activity cannot be ruled out solely from the counter. The active test runner [`run_pc3_r580_kprobe_observer.py`](../tools/run_pc3_r580_kprobe_observer.py), SHA-256 `4ca94480a786dc298b5b874d62ffd1a5ff073c384a0811d6ec12d4758f92f381`, refuses unknown/busy compute-PID state, records no raw GPU UUID output, and checks cleanup. The previous source/path review and current Linux kprobe implementation remain relevant: [Linux v6.8 kprobes source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/kernel/kprobes.c), [kprobe documentation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/v6.8/Documentation/trace/kprobes.rst).

The next observation must not read through an unverified `nv_state` argument inside the handler. First resolve the target's exact calling convention and identify a nonfaulting scalar-only capture mechanism with proven detach behavior; otherwise keep the runtime result at entry-count level and proceed with static analysis. No driver replacement, persistent hook, PCI rebind, or reboot was performed.

## Proprietary R580 runtime callback fields (2026-10-22)

This bounded analysis uses the pinned proprietary R580.178.04 `nv-kernel.o_binary` ET_REL object (SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`). The call-site report v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-indirect-callback-sites-v2-20261022.json`) records the indirect calls. The corrected field-provenance report v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-callback-field-provenance-v2-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `d019095ffda02c45593c5b84fbdb688b4df6eace954394a8ad2bdbcdb1e8d642`, corrects the PC-relative arithmetic and records candidate tables. The registered-object constructor-chain report v3 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-registered-object-callback-initializer-v3-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `44e68e6385af4b7a546a5afd08a662d91e75ef1ec7048a3b5e0ec2ef8352fca1`, and mode-0600 excerpts (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-registered-object-callback-initializer-v3-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `bdfb769e27c83a0c69e0aa525620af572b58f2dec69ca7bc9576e1f83f5e998b`, extend the object path through the final initialization calls and registry insertion. The first field-provenance v1 report is retained as historical evidence but superseded because it used the wrong effective address for `R_X86_64_PC32` RIP-relative loads.

The object path is concrete at the registry level. `_nv031364rm` obtains an object through `_nv006647rm`, calls `_nv030179rm` on it, and stores the pointer and key into a 16-byte registry record. `_nv006647rm` requests an object of size `0x6e70`; both successful routes pass the same allocated object through `_nv005722rm`, then `_nv003368rm`, and publish it only after success. One route also performs a wrapper/attachment step. `_nv005722rm` initializes the embedded objects, calls `_nv005978rm`, `_nv005980rm`, and `_nv005982rm` on the root base, then tail-calls `_nv005984rm` before returning to `_nv006647rm`. The first two constructors directly assign branch-dependent targets to root offsets `+0xb8`, `+0x140`, and `+0x3c0`; direct operand scans found no writes to those offsets in `_nv005982rm`, `_nv005984rm`, or the later selected initialization helpers. `_nv031424rm` selects a set-bit index and tail-calls `_nv031398rm`; `_nv031398rm` walks the registry records, matches the requested index at record `+8`, and returns the non-null object pointer at record `+0`. In `_nv000797rm`, one loop calls the returned object's `+0x140` slot, and a second loop calls `+0x3c0` on the same enumerator-returned pointer after checking its `+0x6d2f` byte. Separately, `_nv031502rm` calls through its entry object's `+0xb8` slot; this trace has not tied that entry object to this exact registered instance. The exact constructor branch and live targets remain unknown.

The first field-provenance pass also found other candidate layouts in `_nv020xxx` routines. For `R_X86_64_PC32`, the linker writes `S+A-P` at the relocation field and the CPU adds it to next RIP (`P+4`), giving effective address `S+A+4`; therefore addend `+0xc` addresses table slot `+0x10`, while `+0x4` addresses slot `+0x08`. Those candidate tables resolve to `_nv020591rm`, `_nv020588rm`, or `_nv021090rm`. The direct constructor-to-registry trace is more specific for the selected registered object: its field stores instead resolve to `_nv031217rm` or `_nv031218rm` at `+0xb8`; `_nv030294rm` or `_nv030846rm` at `+0x140`; and `_nv030209rm`, `_nv030211rm`, `_nv030937rm`, or `_nv030939rm` at `+0x3c0`, depending on internal branch paths. The constructors read 16-bit values at `+0x10` from two embedded objects; those values are initialized from arguments populated using a descriptor selected or created by `_nv031399rm`. The descriptor values for GV100 and the corresponding live branch are not yet known.

The normalized selector report v1 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-normalized-descriptor-selector-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `998d3317f0289cf0f6a9e0402bd61acec473c6afa80a1b19c25901279a42781a4`, closes the key transformation for this call path. `_nv000790rm` copies its input object's 32-bit `+0x274` field to the record's `+0x12c8` field and passes that record to `_nv031364rm`. That function calls `_nv031399rm` with `EDI=0`, `ESI=record+0x12c8`, and `EDX=0`. `_nv031399rm` derives key words `+0x88=0`, `+0x8c=0`, `+0x94=0`, and `+0x90=(family<<4)|variant`, where `family=(selector>>8)&0xff` and `variant=(selector>>4)&0xf`, except the exact pair `(0x23,0xa)` maps to variant `0x4`. It exact-matches those words or creates a 0x98-byte descriptor whose constructor stores them at those offsets; `_nv031364rm` then forwards the words into `_nv006647rm`. The executable [decoder](../tools/r580_descriptor_key.py) and [five boundary tests](../tests/test_r580_descriptor_key.py) implement and check that arithmetic. This establishes how to decode an observed selector, not its runtime value or selected callback branch. The wrapper field at `+0x274` is identified separately below; this report alone does not establish a GV100-specific branch. The mode-0600 disassembly excerpts (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-normalized-descriptor-selector-v1-20261022.log`) are pinned to the same proprietary object; no runtime behavior is claimed.

The selector field provenance report v1 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-selector-state-field-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `5ea9edfceda6a969e095fa1304049c6cd420d4e83f2603a5c72e08cbdb832353`, matches the proprietary call register flow to 23 identical wrapper `nv_state_t` DWARF layouts (784 bytes) and names `+0x274` as `disp_sw_soc_chip_id`. The pinned source places `nv_state_t` first in the PCI-allocated `nv_linux_state_t`; `NV_KZALLOC` wraps `kzalloc`, and the named source setter reads the device-tree property `nvidia,disp-sw-soc-chip-id`. Thus the initial PCI allocation value is zero by source inference. Earlier opaque RM helpers run before `_nv000790rm`, so the value at the actual callsite remains unknown; it was not observed on a live V100. The field name denotes a display SoC software identifier and does not classify Volta or Blackwell. The [reproducible audit](../tools/audit_r580_selector_state.py) is covered by five tests; its result is static only.

The follow-up callsite store audit (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-selector-callsite-store-audit-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `0b1d1b4b4047a6850416772e3f5dde18608ffecf04e5e13a43f3240bbf21fc66`, inspects the pinned call paths through `rm_is_supported_device`/`_nv054160rm`, `rm_init_private_state`/`_nv000794rm`, and `rm_init_adapter`. No direct store to `nv_state_t+0x274` was observed in the selected bodies. The constructor-miss callback targets for the code-zero case are now statically resolved below; callback targets for a cache hit, aliases, and indirect global effects remain unknown. `_nv054250rm` updates global `.bss` state and is reached after `_nv000790rm`/`_nv000815rm` on the normal path, or directly on the `_nv031472rm` short path. It therefore does not write the selector before its normal same-invocation consumer, but prior activity and indirect effects are not ruled out. The mode-0600 disassembly/source log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-selector-callsite-store-audit-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `7b3b12002cc8ddfb73bea4cd919e4ed4b35e844c79ab893e6f0d63b34582883e`, records the exact binary and source input. The live field value remains unknown.

### R580 `_nv000794rm` constructor callback closure (2026-10-22)

The hash-pinned audit report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-constructor-callback-closure-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `932ea5eb754fe73c567d3b535600c9e7229d8e08dfbf89016df34bf87b09b10a`, and mode-0600 disassembly log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-constructor-callback-closure-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `f76062a6d3f1d79d2e6e059760af3a8b08ab77f739d0ac561df3c9224d1cabd2`, resolve the `R580.178.04` `_nv000794rm` constructor-miss path. `_nv000794rm` reads `nv_state+0x274` only when `flags & 0x10` is set, and passes that value to `_nv031399rm` with the other key words zero. On a descriptor cache miss, `_nv031399rm` calls `_nv006506rm`, which creates a 0x98-byte object, zero-fills it, and reaches `_nv005882rm` through an `R_X86_64_PC32` relocation at `0xbfe80a`; `S+A+4` resolves to `.text+0xbfe4b0`.

For this caller's zero class words, the 16-bit selector stored at root offset `+0x48` remains zero. `_nv005882rm` therefore writes `+0x68 -> _nv031304rm`, `+0x70 -> _nv031301rm`, `+0x78 -> _nv031308rm`, and `+0x80 -> _nv031310rm`. The first target returns `0x34`; the other three return zero. In the same `flags & 0x10` path, `_nv000794rm` invokes `+0x70` and then falls back to `+0x68`, passing `0x34` to `nv_set_dma_address_size`; it skips calls to `+0x78/+0x80`. This records the value passed to the wrapper but assigns it no hardware meaning.

This result is conditional static evidence. It does not show that the object was created this way on a live GPU; a cache hit does not rewrite these slots in `_nv031399rm`, so cache-hit targets remain unknown. The value at `nv_state+0x274` during the call also remains unknown. The executable [audit](../tools/audit_r580_constructor_callbacks.py) enforces the proprietary object's SHA-256 and the exact relocation/dataflow signatures; its [two focused tests](../tests/test_r580_constructor_callbacks.py) cover the expected targets, return paths, private evidence files, and rejection of an unpinned object. No GPU or kernel module was touched.

### R580 descriptor-cache direct callers (2026-10-22)

The relocation census report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-descriptor-cache-callers-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `4c0fa3038db6cc903d698240743549d6420e77b3b037a863ddf0f9b99205b169`, and mode-0600 raw disassembly/relocation log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-descriptor-cache-callers-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `34203bc60e9e974810f4bbdde6587aa86bc916f5af7ff16f1228c1c4cf32ff29`, find two direct `R_X86_64_PC32` references to `_nv031399rm` in the pinned ET_REL object: `_nv000794rm` at `0xd6d3cd` and `_nv031364rm` at `0x3c193`. Both pass zero as the first and third key arguments; their selectors come respectively from `nv_state+0x274` and `record+0x12c8`. The route census places `_nv031364rm` under `_nv000790rm`, reached by `rm_init_adapter` when flag bit 3 is clear; `_nv000794rm` is called by `rm_init_private_state`.

Both visible creation paths therefore use the zero class words that select the code-zero constructor path on a miss. The relocation census does not establish per-device cache ownership, global lifetime, device enumeration order, or runtime contents, and it does not rule out later field mutation. A cache hit before a given `rm_init_private_state` call cannot be excluded from this static result. The [reproducible caller audit](../tools/audit_r580_descriptor_cache_callers.py) is hash-pinned and has two focused tests. No runtime GPU or kernel-module path was exercised.

The same numeric offsets are not type identities. In the inspected candidate routines, other `+0x140` writes store interior data addresses, and `_nv034588rm` writes a scalar bit mask at `+0xb8`. A full syntactic displacement scan contains many unrelated structures and cannot replace object-type or constructor-to-registry dataflow. Direct instruction scans of the later selected initialization bodies found no stores at the three root-object callback offsets; `_nv030237rm` does make a later call through `+0xa0`, whose inspected targets do not directly store those fields. These bounded scans do not prove the absence of all transitive side effects. The exact runtime branch, callback semantics, and any relationship to GPU interrupt mask/ack logic remain unresolved.

The bounded Ghidra 12.1.4 helper audit covers 11 selected functions and finds no concrete GPU interrupt-source mask/ack/enable operation in the `_nv037755rm` direct path. This does not establish global absence of such a mechanism or runtime reachability. No module, RM, firmware, or GPU was executed. Keep the experimental IRQ drain at `-EOPNOTSUPP`; no register operation is inferred from these callback fields.

### R580 same-device selector reuse (2026-10-22)

The hash-pinned joined audit (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-same-device-selector-reuse-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `f55de4d4a24c03aaa9445aa9836bd98618807b81775e9d82123b4b60ccfe54fd`, connects the proprietary R580.178.04 binary call graph to its Linux wrapper object lifetime. In `nv-pci.c`, the PCI probe zero-allocates one `nv_linux_state_t`, derives its `nv_state_t` with `NV_STATE_PTR`, calls `rm_init_private_state`, then publishes that `nvl`. On the first device open, `nvidia_open` resolves the published minor entry and applies the same macro to that `nvl`; `nv_open_device` reaches `nv_start_device`, which calls `rm_init_adapter`. This is source evidence that the two initialization stages address the same per-device `nv_state` member.

The binary reads that member at `nv_state+0x274` in both paths. `_nv000794rm` reaches `_nv031399rm` only when flag bit `0x10` is set. The normal `_nv000790rm` route copies `+0x274` to record `+0x12c8` and reaches the same lookup through `_nv031364rm` when the separate adapter branch allows it. Both call sites pass zero for the other two key words and use the same normalization. Therefore an earlier private-state lookup **could** seed a later adapter-init hit if both gates are taken, the field value stays unchanged, and the registry entry persists.

That condition is not established across the earlier private-state and later adapter stages on a live V100. The pinned platform-display probe also reads `nvidia,disp-sw-soc-chip-id` into this member after its own `rm_init_private_state` call; this proves the field can change on that platform path, but does not show that it applies to the PCI V100 or that both stages share that route. Other opaque alias or callback writes also remain unexcluded. Runtime selector equality across the two stages and an actual cache hit remain unknown. The [audit tool](../tools/audit_r580_same_device_selector_reuse.py) pins the RM object, wrapper sources, and headers; its [two focused tests](../tests/test_r580_same_device_selector_reuse.py) reject source drift and check the conditional conclusion. The mode-0600 disassembly log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-same-device-selector-reuse-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `f85df1c0a66e8bfd639a3c5df627c66a2a550e44f129ada8902c64015696c065`, contains the selected code paths. No driver or PCI binding was changed.

### PC3 V100 selector-path runtime observation (2026-10-22)
