The bounded [PC3 report](../evidence/pc3-r580-kprobe-selector-state-correlated-v3-20261022.json), SHA-256 `38cf1b58d5575f183550960de392f5e4af0cdc8617283b24417a3a6c23685aa5`, records one `rm_init_adapter`, one `_nv000790rm`, and one `_nv031399rm` entry in the same successful `nvidia-smi -L` observation window. At `rm_init_adapter` entry, the two documented nofault scalars were `flags=0xA0` and `disp_sw_soc_chip_id=0`, with zero read failures. Since bit 3 is clear, the observed `_nv000790rm` entry agrees with the statically predicted adapter route. The observer used fixed symbol targets; the two internal handlers only incremented atomic counters and did not read arguments.

This is direct evidence that the functions were entered during that window and that the snapshot values existed at adapter-init entry. It does not attribute each entry causally to the `nvidia-smi` process, reveal the selector passed to `_nv031399rm`, compare it with the earlier private-state value, or distinguish a descriptor-cache hit from a miss. It does not validate CUDA or a hybrid driver. All probes and the temporary observer module were removed; the report shows unchanged V100 identity and kernel taint. PCI-binding and VM/firmware state changes are reported as unmeasured, while the runner's requested changes are false.

## RM_CONTROL ABI comparison (2026-10-21)

The exact-version NVIDIA [R580.178.04 product list](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/580.178.04/README/supportedchips.html) lists Tesla V100 variants and the GeForce RTX 5080 (`2C02`). The same release's [kernel-module guide](https://download.nvidia.com/XFree86/Linux-x86_64/580.178.04/README/kernel_open.html) qualifies that package-level list: the proprietary and Open kernel-module flavors cannot be installed or loaded together; proprietary supports Volta but not Blackwell, while Open supports Turing and later. The driver user-space components are identical between the flavors. Consequently, R580 package product coverage does not establish one stock module serving both cards; it supports the same-version dual-backend candidate, whose integration remains custom and unvalidated on one host.

Hardware-observed fixed RM_CONTROL headers were compared from two distinct installed-driver environments: RTX 5080/R595.99.02 on the PC3 host and V100/R580.178.04 in the PC3 guest. The complete observer recorded 165 and 170 RM_CONTROL records, respectively, with no syscall failures. There are 58 common command IDs. The corrected [comparison v2](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-control-header-comparison-v2-20261021.json) reports three command IDs whose declared sizes differ and separately reports four same-size commands with differing observed call counts. It records an embedded status value `0x56` for RTX command `0x2080012f`; no conclusion about its cause is drawn. Comparison v1 is retained but superseded because its first implementation classified call-count differences as size drift.

### Paired PC3 RM_CONTROL header recapture (2026-10-23)

The fresh v23 header-only observer captured 71 records from the PC3 RTX 5080/R595.99.02 host and 70 from the V100/R580.178.04 guest. Each contains two valid zero-size parameter declarations, so the validators now accept the full 32-bit size field rather than assuming a nonzero lower bound. The [comparison v5](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-control-header-comparison-v5-20261023.json), SHA-256 `1d9e193a1fc06c1cbb75194d6de854f48b17a7a99786beadc524e36872ba302b`, reports 48 common command IDs, three shared-command size drifts, two same-size call-count differences, and no same-command/same-size status-set drift. Source inspection identifies `0x2080012f` as `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_GPU_QUERY_ECC_STATUS`. Its pinned SDK documentation lists `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` as an allowed result, which matches the RTX embedded status `0x56`; the numeric status mapping is therefore not by itself evidence of a malfunction. The pinned R580/R595 C11 layout probe measures this command at 1464/1664 bytes and attributes the change to 36/41 ECC unit slots in `NV2080_CTRL_GPU_ECC_UNIT_COUNT`. The header-only capture does not reveal the request flags, unit contents, or the reason that the RTX path returned the documented status. The observer retained fixed header fields and relation/presence booleans only; it did not read nested payloads or retain raw handles and pointers. The two captures used separate Linux kernels and stock drivers, so they say nothing about cross-version forwarding safety or same-kernel hybrid operation.

### Paired stock enumeration recapture and SDK correlation (2026-10-28)

Fresh isolated enumeration on the RTX 5080/R595.99.02 host and V100/R580.178.04 guest selected SM120 and SM70 respectively and captured 71 and 70 fixed headers. Both used the same probe binary and SM70+SM120 fatbin; [comparison v2](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-control-header-comparison-v2-20261028T0955Z.json) verifies the hashes and byte counts, records 48 common IDs, three shared size deltas and two same-size call-count deltas. The comparator was upgraded to output schema v3 and now fails closed on missing, malformed, or mismatched probe/fatbin identities; its CPU tests include these negative cases. Input and comparison reports are mode 0600.

The recaptured sizes match freshly compiled R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 SDK declarations exactly: `GPU_GET_NAME_STRING` (`0x20800110`) 132 vs 68 bytes, `GPU_QUERY_ECC_STATUS` (`0x2080012f`) 1464 vs 1664 bytes, and `NVLINK_GET_NVLINK_STATUS` (`0x20803002`) 13344 vs 13864 bytes. R580 name-string parameters contain a 128-byte ASCII/Unicode union while R595 uses a 64-byte ASCII array. ECC slots increase from 36 to 41. NVLink status adds an aligned per-link `nvlpwIdx` field (208 to 216 bytes for each of 64 slots) and a trailing 8-byte enabled-mask, explaining the total 520-byte change. The [fresh C11 layout report v4](../evidence/rm-control-layout-r580-r595-v4-20261028T0945Z.json) pins the exact SDK trees, headers, compiler and fixture. R595's `0x56` maps to `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, an allowed status for the ECC query; the V100 call returned 0. No nested request bytes were read, so the observed status does not identify its cause.

This remains a **cross-release** comparison: R595 `libcuda` was paired with R595's driver on the RTX host and R580 `libcuda` with R580 on the V100 guest. It does not show a mismatch between the R580 proprietary and Open backends proposed for the same-version design, whose client ABI is R580. It does show why choosing an R595 client library over an R580 RM requires typed translation for these commands, and why the current fail-closed generic RM_CONTROL policy is safer than blind forwarding. It supplies neither a translator nor same-kernel/same-process proof.

An independent compile-time probe uses `tests/fixtures/rm_ctrl_layout_probe.c` and exact cached source archives to measure R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 SDK declarations. The [reproducible report](../evidence/rm-control-layout-r580-r595-v2-20261021.json) pins both archive hashes, the three SDK header hashes per release, compiler/version, fixture hash and tool hash. C11 compilation with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` confirms:

| RM command | R580.178.04 size | R595.99.02 size | Header-level difference |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `GPU_GET_NAME_STRING` (`0x20800110`) | 132 bytes | 68 bytes | R580's name buffer is 128 bytes; R595's is 64 bytes. |
| `GPU_QUERY_ECC_STATUS` (`0x2080012f`) | 1464 bytes | 1664 bytes | ECC unit count changes from 36 to 41. |
| `NVLINK_GET_NVLINK_STATUS` (`0x20803002`) | 13344 bytes | 13864 bytes | Per-link status structure changes from 208 to 216 bytes; array bound stays 64. |

The release-tagged R595 headers show the 64-byte ASCII name buffer and the expanded NVLink fields, including `nvlpwIdx` and the trailing `enabledNvlpwMask` ([`ctrl2080gpu.h`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/595.99.02/src/common/sdk/nvidia/inc/ctrl/ctrl2080/ctrl2080gpu.h), [`ctrl2080nvlink.h`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/595.99.02/src/common/sdk/nvidia/inc/ctrl/ctrl2080/ctrl2080nvlink.h)). R580 reports ECC unit count `0x24`; R595 reports `0x29`. The strict R580 RM control entry validates the fixed method size and returns an error when it differs: `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/control.c:450-461`; internal method dispatch also checks exact `paramSize` at `src/nvidia/src/kernel/gpu/gpu.c:761-766`. This makes blind cross-release forwarding unsafe for these methods. The captures do not include nested parameter bytes, pointer values, or enough evidence to build a translator.

Scope: this is one `libcuda` workload trace on each of two different kernels, not an experiment that sent an R595 packet to R580. The compiled size deltas compare SDK releases, not proprietary-versus-Open R580 declarations. No user payload was decoded, no experimental module was loaded, and no same-Linux CUDA execution or performance was tested.

## Typed `NV01_DEVICE_0` allocation boundary (2026-10-21)

The privacy-preserving [RM_ALLOC header summary](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-alloc-header-summary-v1-20261021.json) finds93 RTX5080/R595 and96 V100/R580 allocation records. Both installed-driver traces contain one successful `NV01_DEVICE_0` allocation (class `0x80`) parented by ROOT_CLIENT (`0x41`); its fixed header has a non-null `pAllocParms` and outer `paramsSize=0`. This is metadata only: the observer did not read the nested structure, pointer address, or object handles, and the devices were tested under separate kernels.

The pinned Open R580 source explains why `paramsSize=0` must not be treated as an empty payload. `serverAllocApiCopyIn()` calls `rmapiParamsCopyInit()`; `rmapiParamsCopyInit()` asks `rmapiGetClassAllocParamSize()` for the known class-specific size and then copies that size from the user pointer. The R580 class table marks `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` optional, while `deviceConstruct_IMPL()` reads its `deviceId` when present. The `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` root control returns `gpumgrGetDeviceInstanceMask()`, and the SDK documents those bits as device instances usable for `NV01_DEVICE_0` allocation. Source locations in the pinned tree are `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/alloc_free.c:110–151`, `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/param_copy.c:297–315`, `src/nvidia/src/kernel/gpu/device.c:70–105`, `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/client_resource.c:1437–1454`, and `src/nvidia/src/kernel/gpu_mgr/gpu_mgr.c:1765–1785`.

The updated [C11 probe](../evidence/rm-control-layout-r580-r595-v3-20261021.json) compiles both exact SDK releases and measures the `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` size as56 bytes, `deviceId` at offset0, and `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` as a 4-byte structure with the same command and layout. This is a release-header comparison, not hardware evidence for either R580 flavor. The pure [`hybrid580-device-instance-map`](../src/hybrid580-device-instance-map.c) core groups GPUs by `(backend, deviceInstance)`, gives groups deterministic BDF-ordered synthetic IDs, and rejects stale GPU-ID observations, extra/missing device-mask bits, malformed local IDs, and more than32 merged instances. Its UBSan contract covers local-ID collisions between backends. It is now called by the frontend: both backend sessions supply device-instance masks, per-GPU `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` results are routed through the BDF catalog, and the cached synthetic map drives `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` plus `NV01_DEVICE_0.deviceId` translation. The Open and proprietary R580 wrappers expose the matching typed query and device-allocation bridges, while frontend child handles retain backend ownership. This remains source/CPU-model evidence; no experimental frontend RM call has executed on hardware. Installed traces observed `deviceInstance=0` on each single-GPU stock path but did not issue `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` or reveal the nested allocation fields.

The current typed `NV01_DEVICE_0` bridge validates the fixed class, root parent, client-share identity, zero target handles, outer flags/rights, and synthetic device instance before dispatch. It then copies the exact 56-byte pinned structure and translates only `deviceId`; it presently forwards the class fields `flags`, `vaSpaceSize`, `vaStartInternal`, `vaLimitInternal`, and `vaMode` without a frontend allowlist. R580 `deviceConstruct_IMPL` consumes these values, using internal VA bounds when `RESTRICT_RESERVED_VALIMITS` is set, while the SDK declares allocation flags for VASpace management and vGPU/plugin contexts. No nested payload was captured in the existing traces, so the next evidence step is an opt-in, handle/address-redacted fixed-layout capture on the ordinary CUDA initialization path. Do not invent a flag allowlist or reject valid CUDA modes before that measurement; afterward add negative CPU/UBSan cases for fields not supported by the physical dual-backend bridge.

## UVM differential evidence

### Cross-release UVM payload layout audit (2026-10-23)

Four same-named standard UVM APIs have different R580-provider and R595-public CRCs; event registration also changed symbol and table type. CRC differences or matches alone do not prove payload safety. I compiled the exact pinned R580 and R595 `nv_uvm_types.h` / `nv_uvm_user_types.h` against layout probes and compiled the adapter fixtures with UBSan.

| API payload | R580 | R595 | Bridge result |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| `UvmGpuP2PCapsParams` | 64 bytes; `NVLINK_1=2`, `C2C=7` | 72 bytes; `PCIE_BAR1=2`, `NVLINK_1=3`, `C2C=8` | Explicit enum and BAR1 translation; atomics false because R580 has no field; invalid enum/BAR1 combinations fail closed. |
| `UvmGpuCopyEngineCaps` | 8 bytes; `cePceMask` offset 4 | 8 bytes; `cePceMask` offset 4; extra `scrub` bit | Per-field copy, new bit false. |
| `UvmGpuChannelInstanceInfo` | 1,120 bytes; runlist base offset 1,104; SMC fields start 1,112 | 1,128 bytes; work-submission offset/token at 1,104/1,112; SMC fields start 1,116 | R580 provider writes its own-sized buffer; R580 doorbell source derives base+`0x90` and `CHID[11:0]`, which the adapter translates; shifted SMC fields are copied by name. |
| `UvmPmaStatistics` | 48 bytes | 96 bytes, with appended localized-region arrays | Same live pointer is used only through the shared prefix. Pinned R595 runtime reads `numFreePages64k` and `numFreePages2m` (offsets 8 and 16); it does not read the appended arrays. |
| `UvmOpsUvmEvents` / `UvmEventsLinux` | 56 bytes; seven slots, `suspend` at 0 and `isrTopHalf` at 32 | 40 bytes; five slots, `isrTopHalf` at 0 and `suspend` at 8 | Field-by-field callback mapping; R580-only `startDevice` and `stopDevice` are NULL. Exact branch layouts are measured; the R595 fixture uses pinned types. |

### R595 P2P capability producer boundary (2026-10-27)

R595 UVM's `get_parent_p2p_caps()` orders the two RM device handles by parent GPU ID, clears the 72-byte output, then calls `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps()` under the UVM RM lock. `parent_peers_init()` creates the P2P RM object and copies both directions' peer IDs, BAR1 DMA base/size, and `bar1PcieAtomics` bits. When either BAR1 window is nonempty, UVM asserts that the returned link type is `UVM_GPU_LINK_PCIE_BAR1`. The interface wrapper allocates its RM stack and calls `rm_gpu_ops_get_p2p_caps()`.

The 2026-10-27 audit searched 194 UVM/interface C files and did not include `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi`; its statement that the producer was beyond the open-source boundary is superseded. The producer is available as open NVIDIA RM source in R595.99.02 and R595.112.07. The new [source auditor](../tools/audit_r595_p2p_producer.py) verifies both public tag objects and commit IDs, cross-checks the R595.99.02 compressed archive and extracted tree against the tagged Git blobs, checks the R595.112.07 working files against the pinned commit, and confirms identical normalized code for `getSystemP2PCaps()`, `_nvGpuOpsIsBar1P2pAtomicEnabled()`, and `nvGpuOpsGetP2PCaps()`. The mode-0600 [receipt](../evidence/r595-p2p-producer-v2-20261102.json), SHA-256 `342152cf82b334c141efcdd97d64d87abc3629e8906e65b75ba50dce34f2f19b`, contains the line and hash evidence. Short source excerpts are preserved under `tests/fixtures/upstream/nvidia-r595-p2p/` with NVIDIA's copyright and MIT notice so the semantic test remains runnable from a clean checkout without vendoring either full RM source tree.

The source sets `bar1PcieAtomics[0]` for `device1` targeting `device2`'s BAR1 and index 1 for the reverse direction. Its helper returns true when the BAR1-owning GPU is Blackwell GB1xx or newer; its mapping-GPU parameter is unused. The neighboring TODO still calls out PCIe bus topology and routing. This narrows the code-level unknown but does not establish that RM will expose a BAR1 window, that the atomic flag is valid for a particular route, or that a mixed Open/legacy-backend query reaches this producer. The UVM BAR1 address/range host harness still uses synthetic capability records and proves no RM response or PCIe transaction.

The R580 P2P payload is 64 bytes and has no PCIe-atomics fields; R595's is 72 bytes and inserts `PCIE_BAR1=2`, shifting NVLink/C2C enum values. The adapter maps legacy PCIe plus a nonempty BAR1 window to `PCIE_BAR1`, translates the remaining link values, preserves each directional BAR1 range, and supplies false atomics flags because the R580 payload has no such fields. That is a conservative compatibility default, not a measured V100 capability. R595 then selects `SYS_NON_COHERENT` for that BAR1 peer path. Static contract audit v3 passes **23/23** checks and pins the candidate interface-wrapper/header hashes; report [v3](../evidence/r595-bar1-p2p-producer-audit-v3-20261027235852Z.json), SHA-256 `61c243803c8234560da153fc7cf1dc47ea3bde6b5ef25da5b0ae0719b63acae0`.

### PC3 PCIe path observation (2026-10-28)

Read-only snapshot v37 records the RTX 5080 under bridge `0000:00:06.0` and the V100 under bridge `0000:80:1b.0`; their captured upstream paths have no shared ancestor and their IOMMU groups differ (13 with two devices versus 23 with one). Both root-port ACS control lines show `ReqRedir+` and `CmpltRedir+`; the RTX link was observed at 2.5 GT/s x16 (maximum 32.0 GT/s x16), and the V100 at 8.0 GT/s x4 (maximum 8.0 GT/s x16). This is a point-in-time topology snapshot, not an RM capability result or a P2P test. The V100 remains owned by `vfio-pci`, so the two devices are not currently available to one Linux kernel. [Snapshot v37](../evidence/pc3-canary-host-state-v37-20261028T001026Z.json), SHA-256 `17d0e7360630ec3bda00467600c05ac65a835a9720369e2fd8adecd8041d3550`.

The reproducible [ABI audit](../tools/audit_hybrid_uvm_abi.py) writes the mode-0600 [report v2](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-branch-abi-audit-v2-20261023.json) with both header-tree hashes, measured payload/event layouts, R580 doorbell-source checks and explicit non-hardware limitations. The compiled payload and event adapter fixtures passed. The frontend build report classifies all 76 standard UVM exports: six adapted, five intentionally fail closed, and 65 with payload semantics not yet independently audited. This is a source/header and CPU-model result; proprietary RM function execution and hardware behavior remain unknown.

The follow-on [report v3](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-branch-abi-audit-v3-20261023.json) closes one high-value registration-format question: host-compiler probes against both pinned `nv_uvm_types.h` trees measured `UvmGpuInfo` at **240 bytes**, with all **35** named field offsets equal; `UvmGpuClientInfo` and `UvmGpuConfComputeCaps` layouts also match. The Linux-only `UvmGpuPlatformInfo` typedef is text-normalized after comment removal and matches exactly, including `pci_dev`, `dma_addressable_start`, and `dma_addressable_limit` in that order. Directly including `nv_uvm_interface.h` in a standalone host probe is not viable because it includes kernel build configuration, so this last declaration comparison is source-level rather than a compiled kernel-ABI assertion. This supports using the same registration payload shape at this boundary; it does not verify returned values, callback semantics, RM execution, Volta HAL setup, or UVM runtime.

The memory-path [report v4](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-branch-abi-audit-v4-20261023.json) adds a second concrete boundary. Both pinned headers declare `nvUvmInterfaceGetFbInfo(uvmGpuDeviceHandle, UvmGpuFbInfo *)` identically; compiled host layouts measure `UvmGpuFbInfo` at **64 bytes** with offsets `maxAllocatableAddress=0`, `heapSize=8`, `reservedHeapSize=12`, `bZeroFb=16`, `maxVidmemPageSize=24`, BAR1 booleans at 32/33, BAR1 start/size at 40/48, and `heapStart=56`. Static field census of the R580 consumer (`nvidia-uvm/uvm_gpu.c:255-272,1437-1447`) and R595 consumer (`nvidia-uvm/uvm_gpu.c:257-274,1477-1487`) finds the same nine reads. `bStaticBar1Enabled` is present in both layouts but is not read in either pinned UVM source. The source computes memory size from heap plus reserved heap, and uses BAR1 fields only on the non-zero-FB branch. The RM-returned values and actual BAR1 mapping remain unknown; no GPU was used.

The PMA-specific [report v5](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-branch-abi-audit-v5-20261023.json), SHA-256 `d2295a9e6ab0bd85bc220d1121c23c61ec3004900b211b190832f5e7745f5789`, extends the same comparison through page allocation. Both pinned branches declare `nvUvmInterfacePmaAllocPages(void *, NvLength, NvU64, UvmPmaAllocationOptions *, NvU64 *)` identically; compiled `UvmPmaAllocationOptions` layouts are **56 bytes** with offsets `flags=0`, `minimumSpeed=4`, `physBegin=8`, `physEnd=16`, `regionId=24`, `alignment=32`, `numPagesAllocated=40`, and `resultFlags=48`. The R580 and R595 UVM consumers explicitly access the same five fields (`flags`, `numPagesAllocated`, `physBegin`, `physEnd`, `resultFlags`); they do not explicitly read `minimumSpeed`, `regionId`, or `alignment`. Eight PMA bits used by the pinned UVM sources match numerically: allow-partial 4096, contiguous 64, don't-evict 1, no-zero 1024, pinned 2, protected-region 256, result-is-zero 1, and specify-address-range 8. The source describes PMA allocation as synchronous and potentially eviction-triggering; UVM's don't-evict flag is a deadlock-avoidance condition, and contiguous allocation must be reflected in freeing. This does **not** establish that the proprietary provider implements identical semantics across releases, nor actual allocation results, zeroing, eviction, or stable physical addresses. No proprietary allocator was executed, no module was loaded, and no GPU was touched.

The fault-path extension [audit v8](../evidence/hybrid-uvm-branch-abi-audit-v8-20261023.json), SHA-256 `e17908514fff7b8f6a523368663bafe3f39fb33519114057ac7eb443ab08f8d6`, pins both UVM callback sources and the corresponding Volta/Turing `dev_fault.h` headers. `parse_fault_entry_common()` has the same normalized definition in R580 Volta and R595 Turing UVM, and the fault-type decoder definitions also match after normalizing only their architecture-specific function names. The MMU-engine callback does not match: R580 classifies the host range through HOST13=45, with BAR1 enum value 4 and no BAR1 upper-bound check in the graphics guard; R595 extends the host range through HOST14=46, BAR1 begins at 128, and the graphics guard excludes BAR1 IDs. This is a meaningful porting constraint for preserving Volta ID semantics, but the static comparison does not establish observed IDs or callback behavior on GV100. It does not justify copying the R580 routine blindly into R595. Focused tests pass 2/2; the fresh pinned software suite passes 919 tests in 44.681 s ([report](../evidence/software-verification-uvm-fault-hal-callback-v1-20261023T084800Z.json), SHA-256 `659fcf53b35543d237f5f3401a6cd23cf88f180577bccb476646c8b51351b320`). All results remain source/compiler/CPU-only; no module, RM fault path, or GPU was exercised.

The official R580.178.04 module-source archive was downloaded from NVIDIA’s module-source index for read-only inspection. HTTP length was 22,267,204 bytes; the official MD5 sidecar matched `98908bf75993d8cb938b5afe3683a43a`; local SHA-256 was `060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`. Its `COPYING` says the individual files are MIT unless otherwise noted and the linked Linux module is dual MIT/GPLv2. The verified archive is now cached in `.cache/upstream/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04.tar.xz`; the source URL and hashes identify the inspected input.

R580 UVM contains six Volta-specific source/header files: `uvm_volta.c`, `uvm_volta_ce.c`, `uvm_volta_fault_buffer.c`, `uvm_volta_fault_buffer.h`, `uvm_volta_host.c`, and `uvm_volta_mmu.c`. The old `uvm_hal.c` includes Maxwell/Pascal/Volta parent classes and explicit GV100 entries. Modern R595 UVM begins its class tables at Turing and has no `uvm_volta*.c` sources; its `uvm_hal_init_gpu()` returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS` when it cannot find a matching architecture class.

Isolated compile experiment (never loaded or installed): copied local `/usr/src/nvidia-595.99.02` to a new `/tmp` tree, overlaid the six R580 Volta UVM sources, source-list entries, the required Volta register/class headers, and the two subchannel mappings present in R580. The first command built only `nvidia-uvm`; that omitted shared conftests emitted by the `nvidia` Kbuild fragment, so its API errors are not treated as source incompatibilities. A fresh, reproducible `-j8` run through `tools/probe_volta_uvm_port.py` compiled the Volta CE, host, fault-buffer, and MMU objects, then stopped in:

- `uvm_volta.c`: R595 removed several `uvm_parent_gpu_t` capability fields and TLB invalidation flags set by the R580 Volta initializer.
- `uvm_volta_mmu.c`: the Volta MMU implementation depends on the removed Pascal MMU helper.
- Prototype warnings also show that Volta UVM function declarations were removed from the modern headers.

The machine-readable result is `evidence/r580-uvm-r595-port-repro-j8-20261018.json`; the command writes its detailed build output to `/tmp/nv-hybrid-r595-uvm-port-repro-j8-20261018/uvm-port-build.log`. A previous incremental scratch build also confirmed the two error families above. This confirms source-level reuse of some Volta functions but disproves a drop-in restoration. The UVM module was not linked, no RM/PCI dispatch was added, and no GPU behavior is implied. Those historical logs remain in temporary paths. The current hardened rerun has a durable full log under `evidence/logs/`. The local OS-agnostic object filename does not identify it as proprietary; full official OpenRM sources build that object.

### R595 BAR1 noncoherent PTE adapter (2026-10-27)

The later R595 source path can select `UVM_APERTURE_SYS_NON_COHERENT` for PCIe BAR1 peer memory when atomics are not available. The copied Volta `make_pte_volta()` asserted on the newly introduced aperture. The GV100 VER2 PTE definition encodes system-noncoherent aperture as `3`; the address remains system physical memory. The adapter adds that value to the PTE selection and uses `ADDRESS_SYS`, leaving PDEs and page-table allocation unchanged. The callback passes the 32-vector host harness, including expected PTE `0x399999999999c7`; see [host report v8](../evidence/volta-mmu-host-harness-v8-20261027T232406Z.json) and [R595 build report v10](../evidence/r595-gv100-hal-standard-nvidia-frontend-v10-20261027T232820Z.json). These are compile and host-CPU results; GPU PTE interpretation, BAR1 mapping, RM interaction, and P2P remain untested.

The exact R595 v15 candidate was later compiled beside the pinned R580 Volta callback and compared over 8,448 deterministic inputs spanning every supported aperture, three protections, all four accepted flag combinations, boundary addresses and a fixed-seed sequence. The noncoherent oracle starts from the R580 SYS PTE and substitutes only bits 2:1 using `DRF_SHIFTMASK64` and the generated GV100 aperture value; an unshifted `DRF_MASK64` would also clear VALID and is therefore not a valid field-replacement oracle. The candidate matches all vectors; a deliberate high-address mutation is rejected. [Host report v2](../evidence/volta-mmu-host-harness-r595-v15-v2-20261030T0535Z.json) records the exact source and harness hashes. This remains host execution of selected callbacks, not GPU page-table validation.

The same aperture also reaches physical copy-engine methods. The R580 Volta C3B5 `ce_aperture()` accepted SYS and VID only, although its pinned generated header defines `NONCOHERENT_SYSMEM=2` for both source and destination. The staged `VOLTA_DMA_COPY_A` table's `phys_mode` callback is `uvm_hal_maxwell_ce_phys_mode()`, however, so the C3B5 helper is the memset route and cannot alone fix memcopy. Its shared Maxwell B0B5 helper also rejected noncoherent addresses. The v11 source adapter covers both; R580/R595 generated headers match for each class, with target bits `1:0` and local/coherent/noncoherent values `0/1/2`. Compile-time assertions preserve source/destination field agreement. The host harness executes ten route vectors and four assertion guards, and verifies table dispatch. No copy engine ran, so BAR1 access, CE ordering, and P2P remain unvalidated. See [CE host report v2](../evidence/volta-ce-aperture-host-harness-20261027T233441Z.json) and [R595 v11 build report](../evidence/r595-gv100-hal-standard-nvidia-frontend-v11-20261027T233405Z.json).

The v11 consumer audit checks 18 source contracts across unchanged R595 UVM consumers and the staged R580 Volta fault callback. R595's atomics capability selects coherent SYS or `SYS_NON_COHERENT` for PCIe BAR1; the same SYS predicate is used when constructing and classifying the BAR1 peer address. The Volta PTE adapter encodes aperture value 3 and uses the system address field, while the CE table dispatches copies to the adapted B0B5 helper and memset through C3B5. PTE/page-table storage and channel instance pointers retain SYS/VID-only assertions, and hardware noncoherent fault-instance encodings normalize to UVM SYS. GV100 access-counter callbacks remain unsupported. The machine-readable [audit report](../evidence/r595-noncoherent-bar1-consumer-audit-v1-20261027T233847Z.json) records source hashes and the scope. It is static analysis only; no R595 RM BAR1 mapping, PTE write, CE command, or UVM operation has been tested on GPU hardware.

The exact R595 aperture, physical-address, and BAR1 peer-range functions were host-executed with 18 controlled vectors. They verify both atomics directions, BAR1 base-plus-offset addresses, noncoherent propagation, and the inclusive start/exclusive end of the peer window; non-SYS, non-BAR1, and empty-peer-mask cases are rejected. Candidate `uvm_gpu.c` is byte-identical to pinned R595. RM peer records are synthetic, and lock calls are no-ops, so capability acquisition, RM mapping, concurrency, and actual PCIe access remain unverified. See [host harness report v2](../evidence/r595-bar1-peer-host-harness-v2-20261027T235000Z.json), [runner](../tools/run_r595_bar1_peer_host_harness.py), and [tests](../tests/test_r595_bar1_peer_host_harness.py).

R580 sets Volta `plc_supported=false` and returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_DEVICE` before address validation or mapping side effects for `UvmGpuCompressionTypeEnabledNoPlc`. R595 removed this per-GPU property and carries the compression type through its external-map parameters to the RM path. The v9 overlay restores that early status for GV100 only, keyed by the already populated `UvmGpuInfo.gpuArch`; it does not change the mapping request or behavior for Blackwell. The source-order test and compiled extracted guard expression pass, and the complete UVM overlay compiles. The matching RM outcome and actual external-map call remain untested on hardware.

### R580-to-R595 UVM property/API map

A second, explicitly compile-only overlay was used to reveal the next source boundary. It restores the R580 Pascal MMU base used by Volta, removes the eleven property assignments whose fields no longer exist in R595, adapts the two MMU callback signatures to the R595 type, and compiles against the installed `595.99.02` source package. The result linked `nvidia-uvm.ko` (`module_sha256=1774f0758ff1a71e4561bfc11392609a4b999885bedfbe4996c7665329bb1fb4`) and compiled all five Volta objects plus `uvm_pascal_mmu.o`. This proves those selected sources can be made to compile and link against the local R595 UVM core; it does **not** validate the altered unmapped-PTE behavior, initialization, HAL dispatch, RM interface, or hardware operation. The exact report is `evidence/r580-uvm-r595-compile-adapter3-20261018.json`; the build tree and full log remain at `/tmp/nv-hybrid-r595-uvm-compile-adapter3-20261018/`.

The property audit distinguishes a modern behavior path from an equivalent per-GPU capability bit:

| R580 Volta property | R580 use / R595 observation | Port status |
|---|---|---|
| `tlb_batch.va_invalidate_supported`, `va_range_invalidate_supported` | R580 can fall back to invalidate-all or count pages. R595 `uvm_tlb_batch.c` always dispatches per-VA invalidation through `host_hal->tlb_invalidate_va`, with a range-count fallback only. Volta set both flags true. | Candidate mapping through a GV100 host HAL that implements VA invalidation; not runtime tested. |
| `can_map_sysmem_with_large_pages` | R580 falls back to 4K when false. R595 `uvm_mem.c` selects the largest page supported by the active MMU HAL and chunk size. Volta set the flag true. | Candidate behavior moved to the MMU page-size HAL; requires correct Volta page-size/PTE implementation. |
| `prefetch_fault_supported` | R580 gates prefetch-fault controls. R595 enables prefetch during replayable-fault initialization and calls the architecture HAL; no capability bit remains. Volta set it true. | Requires Volta prefetch callbacks and fault-buffer registration; compile-only overlay does not register them. |
| `gpfifo_in_vidmem_supported` | R580 channel code checked this bit; R595 no longer has the gate. Volta set it true. | No direct bit or proof of the modern allocation path for GV100; HAL/RM channel creation remains untested. |
| `replayable_faults_supported`, `non_replayable_faults_supported` | R580 used these to gate fault-buffer setup and ISR paths; R595 initializes both fault paths through `fault_buffer_hal` without these booleans. Volta set both true. | Requires a complete GV100 fault-buffer HAL. The modern class tables have no GV100 entry. |
| `fault_cancel_va_supported` | R580 branched between VA-cancel and older fault-cancel flows; R595 dispatches cancellation through host HAL callbacks. Volta set this true. | Candidate mapping to a Volta host callback, absent from the modern table until explicitly added. |
| `scoped_atomics_supported` | R580 sets it true for Volta and uses it to reject the legacy enable/disable system-wide-atomics UVM ioctls. R595.112.07 and R615.78.08 remove those definitions, routes, and handlers. | The old per-GPU UVM policy was retired. This does not prove CUDA atomic-scope behavior or compatibility with an older user-mode client; see the [pinned archive receipt](../evidence/uvm-archive-compatibility-audit-v2-20261102.json). |
| `sparse_mappings_supported` | R580 rejects sparse external mappings when false. R595 has no capability guard and dispatches the sparse mapping request onward; Volta set it true. | Likely moved to RM validation, but the no-GSP GV100 RM result is unknown. |
| `plc_supported` | R580 rejects `EnabledNoPlc` external compression when false; Volta set it false. R595 forwards the compression type to RM without the old UVM precheck. | v9 restores the early `NV_ERR_INVALID_DEVICE` result for GV100 only, before address checks, unmapping, allocation, or RM calls. The exact condition and ordering compile/test; matching RM behavior and actual external mapping remain untested. |

The compile result still does not create a usable UVM architecture path. R580 `uvm_hal.c` places Volta CE under Pascal DMA-copy classes, Volta host under Pascal channel classes, GV100 architecture under GP100, and GV100 fault-buffer operations under GP100. The modern R595 tables start with Turing classes; they contain no Maxwell, Pascal, or Volta class rows. The next UVM experiment must resolve those inheritance dependencies and the capability changes above before treating the linked module as a port. More critically, generated OpenRM GV100 dispatch remains absent and complete physical HAL initialization is unproven. A monolithic RM variant survives in the full source, but a complete UVM port alone cannot establish GV100 initialization.

### Same-version R580 UVM module-pair build (2026-10-21)

The first R580 UVM-only build was not a valid kernel-compatibility experiment. It omitted the `nvidia` Kbuild fragment that generates shared kernel conftests, and the follow-up combined build reused that tree after its UVM-only `symbols.h` aggregate had been written. The stale aggregate contained only UVM-requested symbol probes even though the combined invocation compiled NVIDIA objects; it then produced false missing-feature and old-MM API errors (`mmap_sem`, read-only `vm_flags`, and absent APIs). A fresh archive extraction with both modules selected regenerated the full conftest set and removed those errors. A separate fresh attempt initially stopped before compilation because the official Open module-source archive omits the generated `nv-kernel.o_binary`; the build requires the separately compiled same-version Open RM object.

The successful run used archive SHA-256 `060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`, Open R580 RM core SHA-256 `c5f7a58442286f02f9caebf3279320dd8a846883d7514c183981efa754d11248`, and combined `NV_KERNEL_MODULES='nvidia nvidia-uvm'` against Linux7.0.0-38. Both `nvidia.ko` and `nvidia-uvm.ko` are ELF64 x86-64 relocatable modules, version580.178.04, with matching vermagic; UVM declares dependency `nvidia`. `nm -u nvidia-uvm.ko` and the actual generated `Module.symvers` have exactly76 matching `nvUvmInterface` names. Every provider row is `EXPORT_SYMBOL`. The [report](../evidence/r580-uvm-clean-thunk-pair-v1-20261021.json) is SHA-256 `3827a25a4d0767111a7f4b79ef9e2e808146436e9e557ceebe4be452d398caf5`; the private build log is SHA-256 `036e7304f8db51ba67f290635ce0f93f02c36c8912a6969b2ae974b00523a766`. Resulting module hashes are `46e81c91316bee30bfe4593afc9c01f3d74b17a6c03e2a4fd8bd3f04b950e7ec` (`nvidia.ko`) and `270a9ec4a0647cb8fd23a52296e955fe83a307fe729419a1e9c71758c67143a6` (`nvidia-uvm.ko`). The Open RM was built with return thunks: objtool naked-return warnings fell from21,714 in the unmitigated-core comparison to0. Thirty warnings remain, including the unavailable `pahole` version check and upstream OpenRM compiler warnings. `tools/audit_r580_uvm_module_pair.py` verifies the hashes, ELF/module metadata, build trace and exact import/export closure; five focused tests pass.

No load or UVM workload followed. This does **not** mean the R580 UVM provider serves the private Open and proprietary backends together. Both private backend builds separately rename all76 `nvUvmInterface` exports: see `evidence/private-open-device-object-v5-20261021.json` (76 `hybrid580_open_…` names) and `evidence/private-proprietary-rusd-v4-20261021.json` (76 `hybrid580_legacy_…` names). The two pinned R580 `nv_uvm_interface.c` files are byte-identical (SHA-256 `b41c704d29363dd52dd0ea669ee253a37185f5826c7db56a07dec21c3d24eddc`), but separate private symbols are not one stock provider.

The source contract is global and stateful: `uvm_global_init()` registers `g_exported_uvm_ops` once and creates `g_uvm_global.rm_session_handle` once; first GPU retention registers by UUID and then asks for GPU info. `nvUvmInterfaceDeviceCreate()` receives the global session and UUID, while address-space, memory, fault and channel interfaces exchange provider-created opaque handles. `nvUvmInterfaceGetP2PCaps()` receives two device handles. A UUID-to-backend lookup alone cannot route the later handle-only calls.

The [`R580 UVM interface ownership audit`](../evidence/uvm-interface-ownership-r580-v3-20261021.json), SHA-256 `65686cd1ce0c73ef46ae0ac92eaf77388ec02506700f1833bfc42f67b5cb1d1c`, is generated by `tools/audit_uvm_interface_ownership.py` from the pinned R580.178.04 header, provider implementation, UVM C callsites and handle-structure header. It confirms all76 declarations match all76 provider exports and every API has a UVM C callsite. Its routing classes include48 APIs with typed opaque-handle inputs; five APIs with multiple object handles; two explicit paired-device APIs (`GetP2PCaps`, `P2pObjectCreate`); eight APIs routed by provider-owned opaque pointers; six APIs taking `UvmGpuFaultInfo`, whose source struct embeds an RM `Device *` and register mappings; and nine APIs taking an RM-private `UvmCslContext` with both a CSL-context pointer and NVIDIA stack pointer. `GetPmaObject`, CPU mapping and retained-channel APIs also return or consume `void *` state that must be translated. The source records struct-field facts and hashes; routing is not inferred from function names alone.

These findings tighten the first bridge contract: pair child RM sessions behind the one UVM session; resolve opaque object handles by kind and backend; associate `UvmGpuFaultInfo` and CSL context storage with the provider; wrap PMA and retained-channel pointers; and reject mixed-provider object pairs until an operation-specific path is validated. `src/hybrid580_uvm_owner_map.h` and `src/hybrid580_uvm_session_pair.h` now implement CPU-side typed ownership, generation-checked references, split begin/finish retirement, transactional child-session construction, reverse rollback, retryable cleanup, and quarantine for ambiguous destruction. A composed test verifies a logical session maps to distinct Open/legacy handles and that duplicate publication rolls both child sessions back. These are portable primitives only: they are not wired to the 76 exports or frontend, and provider callback retry guarantees remain a runtime integration obligation.

### UVM callbacks and power-management entry points (2026-10-21)

`tools/audit_uvm_pm_lifecycle.py` now audits three R580.178.04 trees: baseline Open, patched private Open, and patched private proprietary. Its [v5 report](../evidence/uvm-callback-pm-lifecycle-v5-20261021.json), SHA-256 `9d96005303c55eb4f837543a46b7ba35728ac842607b81cb8f65d3f5de3188fa`, pins callback prototypes/registration and the UVM global-lock contract. Global UVM suspend/resume callbacks have no UUID; device, ISR and P2P callbacks do. Each RM provider stores one callback-table pointer, rejects a second registration with `NV_ERR_IN_USE`, and clears it before `on_each_cpu()` quiesces outstanding top halves. The ISR path reads that pointer once without the registration semaphore. These are still one-slot-per-provider interfaces, not an integrated dispatcher.

The PM audit was refined to distinguish two different R580 paths. [`nvidia-pm-entrypoints-v1`](../evidence/nvidia-pm-entrypoints-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `ab1d647a22fe845240a7458a56221eadec47e8a48d4dbb8729c37ef388ad0e42`, compares the three R580 trees with Open R595.104.02. In all four trees, PCI `nv_pmops_suspend/resume` invokes per-device `nvidia_suspend/resume`; those callbacks do not explicitly call UVM suspend/resume or the channel-preempt helpers. The `nvidia_suspend()` helper reaches opaque `rm_power_management()`, so this static finding does not prove what the RM performs internally. The global NVIDIA sequence instead enters through `nv_set_system_power_state()`, called by writes to the driver's procfs `suspend` file. Its local device-list order is channel preemption, UVM suspend, then local GPU suspend; resume restores local GPUs, resumes UVM, then restarts local channels.

R580 Open and proprietary sources have no kernel PM-notifier registration path, while Open R595.104.02 adds one guarded by `NVreg_UseKernelSuspendNotifiers`. This is source support, not proof of the runtime parameter value. The private R580 modules create separate procfs directories derived from their module names. The private path-size patch expands the fixed stock 20-byte buffer; without that change, the two private names would be truncated.

The [PC1 installed userspace inventory](../evidence/pc1-nvidia-sleep-hook-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `ad9514a34583487a10d34c69131487f29b3cd88a0a680fbed32983e71fd6c5e3`, records `nvidia-kernel-common-595` `595.99.02-0ubuntu0.26.04.1`. Its `nvidia-suspend.service`, `nvidia-resume.service` and system-sleep hook invoke `/usr/bin/nvidia-sleep.sh`; that script checks and writes only `/proc/driver/nvidia/suspend`. It will not orchestrate `/proc/driver/hybrid580_open/suspend` and `/proc/driver/hybrid580_legacy/suspend` without a dedicated integration. This is an observation about PC1's installed package, not PC3's configuration.

Two unsafe shortcuts are now ruled out. The standard PC1 sleep hook silently exits when the canonical procfs file is absent, so private module paths would skip the R580 global UVM transition. A custom script that naively writes `suspend` to both private endpoints would call the same bridged UVM global suspend twice; the first call retains UVM's global write lock until resume, so the second call can block. Conversely, writing only one endpoint preempts/suspends only that backend's local device list before UVM; the other backend's channel state is left to its PCI DPM path, whose opaque RM behavior is not proven here. A valid hybrid design needs one coordinator with explicit all-provider channel-quiesce, exactly-once UVM suspend/resume, and ordered per-provider GPU suspend/restore phases. No such coordinator is implemented.

The audits are static and the installed systemd report only inventories text files. No system sleep was attempted, no experimental module was loaded, and no Linux PM callback order was measured. The PM protocol remains a production-readiness blocker, but its exact entry points are now known well enough to design the coordinator against source rather than conflating PCI DPM with the procfs global path.

### Hibernation DPM callbacks and recovery ordering

`tools/audit_nvidia_hibernate_pm.py` now compares the R580 baseline, the private Open and proprietary R580 trees, and Open R595.104.02 against pinned upstream Linux v7.0 `drivers/base/power/main.c` and `kernel/power/hibernate.c`. The mode-0600 [report](../evidence/nvidia-hibernate-dpm-contract-v2-20261021.json) has SHA-256 `d71db0c11a825e5b9ea01566dce1465af4e29265be7da43fdcc36ed0115cdaef`; the two Linux inputs are retained in `build/reference-sources/linux-v7.0/` with hashes recorded in that report. This is the upstream v7.0 source, not a claim that it exactly reproduces Ubuntu's patched kernel source.

Linux DPM maps both `PM_EVENT_THAW` and `PM_EVENT_RECOVER` to `dev_pm_ops.thaw`; `PM_EVENT_RESTORE` maps to `.restore`. The hibernation snapshot suspends with `PMSG_FREEZE`, then resumes with `PMSG_THAW` on success, `PMSG_RECOVER` on failure, and `PMSG_RESTORE` after image restoration. NVIDIA's R580 and R595 tables map these phases to `nv_pmops_freeze`, `nv_pmops_thaw`, and `nv_pmops_restore`; the freeze callback calls `nvidia_suspend`, thaw returns zero without resuming, and restore calls `nvidia_resume`. `nvidia_suspend` increments `suspend_count` when the device is already suspended, while a nested `nvidia_resume` only decrements that count; physical resume and clearing `NV_FLAG_SUSPENDED` happen after the count reaches zero.

This exposes a conditional recovery hazard: when a procfs/global path has already suspended a GPU before DPM FREEZE, FREEZE adds a nested count, Linux RECOVER reaches the no-op thaw, and a single later global resume only removes one count. The report preserves this as a source-level interaction, not a claim that every hibernation attempt leaves a GPU suspended. R580's global procfs path applies its GPU transition to its devices; R595's global path limits that GPU phase to devices requiring video-memory preservation. It is another reason not to run each private provider's whole global suspend procedure against shared UVM.

The revised coordinator rule is to quiesce channels on both providers and suspend shared UVM once during PREPARE, leaving each GPU's power transition to its own PCI DPM callback. After DPM, POST should resume any backend GPU still suspended, resume shared UVM once, and restore channels only after the GPU/UVM phases succeed. A frontend I/O admission/drain gate and provider phase API are still required; this order is not implemented or runtime-validated. No sleep, hibernation, module-load or recovery transition was attempted.

## Full OpenRM source experiment and corrected interpretation

The complete official R595.104.02 source archive is pinned to commit `ac24e02fc090c3e05c1fc03384b317ac2cc301b5`, SHA-256 `da4e454d493818027998fc9d7ef95fd5dd291d3d37119f1f81d12db85fc33190`. Local source: `.cache/upstream/open-gpu-kernel-modules-<commit>`. Its pristine OS-agnostic RM built successfully: `evidence/openrm-source-baseline-20261018T233431Z.json`. NVIDIA's [README](https://github.com/NVIDIA/open-gpu-kernel-modules/blob/595.104.02/README.md) explains that the build produces the OS-agnostic object. The name `nv-kernel.o_binary` is **not** proof of proprietary or unavailable source; earlier notes overstated that inference.

The shipping support gate precedes both private-state and adapter initialization: `kernel-open/nvidia/nv-pci.c` calls `rm_is_supported_device`; `osapi.c:3666-3730` reads chip registers and calls `halmgrGetHalForGpu`, which rejects unregistered GV100. Its earlier generic PCI predicate (`osapi.c:3813+`) checks NVIDIA display class/vendor and legacy exclusions; adding a PCI ID alone does not supply the missing HAL. The GpuArch fallthrough below is a conditional risk once that earlier gate is restored, not an observed path past the stock rejection.

Static initialization chain, all paths relative to the pinned source root:

| Source | Established fact | Limit |
|---|---|---|
| `src/nvidia/generated/g_chips2halspec_nvoc.h:89-99` | PF_MONOLITHIC describes RM owning client interface and hardware. | A surviving variant is not a complete GV100 physical HAL. |
| `src/nvidia/src/kernel/gpu_mgr/gpu_mgr.c:1279-1291` | Nonvirtual, nonfirmware clients select monolithic RM. | Many generated per-method chip/variant bindings still require coverage. |
| `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osapi.c:5079-5094` | Default firmware request enabled, monolithic fallback forbidden. | Supported shipping policy; an experiment can change policy but must supply backend. |
| `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osinit.c:1576-1675` | Private-state init obtains GpuArch and DMA/system address widths before adapter init. | Missing generated selection already matters before firmware fetch. |
| `src/nvidia/generated/g_gpu_arch_nvoc.c:262-270,167-201` | GpuArch storage starts zero. Unmatched ChipHal index0 takes system-width callback GH100 and default DMA callback. | CPU selector observation, no numerical Volta width or hardware fault inferred. |
| `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/osinit.c:1952-2002,2063-2075` | Adapter init fetches firmware before mappings/attachment; no image with fallbackfalse returns NOT_SUPPORTED. | This is one barrier, not proof it is the first failing stage. |
| `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/common/inc/nv-firmware-chip-family-select.h:33-73` | No Volta family; Turing/Ampere/Ada/Hopper/Blackwell families present. | No GV100 GSP firmware can be inferred from modern images. |
| `src/nvidia/generated/rmconfig.h:27,134`, `g_hal_archimpl.h:65`, `g_hal_register.h:242-294` | GV10X/GV100 disabled, chipID entry zeroed, no GV100 registration. | Reenabling one macro does not regenerate bindings. |
| `src/nvidia/src/kernel/core/hal_mgr.c:157-197`, `gpu/gpu.c:681-708` | Lookup skips missing HALs and returns NOT_SUPPORTED if none matches; bind propagates it. | Executed by `tests/test_openrm_legacy_hal_lookup.py`: GV100 NOT_SUPPORTED, output unchanged; modern controls match; absent slots skipped. |

Source experiment: `patches/r595.104.02/0001-gv100-monolithic-firmware-policy.patch` adds a compile-time opt-in to the actual `RmFetchGspRmImages`. Exact Volta/GV100 skips image retrieval, nulls handles, clears firmware-client request and returns OK. All other architectures retain upstream logic. The patch does not add any generated HAL, PCI matching or physical method. `tests/test_openrm_firmware_policy.py` compiles the actual function and actual pinned family selector with fake OS firmware I/O, testing stock/disabled/enabled variants and missing/present firmware on modern controls. It is an isolated CPU contract, not GPU initialization.

Full patched build via `tools/build_openrm_gv100_probe.py` succeeded for RM, `nvidia.ko` and `nvidia-uvm.ko`; exact artifacts/logs are in `evidence/openrm-gv100-firmware-probe-20261018T234225637447Z.json`. No load/install. This candidate uses stock modern UVM. The separate legacy UVM overlay is not merged into it.

`tests/test_openrm_hal_selection.py` executes the actual pinned ChipHal/GpuArch selectors. GV100 (arch0x14, impl0) leaves a sentinel index unchanged; zero storage takes the GH100 callback default. TU102/GB202/GB203 controls select indices37/67/68. This disproves treating the surviving monolithic enum as sufficient for valid GV100 dispatch. Existing GV100 FIFO/GMMU/BUS/CE/NVLink functions remain useful source, but no complete dependency closure or generator was found.

`tools/compare_openrm_gv100.py` compares the two pinned source archives without extraction. Its result `evidence/openrm-gv100-580-595-generated-comparison-20261018.json` records both versions with GV100/GV10X disabled, zeroed GV100 chipID rows, no GV100 registration symbol or ChipHal label, and byte-identical `hal_mgr.c`. This is the Open R580 profile, not the proprietary R580 implementation. Restoring modern tables by copying Open R580 therefore does not supply the missing GV100 backend.

The hardened UVM rerun `evidence/r580-uvm-r595-hardened-v1-20261018.json` proves fresh linkage using compiler/link trace, changed ELF artifact and all imported objects. Its archive digest/root are checked. Full durable log: `evidence/logs/r580-uvm-r595-hardened-v1-20261018.log`. License-text detection is reported as such. Successful compile remains a diagnostic result with `runtime_semantics_validated=false`; none of the eleven capability/PTE obligations above is waived.

### Physical-control closure after HAL registration

The stock `g_kernel_graphics_nvoc.c:514-523` selects `kgraphicsLoadStaticInfo_KERNEL` for the non-VF runtime variants, including monolithic. That function in `kernel_graphics.c:1223,1236` requests `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_INTERNAL_STATIC_KGR_GET_CAPS` and `...GET_INFO`. Their generated Subdevice entries (`g_subdevice_nvoc.c:2109-2123,2229-2245`) have flags0x100c0/0x1c0c0. The actual `NVOC_EXPORTED_METHOD_DISABLED_BY_FLAG` in `rmapi/control.h:156-158` compiles their callback pointers to NULL: ROUTE_TO_PHYSICAL is set, PHYSICAL_IMPLEMENTED_ON_VGPU_GUEST is absent. Local GetChipInfo (flags0x404c0) provides the control case with a retained symbol reference.

`tests/test_openrm_physical_controls.py` compiles those actual initializers/macros to an object without linking or invoking callbacks. `nm -u` confirms GetCaps/GetInfo references absent and GetChipInfo present. Parameter placeholders in that harness are opaque and establish no ABI or payload size. This is stronger than merely finding a monolithic enum: the selected host kernel path still requires physical services without local callbacks. It does not establish the runtime control status or a hardware crash.

`rmresControl_Prologue_IMPL` in `rmapi/resource.c:259-301` routes these requests to RPC for firmware clients or virtual guests. Clearing the firmware-client request does not implement physical controls. Static follow-up: `objGetExportedMethodDef_IMPL` / `nvocGetExportedMethodDefFromMethodInfo_IMPL` in `libraries/nvoc/src/runtime.c:300-354` find the descriptor by methodId without testing its callback. The internal nonfirmware fast path in `gpu.c:778-814` checks the descriptor and parameter size, then calls its function pointer. This is a conditional source hazard if the unsupported path were activated; it is not an executed crash or GPU-init result. The required real physical implementations remain to be identified. Do not substitute successful no-op callbacks or fabricated GR information.

## Local binary and tools

A separate proprietary R580 binary reference was acquired without installation: Ubuntu package `nvidia-kernel-source-580=580.178.04-0ubuntu0.26.04.1`, package SHA-256 `31666b2940d0375d9ca984bd78a195c2da8d353998f92f745e0831e5c7bd2a5c`, matching the configured repository index. `dpkg-deb --extract` ran no package scripts. The object is `.cache/vendor-proprietary/r580.178.04-ubuntu/usr/src/nvidia-580.178.04/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary`, 112677456 bytes, SHA `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`. Provenance: `evidence/r580-proprietary-reference-acquisition-20261018.json`. This binary is an analysis input and an unchanged input to the later offline ET_REL experiments; it is not relicensed source.

PC1’s `/usr/src/nvidia-595.99.02/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary` is ELF64 relocatable x86-64, SHA-256 `c19870e43606b4358d9274c4d3dec2adb1822d65ce1203520a8184cf043cafe8`. Static `readelf` audit found 13,576 global defined names and 409 undefined names, with no `__ksymtab` export markers. Global ELF names do not prove kernel exports. A local symbol inventory shows GSP-related names for modern generations; it is not a Volta code map or recovered source. PC1’s installed `nvidia`, `nvidia_uvm`, `nvidia_modeset`, and `nvidia_drm` modules are all Open `595.99.02`; the exact module paths, `libcuda.so.1` and NVML hashes, and Driver API symbols are in `evidence/user-space-driver-inventory-pc1-20261018.json`.

REA 6.7.0 and Ghidra 12.1.4 now run locally. Ghidra and JDK 21 were verified from pinned archives/packages and extracted under `build/tools`; Java was not installed system-wide and no driver or GPU state changed. The toolchain report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-ghidra-toolchain-v1-20261021.json`), SHA-256 `a1abe0137fec628c44f96f1dc2be4017fc63eb9b8fff5998dfad3cd8c4414def`, records Ghidra headless and native decompiler checks. This supersedes the earlier tool-path observation. IDA and Hopper were not installed or used.

Bounded Ghidra analysis targeted three routines in the pinned R580 `nv-kernel.o_binary` input (SHA-256 `5e9219a4a263315d20757159852b35be61dfd2ce75dcd2ca90b1cd1764d6bb8a`): GV100 packet handler (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-private-r580-gv100-handle-nonreplayable-packet-v1-20261021.json`), evidence `ev_25cb035cf88ba3dc5d52967d4bad8c069b83854f14fb238512a9ddc6785e3f68`; GV100 non-replayable service loop (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-private-r580-gv100-service-nonreplayable-v1-20261021.json`), evidence `ev_fa57cfbbfcdd51c356523ae817b443e2b104d21dd8955147188ebce38fd2f194`; and `nvGpuOpsServiceDeviceInterruptsRM` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-private-r580-service-device-interrupts-rm-v1-20261021.json`), evidence `ev_5ca8abec4c304b7dc3c51ebeda058479c7dd9f18468a79a683612894f9867420`. The packet routine appears to decode a packet and call an indirect handler; direct callers were not recovered. The service routine appears to iterate a queue and invoke entry/drained callbacks. REA reconstructed a call using control ID `0x20801702`; that result is not source. Pinned R580 Open source identifies it as `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_MC_SERVICE_INTERRUPTS` and establishes the all-engine dispatch, pending GMMU detection, and RM-owned software-shadow queue service. REA output remains decompiler reconstruction, not original NVIDIA source. `shadowBufferAddress` is the RM-fed CPU client queue, distinct from the GPU hardware fault buffer and Hopper CC indexed shadow path. See the next subsection and the [source audit v9](../evidence/r580-fault-interrupt-service-v9-20261021.json) for the bounded HAL, queue-publication, and IRQ/PM conclusions.

### R580 interrupt and suspend ordering

The expanded [fault-service audit v9](../evidence/r580-fault-interrupt-service-v9-20261021.json), SHA-256 `862b692b840af9e67aa99d1fdb352ddf56f257289fb8da735a96b0e31fb8e4f9`, hashes 19 original NVIDIA source files and establishes 39 static predicates with 35 raw-source line entries. It pins auditor SHA-256 `c44f5aa5fc2bf2ca3585ecd3c8572e1e4bf4b58cd75f4fb110f90739085559d9`. The lexer translates all nine C trigraphs before line splicing, normalizes LF/CRLF/bare-CR lines with raw-offset provenance, then removes comments and rejects preprocessor directives in the procfs functions. Mutation tests cover a trigraph-created splice that hides a macro override, bare-CR macro lines, all trigraph mappings, and physical-line mapping. In `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c`, `nvidia_isr` calls RM MMU-fault copying before `nv_uvm_event_interrupt`; normal IRQ registration targets `nvidia_isr`, while MSI-X registration targets a wrapper that serializes MSI-X calls. The PM path does not take that MSI-X lock. In `dynamic-power.c`, the hibernate/standby cases set RM interrupt state to disabled before `gpuEnterHibernate`/`gpuEnterStandby`; `intrSetIntrEn_IMPL` changes RM software state, and `kern_gmmu_gv100.c` returns early when the copier observes that state. None of these operations waits for an ISR that already passed the check. Additional R580 source inspection shows that `intrSetIntrEnInHw_HAL` uses a generated no-op fallback outside the named Turing ChipHal set; `osDisableInterrupts()` separately calls the non-Tegra `intrSetStall_TU102` path. GPU state unload dispatches to `intrStateUnload_TU102` outside T234D/T264D, and that routine clears top and leaf enable registers. This does not establish the runtime Volta HAL selection or make early state unload safe: ordering against in-flight RM copies and restore semantics are unknown. The audited PM wrapper chain contains no `synchronize_irq`, `disable_irq`, or `free_irq`; `free_irq` occurs on adapter shutdown.

The earlier broad PM-order claim is now scoped. In the procfs coordinator, MODESET depth returns before channel preemption or UVM suspend; UVM depth returns after `nv_uvm_suspend` but before per-device `nvidia_suspend`; only its successful full-depth path performs all three in order. The Linux PCI `.suspend` callback calls per-device `nvidia_suspend` directly and does not call the global UVM suspend wrapper.

Linux's generic DPM implementation at upstream commit [`028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/tree/028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8) runs `suspend_device_irqs()` in the later noirq phase, after the ordinary device callbacks; [`kernel/irq/pm.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8/kernel/irq/pm.c#L118-L132) disables relevant IRQ lines and calls `synchronize_irq()`. This is a later barrier, not proof that an earlier queue drain was closed to RM copies, and the commit has not been matched to the exact Ubuntu `7.0.0-38-generic` kernel source on PC3. No ISR race or PM failure has been observed on hardware. Keep patch `0001` unqualified until vector handling, producer quiescence, and restore ordering have a source-backed design and a discriminating test.

### R580 provider IRQ lifetime and fail-closed integration

The [IRQ-lifetime audit](../evidence/r580-pm-irq-lifetime-audit-v1-20261022.json), SHA-256 `f919e93566f2387c5849253053cd60f5722ad38b6a4f08157ad14ca38a7946c6`, pins the R580.178.04 source files used for this review. The provider channel-suspend API holds `nv_linux_devices_lock` while it calls `nv_preempt_user_channels()`, which acquires and releases each device's `ldata_lock` internally. Thus the list lock alone does not retain a device's IRQ lifetime after a stop operation. IRQ registration occurs before `NV_FLAG_OPEN` is set. In non-persistent mode, last close can stop the bottom-half queue and free legacy/MSI or MSI-X vectors and its array; with `NV_FLAG_PERSISTENT_SW_STATE`, last close calls `rm_disable_adapter()` and keeps IRQ registration and queue state. PCI removal also shuts the adapter down while holding the list/device locks, but does not take the ordinary `nv_system_pm_lock`.

The ISR can enqueue `bottom_half_q` work in addition to the IRQ thread. Linux `synchronize_irq()` waits for handlers already running and can deadlock if the caller holds a resource a handler needs; it neither masks future IRQs nor flushes NVIDIA's separate queue. The existing `nv_system_pm_lock` read side covers ordinary opens, closes and many RM calls, while its write side is used by NVIDIA's separate procfs system-PM path. The custom provider PM APIs do not currently acquire that write side, and PCI removal bypasses it. The lock is a candidate gate, not a proven solution; UVM work coverage, start/remove races, two-backend ordering, and error release timing remain under review.

The follow-up [audit v2](../evidence/r580-pm-irq-lifetime-audit-v2-20261022.json), SHA-256 `fa985520abbc17cedad76902aa348b69efd2c0d86c0e3766ff2484377aa7d0f5`, resolves that candidate question for the pinned Open wrapper. The comparative [entrypoint audit v3](../evidence/r580-pm-entrypoint-lifetime-v3-20261022.json), SHA-256 `8e168e52797a32261fcc3899b3164f139235b9fda04919b31f6992a2f3684171`, checks the proprietary wrapper too. The Open `nvidia_open` and `nvidia_close` control branches dispatch before their ordinary read-lock acquisition; the proprietary R580 branch acquires `nv_system_pm_lock` around those control handlers. Both source trees show the deferred GPU-open worker running outside this rwsem and relying on the native suspend path to flush `open_q`; both have native PCI `dev_pm_ops` that call per-device suspend/resume directly and PCI removal that can shut down the adapter and free IRQ metadata without the rwsem writer. UVM in each tree uses a separate `g_uvm_global.pm.lock` and queue drains. Thus the control-path difference is branch-specific, but the gate rejection is common to both. These are source coverage facts, not proof of a hardware PM failure.

The updated [schema-v4 entrypoint audit v5](../evidence/r580-pm-entrypoint-lifetime-v5-20261022.json), SHA-256 `fe11a76d316e9a615543014d6056b7bf91ba6169cc203810c35351721d0bb49c`, hashes eight source files per backend, including IRQ registration, queue implementation and API contract. Both branches set `IRQF_SHARED` for legacy interrupts, register MSI-X top/thread handlers per vector, serialize those callbacks with the recorded MSI-X lock/mutex, and let `nvidia_isr()` schedule `bottom_half_q`. In both queue implementations, `nv_kthread_q_stop()` flushes before setting `main_loop_should_exit`; the scheduling entrypoint checks that flag before `_raw_q_schedule()`, while the raw enqueue path does not recheck it under `q_lock`. Adapter teardown stops this queue before its `free_irq()`/MSI-X release calls. Together these source facts permit a post-flush enqueue interleaving if a producer is still active. This is not a reproduced failure: the documented queue API requires producers to stop first, and opaque `rm_disable_adapter()` may enforce part of that contract. The audit's mutation tests recognize a synthetic close-under-lock plus enqueue-recheck gate, but neither vendor backend has that shape in the pinned sources.


The pinned Open R580.178.04 `nv_start_device()` source establishes this order: legacy `request_threaded_irq()` at `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c:1416` or MSI-X dispatch at `:1423` (per-vector `request_threaded_irq()` is at `kernel-open/nvidia/nv-msi.c:152`); then `bottom_half_q` initialization at `nv.c:1448`; `rm_init_adapter()` at `:1468`; and `NV_FLAG_OPEN` only at `:1493`. The pinned `nv.c` SHA-256 is `3fddec7956bd263ac1d238a82861894c93cd9ca904067b770cca5a7beec2631a`; `nv-msi.c` is `444917fc7855970c64c6d3f81e2ba71a7a2f062add6e24f27e6e24ca97b3a913`. The ISR can schedule `bottom_half_q` at `nv.c:2918`. This establishes registration-before-RM/queue setup in this function, but does not establish whether PCI device interrupt sources were already masked before entering it, whether a handler can run in the interval, or whether an opaque RM path gates/unmasks those sources. It is not a reproduced failure or proof of safety.

The isolated [CPU lifecycle model](../src/hybrid580_irq_lifecycle_contract.h) deliberately marks source masking as an external precondition. Its fixture exercises handler/worker interleavings, owner races and partial MSI-X rollback, but does not connect to either R580 provider. Do not treat a successful model test as evidence of a usable hardware mask primitive. The next static discriminator is the complete probe-to-`nv_start_device()` device-source state path plus the exact RM initialization mask/unmask sequence; the private `rm_disable_adapter()` producer/lock boundary remains unresolved too.

The decision remains to retain the provider IRQ-drain `-EOPNOTSUPP` guard. The next proof must trace device-source mask state from probe through `nv_start_device()` and establish whether `rm_disable_adapter()` stops every IRQ producer or can hold a lock needed by the ISR or IRQ thread. Until then, neither moving `free_irq()` ahead of queue shutdown nor adding `synchronize_irq()` is justified. After that opaque boundary is bounded, implement a provider-owned lifecycle gate that participates in IRQ start/setup, deferred-open admission, last close, PCI removal and failure rollback. Its CPU concurrency contract must cover partial MSI-X setup, PM racing close/remove, two-provider acquisition failure, queued opens, bottom-half work and every resume/abort release; compile any resulting changes independently against both R580 branches. No runtime IRQ or DPM behavior is established.

The classification is reproducible with the expanded [`audit_nvidia_pm_entrypoints.py`](../tools/audit_nvidia_pm_entrypoints.py), tool SHA-256 `c9a292000167326a77a12b3cea22b9485438128a5deab5a52458d16bbe748482`. Schema-v2 [report v3](../evidence/r580-pm-entrypoint-lifetime-v3-20261022.json) hashes four source files in each backend tree and records per-function source lines. Four audit unit tests pass, including a mutation that inserts a read-side gate into PCI removal and verifies the reported fact changes. This static audit rejects the rwsem as a complete lifetime gate in both backends; it does not prove an IRQ or DPM failure on a GPU.

Accordingly, the common frontend's `drain_irqs` callback validates its backend and returns `-EOPNOTSUPP` until a provider-side lifetime and producer-quiescence contract is implemented. CPU tests prove the coordinator stops before UVM, restores attempted channels, and keeps admission closed until the matching post notifier. The current module build proves symbol/build closure only. No IRQ drain, module load, driver PM transition, UVM hardware operation, or same-Linux CUDA run is claimed. A correct follow-up must also account for partial MSI-X registration, persistent and SoC paths, PCI removal, the separate bottom-half queue, and restore ordering.

### Targeted private R580 ISR decompilation

The bounded Ghidra report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-private-r580-isr-order-v1-20261022.json`) analyzes the unchanged R580.178.04 `nv-kernel.o_binary` (SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`) with local Ghidra 12.1.4. The headless import and four follow-up passes completed; 22 selected routines decompiled. The first pass logged three unrelated external-block/p-code warnings, while all requested target decompilations completed. Raw logs are mode 0600 and their hashes are pinned in the report. The new [Ghidra script](../tools/GhidraAuditR580Isr.java) records selected function bodies, resolved call references, and pseudocode; none of that output is NVIDIA source.

The binary reconstruction shows `rm_gpu_copy_mmu_faults()` registering per-device state through `_nv059483rm`, then calling an indirect function-table target, and only afterwards invoking its cleanup helper. `_nv059483rm` uses `_nv052743rm` and `_nv052747rm` around a shared indexed-table insertion; those helpers call `os_acquire_spinlock` and `os_release_spinlock`. That observed bookkeeping spinlock ends before the indirect fault-copy callback. The matching registration path in `rm_gpu_handle_mmu_faults()` also returns from its table update before dispatch. This narrows the suspected lock but does not establish that the indirect callback has no other locks.

The follow-up rm_disable quiescence report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-private-r580-rm-disable-quiescence-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `d64a35bdcfcc74995bb5d31ae076d6abdc45fe76cc91df2e503f1a1eec623d74`, boundedly decompiles 11 internal functions from the same unchanged object. `rm_disable_adapter()` flushes an RM work queue twice around internal RM adapter-disable callbacks; `os_flush_work_queue()` itself is external to this RM object. The pinned open wrapper implements it by flushing `queue->nvk` or a global RM queue, not the Linux ISR `nvl->bottom_half_q`. A selected descendant uses indirect per-device callbacks and has a separate SoC IRQ-control branch, but its PCI callback target and hardware behavior remain unresolved. The result does not prove a Linux IRQ barrier, producer stop, or completion of a handler already in flight. Ghidra reconstruction remains analysis output, not NVIDIA source; raw logs are mode 0600.

`rm_isr_bh()` calls `_nv000801rm()`, whose decompilation walks RM objects and selects indirect callbacks based on the current IRQ type. The callback targets remain unresolved. This private-RM spinlock is not a Linux `synchronize_irq()` barrier and does not join an in-flight handler. Combined with the source-audited `nvidia_isr()` ordering, the UVM drain candidate still has no proved interrupt-completion barrier. Keep it unqualified; next inspect the complete Open/proprietary provider PM and vector ownership surface before designing a pre-drain IRQ-quiesce API.

### PC6 adversarial R580 IRQ source-mask audit

The [Claude Code PC6 source audit](../evidence/claude-pc6-r580-irq-source-audit-v1-20261022.md) was generated from a 20-file read-only snapshot. Its report SHA-256 is `631d17d7e89ac810d9e0deadbac9727882c8e875db4d13c3e09d09638b4f9373`; the companion [independent validation record](../evidence/claude-pc6-r580-irq-source-audit-validation-v1-20261022.json) verifies all 20 listed source sizes and hashes against the canonical checkout. Claude Code 2.1.307 used Opus 5.5, two Sonnet 5.5 subagents, and the bounded official Ralph Loop plugin; the completion marker was observed by the separate read-only monitor.

The source review confirms the PCI registration-before-queue/RM-init ordering, but does not show a PCI main-GPU source-mask operation in the Linux wrapper. The explicit `enable_irq()`/`disable_irq()` paths found in `nv-platform.c` and `nv-gpio.c` are SoC/GPIO line controls; UVM masks only its replayable-fault and access-counter sources. The private RM may mask PCI sources, but that remains unknown. The review also distinguishes `NV_FLAG_PERSISTENT_SW_STATE` (IRQ remains registered across last close) from non-persistent teardown, and flags PM queue stop/recreation while handlers remain registered. These are source-level ordering hazards only: no interrupt, crash, or loss of safety was reproduced.

The adversarial pass found that the CPU lifecycle model is incomplete for production design: it has no MSI mode, does not separately account for the IRQ threaded callback, collapses several deferred-work domains, and omits the persistent-mode and PM queue-recreation transitions. The open and proprietary provider APIs also have no source-mask, handler-completion, or teardown-participating IRQ pin. The frontend therefore continues to fail closed with `-EOPNOTSUPP`. Do not reorder IRQ release or add `synchronize_irq()` until the RM source-mask semantics, threaded completion, deferred-work ownership, and close/remove lifetime are established for both providers.

### R580 private-state identity and pre-ISR MMU-fault path (2026-10-22)

The structured static identity report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-private-state-identity-v2-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `b7da9ef16f7deb0d9a4a30a5cb9207964293d8b60277a896329cf124f540fddf`, resolves the normal-path pointer chain and records its limits. A fresh verification log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/verify-rea-r580-private-state-identity-v2-20261022.log`) confirms all 13 pinned object/source/log hashes and checks both source orderings; it is a static evidence check, not a runtime test. The proprietary Ghidra project analyzed `build/experiments/proprietary580-wrapper-baseline-v1/nvidia-580.178.04/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary`, SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`. The separate object under `private-open-hybrid-pm-v2-20261021/.../kernel-open/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary` hashes to `5e9219a4a263315d20757159852b35be61dfd2ce75dcd2ca90b1cd1764d6bb8a`; it is not the proprietary Ghidra input. The pinned Open wrapper source and headers are separate citations, with their hashes listed in the report. Ghidra 12.1.4 pseudocode and `objdump` output are analysis artifacts, not NVIDIA source.

The pinned Open wrapper provides the call order: `nv_start_device()` requests the threaded IRQ at `nv.c:1416-1418`, sets up `bottom_half_q` at `:1444-1449`, and calls `rm_init_adapter()` at `:1468`; PCI probe has already called `rm_init_private_state()` at `nv-pci.c:1507`. In `nvidia_isr()`, `rm_gpu_handle_mmu_faults()` runs at `nv.c:2824`, before optional UVM dispatch at `:2841` and `rm_isr()` at `:2845-2846`.

The static pointer chain is now resolved for the normal `_nv000790rm()` path. Its RM creation inputs carry `nv_state_t *` into `_nv031364rm()`. That function passes the state to `_nv049556rm()`, which writes the per-device RM child through the first qword of `nv_state.priv` and stores the `nv_state_t *` backpointer at child `+0x9d8`. Later in `_nv000790rm()`, the successful path follows child `+0x9d8` back to the same state, loads `nv_state.priv`, and sets bit 3 at `priv+0x48`. The Ghidra instruction windows and decompilation evidence are pinned in the report. This supersedes the earlier unresolved pointer-identity claim.

The early binary path in `rm_gpu_handle_mmu_faults()` checks `nv_state`, `nv_state.priv`, and `priv[0]` for null. Once a child exists, it calls `_nv052690rm()`, `_nv059521rm()`, and `_nv059484rm()` before later child-field access and conditional indirect dispatch. The bounded disassembly shows no test of the outer readiness bit at `priv+0x48` before those calls. By contrast, `rm_isr()` separately tests bit 3 and a non-null child before its nested dispatch. That later guard does not prove the earlier top-half call safe.

This yields a **static possible initialization window**, not a demonstrated runtime race: the Linux handler is registered before RM adapter initialization; the child can be published during that initialization; and the observed `rm_isr()` readiness bit is set later on its successful path. The actual GPU interrupt-source mask, whether a relevant interrupt can reach the handler during this interval, what pending-fault state exists, and how the helper callees behave on partially initialized state are unknown. No live interrupt, hardware test, provider change, or module operation was performed. Keep frontend `drain_irqs` at `-EOPNOTSUPP`.
