There is a separate wrapper-level scheduling window. `nvidia_isr()` schedules `bottom_half_q_item` when the fault helper reports serviceable MMU faults and `rm_isr()` does not request the threaded bottom half (`nv.c:2917-2918`). The item is initialized at `:1447`, directly before queue initialization at `:1448`. The pinned `nv_kthread_q.c` initializes the queue list head, spinlock, and semaphore in `nv_kthread_q_init_on_node()` (`:215-221`); the scheduler only checks `main_loop_should_exit` before entering `_raw_q_schedule()` (`:252-291`). Thus an IRQ with the required fault result during this tiny interval could reach a scheduler whose queue fields are not yet initialized. This is a conditional static hazard, not a demonstrated crash: source masking, delivery timing, the fault-count condition, and target-kernel behavior remain unverified. The report pins the added queue implementation and records these limits.

Focused mode-0600 disassemblies are the early MMU entry (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-pre-isr-mmu-fault-disasm-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `7d6591b48d7e4181f680821cf9cc23996f73de40b0f3723b0414a68133b8b577`, and its internal helper candidates (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/rea-r580-pre-isr-helper-disasm-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `60b3ab2ece5541fa2ec7650370812cca76c298fd75f786a114a54ecf65bdcf61`. The earlier broader Ghidra report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-init-irq-callback-v1-20261022.json`) remains useful for the unresolved `+0x2110` ISR callback identity; its raw logs and all source/binary hashes are retained.

### R580 adapter startup order and focused Ghidra follow-up

The comparative [schema-v5 startup/IRQ audit v6](../evidence/r580-pm-entrypoint-lifetime-v6-20261022.json), SHA-256 `3ebe68257716467c207611141b3822fa10c8f03f0f221ca451ba73ee119ffb9d`, pins eight wrapper sources in both R580.178.04 trees. Its tests show that both legacy and MSI-X registrations precede bottom-half item/queue initialization; queue initialization precedes `rm_init_adapter()`, which precedes setting the wrapper's open flag. The PCI probe calls `rm_init_private_state()` before enabling PCI bus mastering. These predicates make the wrapper sequence reproducible and mutation-sensitive; they do not imply that the GPU interrupt source is enabled, unmasked, or able to reach the handler during that window.

The completed Ghidra initialization log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/ghidra-r580-init-sequence-followup-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `d6d653bc5a7aef1d94284ceb890bcc9c1ea78699975e01a6888c579457623587`, decompiles proprietary `_nv000790rm()` from the separately pinned `nv-kernel.o_binary` (SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`). It follows RM child creation and successful-path state-bit updates. Nearby calls `_nv037755rm()`, `_nv000797rm()` and other internal routines still lack semantic identities tying them to GPU interrupt source mask/ack/enable operations. Do not label them as interrupt controls based on call position or pseudocode alone.

The bounded startup-helper report v2 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-startup-helper-audit-v2-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `6fd49d8459b1bd9bb57bce82dac2fe247d2d5736d97dd1f54b6ad08f24a73ad9`, records **11/11 selected decompilations** from the same pinned R580.178.04 binary. Besides the caller log, the follow-up resolves `_nv047871rm()` as a message formatter that dispatches through `_nv047877rm()`, and `_nv053856rm()` as a wrapper into `_nv015880rm()`'s assert/debug path. It does not identify a concrete GPU interrupt-source mask, acknowledge, or enable operation. The report's four declared logs, binary hash, Ghidra script hash, and 0600 permissions were revalidated.

The exact selected callback targets in `_nv000797rm()` (fields `+0x3c0` and `+0x140`) and `_nv031502rm()` (field `+0xb8`) remain unresolved because the constructor branch values are unknown; branch-specific function symbols are recorded above. `_nv047877rm()` also invokes an unresolved callback with a message buffer. A separate ELF callback-site report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/rea-r580-indirect-callback-sites-v1-20261022.json`), SHA-256 `caafec2c141e7ba9654a41536bca72e66bc8fcbbab854f1f2c540107ce6e6e27`, and mode-0600 objdump/readelf log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/ghidra-r580-callback-sites-objdump-v1-20261022.log`), SHA-256 `5e9630f1fb9b0e575f4809a129497fb4fd68d685f09e6d3f22f8723d890fcb3b`, pin the ET_REL code offsets. For `_nv031502rm`, the first argument is retained in `RBX` before loading `+0xb8`. In `_nv000797rm`, the `+0x140` call lies on a loop reached after `_nv031424rm()` returns a non-null object; the `+0x3c0` field is loaded through `RAX`, whose upstream object provenance is not resolved here. Each relocation names only an x86 indirect-call thunk, not the callback implementation. This establishes dynamic object-field dispatch, not callback semantics.

This is negative evidence only within selected bodies and relocation sites. It neither proves global absence of interrupt control nor that the wrapper-level early IRQ window is reachable. Pseudocode/disassembly is not recovered NVIDIA source. Keep the provider IRQ-drain callback at `-EOPNOTSUPP`. The constructor-to-registry object path is now closed statically; next resolve the selector and descriptor words for the V100 path, then inspect only the corresponding callback bodies and callers. If those values cannot be established safely, retain the unknown and continue on a separate code/testable boundary. Do not infer GPU register effects from call position or perform a register experiment on an operating GPU.
Nouveau/NVK sources remain behavior references only. Nouveau’s GV100 GR/context/VMM and no-GSP paths do not implement the NVIDIA CUDA Driver API. References: [GV100 GR](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/f909e534f5e34feb2ea2701d54df63e21aa506fc/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/engine/gr/gv100.c), [GV100 contexts](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/f909e534f5e34feb2ea2701d54df63e21aa506fc/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/engine/gr/ctxgv100.c), [GP100+ VMM](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/f909e534f5e34feb2ea2701d54df63e21aa506fc/drivers/gpu/drm/nouveau/nvkm/subdev/mmu/vmmgp100.c), [Mesa NVK documentation](https://docs.mesa3d.org/drivers/nvk.html), and [REA](https://github.com/morluto/rea).

## CUDA toolchain and existing work

The existing CUDA13-Volta project provides a frontend/Clang route that emits SM70 PTX and uses `ptxas`/`nvlink` 12.9.86 for offline Volta images. Its 2026-10-03 checkpoint reports 108 software tests and 18 cubins checked, but no V100 execution. CUDA 13 itself removed native SM70 targeting from the supported compilation path; this workaround does not make CUDA 13 libraries or Blackwell-only instructions available on Volta. The existing mixed fatbin was run on PC1 and selected SM120; it did not execute the SM70 image.

VoltaFabric A52 provides an SM70 inference kernel/FFI candidate and 386 CPU tests; its checkpoint explicitly leaves GPU execution pending. These projects remain the canonical compiler, kernel, and workload assets; this experimental folder does not duplicate them.

No inference benchmark, UVM workload, P2P transfer, GPUDirect/RDMA test, long-run stability test, or cross-generation NVML test has been run. No performance gain is claimed.

## Two-core namespace experiments

The proprietary symbol audit `evidence/r580-proprietary-vs-r595-open-symbol-targets-20261018.json` finds213 strong defined global names in the R580 core and13582 in R595 Open, with210 common names. Internal proprietary symbols are mostly numbered `_nvNNNNNNrm`; readable-name absence does not establish absence of GV100 or Blackwell behavior. Named policy gates `rm_is_supported_pci_device` and `rm_is_supported_device` remain useful analysis entry points. The wrapper's special self-hosted Hopper/Blackwell Open requirement is not general RTX support proof.

The direct `ld -r` experiment fails with210 duplicate-definition diagnostics (`evidence/r580-proprietary-r595-open-direct-link-20261018.json`). `tools/probe_rm_namespace_link.py` then renames only colliding strong definitions in a copy of the MIT Open object, retaining the proprietary input unchanged. Exact symbol-set checks and input hashes are verified before qualification. The corrected tool refuses implicit cross-backend references in both directions, unsupported weak/absolute definitions and preexisting destination names. These guards were added after independent review reproduced an unintended inter-backend binding with small C fixtures. No hidden bridge is accepted.

The corrected R580/R595 experiment links an ET_REL object with210 renames and416 unique undefined names; the R580/R580 experiment links with213 renames and411 undefined names. Reports: `evidence/cross-branch-rm-link-v2-20261018.json`, `evidence/same-branch-rm-link-v2-20261018.json`. Undefined names are not all identified as OS callbacks; they also include library/compiler dependencies. No combined object has been loaded or used to initialize a GPU.

The full Open R580 RM was built from the pinned source archive (`evidence/openrm580-source-baseline-v1-20261018.json`). Its shared `nv.h`, `nv-gpu-info.h` and `nv_uvm_interface.h` match the R580 proprietary wrapper byte for byte. None contains NV_OPEN_KERNEL_MODULES; the inspected `nv_state_t` body has no conditional member in that text. This is a concrete reason to investigate same-version backends before cross-version ABI translation, not a proof that every other declaration, configuration, global object, client handle or runtime interface is compatible.

The five namespace tests execute project-owned CPU fixtures only; they verify distinct legacy/Open calls, preserved Open internal bindings, retained unresolved host callback, cross-reference rejection and refusal to overwrite work trees. They never invoke proprietary RM code. The combined artifact's redistribution rights are unestablished; no license is rewritten or inferred from linking.

## R580 client and file-domain contracts

The targeted [client audit](../evidence/r580-client-domain-contract-20261018.json) pins22 source files. ROOT allocation consumes `NVOS21.hRoot` as an optional requested client ID under the actual `RS_COMPATABILITY_MODE=1` build. `hObjectNew` is the returned resource/client handle. Root free uses `NVOS00.hRoot=hObjectOld=clientID`, parent0. The allocator writes hObjectNew on some failed paths too, after server-side cleanup may already have occurred; an error output is not proof of an owned client. These findings directly determine the C broker's no-blind-free and quarantine rules.

The [wrapper audit](../evidence/r580-wrapper-flavor-routing-20261018.json) pins18 source references. Ordinary RM root/control/free requests enter the control node. `rm_init_rm(stack)` creates RM global state without a GPU parameter. `nv_get_file_private(fd,ctl,os_private)` returns the one nvfp attached to the Linux file and provides no backend selector; REGISTER_FD links only one control nvfp. `nv_get_nvlfp_from_nvfp` in `common/inc/nv-linux.h:1556` uses `container_of` for that exact embedded member; `nv_post_event` and `nv_get_event` consume it in `nv.c`. A second naked nvfp cannot be handed to those callbacks without an adapted ownership path.

The header-layout report measures host declarations, with the Open SDK supplying NVOS declarations missing from the proprietary package. The closed binary's layout remains opaque. The real Open580 wrapper compile supplies398 of the411 RM imports in actual constituent objects;13 are kernel-exported thunks. This supersedes the earlier source-candidate-only classification without asserting a compatible merged module. The Open wrapper retains its official Dual MIT/GPL metadata and104 GPL-only imports; a mixed proprietary/Open artifact has no selected lawful module form. No proprietary core is relabeled or executed. The original unmodified proprietary object is used in offline ET_REL link experiments only.

## Installed-driver CUDA IOCTL traces

### Paired CUDA node-open lifecycle

An isolated enumeration capture on the PC3 RTX5080/R595.99.02 host and the PC3 V100/R580.178.04 guest used the same probe and SM70+SM120 fatbin. The process opened `/dev/nvidiactl`, `/dev/nvidia0`, and `/dev/nvidia-uvm` 3/6/2 times on the RTX5080 and 2/5/2 times on the V100. Observed ioctl totals were respectively 93/5/6 and 90/3/5. RTX-only observed request codes include `0x46` on `nvidia-uvm` and `0xc01046ce` on `nvidia0`; common calls are retained as per-node counts in the [mode-0600 comparison v2](../evidence/pc3-cuda-node-lifecycle-v1-20261023/comparison-v2.json), SHA-256 `275ded2fbce3098909e9d209c21e6b845426cf5697fb1a7fcf78bb0857c408d5`. The report is generated by [`compare_cuda_node_open_lifecycle.py`](../tools/compare_cuda_node_open_lifecycle.py): it reopens both private reports and gzip sidecars, verifies hashes and exact selected GPU/driver/kernel/API identities, reparses both traces, and emits aggregate lifecycle counts and request-code histograms; no trace event rows or syscall arguments are copied into the comparison. The original V100 v1 trace used ioctl-only selection and is not used as node-open evidence; v2 captures the complete lifecycle.

This is direct observation of two separate stock-driver processes. It supports tracking the control, GPU and UVM node descriptors and their lifetimes in the frontend. The descriptors still open at trace end are not evidence of leaks; the trace does not establish kernel `struct file` identity, mixed-driver file ownership, same-kernel coexistence, CUDA context creation or hybrid-runtime behavior. Negative syscall returns and unrelated opens are retained only as counts and are not assigned NVIDIA-specific meaning.

Fresh PC3 host testing used RTX5080 UUID `GPU-819a4547-5ff3-130b-ea3b-1d1a837b1c77`, PCI `0000:02:00.0`, kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`, driver `595.99.02` and Driver API `13020`. The traced SM120 numerical kernel, five synchronous/asynchronous sizes and cleanup passed; the combined probe exits77 because SM70 is absent. Its [full trace report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json) records599 ioctl lines.

The separate V100 guest run used UUID `GPU-42d06a85-027a-75ea-7857-a8de27ad465e`, guest PCI `0000:00:05.0`, kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, driver `580.178.04` and Driver API `13000`. SM70, five synchronous/asynchronous sizes and cleanup passed. Its [full trace report](../evidence/pc3-v100-guest-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json) records607 ioctl lines. The corresponding raw captures are retained as mode0600 compressed sidecars: RTX5080 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json.strace.gz`) and V100 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-guest-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json.strace.gz`).

The independently hash-verified [metadata comparison](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-full-ioctl-comparison-v1-20261020.json), generated by [compare_ioctl_trace_evidence.py](../tools/compare_ioctl_trace_evidence.py), reports599/607 calls, a common normalized prefix of56 calls and first divergence at RTX `/dev/nvidia0` request `0xc00446c9` versus V100 `/dev/nvidiactl` request `0xc020462a`. Both traces have 100/103 requests respectively encoded as `0xc030462b` (48-byte ioctl): 93/96 on `/dev/nvidiactl` and7/7 on `/dev/nvidia0`. Both include `/dev/nvidiactl`, `/dev/nvidia0` and `/dev/nvidia-uvm`; UVM call counts are148/147. These differences are observations from two driver releases on separate Linux kernels, not evidence of ABI incompatibility or same-kernel co-backend behavior.

Static mapping was checked against the installed R595.99.02 headers/source and the pinned R595 Open source snapshot. R595 maps `0xc030462b` to `NV_ESC_RM_ALLOC` with the accepted48-byte NVOS64 form, and `0xc00446c9` to `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD`, whose public payload contains `int ctl_fd`. In `escape.c`, R595 resolves the supplied control fd, rejects self/cyclic linkage and retains its file-private reference until the GPU file closes. Both traces contain this REGISTER_FD request (RTX10, V1009); trace metadata exposes neither fd values nor referenced Linux file identity, and this static R595 result does not prove R580's internal implementation is identical. This makes GPU-file creation and backend-correct control-fd translation/lifetime the next concrete frontend seam.

Only the RTX trace contains UVM request `0x46` (one call). R595 source maps it to `UVM_PAGEABLE_MEM_ACCESS_ON_GPU`, a query whose boolean result is in a payload not captured here. Both CUDA probes use ordinary device allocation and pinned host memory, not managed allocation; the captures therefore establish neither UVM migration nor a Volta capability result. No same-process CUDA, UVM, P2P, or performance comparison follows from these traces. The requests were summarized from `strace -f -qq -yy -X raw -e trace=ioctl`; unknown/split lines are rejected rather than counted as complete coverage. Arguments and embedded RM status fields were not captured; syscall return0 proves transport only.

This falsifies assuming the NVOS21-only32-byte broker suffices for the observed allocation transport. `hybrid_root_alloc64` now uses the genuine NVOS64 layout and status offset;33 CPU scenarios check both forms, rollback and unsupported-input rejection. R580 `entry_points.c:282-296` maps flags NONE/null rights to the ordinary allocation API, but that source fact does not prove the real595 request contents are supported. Current scope deliberately rejects non-null parameters/rights and serialized/unknown flags. No proprietary payload or source was recovered. PC3 has strace; GDB was absent from the checked executable path.

## Private Open wrapper source experiment

Patch0002 changes Kbuild/module name and source export macros, preserving the Open core and Dual MIT/GPL metadata. Actual Module.symvers and ELF export strings contain exactly93 private names with original export flavors. This avoids treating binary-symbol renaming alone as proof of exported-name isolation.

Patch0003 filters PCI counts and probe by a read-only exact BDF selector, absent by default. `ExcludedGpus/GpuBlacklist` was inspected and rejected as an early guard because its UUID path uses RM after PCI setup. The actual selected guard/probe prefix is CPU-executed with19 cases under UBSan; rejection precedes BAR helpers and RM/PCI effects in those paths. Module-wide initialization, platform probes, major195, procfs/caps/devnodes and frontend/event/UVM ownership remain outside that guard. Fresh module linkage succeeds in `evidence/private-open-pci-v2-20261019.json`; retained objtool warnings and the lack of a module load prevent runtime coexistence claims.

## Global-resource differential and correction

The first private-name experiment exposed a source defect: `nv-procfs.c` used a20-byte buffer for `driver/%s`, while the private root needs22 bytes including NUL. Its truncated root disagreed with `nv_caps_root_init` in `nv.c`. Patch0005 changes capacity to the exact module-name literal; the original truncation and corrected default/private paths are CPU-reproduced.

`nv-caps.c` had hard-coded `/driver/nvidia/...` and `/driver/nvidia-nvlink/...` in4324 capability entries and four reader formats. Exact string comparison in `nv_cap_find_minor` makes a directory-only rename insufficient. Patch0006 aligns those paths with MODULE_NAME, including NVLink/NVSwitch roots and IMEX class/devnode/character-region names. Source-extracted tables/readers are compiled and executed for both default and private names.

`nv.c` initializes caps/NVLink/NVSwitch before GPU PCI registration. Patch0007 retains NVLink core while removing NVSwitch driver init/exit and its presence bypass, platform registration/counting, and the public PCI/OF autoload tables for this prototype. Audit found no second module-lifecycle call to those registration entry points. This restricts registered hardware drivers; it does not remove global RM initialization or subordinate Tegra devices created behind an allowed ARM64 PCI GPU. No runtime failure or successful coexistence is inferred from the static result.

Patch0004 replaces the main wrapper's fixed195 reservation with a dynamic256-minor region and checks exact file_operations ownership in fd lookup. Input core, copyright/license header and official baseline artifacts are unchanged. [Build and source identities](../evidence/private-open-resources-v3-20261019.json) record the integrated source/ELF result; significant objtool warnings remain. No proprietary source or binary was transformed by these patches.

## Matched R580 private backend pair: ELF and namespace preflight

[`audit_r580_backend_pair.py`](../tools/audit_r580_backend_pair.py) inspects the built R580.178.04 private Open and proprietary modules without inserting or installing either. It checks module name/version/license/vermagic, disabled PCI autoload aliases, the BDF selector predicate, required dynamic cdev/procfs imports, exact resource-name markers, and the pairwise `Module.symvers` export intersection. Module data is inspected from a private snapshot whose hash is bound to the opened input; both `Module.symvers` identities are also captured. Nine tests cover the valid pair and negative export, resource-path, import, ABI, provenance and malformed-symvers cases.

On the pinned `7.0.0-38-generic` artifacts, the module identities are `hybrid580_open` (`Dual MIT/GPL`, 139 exports) and `hybrid580_legacy` (`NVIDIA`, 142 exports); the pair has zero exported-name collisions. Both have the private caps, NVLink, NVSwitch and IMEX names. No exact `driver/nvidia-*` path is present. The bare `nvidia-nvswitch` literal remains in each ELF and is classified as ambiguous: `strings` cannot show whether a registration path is reachable. Source inspection of the patched `nv.c` finds NVLink initialization but no NVSwitch init/exit lifecycle call; the existing Patch0007 audit separately checks for additional lifecycle call sites. This is useful static evidence, not VFS, module-loader, Resource Manager or GPU proof.

The mode-0600 [v2 pair report](../evidence/r580-backend-pair-audit-v2-20261023T1937Z.json), SHA-256 `1fc84842016a794cfdfd2af5a1faddc70f8d3b2cdc27e93521945d8f8cc9ffde`, records both module and `Module.symvers` hashes, `runtime_validated=false`, `loaded_or_installed=false`, and `gpu_accessed=false`. It does not check module signature policy, resolve imports against the running kernel, establish cdev/procfs ownership at runtime, or prove independent NVLink library semantics. Separately, the common R580 frontend's 76 UVM exports overlap 74 names in the official R595 `nvidia` table; that cross-version finding is not a failure of this matched R580 backend pair, but means a canary cannot mix the R580 frontend with the resident R595 provider.

## Open RM return-mitigation mismatch

The target kernel enables CONFIG_MITIGATION_RETHUNK. The wrapper's actual command applies `-mfunction-return=thunk-extern`, while Open580 RM's Makefile adds indirect-branch retpolines without that return flag. A baseline RM witness `resIsPartialUnmapSupported_d69453` ends with byte c3/ret. Its corresponding wrapper witness jumps to `__x86_return_thunk`.

`tools/build_open580_return_thunk.py` uses the existing EXTRA_CFLAGS hook without replacing CFLAGS. A fresh full RM build records1013 C commands with the exact flag,3797 original archive files unchanged, and a witness e9 jump whose displacement carries the thunk relocation. The resulting core SHA is `c5f7a58442286f02f9caebf3279320dd8a846883d7514c183981efa754d11248`. Fresh wrapper linkage reduces10857 naked-return warnings to zero. The actual objtool command still includes --rethunk/--retpoline/--sls/--stackval. Thirty other warnings remain. See [RM evidence](../evidence/r580-open-return-thunk-20261019.json) and [integrated projection module](../evidence/private-open-file-projection-v2-20261019.json). No closed binary was patched; proprietary reference warnings remain unresolved.

## Projection integration negative evidence

The first patch0008 Kbuild failed: the vendor NV_STATE_PTR macro is not globally parenthesized, and `NV_STATE_PTR(nvl)->flags` therefore had an invalid expansion. A more-parenthesized CPU substitute had masked it. The failing full-build report remains `evidence/private-open-file-projection-20261019.json`. The CPU test now extracts the exact vendor macro and OPEN/CONTROL constants, reproduces the failure, and the patch uses `(NV_STATE_PTR(nvl))->flags`. The fresh v2 build passes. This correction demonstrates why the source/CPU checks do not replace real Kbuild.

Independent review also reproduced a missing-core reporting failure in the builder: final hash checks raised after the original exception and lost the report. A new negative test and guarded final checks preserve the original failure reason and a durable failed report. Neither failure was reclassified as a successful build.

## VFS/session ownership and SDK integration

Patch0009 reuses original nvidia_open/nvidia_ctl_open/nvidia_ctl_close storage and cleanup, with a NULL-private-data guard in nvidia_close. The guard matters because upstream alloc_file_pseudo already marks a file FMODE_OPENED: a later vendor open failure still triggers release. Clearing FMODE_OPENED would skip other fput cleanup. Anchor, inode, fops, pseudo-file and session references have explicit failure unwinds; calls never publish a user FD.

Primary VFS references inspected: [Linux v7.0 file_table.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v7.0/fs/file_table.c), SHA256 `1b42d47387a6635c73bdf61363425a23fcdb5496fe2ca665f7ccb03e541b1454`; [libfs.c](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v7.0/fs/libfs.c), SHA256 `937e198e28a1611533c3cb89d95e8b4b905381193d12792452fcefb23eefa47e`. These are upstream source observations, not verified identity of Ubuntu7.0.0-38's complete VFS implementation. Kbuild confirms headers/exports; runtime ownership remains unvalidated.

The source RM path preserves current-thread privilege and PARAM_LOCATION_USER. The adapter accepts only ROOT allocations with no nested parameters/rights and root FREE, and uses a fresh stack under PM read protection per call. It does not translate user pointers or replace caller credentials. An absent embedded status write remains a sentinel even if transport returns success.

The first full build exposed duplicate RS_ACCESS_* declarations: SDK nvos.h quoted its local rs_access.h after os-interface.h had included the wrapper's physically distinct pragma-once copy. The fix copies nvos.h byte-identically to kernel-open/common/inc for compilation so its relative lookup reuses the wrapper types. No SDK declaration is hand-recreated or guard suppressed. The v2 report records the copied header's identity and a full successful module build.

## Typed ROOT payload capture, RTX5080 installed driver

The new own-child observer uses Linux `PTRACE_GET_SYSCALL_INFO` rather than an entry/exit toggle; it reads only top-level SDK NVOS packets with PEEKDATA, follows threads of its launched process, rejects forks and x32, and fails the trace on unreadable packets or timeout. [ptrace API](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/ptrace.2.html). It does not attach to an existing process, dereference nested parameters, modify tracee memory/registers, or validate kernel-internal atomic snapshots. Other threads can mutate shared memory; FD numbers are recorded without dup/reuse identity tracking.

`evidence/pc3-rtx5080-root-payload-v1-20261019.json` is a fresh exact-UUID/PCI-selected RTX5080 observation with installed595.99.02, not an experimental module run. Numerical architecture/five sync+async sizes/cleanup pass, exit77/missingSM70/hybridfalse. The complete trace contains188 ALLOC/FREE records (100 ALLOC,88 FREE). Two NVOS64 ROOT inputs have class0, hRoot/parent/object0, NULL parameters/rights, paramsSize0 and flags0; both return transport0/status0, distinct nonzero hObjectNew and class0x41. Both use FD number8, which alone does not establish immutable file identity. This motivates multi-client frontend ownership rather than assuming one client per control file. One explicit root FREE uses parent==root and succeeds; the other client has no explicit observed root FREE. No leak is inferred: file/process cleanup is outside these packet records.

The initial summary v1 counted root FREE only with parent0 and missed this real operation. A failing regression reproduces the omission; v2 counts successful hRoot==hObjectOld independently of parent. Actual R580 `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/entry_points.c::_nv01FreeWithSecInfo` passes hRoot/hObjectOld to FreeWithSecInfo and ignores hObjectParent. Internal broker cleanup keeps its canonical parent0; the experimental frontend accepts parent0 or root. `escape.c::RmIoctl`, ROOT allocation switch, forces hClass to ROOT_CLIENT. The broker now publishes this normalized output after both backends confirm allocation. Four added CPU cases initially failed on missing alias normalization and now pass;86 broker scenarios total. This output has hardware evidence on installed595; the R580 correspondence is source evidence only.

The earlier RTX-only summary, dated 2026-10-19, is `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-root-payload-summary-v2-20261019.json`: two observed root inputs fit the broker's bounded slice, two confirmed root allocations and one confirmed explicit root free. It does not establish that both experimental RM backends can execute the packets, nor that other CUDA allocations/controls/UVM are routed.

## Matched V100 and Blackwell ROOT observations (2026-10-20)

Fresh installed-driver traces were captured on the V100 guest (R580.178.04, kernel6.8.0-147) and RTX5080 host (R595.99.02, kernel7.0.0-38). The raw reports are `evidence/pc3-v100-rm-ioctl-reference-v1-20261020.json` and `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-postcanary-reference-v1-20261020.json`; their typed summaries are `evidence/pc3-v100-rm-ioctl-summary-v1-20261020.json` and `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-postcanary-root-summary-v2-20261020.json`. Reproduction and normalized BDFs are in [comparison v3](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-root-protocol-comparison-v3-20261020.json).

Both ordinary probes made two successful NVOS64 ROOT requests with `hRoot=0`, class0 on entry, null nested parameters/rights, zero flags, and output class0x41. Each trace confirms one root FREE with `hObjectOld==hRoot` and parent==root. The V100 reference exits0. The RTX5080 per-device SM120 kernel and all five sync/async sizes pass, while the combined acceptance exits77 because that Linux instance has no SM70 device. No experimental backend was loaded.

The pinned R580 source audit `evidence/r580-client-domain-contract-20261018.json` says that under `RS_COMPATABILITY_MODE=1`, nonzero `hRoot` requests a root client ID and the server rejects duplicate IDs within one server. That is **static source evidence**. The matched traces contain only `hRoot=0`, so two bounded probes exercised the mirror packet on the installed servers in both directions. The first selected V100 output (`3251650101`) and sent it to RTX5080/R595; the second selected RTX output (`3251634254`) and sent it to V100/R580. Both NVOS64 requests returned the requested ID unchanged; transport and embedded statuses succeeded for both allocation and root FREE. The helpers and staging payloads were removed, and the GPU UUID/PCI/driver identities matched before and after. Reports: [V100 to RTX](../evidence/pc3-rm-root-id-v100-to-rtx5080-v3-20261020.json) and [RTX to V100](../evidence/pc3-rm-root-id-rtx5080-to-v100-v2-20261020.json). They hash-pin source traces, helpers, runners and remote scripts. Builder/probe/runner code is in `tools/build_rm_root_id_probe.py`, `src/rm_root_id_probe.c`, `tools/run_pc3_rm_root_id_probe.py` and `tools/run_pc3_v100_rm_root_id_probe.py`; the CPU contracts are `tests/test_rm_root_id_probe.py`, `tests/test_build_rm_root_id_probe.py`, `tests/test_pc3_rm_root_id_probe.py` and `tests/test_pc3_v100_rm_root_id_probe.py`.

The [live co-lifecycle reports](../evidence/pc3-live-v100-root-on-rtx5080-v3-20261020.json) and [reverse run](../evidence/pc3-live-rtx5080-root-on-v100-v1-20261020.json) now demonstrate both directions: each source ROOT remained live until the other installed server allocated and freed a ROOT_CLIENT with the same numeric ID. Both cleanup paths, control closes and before/after identities passed. This proves the packet behavior for simultaneous ROOT lifetimes across these installed R580 and R595 server instances. It does not validate the proposed same-version R580 proprietary/Open backends, private VFS/session cleanup, same-Linux device enumeration or CUDA. The comparison tool continues to report original ordinary-input shapes separately from these targeted mirror requests.

## Private proprietary backend port

`patches/r580.178.04/proprietary/0002–0009` port namespace/BDF guard/private chardev/resources/projection/control-session boundaries to the actual proprietary R580 wrapper. Original NVIDIA module license/notices and binary core remain unchanged. The flavor has94 original exports, including nv_vma_start_write (Open has93), and its original export flavors are preserved;15 P2P/RM exports differ from the Open flavor. Its IMEX class/device creation is stubbed originally; the port preserves those stubs.

Latest build `evidence/private-proprietary-session-v2-20261019.json`: hybrid580_legacy.ko SHA256 `6d6ea2efcdbd0739e5d34bfdbb2f89bc1bcf421d49fceccb5ac838b8c77a1f03`,100 private exports,303 resolved kernel imports and zero GPL-only. Seven ordinary VFS/rwlock imports are added; register_chrdev_region is removed.54 control-session,38 projection and19 PCI CPU scenarios execute the actual patched proprietary wrapper helpers with OS/RM substitutes. No proprietary RM function, VFS kernel path, GPU or module was run.

Objtool remains active:44,969 naked-return,1,758 frame-pointer and9 other warnings remain. These warnings cannot be fixed by the source-only Open RM compiler flag; no mitigation qualification or deployment readiness is claimed. Private UVM/modeset consumers, runtime coexistence and authentic VFS/teardown still require integration and hardware evidence.

## BDF-selected GPU-file route (2026-10-21)

The installed-driver traces showed `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD` on both `/dev/nvidia0` streams, but they retained no arguments or immutable Linux file identity. The Open R580.178.04 source in `build/experiments/private-open-gpu-session-v3/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/escape.c` provides the needed public source contract: `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD` checks the packet size, takes the RM API lock, resolves `params->ctl_fd` using `nv_get_file_private(..., NV_TRUE, ...)`, rejects repeated registration and stores the control file-private link. The call is valid on actual GPU files; its control-fd input must refer to a control file. R580 `NV_ESC_RM_CONTROL` resolves a GPU file with `NV_FALSE`. This source observation does not expose the proprietary core implementation, and the installed trace does not prove that the target frontend exercised this path.

The pinned R580.178.04 `nvidia_ioctl()` wrapper in `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c:2503-2550` separately unwraps `NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD`: after requiring the native 16-byte `nv_ioctl_xfer_t`, it copies `cmd`, `size`, and `ptr`, then copies the inner payload and dispatches by the inner command number. It has no lower-bound check requiring the payload to exceed `_IOC_SIZE` capacity, so a four-byte `nv_ioctl_register_fd_t` is accepted inside XFER and reaches the same `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD` source case. This proves wrapper-level possibility, not that stock libcuda sends this form in the captured workload or that the closed RM implementation behaves identically internally. The frontend now handles both envelopes before provider dispatch: it copies the XFER envelope and nested request to kernel locals, validates exact sizes and same-BDF/backend control ownership, then invokes the provider's typed registration API with that exact control session. The CPU harness includes both backends, fd reuse and user-memory mutation cases; no target VFS/RM execution has occurred.

The new frontend calls backend-local `gpu_session_create_by_bdf_v1`; the wrapper scans only its private minor table and rechecks the requested BDF around genuine `nvidia_open()`. It tries the proprietary backend first and queries Open only when the first backend returns `-EXDEV`. GPU and control descriptors deliberately share `hybrid_front_fops`: the projection provider has one registered fops key and selects the corresponding backend file from its `ctl` argument and an immutable file-kind tag. The GPU ioctl entry forwards to exactly one backend session, while `ctl=true` resolves the control descriptor to that same backend when `REGISTER_FD` asks for it. This is the intended source-level flow, not runtime proof.

`src/hybrid580_ioctl.h` exposes a versioned project-specific BDF/open request and private GPU-count/info queries on `/dev/hybrid580ctl`. The anonymous descriptor returned by that private request is not itself enumerable as `/dev/nvidiaN`; a separate standard-node path now registers major-195 `nvidiactl` and BDF-ordered `nvidiaN` devices. Its source routes standard control opens, `NV_ESC_CARD_INFO`, BDF-selected GPU files and selected typed operations, but this has not passed through a real Linux VFS, RM or `libcuda`. Generic RM controls, a complete child-handle map, RM event-object management and UVM runtime semantics remain open; the source-level UVM callback bridge is not runtime qualification. Narrow GPU `.mmap` and `.poll` callbacks are also compiled and modeled. `evidence/frontend-gpu-inventory-v6-20261021.json` is an earlier frontend build; `tests/test_hybrid_frontend.py` models both backend mmap and poll routes, VMA ownership, waitqueue references and fd/epoll-style lifetimes under UBSan. Beyond that frontend model, `tests/test_private_backend_file_projection.py` and `tests/test_private_proprietary580.py` execute the pinned R580 `NV_ESC_REGISTER_FD` source case and close cleanup against the actual patched Open and proprietary `nv_get_file_private`/projection wrapper code. Each backend harness covers11 cases, including wrong-backend control files, invalid inputs, repeat/cycle guards, reference retention through GPU lifetime and close cleanup. The harness models Linux `fget`/`fput`, module references and RM locks; it does not execute NVIDIA's case through a real kernel VFS, RM binary, module or GPU. The next source boundary is identifying the stock `libcuda` open/control sequence and implementing only the missing typed operations it requires.

## RM map-context and VMA file identity (2026-10-21)

The installed-driver RTX5080/R595 observer now assigns opaque identities to selected `open` and `dup` file lifetimes. Reuse of a numeric fd after `close` produces a new identity; `dup` preserves the source identity. The fresh report `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-map-mmap-v5-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `524dccc6abe9bf0bf35ad6e34ee22ded9b59699dbcfc4b8925f3d179ef379c0b`) records three successful GPU `NV_ESC_RM_MAP_MEMORY` packets, three successful `mmap` calls on matching GPU file identities and complete selected-fd lifecycle capture. The run exits77 because the installed probe also requires SM70, which the RTX-only host lacks; the RTX5080 reference kernel itself passes.

The Open R580 source in `build/experiments/private-open-gpu-session-v3/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia/nv-usermap.c:29` stores one mapping context on the selected backend file-private mapping list. Its `nv-mmap.c:738` `nvidia_mmap()` obtains `nv_linux_file_private_t` from the mmap callback's `struct file`, and helper `nv-mmap.c:506` looks up that file's context. R595 Open source has the same file-owned shape. Therefore the frontend's RM map ioctl projection and the later VMA callback must resolve to the same backend GPU file.

The frontend now uses `vma_set_file()` during initial VMA setup, invokes the selected backend file's native `.mmap`, and releases only its temporary reference. Linux v7.0's `mm/util.c` implementation holds the replacement reference and drops the old VMA file reference; Linux's dma-buf mmap path uses the same API before invoking the exporter. The exact target `Module.symvers` exports `vma_set_file`, and the linked frontend reports that import from `vmlinux`. The callback is serialized with GPU ioctls and rejects control files, interrupted lock acquisition, absent sessions and missing backend mmap operations.

CPU/UBSan scenarios send an `NVOS33+fd` packet through both selected backend routes, bind the mock RM mapping context to the session's native file, require that same file in `.mmap`, and verify that the VMA reference survives closing the frontend fd. Backend mmap failure, control-file rejection and interrupted locking are also covered. The build and model do not execute Linux VFS or NVIDIA RM. Stock-driver captures now exist on both RTX5080/R595 and V100/R580: each records three successful map-to-mmap pairs with complete opaque file identity tracking, and the V100 run also passes its SM70 numerical probe. No frontend page fault, VMA clone/split, frontend unmap/close ordering, UVM, `libcuda`, or hybrid CUDA result is claimed.

The guarded V100 runner's isolated `--map-memory` mode validates observer schema v13, exact payload redaction, complete selected-file identity capture, fd open/dup/close invariants and map-to-mmap correlation using the same contract as the RTX5080 runner. The current [V100/R580 capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-map-mmap-v2-20261021.json), mode0600, contains25 map packets,25 mmap calls,23 successful unmap packets and three correlated GPU-file pairs. The five-size SM70 probe and cleanup pass under the installed guest R580.178.04 stack; guest kernel is6.8.0-147 and Driver API13000. The report pins the exact runner source SHA-256 `a4b11606b0c3652e0d4b60f79c8a8ee5017b91c630b28e6f973fef87b86cc9dc`. The [RTX5080/R595 report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-map-mmap-v5-20261021.json) has26 map/mmap calls and the same three-pair correlation; its SM120 path passes, with exit77 solely because that combined probe requires the absent SM70 image. Both branches observed the same fixed map/unmap request and packet-size shapes, while allocation lengths and flags vary. This is evidence about these captured installed-driver paths, not a universal cross-branch ABI claim and not execution of the frontend. V1 is retained as the earlier capture.

## GPU-file poll and waitqueue ownership (2026-10-21)

Pinned Open R580.178.04, proprietary R580.178.04 and Open R595.99.02 sources all install `nvidia_poll` in their GPU-file operations. The callback obtains `nv_linux_file_private_t` from its `file`, checks open/GPU-loss state, uses that file's `f_flags & O_NONBLOCK`, and calls `poll_wait(file, &nvlfp->waitqueue, wait)` for blocking descriptors before reading/clearing event state under its spinlock. This supports a per-selected-backend callback; forwarding only the readiness bits or registering a frontend-owned queue would lose native RM state and wakeup semantics.

The frontend now obtains one temporary reference to the BDF-selected session file, mirrors the frontend's current `O_NONBLOCK` bit to that backend open-file description with `READ_ONCE`/`WRITE_ONCE`, calls its native `.poll` with the original `poll_table`, then drops the temporary reference. It takes no transaction mutex. The session itself owns the backend file throughout frontend lifetime. Linux v7.0 `select.c`'s default poll qproc pins the `file` passed to `poll_wait` until `poll_freewait`; `eventpoll.c` stores the waitqueue and owning epitem, while the epitem retains the file registered by the user. This explains why the direct call must pass the backend file to native `poll_wait` while the frontend fops remains the epoll-visible file. Source references: R580 Open `nvidia_poll` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/experiments/openrm580-baseline-v1/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c`), R595 Open `nvidia_poll` (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `build/research/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/source/open-gpu-kernel-modules-595.99.02/kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c`), [Linux v7.0 `select.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v7.0/fs/select.c) and [Linux v7.0 `eventpoll.c`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v7.0/fs/eventpoll.c).

UBSan models cover both backend masks, no-callback error, private-control readiness, blocking/select wait-file references, `O_NONBLOCK` on/off and epoll-style frontend reference retention after fd close. The current Linux7.0.0-38 build [`frontend-gpu-poll-v3`](../evidence/frontend-gpu-poll-v3-20261021.json) resolves all imports and has no objtool/naked-return warnings; the pahole version difference is retained. This does not establish that the vector probe called poll, execute epoll or select in a real process, or verify event posting, waitqueue wakeup, POLLFREE, RM file close or VFS teardown. No experimental module was loaded.

## NV_ESC_CARD_INFO and the next enumeration boundary

The pinned Open R580.178.04 wrapper in `build/experiments/private-open-gpu-session-v3/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c` routes `NV_ESC_CARD_INFO` only after `NV_CTL_DEVICE_ONLY(nv)`. It derives the array capacity from the ioctl byte size and calls `nvidia_read_card_info()`. That routine holds `nv_linux_devices_lock`, rejects a buffer smaller than the backend's `num_nv_devices`, skips `NV_FLAG_EXCLUDE`, and returns the backend's `gpu_id`, PCI/resource fields and `nvl->minor_num`. These are observations in the open wrapper source; they do not reveal proprietary-core internals or prove the hybrid route at runtime.

Consequence: a naïve merge of two backend `CARD_INFO` arrays can publish duplicate local minors and backend-private GPU IDs. The frontend maps `minor_number` to the BDF-sorted frontend index and assigns a nonzero synthetic `gpu_id`; this translation is now reached through both the project-private control descriptor and the registered standard `/dev/nvidiactl` path. Other controls consuming GPU IDs still need an explicit route, and neither path has been exercised through the target VFS/RM.

Both private R580 backends now export a versioned inventory API that snapshots initialized PCI entries under that backend's device-list lock, excluding non-PCI and `NV_FLAG_EXCLUDE` entries. The frontend calls the APIs separately on each open, merges them through `src/hybrid580-gpu-catalog.c`, and exposes private versioned count/info queries. The merge validates BDF/PCI consistency and NVIDIA vendor identity, bounds each backend-local minor to0–247, rejects duplicate BDF ownership and per-backend minor collisions, then assigns deterministic BDF-sorted front indices. UBSan and CPU-model tests cover invalid data, duplicates, empty inventories, API errors and query failures; the kernel module build report is `evidence/frontend-gpu-inventory-v2-20261021.json`.

The snapshots are immutable for the life of that frontend control file, but the two backend reads are not one atomic transaction and may become stale after hotplug. The BDF-open path rechecks the selected device against the live backend table. The catalog translates attach requests and supplies the typed metadata routes. The frontend now registers standard major-195 nodes, reserving GPU minors `195:0–247` and control minor `195:255` separately so the NVKMS modeset minor `195:254` remains available. Pinned R580 headers define those ranges, and pinned `nvidia-modeset-linux.c` reserves `195:254`; the frontend and local fixture agree on the non-overlapping map. The current Kbuild report binds the compiled module to the exact source hash, and the CPU/UBSan fixture simulates NVKMS owning the modeset minor while checking ten initialization and unwind cases. This establishes a source/build/model result only: no VFS open or real character-device registration has run. `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2`, `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS`, and `CARD_INFO` have standard-node source routes, but general RM controls, libcuda enumeration and CUDA usability remain unvalidated. A full CUDA-relevant control audit remains necessary.

## NV_ESC_CHECK_VERSION_STR provider consensus (compiled, CPU-modeled)

Pinned R580.178.04 declares nv_ioctl_rm_api_version_t as a fixed 72-byte packet: 32-bit command, 32-bit reply, and a 64-byte version string. The source wrapper handles this control on the NVIDIA control device and calls rm_perform_version_check(). The authored provider patches [0043-private-open-version-check.patch](../patches/r580.178.04/0043-private-open-version-check.patch) and [0043-private-legacy-version-check.patch](../patches/r580.178.04/proprietary/0043-private-legacy-version-check.patch) expose typed kernel-buffer entry points to the same-version Open and proprietary RMs; they do not pass a user pointer into either backend.

The R580 frontend copies and size-checks the packet, duplicates it for each provider, serializes the operation with the per-open transaction lock, and compares both transport results plus all 72 reply bytes. Inspection of pinned R580 kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c and the RM version-check implementation shows that the native wrapper copies the packet back for a version mismatch too; it returns -EINVAL while retaining RM’s updated version string. The frontend therefore copies out when both providers return the same native result and identical packets, including matching rejection replies. Different statuses or bytes fail closed without copyout. It supports both the direct size-encoded ioctl and nested NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD forms. tests/test_hybrid_frontend.py models matching success, matching native rejection with response copyout, disagreement, malformed sizes, copy faults, and XFER dispatch; the provider CPU harness checks status/error cleanup. The rejection test failed before the fix and passed after it. Exact build and software-suite reports are listed in [STATE](STATE.md) and [VALIDATION](VALIDATION.md).

Qualification stops at source, compile, and CPU-model evidence. The route has not executed through Linux VFS, either experimental RM module, installed R580 libcuda, or a GPU. It does not establish cuInit, enumeration, context creation, or CUDA compatibility by itself.

## GPU-ID and attachment routing audit (static, incomplete)

Pinned source: Open R580.178.04 in `build/experiments/private-open-gpu-inventory-v2/NVIDIA-kernel-module-source-580.178.04/`. This establishes the Open wrapper/core call paths and public R580 declarations; it does not prove identical proprietary-core internals or runtime behavior.

| Interface | Source observation | Hybrid consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `NV_ESC_CARD_INFO` | `kernel-open/nvidia/nv.c:nvidia_read_card_info()` (around lines 2318–2364) returns `gpu_id`, `minor_number`, PCI/resource values, and skips `NV_FLAG_EXCLUDE`; its ioctl handler is control-file-only. | Source routes the merged records through both `/dev/hybrid580ctl` and the registered `/dev/nvidiactl` control-file handler, checking backend records against the catalog and substituting frontend minor/GPU ID. This has not been exercised through VFS or a real RM. |
| `NV_ESC_ATTACH_GPUS_TO_FD` | `nv.c` (around lines 2525–2588) accepts a nonempty array of `NvU32`, rejects a second attachment on the same control file, stores the array, skips ID zero, and calls `nvidia_dev_get()` for every nonzero occurrence; it does not deduplicate repeated IDs. Failure releases earlier refs in that call; control close later calls `nvidia_dev_put()` for stored IDs (around lines 3136–3147). | The frontend translates IDs and calls a shared native-equivalent pin/rollback helper on disposable backend control sessions. It publishes both candidate sessions only after all nonempty partitions succeed. A failure leaves the prior pair selected and quarantines further stateful use; candidate `fput` cleanup can be deferred. This is compiled and CPU/source-tested, not a proof of VFS/RM runtime rollback. |
| `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` (`0x201`) | `ctrl0000gpu.h` defines a fixed 32-ID response. `client_resource.c:1375–1385` dispatches it to `gpumgrGetAttachedGpuIds()`; `gpu_mgr.c:2318–2340` returns that RM instance's attached IDs and fills unused entries with `NV0000_CTRL_GPU_INVALID_ID` (`0xffffffff`). | Implemented on the private control descriptor: query both R580 sessions, require contiguous nonzero unique backend-local IDs that exactly match the catalog, and emit frontend IDs in BDF order. Stale or over-capacity results fail closed. The public ABI remains limited to32 IDs even if the private catalog is larger. |
| `NV0000_CTRL_CMD_GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` (`0x205`) | In pinned R580.196.07 and R595.99.02, `client_resource.c` calls `gpumgrGetGpuIdInfoV2()`, which calls `gpumgrGetGpuFromId()`. That lookup scans `gpumgrGetGpuAttachInfo()`'s Resource-Manager-wide GPU mask and resolves an initialized GPU's backend-local `gpuId`; absent IDs return `NV_ERR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`. `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS` separately returns the RM-wide valid device-instance mask. This is distinct from the Linux `NV_ESC_ATTACH_GPUS_TO_FD` ioctl, which tracks references for a control file. | The frontend maps every catalog GPU through its owning RM, then builds a BDF-ordered device-instance namespace only when metadata exactly agrees with both provider masks. The source path supports this complete-catalog query model; hardware execution through the experimental frontend remains unvalidated. |

The zero test in `NV_ESC_ATTACH_GPUS_TO_FD` makes zero unsuitable as a synthetic frontend GPU ID; the RM's public invalid-ID sentinel is `0xffffffff`. The catalog assigns a bounded nonzero ID from deterministic order and resolves it to its owning backend and local ID. `hybrid580_gpu_catalog_plan_attach_v1()` preserves request order and repeated IDs while retaining zero placeholders in the frontend view and omitting them from backend partitions, matching the native skip-zero loop. The attached-ID request list is bounded by496 entries, below the pinned `NV_ABSOLUTE_MAX_IOCTL_SIZE`. The attach route is now connected to both backend APIs and the frontend, but remains only compiled/source/CPU-tested. The source also contains other RM controls that call `gpumgrGetGpuFromId()` or accept GPU-ID arrays (including SVM/P2P-related handlers in `src/nvidia/src/kernel/rmapi/client_resource.c`); the relevant CUDA call surface has not yet been fully classified. Blindly forwarding a root control to both backends would be unsafe because both have independent GPU-manager and client state.

Fresh installed-driver observation closes one earlier payload gap. The RTX5080/R595 trace [v3](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-attached-ids-observation-v3-20261021.json) records `GPU_GET_ID_INFO_V2` and `GPU_GET_ATTACHED_IDS` twice each, both successful, returning backend ID512 and `[512, 0xffffffff, ...]`. The V100 guest/R580 trace [v2](../evidence/pc3-v100-gpu-id-and-attached-ids-observation-v2-20261021.json) likewise records both commands twice, returning ID5 and `[5, 0xffffffff, ...]`. The command number, packet size and fixed payload shape match; the backend IDs are branch-local and differ. Static patched `nv.c` inventory adapters source `backend_gpu_id` from `nv->gpu_id`; the installed observations are consistent with that namespace use. This is not proof that the experimental R580 backends execute correctly, nor that IDs from separate drivers can be merged without the BDF catalog.

The pinned RTX5080 (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json.strace.gz`) and V100 guest (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/pc3-v100-guest-full-ioctl-trace-v2-20261020.json.strace.gz`) single-GPU CUDA captures contain no ioctl whose `_IOC_NR` is `NV_IOCTL_BASE + 12` (`212`, `NV_ESC_ATTACH_GPUS_TO_FD`). The new typed captures observe ID-info and attached-ID only; they also do not include an attach call. These tests validate one selected GPU each on separate Linux kernels. The absence remains inconclusive about multi-GPU initialization order and does not establish when attachment occurs relative to ROOT/client creation.

The attach, card-info, ID-info, attached-ID, REGISTER_FD, and device-instance-map contracts cover backend errors, candidate creation failure, direct/XFER forms, stale metadata, bad IDs, copy faults, fd projection, held references, mask/group consistency and ordering against ROOT/GPU operations. The earlier pure map core has since been extended into typed private backend and frontend `MAP_MEMORY`/`UNMAP_MEMORY` routes with target-file mmap binding; the current build/CPU evidence is recorded below. This does not establish Linux VFS or RM runtime behavior. Installed metadata and allocation headers constrain the public ABI, but do not establish Linux file close timing or prove disposable sessions match files used by later CUDA calls. The typed `GPU_GET_DEVICE_IDS`/catalog mapping and `NV01_DEVICE_0` child-handle ownership route have since been implemented and CPU-modeled. Runtime mask meaning, nested allocation bytes, real VFS/RM child-handle mapping and CUDA execution remain unvalidated. Existing observations are single-GPU runs; generic RM controls must never be broadcast across both backends.

## `NV20_SUBDEVICE_0` and observed child classes (2026-10-21)

The fixed-header RM allocation summary `evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-rm-alloc-header-summary-v1-20261021.json` records one class `0x2080` object under `NV01_DEVICE_0` (`0x80`) on each installed-driver path. The parent/class edge counts are header-only: no handles or nested `NV2080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` bytes were retained. The same R580/R595 command comparison reports six V100 and seven RTX5080 successful calls to `NV2080_CTRL_CMD_GR_GET_TPC_MASK` (`0x2080122b`), each with `params_size=24` and embedded status0. This directly establishes the observed fixed shape, not that the new private adapter works on hardware.

The R580.178.04 SDK defines `NV2080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS.subDeviceId` and the fixed 24-byte GR mask control. The frontend chooses a single PCI subdevice (`subDeviceId == 0`) and now binds its tracked frontend handle to the owning backend's returned RM handle. The TPC query is a command-specific stack-buffer wrapper using `NV04_CONTROL` and `KERNEL_POINTER_TO_NvP64`; no generic user pointer or arbitrary control is passed through. Open/proprietary modules and their common frontend compile with the exact new exports/imports documented in `docs/VALIDATION.md`.

The header-only child-class edges reveal additional work. Both GPUs allocate `NV2081_BINAPI` (`0x2081`) under the subdevice. The V100 summary additionally records `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` (`0x503c`), `GF100_SUBDEVICE_MASTER` (`0x90e6`) and `VOLTA_USERMODE_A` (`0xc361`); RTX5080 records `RM_USER_SHARED_DATA` (`0xde`) and `HOPPER_USERMODE_A` (`0xc661`). The R580 SDK describes the `NV2081` allocation structure as a single reserved `NvU32`, and `resource_list.h` marks the payload optional; this is static source/layout evidence, not an observed payload value. For P2P, the installed V100 capture now records the exact four-byte flags payload (`1`, BAR1) at entry and exit. A separate installed V100 control capture records successful VA-space registration (`0x503c0102`), vidmem registration (`0x503c0104`), and vidmem unregister (`0x503c0105`). Both are stock-driver probes that also pass ordinary sync/async numerical kernels, so requests and kernels co-occur; neither trace proves the P2P requests are required for CUDA initialization. The typed `NV2081_BINAPI` and branch-specific usermode routes remain unqualified through Linux VFS/RM; the Volta master and P2P routes have narrow source/CPU/build coverage but no experimental runtime proof.

## `RM_USER_SHARED_DATA` class payload (2026-10-21)

The pinned R580.178.04 `cl00de.h` and R595.99.02 `cl00de.h` were independently measured by C against their own SDK include roots. Both define the RUSD class payload as eight bytes, with `polledDataMask` at offset0 and identical seven polling-bit constants. This supports decoding that field from these pinned interfaces; it does not show that all releases or private core implementations preserve the same ABI.

The new observer mode reads only this typed field for a successful class `0xDE` allocation, at ioctl entry and exit. It rejects a null/invalid nested pointer, snapshots only the declared eight bytes, omits pointer and handle values, and emits a fixed RUSD-only scope. The RUSD mode was exercised against the installed RTX5080/R595 stack: the standard selected CUDA probe produced one successful allocation, and both snapshots reported `polledDataMask=0`. The same mode on the separate V100/R580 guest completed with no class `0xDE` allocations and no nested reads while the SM70 probe passed. These are observations of the current vector-probe path; they do not prove other applications never allocate RUSD or that the mask is semantically unused.

The new [RTX5080 RUSD+map lifecycle capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-rusd-map-lifecycle-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 3e7de1db18d95c6fb45393f7c2511e80709871c0e2115daff96571d330989f6c, refines that conclusion. A dedicated [summary tool](../tools/summarize_rusd_map_mmap_lifecycle.py) reproduces the correlation into a counts-only [report](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-rusd-map-mmap-correlation-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 3d4657af8a7ec7b647787b2d8270a1c8524ef0ce14c1ac4363707784db32b218; it validates the exact documented SM70-missing exit 77 and rejects other partial results. On stock R595.99.02, the selected CUDA path performs one successful class-0xDE allocation and one successful class-resolved RUSD MAP_MEMORY operation; the opaque backend-file identity on that map matches one successful mmap record with the same file role. The trace contains 26 map and mmap calls total. It is a single RTX5080/R595 host observation, not a hybrid frontend test. The separate V100/R580 default probe contains no RUSD allocation, so the route is optional for the observed V100 path, not proven unnecessary for other workloads.

The common frontend now supports this narrowly typed class allocation. It validates the RUSD class and tracked parent ownership, copies the fixed eight-byte SDK payload, and dispatches through the selected R580 backend. The Open wrapper uses the Open RM allocation entry; the proprietary wrapper uses the legacy ioctl path. Actual backend wrappers were compiled and executed in CPU/UBSan harnesses, and the frontend was compiled with both RUSD providers resolved. This does not test the native RM implementations, Linux VFS, user mmap, GPU memory, or CUDA through the experimental modules. The measured map/mmap pair supports retaining the typed route; it does not establish UVM behavior or a performance benefit.

The opaque 992-byte NV2081_BINAPI control remains denied: its inner serialization, pointer ownership, and dispatch contract are unresolved. Its typed allocation constructor is separate from the denied method. Do not infer that allocating the BINAPI object makes the control safe to forward.

## R580 `NV_ESC_RM_MAP_MEMORY` source path and hybrid adapter (2026-10-21)

Pinned Open R580.178.04 `src/nvidia/arch/nvalloc/unix/src/escape.c:513-549` establishes the native order: control-file-only check; force default caching type; call `Nv04MapMemoryWithSecInfo`; on embedded success call `rm_create_mmap_context()` with the ROOT/device/memory, virtual address, length, offset, cache type and the packet's fd; if context installation fails, call `Nv04UnmapMemoryWithSecInfo` to roll back. `osapi.c:2231-2384` creates the `nv_usermap_access_params_t`, validates system-memory or BAR mapping, and attaches the context to the selected file. `rm_create_mmap_context()` at lines2394-2434 requires `pRmClient->ProcID == osGetCurrentProcess()` before the file-context operation. This confirms that the frontend map operation must run synchronously in the same process and preserve the target backend file identity; it does not establish that all NVOS33 variants or cross-version RM internals are equivalent.

The hybrid private backend bridges pass only kernel-owned fixed packets through native `rm_ioctl()` while holding the selected control-session lock and existing PM/stack protections. They return the transport `NV_STATUS` separately from `NVOS33.status`/`NVOS34.status`. The common frontend resolves tracked object ownership and translates root/device/memory handles, reserves a per-target-file map slot, and only exposes a successful map to userspace after copyout. The mmap callback then switches the VMA to the backend file whose RM map context was installed. Static code and CPU models support the intended routing; the native proprietary RM implementation and real VFS callback have not run under the hybrid modules.

Open backend report `evidence/private-open-map-memory-v1-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `a72f9ebff97885c565e93e7214efe1eaa20ac84dc699a38da81aa66748432505`, compiled module SHA-256 `8ff2c7e0e2a7934b58f8f6ddfe44a6f3d7f2ea03f54803c0317be99bb3d3cba2`. Proprietary report `evidence/private-proprietary-map-memory-v3-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `e04d3d226ffd04f388496d7fa191fd335076c0984c1a4c06c5495942202cde84`, compiled module SHA-256 `fcdcd247e05067caf372edf77f6cc692c6c91b9f7beacc8123f5e66eef60425c`. Frontend report `evidence/frontend-map-memory-v3-20261021.json`, SHA-256 `ef1fbb0f4b3d46355e17eb86a9918c3f0f62eaa00800cbeb57affd004f8745cd`, module SHA-256 `aaa54efe3fe5e0d6657303f4fec04548b4b1e6fde247bdaf3ba547fe80fceeca`. The full 385-test CPU/source suite passes; all outputs remain unloaded and uninstalled.

## V100 `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` control audit and bounded route (2026-10-21)

The stock V100/R580 allocation capture has one successful `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` request (`0x503c`) with a 48-byte outer NVOS64 packet, outer `paramsSize=0`, and a present nested parameter pointer. The nested four-byte SDK payload was snapshotted at entry and exit and decodes to `flags=1` (BAR1, no unknown bits); the syscall and RM status were zero. The same selected V100 probe passed ordinary synchronous/asynchronous numeric kernels and cleanup. Its control capture has one successful request each for `0x503c0102` (register VA space), `0x503c0104` (register vidmem), and `0x503c0105` (unregister vidmem); its standard probe does not show VA-space unregister. Each control payload was snapshotted only under its exact typed SDK size, with pointer/address redaction. The separate RTX5080/R595 standard probe contains no `0x503c` request; that absence is workload-specific, not a Blackwell capability conclusion. The V100 guest and RTX5080 host were on separate Linux kernels.

Pinned R580.178.04 `cl503c.h` defines the flags types proprietary=0, BAR1=1, NVLINK=2. `resource_list.h` marks the class as an optional-parameter child of Subdevice. The same class/control/resource implementation files have identical SHA-256 values in the inspected R580.196.07 source. `ctrl503c.h` describes vidmem registration as required to expose memory via third-party P2P APIs; `third_party_p2p.c` validates parent/device/type and maintains per-resource VA/vidmem records; `third_party_p2p_ctrl.c` checks FBMEM, alignment, overflow, and allocation bounds; `p2p.c` consumes the registered token/range to obtain pages. This supports a P2P-specific function, but the observed single-GPU CUDA probe's co-occurrence means necessity cannot be decided without a counterfactual.

The evidence record [`r580-third-party-p2p-necessity-audit-v1-20261030.json`](../evidence/r580-third-party-p2p-necessity-audit-v1-20261030.json) pins the two historical captures, probe binary, seven source files, source-version declarations, observed payloads, and current hybrid-route boundary. Discriminating experiment: on the stock V100, run a fresh-process baseline, then reject only class `0x503c` allocation and compare `cuInit`, context creation, device allocation, numerical kernel, and cleanup. If that succeeds, run independent fresh-process trials that allow allocation but reject each observed control (`0102`, `0104`, `0105`) separately. Do not treat a passing one-GPU probe as evidence of actual peer transfer. A separate two-GPU/topology test is required for P2P behavior.

Pinned Open R580 `src/nvidia/src/kernel/gpu/bus/third_party_p2p_ctrl.c` shows: VA registration obtains a token from `CliAddThirdPartyP2PVASpace`; unregister removes by handle; vidmem registration resolves the memory handle on the P2P object's device, rejects non-FBMEM, zero/unaligned ranges, overflow and ranges beyond the allocation, then indexes the registered mapping; unregister removes by memory handle. `third_party_p2p.c` maintains these maps per P2P resource and rejects duplicate keys. The proprietary source bridge does not reimplement those internals: it validates the command-specific fixed structure size and invokes the native `NV04_CONTROL` RM path with a kernel buffer under the established control-session and PM protections.

The frontend dispatch is restricted to those four pointer-free commands, tracked R580 P2P/VASpace/local-user objects in one client/device, direct and XFER envelopes, and 64-KiB/alignment/overflow guards. Its aggregate reference counters block FREE until successful unregister; transport ambiguity and output-copy failure quarantine the session. RM still checks actual FBMEM type and allocation bounds. PID registration remains rejected because its privilege semantics do not fit this frontend. There is deliberately no Open/Blackwell `NV50_THIRD_PARTY_P2P` control export or frontend route. The hybrid UVM device-pair path currently returns `UVM_LINK_TYPE_NONE` for cross-provider pairs and denies cross-provider P2P-object creation; actual mixed-provider P2P is therefore unsupported by the experiment today.

`patches/r580.178.04/proprietary/0029-private-legacy-p2p-controls.patch` adds four typed exports without modifying the proprietary binary RM core or changing its NVIDIA module license. Open, proprietary and frontend modules compile; only frontend C/UBSan doubles execute the new path. The four added scenarios in `tests/test_hybrid_frontend.py` check handle ownership, direct/XFER calls, reference-held frees, embedded errors, transport quarantine, retry and copyout failure. Full suite v83 passes411 tests; see [validation](VALIDATION.md). These results establish neither hardware execution through the private stack nor actual GPU-to-GPU P2P.

## `NV2081_BINAPI` observed control: correlated object, unresolved payload (2026-10-21)

The complete installed-driver traces now correlate one `0x20810108` RM_CONTROL to a live `NV2081_BINAPI` object (`0x2081`) on both the V100/R580.178.04 guest and RTX5080/R595.99.02 host. In each trace, the outer packet declares 992 bytes, has flags0, the ioctl returns0, and the outer `NVOS54.status` is0. The observer records only the fixed envelope; it did not inspect nested payload bytes or pointers. This establishes transport and outer RM_CONTROL success, but does not establish the semantics of any result field inside the opaque BINAPI payload. The sanitized correlations are `evidence/binapi-v100-object-correlation-v2-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `c71d994fbd86b4ed822fb3fa81b12530ce17b673a36dc4f14636eca4f845b43d`) and `evidence/binapi-rtx5080-object-correlation-v2-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `b48b03fac79f2c4d46d1b557972fe12659802be34ddd1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`). These are separate Linux kernels and stock drivers.

The pinned R580 and R595 SDK probes agree that `NV2081_BINAPI` aliases the generic `FINN_RM_API` interface. Neither SDK has a `cl2081.finn` input, a message-08 structure, or the observed literal in its public BINAPI headers. The `resource_list.h` entry maps external class `NV2081_BINAPI` to internal `BinaryApi`, parent `Subdevice`, with optional `NV2081_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` and `RS_FLAGS_ALLOC_RPC_TO_PHYS_RM`, `...TO_VGPU_HOST`, and `...GSP_PLUGIN_FOR_VGPU_GSP`. Those are allocation flags, not proof that this control reaches physical RM. The source control handler calls physical RM only for a firmware-client GPU; on the ordinary physical path its initialized status remains `NV_OK` without a call. The generic FINN header is 32 bytes, not the observed 992-byte outer buffer; the outer FINN-serialized flag (bit0x4) is clear. The 992-byte buffer is not established to begin with that header, and its pointers, ownership, version rules, and message semantics remain unknown. The v4 report adds this resource-registration source evidence and is current: `evidence/binapi-control-boundary-v4-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `d333e36115f79f71765cbe61e1ab550c1659e055aef03d11277f0bceae0a9ba7`); v3 is preserved as the earlier report.

The proprietary R580 core object is ELF64 x86-64 ET_REL, SHA-256 `5269a23ffba19a17fe16f076463e7ad4151254659441d1711bee6c6221ff6f4b`. REA6.7.0 using GNU objdump retained file/section-header Evidence IDs in `evidence/rea-private-nv-kernel-file-headers-v1-20261021.json` and `...section-headers-v1-20261021.json`. A bounded REA/Ghidra12.1.4 analysis of local symbol `_nv020818rm` at `0x00b41e90` reconstructs only `return 0x56`; the exact R580 SDK defines `0x56` as `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED`. The pseudocode is a decompiler reconstruction, not source. The four REA xref source addresses (`0x6caeb95`, `0x6caebb0`, `0x6caee23`, `0x6cafe00`) map into `.text` after accounting for Ghidra's ET_REL section placement: subtracting Ghidra `.text` start `0x616f810` yields ELF offsets `0xb3f385`, `0xb3f3a0`, `0xb3f613`, and `0xb405f0`. All four lie in local function `_nv005865rm`. A fresh ELF relocation audit finds three `R_X86_64_32S` `.rela.text` entries with `.text` addend `0xb41e90`, at offsets `0xb3f388`, `0xb3f61a`, and `0xb405f7`. Limited disassembly context shows the target address loaded/conditionally selected and stored at object-field offset `+0x240`; this is a callback/function-pointer registration candidate, with field semantics inferred. The no-analysis Ghidra pass establishes section mapping only; the xrefs come from the separate REA result. None of this proves that the callback is invoked or that public command `0x20810108` reaches it. Corrected classification: `evidence/rea-private-nv2081-method08-xref-classification-v3-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `d91e31b3f4aa7648bd63dc181995336fe3684064d4934287e7f049be5111558e`); static relocation audit v2: `evidence/rea-private-nv2081-method08-static-references-v2-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `3358ce23f1a58c87e96f1107903c85f5c99ac6d4f081ede3dd015e599781720b`). The previous v1 raw-VMAs classification is superseded. Ghidra and its JDK remain isolated under `build/tools/`; system Java, REA, and Codex settings were not modified.

Read-only PC3 `libcuda.so.595.99.02` metadata inspection observed SHA-256 `192d4eedb1b39f0568f2b5423d35227d13b32b9025599e1968a9081982da207d`, build ID `d1f87d481dafdcf05cb82430fdee0d8ed8f6b239`, 738 dynamic exports, and no static symbol table. Searches found no BINAPI/FINN/20810108/NV2081 text markers. The 91,505,672-byte library was not copied, and no workload was run by this inspection; absent marker strings do not prove the ABI is absent. Full facts are in `evidence/pc3-rtx5080-libcuda-static-inspection-v1-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `889a408978b08a912817ee4678ac12accf94674aa86fbe61c11920d224b4cc92`).

Observer schema v18 now has a deliberately bounded `--binapi-control` mode. It is isolated with `--rm-control`, requires the explicitly selected GPU descriptor, and reads exactly the fixed 992-byte top-level buffer only after the object registry resolves the live handle to class `0x2081`. It never follows any pointer contained in that buffer. It emits only a 248-slot nonzero-word bitmask and an entry/exit changed-byte bitmask/count, then clears transient raw buffers. The RTX5080 and V100 guarded runners validate the exact schema, class, command, sizes, masks, counts, selected descriptor path, redaction, and empty-observation semantics.

On 2026-10-21, each of six fresh-process phases (initialize, enumerate, context, allocation, module, kernel) was paired with one fixed `0x20810108` capture on each installed reference. All twelve records resolve to class `0x2081`, report a 992-byte payload, zero entry words, zero changed bytes, and equal entry/exit payloads; all phase and cleanup statuses pass. The exact report hashes are indexed in `evidence/pc3-cuda-phase-matrix-v1-20261021.json` (SHA-256 `f9dd3bca741f3b78b8e3445c4830907efe38e1469783dd6f3eb6023a2ad2be16`). This is evidence for the ordinary probe lifecycle on separate kernels, not the full BINAPI contract. It does not justify forwarding. The experimental frontend's `binapi-control-denied` CPU/UBSan scenario continues to verify `-ENOTTY` without nested-parameter reads or backend callbacks. Keep the method denied until a typed contract, status semantics, object ownership, and backend version behavior are established; do not widen the allowlist based only on outer sizes, callback registration hints, or ioctl transport status.

## Paired `NV01_DEVICE_0` payload capture (2026-10-21)

The isolated context phase now reads the exact fixed nested allocation payload through schema-v19's redacted observer. The RTX5080/R595 report is [`pc3-rtx5080-device-alloc-context-v1`](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-device-alloc-context-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `d053b6fcd486635e28b711d295215a9884d0fca2b736c00f60da6593a17db311`; the V100/R580 guest report is [`pc3-v100-device-alloc-context-v1`](../evidence/pc3-v100-device-alloc-context-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `871944344f3a534cb0d092419a5187d7c577b98cbc32d7ceeec1779205b9320f`. Both context operations passed and cleaned up. Each trace contains exactly one successful NVOS64 allocation request `0xc030462b`, 48-byte outer packet, class `0x80`, and two stable snapshots of `NV0080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` (56 bytes).

All emitted typed fields agree: `deviceId=0`, `hClientShare=same-as-root`, zero target client/device, flags0, `vaSpaceSize=0`, VA start/limit recorded only as zero predicates, and `vaMode=2`. No handle or VA value is present in either report. `tools/compare_nv0080_device_alloc.py` validates the outer device/driver/phase identities, calls the shared schema-v19 observer validator, and records per-field differences without converting them into a compatibility claim. Its [comparison result](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-device-alloc-comparison-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `ea196dde44e78fc84e7f5d20e7ca3cbd66bad103eac97e9551abd49bb8994e35`, reports an exact match and the same observer-binary hash.

This closes the payload-shape question for one ordinary context-creation path on each installed stack. It does not establish matching RM object semantics, the real same-version R580 proprietary/Open execution, or a shared Linux CUDA process. Further work follows the measured frontend VFS/RM dispatch and typed `NV01_DEVICE_0` seam: audit every field forwarded by the bridge, add negative CPU/UBSan cases for unsupported inputs, then inspect the next observed `NV20_SUBDEVICE_0` allocation before any future RM call.

## Paired `NV20_SUBDEVICE_0` allocation payload (2026-10-21)

The schema-v20 observer snapshots only the fixed `NV2080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` for a live `NV20_SUBDEVICE_0` (`0x2080`) allocation.
