It accepts the exact 64-bit NVOS64 layout and fixed 4-byte class payload (or the documented null/zero-size form), captures entry and exit, and emits only `classPayloadBytes` and `subDeviceId`; handles and internal addresses are omitted. It never follows pointers. Pinned SDK compile-time checks establish `sizeof(NV2080_ALLOC_PARAMETERS)==4` and `offsetof(subDeviceId)==0`.

Both installed references successfully allocated one subdevice during isolated CUDA context creation. RTX5080/R595.99.02 and V100/R580.178.04 each observed request `0xc030462b`, a 48-byte outer packet, payload size4, `subDeviceId=0`, stable entry/exit values, and zero syscall/embedded status. The [paired comparison](../evidence/pc3-volta-blackwell-subdevice-alloc-comparison-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `db86fcbd5544ab0512c23671bce1db21825c830b744fb8a0a41280e1552b2b6a`, finds the emitted field and observer-binary hash identical. The [V100 capture](../evidence/pc3-v100-subdevice-alloc-context-v1-20261021.json) and [RTX5080 capture](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-subdevice-alloc-context-v1-20261021.json) are mode0600 and hashed in [validation](VALIDATION.md).

This records one common observed constructor value and payload shape; it does not establish identical `Subdevice` semantics, topology, backend-local identifiers, or compatibility of RM object graphs. In particular, it is not evidence that the two devices can share a kernel. The frontend's one-physical-subdevice assumption remains constrained to the tested single-device PCI mapping. The next measurable child edge, the optional four-byte `NV2081_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` on the already observed `NV2081_BINAPI`, is documented below; the opaque 992-byte control remains denied.

## Paired `NV2081_BINAPI` allocation payload (2026-10-21)

The pinned R580.178.04 SDK source and R595.99.02 Open source have byte-identical `cl2081.h` files (SHA-256 `cff48618a6175c82ba293a907e1aa5717157fea4870527c7e71a4f777219da0f`). The class is external ID `0x2081`; its allocation structure contains only `NvU32 reserved` (four bytes). Both `resource_list.h` entries map it to internal `BinaryApi`, parent `Subdevice`, and mark `NV2081_ALLOC_PARAMETERS` optional. The R580 and R595 registrations also carry RPC-to-physical-RM, to-vGPU-host and GSP-plugin allocation flags. These are resource-registration properties; they do not establish which handler is chosen at runtime.

The same installed-driver context path on each generation produced one `NV2081_BINAPI` allocation with a present four-byte payload, reserved-zero predicate true, matching entry/exit snapshots and successful syscall/embedded status. Schema v21 constrains observation to this fixed structure and omits all handle values. A separate optional-absent synthetic observer scenario is accepted as absence; no value is inferred in that case. The [paired comparison](../evidence/nv2081-binapi-allocation-comparison-v1-20261021.json), SHA-256 `818d062499c84cb3d048185671cbe48e74d6d3dd222c5c335ce0855ce3285b93`, checks branch/device provenance and typed fields only. It does not assert that BinaryApi behavior or its RM_CONTROL message format is compatible.

The private source audit remains decisive for the neighboring control boundary: R580 Open `BinaryApi` dispatches to physical RM only when its client is a firmware client. On the ordinary physical-GPU context observed here, a zero-valued 992-byte control packet is accepted by the outer transport, but that does not prove it is executed by physical RM or identify the opaque inner message. The observer reads no control pointer. The frontend continues to deny this method. Resolving the dispatch path would require understanding the private R580 implementation and a reviewed message contract; copying an opaque packet or passing it through without ownership/version checks would not be a valid compatibility result.

## `NVOS64` outer size versus RM class allocation size (2026-10-21)

The public SDK documents `NVOS64_PARAMETERS.paramsSize` as a byte count, but the R580 RM allocation path has a separate class-specific copy-size contract. `_nv04AllocWithAccess()` forwards `pAllocParms` and the outer field to `AllocWithSecInfo()`. Later, `serverAllocApiCopyIn()` initializes `RMAPI_PARAM_COPY`, and `rmapiParamsCopyInit()` calls `rmapiGetClassAllocParamSize()` using the external class ID. That function obtains `allocParamSize` and null-optional behavior from the resource descriptor; the `NV2081_BINAPI` entry registers optional `NV2081_ALLOC_PARAMETERS`. `rmapiParamsAcquire()` then validates and copies the derived class size. Source file hashes are bound to the pinned NVIDIA R580.178.04 archive (`060749a0df733af2d0bc863fff4581faca398048d17dbc691d039141ead391d8`); the source tree is under `build/experiments/private-open-binapi-object-v1-20261021/`.

Direct observations resolve the caller side: both schema-v21 stock traces show `paramsSize=0`, non-null `pAllocParms`, a four-byte payload, reserved-zero, stable entry/exit values, and successful status. The [v2 comparison](../evidence/nv2081-binapi-allocation-comparison-v2-20261021.json), SHA-256 `ddda5cb47c8cbac129103970c6f9ef463a13fcebe233963e503fcfe494e59928`, compares the outer size explicitly and finds it equal across branches. The earlier strict pointer/outer-size guard was a falsified hypothesis; its compiled candidate was never installed or loaded. Current frontend CPU tests preserve the observed shape while ensuring an actual failed fixed-size `copy_from_user` does not reach either backend.

Do not infer that every NVOS64 allocator can ignore `paramsSize`: verify each class's resource metadata, exact SDK layout, and real caller shapes. The current BINAPI path accepts only its bounded known forms, uses the class-specific fixed copy size when a pointer is present, and zero-initializes only the SDK-optional absent form. The opaque BINAPI control message remains denied.

## R580 isolated UVM staged-migration preflight (2026-10-28)

The pinned 580.178.04 `uvm_va_block.c` flow first selects GPU sources the destination can access directly. Remaining requested GPU pages can be copied to CPU staging memory; that source-stage tracker is merged into the VA-block tracker before UVM queues CPU-to-destination work. In `uvm_push.c`, isolated mode rejects a pending tracker entry owned by another GPU, while `uvm_tracker_has_other_gpus()` exempts a tracker whose work has already completed. The source chain therefore established a conditional rejection, not a runtime failure; see [the source audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-staged-migration-isolation-v1-20261028T184655Z.json).

A proposed “wait outside the lock, then return `NV_ERR_MORE_PROCESSING_REQUIRED`” was rejected after tracing the retry boundary: `UVM_VA_BLOCK_LOCK_RETRY` reruns the full migration operation, including the GPU-to-CPU stage. Without a durable staged-page/CPU-residency commit, the retry may enqueue the same source stage again and hit the cross-GPU tracker guard repeatedly. Patch [`0010-reject-unsupported-isolated-staged-migrations.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0010-reject-unsupported-isolated-staged-migrations.patch) instead intersects requested pages with each inaccessible GPU's resident mask, limits that intersection to the requested VA-block region, and returns `NV_ERR_NOT_SUPPORTED` before the first copy dispatch. Directly accessible sources and CPU-to-GPU copies do not match this preflight.

The pinned post-patch `uvm_va_block.c` passes the four static checks in [the preflight audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-staged-preflight-audit-v2-20261028T1910Z.json), SHA-256 `9df4a599c94f5d9a54865d99acaf526cc161b23a1f1a5f181ed6f3df53c84d8b`. This is deliberately a fail-closed capability boundary, not implementation of cross-provider managed-memory migration. A safe enabling design still needs persistent staging state and concurrent fault/unmap lifetime validation.

### Exact staged-page preflight refinement (2026-10-28)

The first preflight was deliberately conservative: it rejected a requested page if it appeared on any inaccessible GPU, even when the same page also had a directly accessible read-duplicate. That did not match `block_populate_pages()`, which unions pages on inaccessible resident processors and then removes pages available from any directly accessible resident processor. Patch [`0012-exact-isolated-staged-page-mask.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0012-exact-isolated-staged-page-mask.patch) mirrors that page-set calculation and filters the requested region and discarded pages. The six-check [audit](../evidence/r580-uvm-exact-preflight-audit-v1-20261028T1922Z.json), SHA-256 `a8b13afb9cc88db89e601c2f418e83d7193f0f2862003c170976982d67ebf28a`, verifies both the accessible-duplicate subtraction and the rejection path.

Patch [`0011-preflight-unsupported-staging-before-unmap.patch`](../patches/r580.178.04/uvm/0011-preflight-unsupported-staging-before-unmap.patch) moves the same decision to the beginning of `uvm_va_block_make_resident_copy()`, before UVM invalidates mappings or populates destination GPU chunks. The late check remains before copy dispatch because allocation retry can release the block lock and require fresh state validation. The default patch series therefore has a conservative early fail-closed boundary and a repeated late guard; it still does not stage or execute a cross-GPU managed-memory migration.

The clean Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` R580.178.04 UVM-consumer build is [report v3](../evidence/r580-uvm-early-preflight-build-v3-20261028T1921Z.json), SHA-256 `0135be074a932bb3830dd7a481f85b099a1d4eb967b2e4471403f8292096a20d`. It binds all **77/77** UVM imports to the exact `nvidia` frontend `Module.symvers`; the produced `nvidia-uvm.ko` SHA-256 is `9f0752ad546b3392c7df2d9a3aade403380f3c042e9d19dd197b2dcebd34e301`. The complete software suite passes **1,767/1,767** in **115.723 s**, zero failures or skips: [report](../evidence/software-verification-r580-exact-preflight-v1-20261028T1922Z.json), SHA-256 `d5ef37b444f66885995d802a0ccb41e28775791ebb8d78c8561d570e5964d2fa`; log (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/logs/software-verification-r580-exact-preflight-v1-20261028T1922Z.log`), SHA-256 `5c09db223df5d3403b6df29d71af6ec3f5390e8261b629e77959c024387bd920`.

This is static, compile, and software-suite evidence only. No candidate module was installed or loaded. The mask calculation has not been exercised on a GPU, and cross-GPU migration, UVM faults, concurrent unmap/fault races, kernel execution, P2P, performance, and same-process V100+Blackwell CUDA remain unvalidated.

## R580.196.07 `libcuda` root-query caller/helper correspondence (2026-10-29)

The verified NVIDIA R580.196.07 runfile (`.cache/upstream/NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-580.196.07.run`, SHA-256 `0aab0518db53cf73c412dc7f32bbc850ca95e839d8923d0de4bff42c1c9169ce`) passed its self-extractor checks. Its exact 96,300,904-byte `libcuda.so.580.196.07` was extracted to `/tmp/nv580.196.07-local-analysis/`, not installed or copied into the repository. The image SHA-256 is `a5c77bf2de83c0ec06ecd368d224c3e0d653d658d6f99319921e976f21867b14`, build ID `5069f257e0f767cc016c1251eb9b9933135d4145`. The bounded Ghidra metadata report (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/r58019607-libcuda-ghidra-callsite-v1.json`), SHA-256 `5016f3039f39ad8c86fa8e90dfa688a85468caa66d5a2ce5cf5ae9386a57ff62`, retains no image, project, machine-code window, or raw decompiler text.

The R580.178.04 and R580.196.07 ioctl-helper windows are each 259 bytes. Ghidra places the helper entries at `0x48bc40` and `0x48be00`; the call to `ioctl@plt` occurs at relative offset `+0x43` in both. A bounded objdump normalization produces the same 85-row instruction/operand shape while raw range hashes differ; this supports a shared generic wrapper shape but does not identify the nested request. The [helper comparison](../evidence/r58019607-libcuda-ioctl-helper-diff-v1.json), SHA-256 `e122c56cc46b1672830648311745323ebb040664ff4f836b4fc44e57f09c44f7`, explicitly says it does not prove the `.196` helper handled `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES`.

The R580.196.07 wrapper at RVA `0x460130` sets the fixed command to `0x1f0`, the parameter size to four bytes, and the parameter pointer to a zeroed local dword. It clears its output byte before the call and, on a successful return, tests bit 3 of the feature mask and writes `1` when set. Its 64-byte bounded instruction window has the same 21-instruction normalized shape as `.178` at `0x4600d0`; each direct call is at offset `+0x27` in its window. Raw bytes differ at the provider call target. This is direct static evidence that `.196` contains the root feature-query wrapper and its bit-3 consumer.

The `.196` wrapper passes `cmd=0x1f0`, `paramsSize=4`, and the mask pointer into the generic RM-control dispatcher at `0x495120`. The exact R580.196.07 dispatcher table is 21 fixed 16-byte records, terminated by a zero command at RVA `0x5aa6b60`; no row matches `0x1f0`. Its bounded CFG sends that miss to the zero-terminator fallback at `0x495c68`, which prepares `NVOS54_PARAMETERS` and calls the helper at `0x495c87`. The packet is 32 bytes with fields at offsets `0/4/8/12/16/24/28` (`hClient`, `hObject`, `cmd`, `flags`, `params`, `paramsSize`, `status`), confirmed by compile-time assertions against the pinned `.196` SDK. The helper passes `ioctl(fd, 0xc020462a, packet)`; pinned `nv_escape.h` defines request number `0x2a` as direct `NV_ESC_RM_CONTROL`, while `NV_ESC_IOCTL_XFER_CMD` is the distinct number `0xd3`. Thus the `.196` query’s static command, payload size, and direct kernel ABI are established without classifying this as an XFER request.

The corrected mode-0600 [static route report v2](../evidence/r58019607-libcuda-system-features-static-route-v2.json), SHA-256 `1db87bdaae5e6d6bb7f0969867a1073c9eba80467e1da2e3b4e6e4511f3b2a0f`, retains bounded hashes and metadata, not disassembly or pseudocode. The earlier caller v2 report is preserved as a superseded diagnostic because it mislabeled direct `RM_CONTROL` as ioctl-XFER; the first normalization report also remains as a diagnostic for the call-target normalization bug.

For `.178`, the runtime call-chain report observed returns `0x4600fc`, `0x495acc`, and `0x48bc88` during the feature-mask sensitivity process; the paired same-day root-query capture reported `SYSTEM_GET_FEATURES=0x1f0` and mask `0x9`. The cross-minor runtime section below subsequently confirms that the exact `.196` library issues the same direct request and receives the same mask from stock `.178` RM. The frontend CPU record [7/7](../evidence/focused-system-features-cpu-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `dda8122d7c5e6031cf1f28408bab4af9440b533b172ea74193c2dd851c609417`, confirms direct/XFER input handling, Open-only provider routing, malformed-input rejection, and untouched output after provider failure. It does not validate whether the Open-only answer is semantically sufficient for mixed-provider Volta/Blackwell.

At the time this static-analysis entry was first written, `.196` had not yet been executed. The subsequent bounded PC3 guest runs below used only a temporary process-local library override; they did not load candidate modules, change the VM, alter PCI binding, or replace the stock driver. Candidate-module activation still requires verified signature trust, device ownership, quiescence, and rollback.

## R580.196.07 `libcuda` cross-minor runtime on the stock V100 guest (2026-10-29)

The same stock PC3 V100 guest was tested with its normal R580.178.04 `libcuda` control, then with the exact 96,300,904-byte R580.196.07 image (SHA-256 `a5c77bf2de83c0ec06ecd368d224c3e0d653d658d6f99319921e976f21867b14`) supplied only through a private, process-local `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. The guest remained on kernel `6.8.0-147-generic`, stock driver `580.178.04`, V100 UUID `GPU-42d06a85-027a-75ea-7857-a8de27ad465e`, guest BDF `0000:00:05.0`, SM70, and Driver API 13000.

The `.178` control and `.196` enumeration each completed one direct `NV_ESC_RM_CONTROL` request `0xc020462a` for command `0x1f0`, `paramsSize=4`, in a 32-byte packet; syscall return and RM status were zero, and the returned `featuresMask` was `0x9`. The `.196` context phase successfully executed `cuCtxCreate_v2`, set and queried the current context, and observed one successful `NV01_DEVICE_0` allocation with two fixed-size 56-byte snapshots. In a separate fresh process, the `.196` kernel phase loaded `hybrid_arch`, called `cuMemAlloc_v2`, launched and synchronized the kernel, copied the result to the host, and verified **700 == 700**. Probe cleanup passed; all three runs started and ended with zero compute processes, guest memory returned to zero, the guest driver identity remained `.178.04`, and staged files and private loader directories were removed.

The mode-0600 [cross-minor report v2](../evidence/r58019607-libcuda-stock-v100-crossminor-runtime-v2-20261029.json), SHA-256 `c65bf0b2fd1cab0c2a14d720cb540b08b43d3ec0de09946f8d75fa67e19cfeb3`, validates and hash-links the raw [`.178` control](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580178-system-features-control-v1-20261029.json), [`.196` enumeration](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580196-libcuda-system-features-v2-20261029.json), [context](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580196-context-device-alloc-v1-20261029.json), [kernel](../evidence/pc3-v100-r580196-kernel-device-alloc-v1-20261029.json), and [static route](../evidence/r58019607-libcuda-system-features-static-route-v2.json). Runner contracts pass **110/110** in the mode-0600 [test report](../evidence/r58019607-override-runner-tests-v1-20261029.json), SHA-256 `05a112f3f47e3d19272ab4e0049db4d40aff7af06bbc97aeac747a5f9cd1dd0c`.

This is a real single-V100 cross-minor CUDA kernel result, not a matched `.196` kernel/user stack or the experimental hybrid stack. It does not validate the same-process V100+Blackwell criterion, UVM migration, P2P, multi-GPU concurrency, long-run stability, or performance. In particular, the V100 remains assigned to its guest while the RTX 5080 is on the PC3 host's separate Linux instance.

## R580.196.07 user library rejected by the stock R595 Blackwell stack (2026-10-29)

The exact 96,300,904-byte R580.196.07 `libcuda.so.580.196.07` (SHA-256 `a5c77bf2de83c0ec06ecd368d224c3e0d653d658d6f99319921e976f21867b14`) was copied to a private, mode-0700 PC3 temporary workspace and selected only for one probe process with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. On the unchanged PC3 host kernel `7.0.0-38-generic`, RTX 5080 `0000:02:00.0`, and R595.99.02 kernel module, `cuInit` returned `CUDA_ERROR_SYSTEM_DRIVER_MISMATCH` (803). The error occurred before `cuDriverGetVersion`, enumeration, context creation, or kernel execution. The installed R595 `libcuda`, loaded module hash, GPU identity and PCI binding matched before and after; the process list was empty and the staged workspace was removed.

The mode-0600 [capture v2](../evidence/pc3-rtx5080-r580196-libcuda-sm120-v2-20261029.json), SHA-256 `b1f66c8d23003b06941fca24f317c6bf0f6ea33ce00eced10c616d8df91933d1`, preserves the negative outcome and cleanup checks. The first runner capture is retained as an unqualified diagnostic; the revised runner recognizes the exact 803 result as a completed negative experiment rather than a host-identity failure. Its [source](../tools/run_pc3_rtx5080_r580196_crossminor.py) is SHA-256 `bc0dbead8984ff1476988c78c62a9f19baec90a92349fcac0fe3a0d028a90e21`; the focused contracts pass **6/6**, and the complete **2,084/2,084** software suite passes in the [mode-0600 report](../evidence/software-verification-crossminor-libraries-v3-20261029.json), SHA-256 `adecac42b08276302045e02e53ac780f85e6deda9b46fec0dd2dfeaf1c8f4e5e`.

NVIDIA's Driver API reference defines 803 as a mismatch between the display-driver and CUDA-driver versions ([official definition](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/cuda-driver-api/cuda_driver_api/group__CUDA__TYPES.html)). The hardware result therefore rejects this particular cross-branch user/kernel pairing. Together with successful SM70 execution from R580.196.07 userland on the stock R580.178.04 V100 guest, it supports a same-branch user/kernel pair for the first hybrid canary. It does not show that every R580/R595 pairing fails, does not test loading both libraries behind a proxy, and does not exercise the statically built R580.196.07 hybrid modules on Blackwell.

## Runtime selection of the common CUDA user library (2026-10-29)

The selected user-space front end remains R595.99.02 `libcuda` (static dossier SHA-256 `192d4eedb1b39f0568f2b5423d35227d13b32b9025599e1968a9081982da207d`) because the candidate kernel front end and UVM are R595.99.02. This is an ABI-coherence choice, not proof that R595 `libcuda` can initialize GV100. The observed counterexample is specific: R580.196.07 `libcuda` on the unchanged R595.99.02 RTX 5080 stack returned CUDA error 803 before enumeration. No experiment in this increment loaded the R595 library on a V100.

The acceptance path now supports `--libcuda-override` plus a mandatory expected SHA-256 and confirms the probe-reported resolved path and post-run file hash. This closes the prior ambiguity where the probe inherited the process loader's library choice and only reported it after execution. The file is supplied by the caller; this work neither packages the R595 proprietary library nor establishes its V100 compatibility. See [`VALIDATION.md`](VALIDATION.md#explicit-hash-pinned-cuda-user-library-selection-2026-10-29) for the CPU-only checks and qualification boundary.

## GT200_DEBUGGER allocation payload and dual-client dispatch (2026-11-01 UTC)

Pinned open-source trees for R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 contain the same `cl83de.h` (SHA-256 `44835665d85dd315f6cb398e6f4a016eea4756acf6088f0c7e800c22c159d21a`) and byte-identical `GT200_DEBUGGER` resource registration (block SHA-256 `59c50239f2437ae512846c8a03ebbcfde7f7d3dfdf9b7cd3a2b0810b50aac537`). The allocation payload is three 32-bit `NvHandle` fields at offsets 0, 4, and 8: obsolete debugger client (required zero), `hAppClient`, and `hClass3dObject`. Registration resolves to `KernelSMDebuggerSession`, allows multiple instances, requires the NV01 device parent and `NV83DE_ALLOC_PARAMETERS`, and sets `RS_FLAGS_DUAL_CLIENT_LOCK`.

In all four branches, the RM second-client lookup for this class selects `hAppClient`; the audited lookup body hash is `0066c8396db3fa1d1dcee37a1bfa66418c19f55f2e754669e07f27e792639d94`. The branch-specific RM source hashes, constructor facts, and resource-list hashes are recorded in [`gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json`](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json), SHA-256 `034709565f6f522c23523aa2b48ac18314fed2f6ae5f50b29490b5f42c6fe9be`. The matching `libcuda` disassembly audit for R580.178.04 and R595.99.02 sets the obsolete field to zero and constructs the class-specific 48-byte NVOS64 allocation packet with a non-null payload pointer and outer `paramsSize == 0`. Existing pinned V100 and RTX 5080 header summaries each show one successful class allocation with parent class `0x80`; those summaries explicitly did not read the nested 12-byte payload.

The open constructor at `nvidia/src/kernel/gpu/gr/kernel_sm_debugger_session.c` separates the SDK's “child of compute or 3D” prose from the outer RM parent. The external RM wrapper's `hObjectParent` is a device-class parent; the constructor separately resolves `hClass3dObject` within `hAppClient`, requires it to be a `KernelGraphicsObject`, checks that the debugger and debuggee use the same GPU, obtains the app channel from the debuggee object's parent, selects the corresponding subdevice instance, and registers the debugger session with that graphics context. A zero `hAppClient` falls back to the caller client. Depending on GSP/hypervisor build and runtime conditions, it validates matching security tokens or requires `RS_ACCESS_DEBUG`. The best reconciliation (inference) is that the SDK comment describes the logical debuggee association; it does not change the outer RM parent contract.

Engineering implication: a hybrid frontend must preserve the app-client as the second-client lock owner while routing the parented RM object and nested debuggee handles in the correct provider/root domain. It must also preserve the debuggee GPU, channel, subdevice, and access/security relationships. Static parity does not prove those handles resolve in a live mixed-provider process, that CUDA always creates this object, or that allocation succeeds in the candidate frontend.

The R580.196.07 source snapshot has a concrete constructor-failure cleanup change: it initializes the internal client before registering the debugger session, tracks `bRegistered`, and routes failures through `_ksmdbgssnCleanup`. The R580.178.04, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 constructor snapshots register before `_ksmdbgssnInitClient` and have no equivalent constructor unwind helper. This may prevent a partially initialized object/client from surviving an allocation failure; that leak consequence remains an inference, and no patch has been ported. The static source hashes and per-branch facts are in [`gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json`](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v4-20261101T0307Z.json), SHA-256 `034709565f6f522c23523aa2b48ac18314fed2f6ae5f50b29490b5f42c6fe9be`.

The new local observer reads only the fixed 12-byte class payload when explicitly requested during an isolated selected-GPU context phase. Its CPU fixture and integration contract pass, but the synthetic ioctl returned `-ENOTTY` and reported no resolved live objects. PC3 is currently powered off per the user's instruction; no connection was attempted. Once available, the next discriminating experiment is the same capture on PC3's RTX 5080; compare nested handle classes against the device parent and determine whether the app-client is the root, caller, or a distinct client. No V100 nested payload has been captured by this new observer, and no hardware behavior is inferred from the synthetic run.

## Targeted Ghidra analysis of the R580 debugger control route (2026-11-01)

Ghidra 12.1.4 is vendored in `build/tools/ghidra_12.1.4_PUBLIC/`, although its launcher is not on `PATH`. The prior inventory omitted this bundled engine. The REA executable and REA MCP capability were unavailable, so the already-installed Ghidra engine was used without downloading or installing software.

Two headless runs imported only the hash-pinned R580.178.04 `kernel-open/nvidia/nv-kernel.o_binary` object and decompiled nine selected symbols related to `KernelSMDebuggerSession`, `GpuResource`, `_gpuRmApiControl`, and `_rmapiRmControl`. The decompiler output is kept private under `build/experiments/r580-gt200-debugger-ghidra-v1-20261101/`, labeled as pseudocode, and is not represented as recovered NVIDIA source. The input and per-function output hashes are in the receipt (local-only artifact; excluded from GitLab transfer; source: `evidence/r580-gt200-debugger-route-ghidra-v1-20261101.json`), SHA-256 `0d6e20c8b07a956732cfc97b3898bb127d4303a2881c1cd38bf18faca6516b9f`.

The object-level pseudocode confirms that the internal-object getter returns constant zero and that the internal-control method dispatches through the generic GPU-resource forwarder. The matching open-source functions show that this forwarder passes the resource client's handle and the virtual getter's object handle to the physical RM API. The GPU fast path requires both the internal client and internal subdevice handles; otherwise it calls the generic RM control interface. Resource Server then looks up the supplied object handle in the client's resource map; standard allocation rejects zero handles. The source auditor pins those function bodies across R580.178.04, R580.196.07, R595.99.02, and R615.71.10 ([audit v7](../evidence/gt200-debugger-alloc-static-v7-20261101.json)).

This establishes a conditional static route conflict, not a live failure: the source-level composition indicates the candidate exception-mask bridge can reach the generated internal forwarder, but no same-host hardware trace yet proves which dispatcher runs or whether a special route intervenes. No code was copied from the decompiler and no binary was modified.

## R580.196.07 Volta UVM callbacks in the R595.112.07 build — 2026-11-03

The isolated HAL probe now uses the latest pinned Volta-capable UVM source subset from the R580.196.07 package (source archive SHA-256 `21914a460f17190c43cffb75f0c35aef957e267bfefea267bc18638beae17084`; package SHA-256 `0aab0518db53cf73c412dc7f32bbc850ca95e839d8923d0de4bff42c1c9169ce`) and the R595.112.07 Open GPU Kernel Modules commit `d8257d95ae5fed364163e4df51db66df8b1ee0d2`. The UVM source tree's `kernel-open` Git tree is `3076623c4aae49985df488e5266cf0a003042603`; its worktree was clean when selected. The adaptation reuses the pinned R580 Volta functions and explicitly stages six parentless GV100 rows into the modern HAL tables, with compile-only adapters for changed MMU/page-size and system-memory aperture contracts. The build resolves all 82 selected callbacks and includes the Volta fault-buffer object.

The transfer-safe [compile receipt](../evidence/r595112-gv100-hal-r580196-sanitized-v1-20261103.json) binds the R595.112.07 module hash and build-log digest without retaining local paths or decompiler pseudocode. This is an open-source static integration result; it does not show that RM selects the rows, that their callbacks are semantically correct on hardware, or that CUDA/UVM works. No proprietary binary was used for this compile result. The targeted REA/Ghidra result is recorded below as pseudocode analysis and is not treated as NVIDIA source.

## REA/Ghidra review of the hybrid GV100 GET callback — 2026-11-03

REA 6.8.0 with Ghidra 12.1.4 analyzed the exact patched candidate `nvidia-uvm.ko` SHA-256 `dea278c8e2593ebf5054da6ff22622f6c2fd8e4a6c9186973a70222d481f99f5`; evidence ID `ev_9f72005d79533aa95760a713a14e7af2b9819350b10bfb2dbf8fc17e0de105ef`. The decompiler output is incomplete: Ghidra classifies the entry `__fentry__` call as terminal and leaves the local GET value unresolved. No claims are taken from this pseudocode.

The exact candidate's `objdump -dr` body independently shows a call relocation to `__fentry__`, a pointer load at parent offset `0x1090`, one 32-bit dereference, masking with `0x000fffff`, and a jump relocation to `__x86_return_thunk` (31-byte function). This is consistent with the pinned source body: read the Volta fault-buffer GET register once, assert that the value is within `max_faults`, then return `FAULT_BUFFER_GET.PTR`. The compiled body has no compare/test branch corresponding to that `UVM_ASSERT`, consistent with assertion elision in this artifact only. The disassembly does not establish MMIO semantics, assertion behavior in other build modes, callback selection, or correctness on GV100 hardware. The [path-free sanitized receipt](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-gv100-read-get-rea-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `b6581b968bd28640f8581083946c03441cc38a0f4136d89918fd27475c67dfbb`, records tool versions, input identities, the decompiler limitation, and the independent instruction observations. Raw REA output and disassembly remain local ignored artifacts.

### Ghidra entry-hook correction follow-up — 2026-11-03

The original headless analysis marked the internal `__fentry__` thunk `hasNoReturn=true`, causing the decompiler to stop at compiler instrumentation. A targeted Ghidra metadata transaction changed only that function-flow attribute to returning; it did not edit the ELF. The new reusable [`ReclassifyKernelEntryHookAndDecompile.java`](../tools/ghidra/ReclassifyKernelEntryHookAndDecompile.java) script was then run against the same program and exact module hash. With the corrected analysis metadata, Ghidra emits a return path, but the optimized local GET value remains unresolved (`in_EAX` plus an unresolved local comment). This is still incomplete pseudocode and is not treated as source recovery.

The independent object disassembly and pinned source remain the useful evidence: they agree on one 32-bit GET-register load and extraction of the low 20 pointer bits. The [v2 sanitized receipt](../evidence/r595112-hybrid-gv100-read-get-rea-sanitized-v2-20261103.json) records both the initial classification and the limited follow-up. No hardware, module load, GPU access, or runtime validation occurred.

## R595.112.07 UVM global and per-GPU initialization call edges — 2026-11-03

REA 6.8.0 with Ghidra 12.1.4 analyzed two functions from the exact project-built open `nvidia-uvm.ko` (Dual MIT/GPL, version 595.112.07, Linux `7.0.0-38-generic` vermagic; SHA-256 `7284d09d2f3ddafc24805ce9702d3bc596a3f2919468f79c5d9a9ac586f3cd48`). The path-free [receipt](../evidence/r595112-open-uvm-add-gpu-ghidra-sanitized-v1-20261103.json), SHA-256 `1df041ce299c910243a52a0d77990f851cc9e382cbb4d7cdfabe879438418508`, records resolved call edges and exact input hashes without pseudocode or local paths.

The first target, `uvm_gpu_init`, is a global initializer: Ghidra reports six resolved direct callees including `uvm_hal_init_table`, and the resolved caller is `uvm_global_init`. The per-GPU registration function is `add_gpu`: it has 66 resolved direct callees, including `uvm_hal_init_gpu`, and the resolved caller is `uvm_gpu_retain_by_uuid`. These results correct the misleading implication that the symbol `uvm_gpu_init` itself is the per-device path.

The result is static evidence about this exact compiled open module. Ghidra reports complete coverage of its resolved call relationships while warning that computed and indirect calls remain unknown. It does not establish which HAL row is selected, the RM-returned architecture or classes, successful initialization, module load, CUDA/UVM behavior, or correctness on a GPU. Raw dossiers remain private with mode 0600; no NVIDIA proprietary binary was analyzed for this receipt.

The matching pinned R595 source explains why this edge matters: `uvm_hal_init_gpu()` looks up the RM-reported CE and host class IDs, the GPU architecture, and the optional `architecture | implementation` chip override. It then requires architecture-indexed fault-buffer, access-counter-buffer, and SEC2 rows; a missing required row returns `NV_ERR_INVALID_CLASS`. The parent GPU's `rm_info` is copied from the UVM GPU information supplied by RM before this initializer runs. Thus adding only a GV100 architecture row is insufficient: the six mandatory lookup tables and the actual R580 provider values must agree. The compiled overlay already stages six explicit parentless GV100 rows, but the current receipt and build do not prove a live RM returned those values or that hardware initialization passed the lookups.
